***aprākṛtaṁ surair vandyam ayutārka-sama-prabham \|\|***

**The place called Vaikuṇṭha is beyond the material world, endowed with six qualities, devoid of the three material guṇas and not obtainable by non-devotees. It is filled with eternally perfect devotees engaged in the five actions. There are halls and palaces, which are resplendent with forests and groves. The place is decorated with wells, ponds and canals and groves of trees. This spiritual place, praised by the devotees, is as effulgent as ten thousand suns. When will I see you, Keśava, source of all pastimes, endowed with excellent, pure powers, with my eyes, as you play with your beloved? \|\|33\|\|**

### Text 34

*Bhagavān sutra-kāraś caivam āha—“antarā bhūta-grāmavat svātmanaḥ” ity asmin adhikaraṇe \|\|34\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord says in Vedānta-sūtras:**

**antarā bhūta-grāmavat svātmanaḥ \|\|34\|\|**

### Text 35

*tad etat sva-mahimādi-śabditaṁ tad dhāma-vaikuṇṭha-dvāravatyādi yathorddhaṁ sphuratīti, tad-gatāvirbhāveṣu tat- tad-abhimāneṣu viśeṣaś ceti viśiṣṭāgamānāṁ viduṣāṁ niścayaḥ \|\|35\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Those abodes described by the words** ***sva-mahimā*** **manifest at higher and higher levels as Vaikuṇṭha, Dvārakā etc. There is variety in the manifestations of the Lord and his identity in these places. This is confirmed by specific scriptures and specific learned people. \|\|35\|\|**

### Text 36

*yāny eva dhāmāni tat-tal-līlārtham ajāṇḍe ‘py aviḥsyur iti skānde smaryate “yā yathā bhuvi vartante puryo bhagavataḥ priyāḥ \| tās tathā santi vaikuṇṭhe tat-tal-līlārtham ādṛtāḥ” iti \| āsu divyatvāsphūrtis tv asaṁskṛta-dṛṣām eva bhagavati nara-dāraka-rūpatvādivat \| tathāpi tad dṛṣṭyā śubha-loka-prāptis tādṛṣa-bhāgavaḍ dṛṣṭyaiveti – “na sāmānyād apy upalabdher mṛtyuvan na hi lokākpattiḥ” iti sūtrāt tad bhāvāc ca \| tasmāt tatra taiḥ sahāsau nityaṁ līlāyate bālya-paugaṇḍa-kaiśora-sambandhās tā līlā nityā vibhāntīti siddham \|\|36\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The abodes of the Lord also appear in the material world for assisting the Lord’s pastimes. In Skanda Purāṇa it is said:**

**yā yāthā bhuvi vartante puryo bhagavataḥ priyāḥ**  
**tās tathā santi vaikuṇṭhe tat-tal-līlartham ādṛṭāḥ**

**For persons with nonspiritual eyes, these places do not seem spiritual, just as people see the Lord as a human child. However, even such people attain an auspicious place by seeing the Lord, according to the Lord’s words.**

**na sāmānyād apy upalabdher mṛtyuvan na hi lokāpattiḥ**

**This is not true. From seeing the Lord in a general way one does not get liberation, just as dying does not give liberation. One attains a higher planet by seeing the Lord without bhakti. This is not liberation. Vedānta-sūtra 3.3.53**

**In that place, the Lord enjoys pastimes with his associates. The eternal pastimes of bālya, paugaṇḍa and kaiśora are revealed there. This has been proven. \|\|36\|\|**

### Text 37

*atrāhuḥ – tad deśa-tat-kāla-tat-parikārāvicchede saty eva tāsāṁ nityatvam syān na caivaṁ sambhavati, deśādi-bhedād ekasya parikarasya pūrvottara-vartitvenāneka-līlā-sambandha-smaraṇāc ca \| pūrvasyā līlāyā nityatvena tat-sambandhinaḥ parikarasyāpi sambandhas tatra nityaṁ syāt \| tathā saty uttarasām tasya sambandho durghaṭaḥ \| svīkṛte ca tasmin pūrvasyā anityatvam \| nityatve cottara-sambandhinas tasyānyatvaṁ bhavet \| tasmād viyat puṣpāyamānaṁ bhavatāṁ līla-nityatvaṁ iti cet \|\|37\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Someone may object. “If the places, times and associates are continuous, they should all be eternal. But that is not possible. One sees that one associate exists previously and later in time, in relation to specific pastimes, with difference in place etc. Since the previous pastime is eternal, the relationship of that particular associate in that pastime with that pastime should be eternal. Then it would be impossible for that associate to participate in a later pastime. If one accepts him to be in the later pastime, then his participation in the previous pastime would not be eternal. If the previous pastime is eternal his participation in the later pastime would not be eternal. Therefore the eternal pastimes you speak of are like a flower in the sky.” \|\|37\|\|**

### Text 38

*atrocyate – anabhijño bhavān acintyasya bhagavat tattvasya yad aupaniṣadam tat \| tathāhi chāndogye “bhūmaiva sukhaṁ bhūmā tv eva vijijñāsitavyaḥ” iti bhūmānam uddiśya tasya lakṣaṇam ucyate, “yatra nānyat paśyati nānyac śṛṇoti nānyād vijānāti sa bhumā” iti \| atra bhumānaṁ prāptasya tad itara-darśanādi pratiṣidhyate anantaraṁ tu mṛtvyādi-heya-darśanaṁ niṣedhya punas tasya sarva-darśanam ucyate \| “na paśyo mṛtyuṁ paśyati na rogaṁ nota duḥkhitām \| sarvaṁ ha paśyaḥ paśyati sarvam āpnoti sarvaśaḥ” iti \| atra sarva-sabdena vividha vaicitryavatyo bālyādi sambandhās tasya līlāḥ kathyante \| tāḥ paśyaṁ paśyati tasya tat parikaratvena tad aṅgatvād āpnoti ca yathādhikāram iti \| iha bālyādi sambandhānāṁ tāsām mitho vailakṣaṇyāt kaiśora-sambandhānāṁ saṁyoga-viyogātmanā sphuṭa-dvaividhyātvāc ca vaividhya-vaicitrye suvyakte \|\|38\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**You are ignorant of the inconceivable nature of Bhagavān, which is explained in the Upaniṣads.**

**bhūmaiva sukhaṁ bhūmā tv eva vijijñāsitavyaḥ**

**Bhūmā is happiness. One should inquire about bhūmā. Chāndogya Upaniṣad**

**Chāndogya then says:**

**yatra nānyat paśyati nānyac chṛṇoti nānyad vijānāti sa bhūmā**

**Attaining bhūmā, seeing other things is forbidden. But after forbidding seeing death and other detestable things, again it is said that one should see everything.**

***na paśyo mṛtyuṁ paśyati na rogaṁ nota-duḥkhitaṁ sarvaṁ hi paśyaḥ paśyati sarvam āpnoti sarvaśaḥ***

**The liberated jīva does not see death, sickness or suffering; seeing everything, he sees and obtains everything in all ways. Chāndogya Upaniṣad**

**In that statement, the word** ***sarva*** **(all) indicates the jīva’s participation in the Lord’s pastimes, full of variety, related to bālya and other ages. Seeing those pastimes (*****paśyaḥ*****), he sees. He experiences those pastimes since he is an associate, a participating element in the pastimes, according to his qualification.**

**Since there are differences among all the pastimes related to bālya and other ages, and divisions of meeting and separation in pastimes related to kaiśora period, it is clear that variety and contrasting types of pastimes are manifested among the devotees. \|\|38\|\|**

### Text 39

*atha mitho vilakṣaṇāsu deśa-kāla-bhedenea jāyamānāsu bahvīṣu tāsu ekasya parikarasya yugapat sānnidyāsambhavāt kathaṁ sarva-prakāraka-darśāna-prāptiḥ sambhavati tad asambhave vā kathaṁ tāsāṁ tad yoga-ghaṭitānāṁ nitya-viśaṅkāyāṁ tad anantaram idaṁ paṭhyate “sa ekadhā bhavati dvidhā bhavati tridhā bhavati pañcadhā saptadhā navadhā caiva punaś caikādaśa smṛtaḥ \| śataṁ ca daśa caikaṁ ca sahasrāṇi ca viṁśatiḥ” iti \| atra siddhi-balād bahūni rūpāṇi tasya lilā-prepsor āvirbhavanti \| tair asau tatra tatra samnidadhyad iti tasya sarvatraikyāt tad aviccheda siddhir ato na kācid anupapattiḥ \|\|39\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since many distinct pastimes manifest at various times and places, how is it possible for one associate to see them all, since it is impossible for him to exist simultaneously in all places. Or with that impossibility, the doubt arises as to the eternal nature of the pastimes if they happen at specific times and places. But this passage is also found in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad afterwards:**

**sa ekadhā bhavati dvidhā bhavati tridhā bhavati pañcadhā saptadhā navadhā caiva punaś caikādaśa smṛtaḥ \| śataṁ ca daśa caikaṁ ca sahasrāṇi ca viṁśatiḥ**

**The Lord is one, he is two, he is three, he is five, he is seven, he is nine, and he is eleven. He is a hundred and eleven and he is a thousand twenty. Chāndogya Upaniṣad**

**Since the Lord is completely full, desiring pastimes, he manifest many forms. Since his associates are close to him in these various pastimes and are all one with him, it must be concluded that the associates are continually with him in the pastimes. There is no contradiction. \|\|39\|\|**

### Text 40

*īdṛśa-līlā-viśiṣṭam eva svarūpaṁ rasa-śabditaṁ tad avāptasyaiva paramānandaṁ śrutir āha—“raso vai saḥ rasaṁ by evāyaṁ labdhvānandī bhavati” iti\| saṁyogasyaiva viyogasyāpi rasatvena bhavad rūpatvāt taj jñānam api tad anubhava eveti tasya sārvadīka-sākṣātkāra-siddhis tena tena ca viśiṣtā khalu līlā nityā \| tatrādye bahir aste tu hṛdyeva sākṣāt-kṛtiḥ \| ithaṁca sārvadika-sāhitya-sattve ‘pi sāyaṁ vīkṣāpratijñā sāmprataṁ pariśvaṅgābhyarthanādayo vyavahārāḥ siddhyeyur iti śruti-tātparya-sahāyavān eṣa nirṇayaḥ\| dhāmnāḥ samayasya ca bhagavatd ātmakasya sarvatra sānnidhyaṁ vakṣyate \| alaukike ‘cintya-vastuni tarkānavatāro na duṣaṇaṁ api bhūṣaṇam eva mahimonnāhāt \|\|40\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The śruti speaks of the Lord’s svarūpa endowed with such pastimes. He is called rasa and one who attains this Lord attains the highest bliss.**

**raso vai saḥ rasaṁ by evāyaṁ labdhvānandī bhavati**

**The Lord is truly rasa. Attaining the Lord who is rasa one attains bliss. Taittirīya Upaniṣad**

**Since the Lord’s nature is rasa in either union or separation from the devotee, the devotee is with the Lord in both these cases, and experiences him. Thus it is proved that the devotee always experiences the presence of the Lord. And thus the Lord is endowed with eternal pastimes. The union with the devotee has the external presence of the Lord and separation has the internal presence of the Lord.**

**Thus, though the Lord is always with the devotee, he promises to the gopīs that in the evening they will see him, and embraces them. This is discerned from the meaning of śruti. It should be said that at all times the abode and time which are nondifferent from the Lord are always with him. Material logic cannot be applied to something which is inconceivalte and extraordinary. This is not a fault but an ornament, since it showed the Lord’s greatness. \|\|40\|\|**

### Text 41

*atrāhuḥ: -- nanu līlāyāḥ kiryātvāt pratyaṁśam añārambhasamāptibhyāṁ tasyāḥ siddhir vācyāḥ tābhyām vinā tat svarūpanāśāpittir ataḥ katham asau nityetic et \|\|41\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Someone may object as follows. “Since pastimes are actions, how can they be eternal? Each portion of a pastime has a beginning and an end in order to be accomplished. Without those elements the essence of the pastime would be destroyed.” \|\|41\|\|**

### Text 42

*atrocyate --bhagavati sadaivākārānantyāt prakāśānantyāl lilānantyād ananta-brahmāṇḍānanta-vaikuṇṭhagatānāṁ tat-tal-līlā-sthānānāṁ tat-tat-pārṣadānāṁ ca vyakti-prakāśānānanyāc ca nānityavaṁ tasyāḥ \| tat tad ākara-prakāśa-gatayos tat-tad-ārambha-samāptyoḥ satyor apy ekatraikatra tat-tat-kriyāṁśā yāvat samāpyante na samāpyante vā tāvad evānyatrāpy ārabdhāḥ syur ity evam tasmiṁs tad avicchedānityaiva seti tatra kvacit kincit vailakṣaṇyena kvacit tv aika-rūpyenārambhaḥ viśeṣaṇānāṁ bhedāsaṁbhedāc ca \|\|42\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The pastimes are not temporary since the Lord’s forms are unlimitied, his manifestations are unlimited, his pastimes are unlimited, and manifestations of his associates and places in unlimited universes and Vaikuṇṭhas are unlimited. Though there are beginnings and ends of various pastimes in his various forms and manifestations, though a portion ends, it begins in another place. Thus each portion of the pastimes is constinously being enacted. Therefore the pastime is not temporary but eternal. Sometimes the action which begins is different and sometimes the same, since there is difference and nondifference in details. \|\|42\|\|**

### Text 43

*nanv astv avicchedaḥ kathaṁ sā vartate ity ucyate pṛthāg-ārambhādi-vairaseti cet? Ucyate – kāla-bhedenoditānām api tulya-rūpāṇāṁ kriyāṇām aikyaṁ yathā pareṣāṁ api “dvir gośabdo ‘yam uccārito, na tu dvau gośabdāv iti pratīti-niścitaṁ śabdaikatvaṁ” yathā dviḥ pāko ‘nena kṛto, na tu dvau pākāv iti kriyaikyaṁ ca tathāitat pratyetavyaṁ \| ataevam āha śrutiḥ “yad gataṁ bhavac cabhaviṣyac ca” iti “eko devo nitya-līlānurakto bhakavyāpī bhakta-hṛdy antarātmā “ iti ca, smṛtiś ca “janma karma ca me divyam evaṁ yo vetti tattvataḥ \| tyakvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti māmeiti s ‘rjuna” iti \| īdṛśāvagatis tv atirahasyā bhagavad anugrahaika-sādhyeti “yāvān ahaṁ yathā bhāvo yad rūpa-guṇa-karmakaḥ \| tathaiva tattva-vijñānam astu te mad anugrahāt” iti tad ukteḥ \|\|43\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“Accepting that the portions of the pastimes are continuous, how do they exist since they have separate beginnings?” The answer is this. The same pastime which arises at different times is actually one pastime. Śaṅkara gives the following example:**

**dvir-go-śabdobhayam uccarito na tu dvau**  
**go-śabdāv iti pratīti-nirṇītaṁ śabdaikatvam**  
**dviḥ pāko ‘nena kṛto, na tu dvau pākāv**

**When one says the word “cow” twice, one does not perceive two different words but one. One can light the fire in the hearth twice, but there is only one fire.**

**One perceives only one action. Thus, śruti says:**

**yad gataṁ bhavac ca bhaviṣyac ca**

**The Lord was present in the past, is present now and will be present in the future. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.8.3)**

**eko devo nitya-līlānurato bhakta-vyāpī bhakta-hṛdy-antarātmā**

**The one Lord, engaged in eternal pastimes, enters the devotees and remains there as many forms in their hearts. (*****Māṭhara śruti*****)**

**Smṛti says:**

**janma karma ca me divyam evaṁ yo vetti tattvataḥ \|**  
**tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti mām eti so ’rjuna \|\|**

**He who understands in truth my spiritual birth and activities attains me on giving up his body, and does not take birth again. BG 4.9**

**However, such an understanding is very secret and is attained only by the Lord’s mercy:**

**By my mercy, may you attain perfect realization of whatever dimensions, intentions, forms, qualities and pastimes I manifest. BG 4.9 \|\|43\|\|**

### Text 44

*kiṁ ca “na paśyo mṛtyuṁ paśyati” ity atraikasyaiva pārṣadasya yugapat sarva-līlā-kārmaka-darśana-prāptyoḥ pratipādanāt bhagavad-rūpas tatra kālas tad-rūpa-ravi-candrādy-udayāstamana-siddha-divā-rātri-mātratayā tad iṣṭa-tat-tal-līlānuguṇyavān pracakāsti na tv ayana-vatsarādi-rūpatāṁ bhajati tāvataiva tat-tal-līlā-rasa-niṣpatter yugapat sarvartūdaya-sattvāc ca \| tad anuguṇatayāiva tad aṁśāvirbhāva-tirobhāvau syātām \| evam abhipretyaiva smaryate--“sa yatra kṣīrābdhiḥ sarati surabhibhyaś ca sumahān nimeṣārdhākhyo vā vrajati na hi yatrāpi samayaḥ \| bhaje śveta-dvīpaṁ tam aham iha golokam iti yaṁ vidantas te santaḥ kśiti-virala-cārāḥ katipaye” iti \| evaṁ dhāmno pi tad rūpasya sarvatra sambhandaḥ \| tad utkaṁ vṛddhaiḥ “ataḥ prabhoḥ priyāṇāṁ ca dhāmnaś ca samayasya ca avicintya-prabhāvatvān nātra kiṁcit sudurghaṭam” iti \| “prakṛtebhyo ‘nye candra-sūryādayo grahāḥ līlā-sthair anubhūyante tathāpi prākṛtā iva” iti ca \|\|44\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, it was said that he who sees the pastimes does not see death. It was explained that one associate sees all pastimes and attains them simultaneously. Thus, the form of the Lord and the time manifested at that time, with day and night caused by the rising and setting of the sun and moon, manifest, following after the quality of the pastimes, and not like material passing of years, since there is the manifestation of all seasons simultaneously to accomplish the rasa of the particular pastime. According to the pastime, there is appearance and disappearace of portions of time. With this meaning īntended, there are various statements in the scriptures.**

***sa yatra kṣīrābdhiḥ sravati surabhībhyaś ca su-mahān***  
***nimeṣārdhākhyo vā vrajati na hi yatrāpi samayaḥ \|***  
***bhaje śvetadvīpaṁ tam aham iha golokam iti yaṁ***  
***vidantas te santaḥ kṣiti-virala-cārāḥ katipaye \|\|***

**I worship Goloka, pure and uncontaminated, where extensive oceans of milk flow from the cows, where not even a moment of time passes, and which only a few rare devotees wandering on this earth have realized. Brahma-saṁhitā 56**

**There is also a relationship between the abode of the Lord and the form of the Lord in all cases. The sage says:**

***ataḥ prabhoḥ priyāṇāṁ ca dhāmnaś ca samayasya ca \|***  
***avicintya-prabhāvatvād atra kiṁ ca na durghaṭam*** **\|\|**

**In Gokula, nothing is impossible for the Lord, the devotees, the abode and everything in the abode, by the power of the Lord’s inconceivable energy. Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta 515**

***prâk?tebhyo grahebhyo ’nye candra-sḹryâdayas tu te***  
***lîlâ-sthair anubhḹyante tathâpi prâk?tâ iva***

**The moon, sun and other celestial bodies are different from the material planets, but they appear to be material by the devotees situated in the Lord’s manifested pastimes. Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta 517 \|\|44\|\|**

### Text 45

*ataeva devarṣiṇā bahubhir ahobhir bhāvya-līlā ekenaivāhnā dṛṣṭa iti śuko nirūpayām āsa –“narakaṁ nihataṁ śrutvā tathodvāhaṁ ca yoṣitāṁ “ ityādinā \| na ca bahubhir ahobhis tena tā dṛṣṭā iti vācyam, tathā sati yogamāyā-mahodaye kṣaṇa-hetu tad-vismayānupapatteḥ \|\|45\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus Nārada, saw in one day many days’ pastimes. Śukadeva describes this:**

***narakaṁ nihataṁ śrutvā tathodvāhaṁ ca yoṣitām***  
***kṛṣṇenaikena bahvīnāṁ tad-didṛkṣuḥ sma nāradaḥ***

**Hearing that Lord Kṛṣṇa had killed Narakāsura and had alone married many brides, Nārada Muni desired to see the Lord in this situation (in Dvārakā). SB 10.69.1**

**One should not say that Nārada saw these pastimes over many days. If that had been the case, he would not have become astonished on seeing the powers of yogamāyā on that occasion. \|\|45\|\|**

### Text 46

*prākṛtāḥ sūryādayo jaḍātmā kālaś ca tatra na santi--“na tatra candrārka-vapuḥ prakāśate na vānti vātā na ca yānti devatāḥ \| yatra devaḥ kratubhir bhuta-bhāvaṇa svayaṁ vibhutyā virajaḥ prakāśate \|” “na tatra sūryo bhāti na candra tārakaṁ nemā vidyuto bhanti kuto ‘yam agniḥ” “mṛtyur yasyopasecanaṁ ka ity aveda yatra saḥ” “ na yatra kālo’ nimiṣāṁ paraḥ prabhuḥ \| kuto ‘nu devā jagatāṁ ya īśire” \|\| “kālā-muhūrtādi-mayāc ca kālo na yad vibhūteḥ pariṇāma-hetuḥ” ityādi-śruti-smtibhyaḥ \| ataeva na tatratyānāṁ kāliko viśeṣaḥ \|\|46\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The material sun and other planets and material time do not exist in the Lord’s abodes. This is shown in the śrutis and smṛtis.**

**na tatra candrārka-vapuḥ prakāśate na vānti vātā na ca yānti devatāḥ \| yatra devaḥ kratubhir bhūta-bhāvaṇaḥ svayaṁ vibhutyā virajaḥ prakāśate**

**In the spiritual relam the material moon and sun do not shine. There the wind does not blow and material devatās do not move about. The Lord, causing the welfare of all beings, manifests by his own will, in a pure state, by his powers. Rudra-hṛdaya Upaniṣad**

***na tatra sūryo bhāti na candra-tārakaṁ***  
***nemā vidyuto bhānti kuto’yam agniḥ \|***  
***tam eva bhāntam anubhāti sarvaṁ***

**There, the sun does not shine, nor do the moon and stars shine. There, lightning does not flash, what to speak of fire. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad 2.5.15)**

**yasya brahma ca kṣātraṁ ca ubhe bhavataḥ odanaḥ**  
**mṛtyur yasyopasecanaṁ ka itthā veda yatra saḥ**

**Brāhmaṇas and kṣatriyas are his food and death is the topping sprinkled on the food. Who knows where this person dwells? Kaṭha Upaniṣad 1.2.25**

**In this person, time, the controller of the** ***devatās,*** **has no influence. What influence can the** ***devatās*****, who control the material realm, have over him? SB 2.2.19**

**kalā-murhūtādi-mayaś ca kālo na yad vibhūteḥ pariṇama-hetuḥ**

**We do not know the Lord who is time, made up of moments and hours and years, whose influence is the source of perpetual change.**  ***Viṣṇu Purāṇa 4.1.83***

**Time does not affect anything in the spiritual world. \|\|46\|\|**

### Text 47

*tad evam bālyādīni harer nityāni taiḥ sambandhās tās tā līlā nityāh sarvaiḥ pārṣadair yathādhikāraṁ sarvāś cānubhāvyā iti \|\| tadi idaṁ coktaṁ sūtrābhyām “vyāpteś ca samañjasam” “sarvābhedād anyatreme” iti \| tathaiva bhāṣitaṁ ca \|\|47\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus the Lord’s qualities such as his ages are all eternal. All the pastimes related to those ages are eternal and are experienced by the associates according to their qualification. Vyāsa states this in the sutras:**

**vyāpteś ca samañjasam**

**Meditation on the Lord's form in various ages is proper because the Lord is all-pervading. Vedānta-sūtra 3.3.9**

**sarvābhedād anyatreme**

**The Lord, his associates and parts of action at a later time exist in the previous action because there no difference between the Lord, his associates and the parts at various times. Vedānta-sūtra 3.3.11 \|\|47\|\|**

### Text 48

*idaṁ tu bodhyaṁ –“svarūpeṇa cic-caktyā ca kṛtā līlā sanātanī \| tena prakṛti-kālābhyām ca kṛtā tu kadācitkī \|\|” sā ca sargādi daitya-vadhadi-rūpaiva syāt \| itarathā prākṛtika-layokti-vyākopaḥ \| nanu tal-laye prapañca-mātra-vināśāt tad gocarāyāḥ prakaṭāyā nityatvaṁ katham iti cen na, tad gocaratāyā vināśe ‘pi tad vyakter avināśāt \| śikhī dhvasta itivat \| saviśeṣaṇe vidhi-niṣedhau viśeṣaṇam upasaṁkrāmata iti hi nyāya-vidāṁ sthitiḥ \|\|48\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The following should be understood. The pastimes arrange by the cit-śakti are eternal by their nature. Actions of the Lord involving material energy and time are temporary. The creation of the material worlds and the killing of demons are of this temporary nature. Otherwise there could be no mention of destruction of the material universes.**

**“Since everything is destroyed with the destruction of the universe, how can the Lord’s visible pastimes in this world be eternal?” Though the pastimes are not visible, the pastimes are not unmanifested. It is like the śikhā of a brāhmaṇa. If the śikhā is not visible it does not mean the brāhmaṇa has died. The logicians have this rule:**

***sa-viśeṣaṇe vidhi-niṣedhau viśeṣaṇam upasaṁkrāmataḥ sati viśeṣye bādhe***

**Statements of affirmation and denial change the meaning of their particular descriptive elements if there is a contradiction to the principal subject. \|\|48\|\|**

### Text 49

*tad evam aiśvarya-mādhuryaudāryādi-guṇa-ratnākarasya nitya-dhāmādeḥ puruṣottamasya pūrṇāvirbhāve svayaṁ bhagavac-cabditasyāpūrṇāvirbhāve tu vilāsāṁśādi-śabda-bodhyasya tasyaiśvarya-mādhurya-viṣayatayā dvividhena jñānena jāyamānā vidhi-pravṛttā ruci-pravṛttā ceti dvidhā bhaktiḥ sādhana-rūpā sādhya-rūpā ca saivātyantikayos tayoḥ siddhi-hetuḥ \| tad icchayaiva tasyām tāsyāṁ te te jīvāḥ pravartante teṣūttare śreṣṭhā iti darśitaṁ bhagavatā sūtrakāreṇa – chandata ubhayāvirodhāt “gater arthavat tvam ubhayathānyathāhi virodhaḥ” “upapannas tal-lakṣṇārthopalabdhir lokavata” itiy atra \| evaṁ eva ca nirūptaṁ bhāṣye \| tathā ca pūrvoditaṁ sarvaṁ susthiraṁ \|\|49\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, an ocean of qualities like power, sweetness and compassion, possessing eternal abodes, is called bhagavān svayam when he manifests these qualities completely. When he manifests these qualities partially he is called a vilāsa or svāṁśa form. By knowledge of either the Lord’s powers or sweetness, two types of bhakti--one inspired by rules and the other inspired by taste--manifest. They have two forms: sādhana and sādhya, which are the means of obtaining complete destruction of suffering and complete possession of happiness. By the Lord’s will, jīvas practice these two paths. The path of taste is the best. This has been shown by the author of the Vedānta-sūtras:**

**chandata ubhayāvirodhāt**

**By the will of the Lord, both paths of bhakti exist. There is no confusion about method, because of scriptural statements concerning both paths. Vedānta-sūtra 3.3.29**

**gater arthavattvam ubhayathānyathā hi virodhaḥ**

**Both paths, containing the Lord, are suitable to attain him. Otherwise, scriptures would be contradicted. Vedānta-sūtra 3.3.30**

**upapannas tal-lakṣṇārthopalabdhir lokavat**

***Ruci-bhakti*** **is best because by this one attains the Lord who is most attracted to such devotees, just as a king is most attracted to the citizen who serves with most attention. Vedānta-sūtra 3.3.31**

**These are similarly described in the commentary on the sutras. Thus all the points raised have been confirmed. \|\|49\|\|**

## Chapter Three

*Defining Viṣṇu’s Supremecy*

### Text 1

*atha sa eṣa bhagavān sarveśvaraḥ puruṣottamo ‘samābhyadhikaḥ parāvān janmadi-ṣaḍ bhāva-vikāra-śūnyo devatā-viśeṣo bhavatīti pratipattavyam “tam īśvarāṇāṁ parameśvaraṁ taṁ devatānāṁ paramaṁ ca daivatam \| patiṁ patīnāṁ paramaṁ parastād vidāma devam; bhuvaneśamīḍyam \|\|” “na tasya kāryaṁ karaṇaṁ ca vidyate na tat samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate \| parāsya śaktir vivdhaiva śruyate svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca \|\| “na tasya kaścit patir asti loke na ceśitā naiva ca tasya liṅgaṁ \| sa kāraṇaṁ kāranādhipādhipo na cāsya kaścij janitā na cādhipaḥ” ityādi-śravaṇāt \|\|1\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Bhagavān is the lord of all beings, the highest person, having no equal or superior, endowed with his svarūpa-śakti, devoid of the six changes starting with birth, worshipped by all devatās. This is how he should be understood.**

**tam īśvarāṇāṁ paramaṁ maheśvaraṁ**  
**taṁ devatānāṁ paramaṁ ca daivatam \|**  
**patiṁ patīnāṁ paramaṁ parastād**  
**vidāma devaṁ bhuvaneśam īḍyam \|\|**

**We know the worshipable Lord, ruler of the world, the great Lord, supreme among controllers, supreme** ***devatā*** **among** ***devatās,*** **the master of masters, greater than the great.**

**na tasya kāryaṁ karaṇaṁ ca vidyate**  
**na tat-samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate \|**  
**parāsya śaktir vividhaiva śrūyate**  
**svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca \|\|**

**He has no body and no senses. No one is equal to him or higher. His supreme power is manifold. He has intrinsic knowledge, strength and action.**

**na tasya kaścit patir asti loke**  
**na ceśitā naiva ca tasya liṅgam \|**  
**sa kāraṇaṁ karaṇādhipādhipo**  
**na cāsya kaścij janitā na cādhipaḥ \|\|**

**He has no master or ruler in this world. He has no mark to distinguish him. He is the cause, the rulers of the senses. He has no father or ruler. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.7-9 \|\|1\|\|**

### Text 2

*sa eṣa sarva-kāraṇa-bhuto bhagavān sarveṣāṁ mumukṣūṇāṁ upāsyo na tv anyaḥ kaścit—“tam eva viditvātimṛtyum eti nānyah panthā vidyate ‘yanāya” \| “tam evaikaṁ jānatha ātmanam anyā vāco vimuñcatha amṛtasyaiṣa setuḥ” ityādi śruteḥ, smaranti cetthaṁ munayaḥ –“īśvaro bhagavān viṣnūḥ paramātmā sanātanaḥ \| eṣa śāntā ca kartā ca yatīnāṁ paramā gatiḥ \| dhyāyate ‘rcayate nityaṁ viṣṇu-liṅgaṁ samāsthitaḥ \| kalpa-koṭi-śatenāpi na gatis tasya vidya te” iti \| tam ekaṁ bahudhātmānaṁ prādhurbhūtam adhokṣajam \| nānya-bhaktāḥ kriyāvanto yajante sarva-kāmadam” iti “tam eva cārcayan nityaṁ bhaktyā puruṣam avyayam \| dhyāyan stuvan namasyaṁś ca yajamānas tam eva ca \|\| anādi-nidhanaṁ viṣṇuṁ sarva-loka-maheśvaraṁ lokādhyakṣaṁ stuvan nityaṁ sarva-duḥkhātigo bhavet \| esā me sarva-dharmānāṁ dharmo ‘dhikatamo mataḥ” iti \|\|2\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Bhagavān, the cause of everything, is worshipped by persons desiring liberation. They worship no one else.**

**yasmin dyauḥ pṛthivī cāntarikṣam**  
**otaṁ manaḥ saha prāṇaiś ca sarvaiḥ \|**  
**tam evaikaṁ jānatha ātmānam anyā**  
**vāco vimuñcathāmṛtasyaiṣa setuḥ \|\|**

**Know the one** ***ātmā*** **within whom heaven, earth*****, antarikṣa*** **and mind are woven along with all the** ***prāṇas.*** **Give up all other words. He is the bridge of immortality. Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 2.2.5**

**The sages speak similarly in the smṛtis:**

**īśvaro bhagavān viṣṇuḥ paramātmā sanātanaḥ \|**  
**eṣa śāstā ca kartā ca yatīnāṁ paramā gatiḥ \|**  
**dhyāyate ‘rcayate nityaṁ viṣṇu-liṅgaṁ samāsthitaḥ \|**  
**kalpa-koṭi-śatenāpi na gatis tasya vidyate**

**Bhagavān, the Lord, is Viṣṇu, Paramātmā, and eternal. He is the controller, the doer, and the supreme goal of the sannyāsīs. The initiated devotee constantly meditates on and worships the form of Viṣṇu. A person who rejects Viṣṇu cannot attain the goal for even millions of kalpas. Mahābhārata**

**tam ekaṁ bahudhātmānaṁ prādhurbhūtam adhokṣajam \|**  
**nānya-bhaktāḥ kriyāvanto yajante sarva-kāmadam**

**The devotees with no other desires worship one Viṣṇu who appears in many forms and awards all desires. Mahābhārata**

**tam eva cārcayan nityaṁ bhaktyā puruṣam avyayam \|**  
**dhyāyan stuvan namasyaṁś ca yajamānas tam eva ca \|\|**  
**anādi–nidhanaṁ viṣṇuṁ sarvaloka-maheśvaraṁ \|**  
**lokādhyakṣaṁ stuvan nityaṁ sarva-duḥkhātigo bhavet \|**  
**eṣa me sarva-dharmānāṁ dharmo ‘dhikatamo mataḥ \|\|**

**One should worship the unchanging Lord daily with bhakti. One should meditate on him, praise him, offer respects to him, and offer sacrifices to him. Praising daily the rulers of the worlds, Viṣṇu, the abode with no beginning, the great lord of all planets, one surpasses all suffering. I consider this to be the best dharma of all dharmas. Mahābhārata \|\|2\|\|**

### Text 3

*evam eva svayam upadiśati—“mayy eva mana ādhatsva mayi buddhiṁ niveśaya \| nivasiṣyasi mayy eva ata ūrdhvaṁ na saṁśayaḥ \|\|” “ daivī hy eṣā guṇa-mayī mama māyā duratyayā \|mām eva ye prapadyante māyām etāṁ taranti te \|\|” ityādi \| “catur vidhā mama janā bhaktā eva hi te smṛtāḥ \| teṣām ekāntināṁ śṛeṣṭhās te vai cānanya-devatāḥ \|\| aham ekāntinās teṣāṁ nirāśīḥ sarva-karmaṇām \| ye tu śiṣṭās trayo bhaktāh phala-kāmā hi te matāḥ \|\| sarve cyavana-dharmāṇaḥ pratibuddhas tu mokṣa-bhāk \| brahmāṇaṁ śiti-kaṇṭhaṁ ca yā cānyā devatāḥ smrtāḥ \|\| pratibuddhā na sevante mām evaisyanti yat param \| bhaktaṁ prati veśeṣas te eṣa pārthānukīṛtitaḥ \|\|” iti ca \| tasmā sarveśvaro viṣṇur eva mumukṣubhir dhyeya iti \|\|3\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord himself instructs as follows:**

**mayy eva mana ādhatsva mayi buddhiṁ niveśaya \|**  
**nivasiṣyasi mayy eva ata ūrdhvaṁ na saṁśayaḥ \|\|**

**Concentrate your mind only on me. Fix your intelligence on me. You will without doubt dwell close to me after leaving the body. BG 12.8**

**daivī hy eṣā guṇa-mayī mama māyā duratyayā \|**  
**mām eva ye prapadyante māyām etāṁ taranti te \|\|**

**My māyā, made of the guṇas, fit for the jīva’s pleasure, is hard to surpass, but those who surrender to me alone can cross over this māyā. BG 7.14**

**catur vidhā mama janā bhaktā eva hi te smṛtāḥ \|**  
**teṣām ekāntināṁ śṛeṣṭhās te vai cānanya-devatāḥ \|\|**  
**aham eva gatis teṣāṁ nirāśīḥ sarva-karmaṇām \|**  
**ye tu śiṣṭās trayo bhaktāh phala-kāmā hi te matāḥ \|\|**  
**sarve cyavana-dharmāṇaḥ pratibuddhas tu mokṣa-bhāk \|**  
**brahmāṇaṁ śitikaṇṭhaṁ ca yā cānyā devatāḥ smrtāḥ \|\|**  
**pratibuddhā na sevante mām evaisyanti yat param \|**  
**bhaktaṁ prati viśeṣas te eṣa pārthānukīrtitaḥ \|\|**

**There are four types of devotees. The best of these worship me and no one else. They have no desire for performing any karmas. I am their only goal. The other three types have desire for results. All are subject to temporary results. The devotees however attain liberation. They do not worship Brahmā or Śiva or other devatās. This person attains me. O Arjuna! This unique feature is glorified in the devotee. Mahābhārata**

**Thus, persons desiring liberation worship only Viṣṇu. \|\|3\|\|**

### Text 4

*pare tu nāvamantavyāḥ--harer eva sarva-deveśvareśvaraḥ \| itare brahma-rudrādyā nāvajñeyā kadācana” iti smṛteḥ \|\|4\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**However one should not offend the devatās.**

***harir eva sadārādhyaḥ sarva-deveśvareśvaraḥ \|***  
***itare brahma-rudrādyā nāvajñeyāḥ kadācana \|\|***

**The supreme Lord should always be worshipped. One should not disrespect Brahmā, Śiva and others. Padma Purāṇa \|\|4\|\|**

### Text 5

*atra kecid āhuḥ--sarveśaro viṣṇus tu devatāviśeṣa iti nopayuktam—“iṣṭāpūrtaṁ bahudhā jāyamānaṁ viśvaṁ vibharti bhuvanasya nābhiḥ tad evāgnis tad vāyus tat sūryas tad u candramāḥ \| agniḥ sarva-devatā” itiyadi-śrutiṣu “ye ‘py anya-devatā bhaktā yajante śraddhayānvitāḥ \| te ‘pi mām eva kaunteya yajanty avidhi-pūrvakam \|\| ahaṁ hi sarva-yajñānāṁ bhoktā ca prabhur eva ca \|” ityādi-smrṭiṣu ca sarva-karma-samārādhyānāṁ sarvāsāṁ devatānāṁ ekatvāvagamāt, tāsām sarvāsaṁ pāramya-śruteś ca \| tasmād ekaiva devatā karma-bhedair ārādhyā nāma-bhedaṁ dhatte ity āto viṣṇāv eva pāramyam iti riktaṁ vacaḥ \|\|5\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Some say that it is not correct to say that Viṣṇu is a special devatā or supreme Lord. Śruti says:**

**iṣṭāpūrtaṁ bahudhā jāyamānaṁ viśvaṁ vibharti bhuvanasya nābhiḥ tad evāgnis tad vāyus tat sūryas tad u candramāḥ \| agniḥ sarva-devatā**

**The navel of the worlds supports the universe produced by the results of sacrifices. He is Agni, Vayu, Sūrya and Candra. Agni and all devatās. Mahā-nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad**

**Smṛtis say as follows:**

**ye ’py anya-devatā-bhaktā yajante śraddhayānvitāḥ \|**  
**te ’pi mām eva kaunteya yajanty avidhi-pūrvakam \|\|**

**Those who are devoted to other gods and with faith worship them, worship me by the wrong method, O son of Kuntī.**

**ahaṁ hi sarva-yajñānāṁ bhoktā ca prabhur eva ca \|**  
**na tu mām abhijānanti tattvenātaś cyavanti te \|\|**

**I am the enjoyer and master of all sacrifices. Those who do not know me as such continue to take birth. BG 9.23-24**

**This means that all the devatās who are worshipped by actions are actually one. Śruti says they are all the supreme entity. Therefore, one devatā is worshipped by different karmas and is designated with different names. To say that the supreme entity is Viṣṇu is not correct. \|\|5\|\|}**

### Text 6

*maivam –“candramā manaso jātaś cakṣoḥ sūryo ajāyata \| śrotṛād vāyuś c aprāṇaś ca mukhād agnir ajāyata \|\|” “nārāyaṇād brahma nārāyaṇād rudro jāyate nārāyaṇāt prajāpatiḥ prajāyate nārāyaṇād indro jāyate nārāyaṇād aṣṭau vasavo jāyante nārāyaṇād ekādaśa-rudrā jāyante nārāyaṇād dvādaśādityā jāyante” ity ādi-śrutiṣu “brahma-śambhus tathārkaś candramāś ca śata-kratuḥ \| evaṁ ādyas tathaivāneyuktā vaiśṇava-tejasā \|\| jagat-kāryāvasāne tu viyujyante ca tejasā \| vitejasaś ca te sarve pañcatvaṁ upayānti te” ityādi-smṛtiṣu ca sarvāsāṁ devatānāṁ parasya ca mitho bheda-darśanāt tābhyas tasya paratvasyāvagamāc ca \| sarva-devatā samānādhikāraṇyaṁ tu tad-āyatta-vṛttikatvād upacaryate \| itarathā “taṁ īśvarāṇāṁ paramaṁ maheśvaraṁ” ity ād-śrutīnāṁ “devān deva-yajo yānti pitṛṇ yānti pitṛvratāḥ \| bhūtāni yānti bhutejyā yānti mad jājino pi mām \|\|” iti phala-bheda-smṛteś ca vyākyopāpattiḥ evaṁ sati sarvāṣaṁ pāramya-śravaṇam āpekśikaṁ stuti-paraṁ vā bhaviṣyatīti \|\|6\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is not so. Śruti says:**

**candramā manaso jātaś cakṣoḥ sūryo ajāyata**  
**śrotṛād vāyuś c aprāṇaś ca mukhād agnir ajāyata**

**The moon arose from Viṣṇu’s mind. The sun arose from his eyes. Vāyu and prāṇas arose from his ears. Agni arose from his mouth. Puruṣa-sūkta**

**nārāyaṇād brahma nārāyaṇād rudro jāyate nārāyaṇāt prajāpatiḥ prajāyate nārāyaṇād indro jāyate nārāyaṇād aṣṭau vasavo jāyante nārāyaṇād ekādaśa-rudrā jāyante nārāyaṇād dvādaśādityā jāyante**

**From Nārāyaṇā, Brahmā was born. From Nārāyaṇa, Śiva was born. From Nārāyaṇa, Prajāpati was born. From Nārāyaṇa, Indra was born. From Nārāyaṇa, the eight Vasus were born. From Nārāyaṇa, the eleven Rudras were born. From Nārāyaṇa, the twelve Ādityas were born. Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad**

**Smṛti says:**

**brahma śambhus tathaivārkaś candramāc ca śatakratuḥ**  
**evam ādyās tathaivānye yuktā vaiṣṇava-tejasā**  
**jagat kāryāvasāne tu viyuyante ca tejasā**  
**vitejasaś ca te sarve pañcatvaṁ upayānti vai**

**Brahmā, Śiva, the sun, the moon, Indra, and other** ***devatās*** **accept the power of Viṣṇu However when work in the universe is finished, they become bereft of powers and in that state they all die. Viṣṇu-dharma**

**From these statements it is concluded that all the devatās are different from the supreme being Viṣṇu and that Viṣṇu is superior to them. Saying that they are nondifferent from Viṣṇu is a metaphorical statement to express their dependence on Viṣṇu. This is confirmed by śruti:**

**taṁ īśvarāṇāṁ paramaṁ maheśvaraṁ**

**He is the supreme Lord of all other controllers. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

**Smṛtis describe different results from worshipping the Lord:**

***yānti deva-vratā devān pitṝn yānti pitṛ-vratāḥ \|***  
***bhūtāni yānti bhūtejyā yānti mad-yājino ’pi mām \|\|***

**The worshippers of the** ***devatās*** **go to the** ***devatās*****, and the worshippers of the Pitṛs go to the Pitṛs. The worshippers of ghosts go to the ghosts, and worshippers of me come to me. BG 9.25**

**These statements should not be contradicted. Thus, statements of nondifference of the Lord and devatās are relative statements or are an exaggeration to praise the devatās. \|\|6\|\|**

### Text 7

*apare tv āhuḥ māstu sarva-devaikyaṁ brahma-viṣṇu-rudrāṇāṁ trayāṇāṁ aikyas tu vartate “yo vai viṣṇuḥ sa rudro yo rudra sa pitāmahaḥ \| ekā mūrtis trayo devā rudra-viṣnu-iptāmahāḥ \|\| varadā loka-kartāro loka-nāthāḥ svayambhuvaḥ \| arddhanārīśvarās te tu vrataṁ tīvraṁ samāsthiṭāḥ\|\|” “brahma nārāyaṇākhyo ‘sau kalpādau bhagavān yathā \| prajāḥ sasarja bhagavān brahma nārāyaṇātmakaḥ \|\| tataḥ sa bhagavān viṣṇuḥ rudra-rūpa-dharo ‘vyavayaḥ \| tataḥ kālāgni-rudro ‘sau bhūtvā sarva-haro hariḥ \| sarva-sthity antakaraṇīṁ brahma-viṣṇu-śivātmikām \| sa saṁjñāṁ yāti bhagavān eka eva janārdanaḥ \|\|” ityādi-smṛti-prāmāñāt \| āha caivaṁ mārkaṇḍeyaṁ prati rudraḥ “brāhmaṇāḥ sādhavaḥ śantā niḥsaṅgā bhūta-vatsalāḥ \| ekanta-bhaktā asmāsu nirvairāḥ sama-darśinaḥ \|\| salokā loka-pālās tān vandanty ārcanty upāsate \| ahaṁ ca bhagavān brahma svayaṁ ca harir īśvaraḥ \| na te mayy acyute ‘je ca bhidām aṇv api cakṣate” iti tasmāt trayāṇaṁ aikyaṁ āstheyam iti cet \|\|7\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Others argue that all devatās are not the same, but Brahmā Viṣṇu and Śiva are one.**

**yo vai viṣṇuḥ sa rudro yo rudra sa pitāmahaḥ \|**  
**ekā mūrtis trayo devā rudra-viṣnu-pitāmahāḥ \|\|**  
**varadā loka-kartāro loka-nāthāḥ svayambhuvaḥ \|**  
**ardha-nārīśvarās te tu vrataṁ tīvraṁ samāsthiṭāḥ\|\|**

**Viṣṇu is Śiva and Śiva is Brahmā, The three devas are one form. They give benedictions, are creators of the world, are masters of the world and are self-manifesting. They have śakti as their consorts and perform severe austerities. Hari-vaṁśa**

**brahma nārāyaṇākhyo ‘sau kalpādau bhagavān yathā \|**  
**prajāḥ sasarja bhagavān brahma nārāyaṇātmakaḥ \|\|**  
**tataḥ sa bhagavān viṣṇuḥ rudra-rūpa-dharo ‘vyavayḥ \|**  
**tataḥ kālāgni-rudro ‘sau bhūtvā sarva-haro hariḥ \|**  
**sarga-sthity-anta-karaṇīṁ brahma-viṣṇu-śivātmikām \|**  
**sa saṁjñāṁ yāti bhagavān eka eva janārdanaḥ \|\|**

**Brahmā, called by the name Nārāyaṇa, at the beginning of the kalpa created the living beings. Similarly Viṣṇu accepting the eternal form of Śiva, becoming the fire of destruction, destroys everything. The one lord called Janārdana is called Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva, who cause creation, maintenance and destruction. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

**brāhmaṇāḥ sādhavaḥ śantā niḥsaṅgā bhūta-vatsalāḥ \|**  
**ekanta-bhaktā asmāsu nirvairāḥ sama-darśinaḥ \|\|**  
**salokā lokapālas tān vandanty ārcanty upaṣate \|**  
**ahaṁ ca bhagavān brahma svayaṁ ca harir īśvaraḥ \|**  
**na te may acyute ‘je ca bhidām aṇv api cakṣate\|\|**

**The inhabitants and ruling demigods of all planets, along with Lord Brahmā, the Supreme Lord Hari and I, glorify, worship and assist those brāhmaṇas who are saintly, always peaceful, free of material attachment, compassionate to all living beings, purely devoted to us, devoid of hatred and endowed with equal vision. These devotees do not differentiate between Lord Viṣṇu, Lord Brahmā and me, nor do they differentiate between themselves and other living beings. Therefore, because you are this kind of saintly devotee, we worship you. SB 12. 10.20-22**

**Thus it should be established that the three are one. \|\|7\|\|**

### Text 8

*maivaṁ yo brahmāṇaṁ vidadhāti pūrvāṁ yo vai vedāṁś ca prahiṇoti tasmai taṁ ha devam ātma-buddhi-prakāśaṁ mumukṣair vai śaraṇaṁ ahaṁ prapadye” iti \| “nārāyaṇād brahma jāyate” ityādi, “yaṁ kāmaye taṁ tam ugraṁ kṛṇoti taṁ brahmāṇaṁ tam ṛṣiṁ tam sumedhām” it caivaṁ aid śravanāt \| smṛtiṣ ca--“prāhur āditya-varṇaṁ taṁ puruṣaṁ tamasaḥ param \| bṛhantaṁ sarva-gaṁ devam īśānaṁ varadaṁ prabhum” ity upakramya “tato brahma samabhavat sa tasyaiva prasadajaḥ \| krodhāviṣṭasya saṁ jajñe rudraḥ saṁhārakārakaḥ \|\| etau tau vibudha-śreṣṭhau prasāda-krodajau smṛtau \| tad adeśita-panthānau sṛṣti-saṁhārakau \| nimitta-mātraṁ tāv agra sarvaprāṇipravartakau \|\| sabrahmakāh sarudrāś ca sendrā deva maharṣibhiḥ \| arcayanti sura-śreṣṭhaṁ devaṁ nārāyaṇaṁ hariṁ \|\| yad brahmaṛṣayaś caiva svayaṁ paśupatiś ca yat \| havyaṁ kavyaṁ ca satataṁ vidhiyuktaṁ prayuñjate \|\| krtsnaṁ ca tasya devasya caraṇāv upatiṣthati \| bhaviṣyatāṁ vartatāṁ ca bhūtānāṁ cai bhārata \| sarveṣāṁ agraṇī viṣṇuḥ sevyāḥ pūjyaś ca nitaśaḥ” \|\| ityādyā \| śivo pi “svayaṁ ca harir īśvaraḥ “ iti harer eva sarveṣvaravtam adarśayat \|\|8\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**That is not so, since the śrutis say otherwise.**

**yo brahmāṇam vidadhāti pūrvam**  
**yo vai vedāṁś ca prahiṇoti tasmai**  
**tam ha devam ātmā-buddhi-prakāśam**  
**mumukṣur vai śaraṇam aham prapadye**

**He first created Brahmā and gave the Vedas to him. I surrender to the Lord, the shelter of those desiring liberation, who inspires jīva’s intelligence. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.18**

**nārāyaṇād brahma jāyate**

**Brahmā arose from Nārāyaṇa. Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad**

**yaṁ kāmaye tam ugraṁ kṛṇomi taṁ brahmāṇaṁ tam ṛṣim taṁ sumedhām:**

**I make whomever I desire into Śiva; I make whomever I desire into Brahmā or a sage. Ṛg Veda 10.125.05.2**

**Smṛtis also state the following:**

**prāhur āditya-varṇaṁ taṁ puruṣaṁ tamasaḥ param**  
**bṛhantaṁ sarva-gaṁ devam īśānaṁ varadaṁ prabhum**  
**tato brahma samabhavat sa tasyaiva prasada-jaḥ \|**  
**krodhāviṣṭasya saṁjajñe rudraḥ saṁhāra-kārakaḥ \|\|**  
**etau tau vibudha-śreṣṭhau prasāda-kroda-jau smṛtau \|**  
**tad-ādeśita-panthānau sṛṣti-saṁhārakau \|**  
**nimittamātraṁ tāv agra sarva-prāṇi-pravartakau \|\|**  
**sa-brahmakāh sa-rudrāś ca sendrā devā maharṣibhiḥ \|**  
**arcayanti sura-śreṣṭhaṁ devaṁ nārāyaṇaṁ hariṁ \|\|**  
**yad brahmaṛṣayaś caiva svayaṁ paśupatiś ca yat \|**  
**havyaṁ kavyaṁ ca satataṁ vidhi-yuktaṁ prayuñjate \|\|**  
**krtsnaṁ ca tasya devasya caraṇāv upatiṣthati \|**  
**bhaviṣyatāṁ vartatāṁ ca bhūtānāṁ caiva bhārata \|**  
**sarveṣāṁ agraṇī viṣṇuḥ sevyāḥ pūjyaś ca nitaśaḥ\|\|**

**They describe the Lord to be the color of the sun, beyond darkness. He is the greatest, pervading everywhere. He is the shining Lord the controller and giver of benedictions. Then Brahmā appeared. He received Viṣṇu’s mercy. Śiva, the destroyer, appeared when the Lord became angry. Brahmā and Śiva are the best of the devatās and are born of mercy and anger. By the Lord’s order they become the creator and destroyer. They are the Lord’s instruments to inspire the jīvas to action. Along with Brahmā, Śiva, and Indra and great sages, the devatās worship Nārāyaṇa, the best of Lords. All the offerings of the great sages and even Śiva are placed at the feet of Viṣṇu. The leader Viṣṇu is to be served and worshipped constantly by all persons of the present and future. Mahābhārata**

**Furthermore Śiva says** ***svayaṁ ca harir īśvaraḥ*****: Viṣṇu is the supreme Lord. (SB 12.10.21) Thus it has been shown that Viṣṇu is lord of all beings. \|\|8\|\|**

### Text 9

*“athāpi yat-pāda-nakhāvasṛṣṭaṁ jagad viriñcopahṛtārhaṇāmbhaḥ \| seśaṁ punāty anyatamo mukundāt ko nāma loke bhagavat-padārthaḥ \|\|” iti smṛtyā tu nirṇitaṁ tat \| abhedoktayas tu tadāyatta-vṛttikatvādibhiḥ saṅgacchante \| “sṛjāmi tan-niyukto ’haṁ haro harati tad-vaśaḥ \| viśvaṁ puruṣa-rūpeṇa paripāti tri-śakti-dhṛk \|\|” “brahmā śambus tathaivārkaḥ” ityādi smṛtyānguṇyāt \| tasmāt viṣṇur eva sarveśara iti siddham \|\|9\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is also the conclusion of smṛti:**

***athāpi yat-pāda-nakhāvasṛṣṭaṁ***  
***jagad viriñcopahṛtārhaṇāmbhaḥ \|***  
***seśaṁ punāty anyatamo mukundāt***  
***ko nāma loke bhagavat-padārthaḥ \|\|***

**Who can be called by the name Bhagavān except Mukunda whose toe-nail water purifies the universe along with Śiva and becomes** ***arghya*** **for Brahmā? SB 1.18.21**

**This is similar to the previous quotation from smṛti,** ***brahmā śambhus tathaivārkaḥ*****. Therefore it has been proved that Viṣṇu is the supreme Lord. \|\|9\|\|**

### Text 10

*kecit tv evaṁ samādadhate—sākṣād viṣṇur eva catur mukhatvaṁ ugratvaṁ cāṅgīkṛtya kvacit sṛjati saṁharati ceti purāṇeṣu nirūpyate \| tad dṛstyā vā tad abhedoktayaḥ pravarteran tathāpi tasya tasya copāsanaṁ buddhavan mumukṣubhir na vidheyam—“ tam eva viditvā’ ityādi śruti-smrṭibhyaḥ \| itthaṁ ca hari-vaṁśoktaṁ harihara-stotram api vyākhyātam iti \|\|10\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Some persons conclude as follows. It is stated in the Purāṇas that Viṣṇu, directly accepting four heads, creates the universe and, accepting ferocity, destroys the universe. Since they are non-different from Viṣṇu, one should not worship them separately just as one does not worship Buddha (though he is an avatāra). Śrutis and smṛtis indicate this with statements such as** ***tam eva viditvā*****: worship only him. This is also explained in the Hari-vaṁśa, in the Hari-hara-stotra. \|\|10\|\|**

### Text 11

*apare tv āhuḥ – māstu trayāṇām aikyaṁ kintūbhayor hari-harayos tad vartate \| śvetāśvagaropaniṣadi hara-rudrādi-śabda-vācyasya sahastra-śīrṣādi-śabdair abhidhānāt,*  
*śivasya hrdayaṁ viṣṇur viṣnoś ca hṛdyaṁ śivaḥ \| yathā śiva-mayo viṣṇur evaṁ viṣṇu-mayaḥ śivaḥ \|\| yathāntaraṁ na paśyāmi tathā me svasti vāyuṣi” iti smṛteś ca \| evaṁ hari-hara-stotrāc ca tad aikyaṁ niścitam \| “śivasya śrī-viṣṇor ya iha guṇa-nāmādi sakalaṁ dhiyā bhinnaṁ paśyet sa khalu hari-nāmāhitakaraḥ” iti tad bheda-darśino nāmāparādha-rūpa-mahā-doṣaś ca tatraiva smṛtaḥ \| sakalam iti hari-hara-stotroktaṁ cakra-triśūlādi grāhyaṁ \| tad api dhiyā bhinnaṁ yaḥ paśyed ity arthaḥ \| tasmad viṣṇu-śivayor advaitam iti cen naitat samicīnaṁ pūrokta-śruti-smṛtibhyaḥ yat tu hara-rudrādi-śabda-vācyasya sahasra-śīrṣādi-śabdair abhidhānāt tayor aikyaṁ ity uktam tan na—tasyām harādi-śabdair viṣṇor evābhidhānāt \|\|11\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Others speak as follows. Let us not accept that all three are the same, but-Śiva and Viṣṇu are non-different. It is stated in Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad that the names of Śiva indicate Viṣṇu’s names. Smṛti says:**

**śivasya hrdayaṁ viṣṇur viṣnoś ca hṛdyaṁ śivaḥ \|**  
**yathā śiva-mayo viṣṇur evaṁ viṣṇu-mayaḥ śivaḥ \|\|**  
**yathāntaraṁ na paśyāmi tathā me svasti vāyuṣi**

**Viṣṇu is the heart of Śiva. Śiva is the heart of Viṣṇu. Just as Viṣṇu is made of Śiva, Śiva is made of Viṣṇu. May my life be auspicious by not seeing difference between them. Mahābhārata**

**Thus from the Hara-hari-stotra it is confirmed that Śiva and Viṣṇu are non-different.**

**śivasya śrī-viṣṇor ya iha guṇa-nāmādi sakalaṁ dhiyā bhinnaṁ paśyet sa khalu hari-nāmāhitakaraḥ**

**The person who with his intelligence sees different between the qualities and names of Śiva and Viṣṇu (and other things as well) is an offender to the name of Viṣṇu. Hari-vaṁśa**

**Thus the smṛtis say that it is an offense to see difference between Śiva and Viṣṇu.** ***Sakala*** **(all) indicated non-difference of the trident of Śiva and the cakra of Viṣṇu for instance. The meaning is “He who sees difference by his intelligence (*****dhiyā*****).” Thus Śiva and Viṣṇu are non-different.**

**This argument is not correct. According to the previous quotations from śruti and smṛti, it is not correct to say that Śiva and Viṣṇu are one, based on the statement that the names of Śiva mean the names of Viṣṇu, since the real meaning is that Śiva’s names, usually used for Śiva, are actually truly employed to describe Viṣṇu. \|\|11\|\|**

### Text 12

*sahasra-nāma-store sarva-śivaśambh-rudrādi-śabdā viṣṇu-nāmāni paṭhyante teṣāṁ pravṛtti-nimittāni coktāni brahmāṇḍe—“rujaṁ drāvayate yasmād rudras tasmāj janārdanaḥ \| īśanād eva ceśāno mahādevo mahattvaḥ \|\| pibanti ye narā nākāṁ muktāḥ saṁsāra-sāgarāt \| tad ādhāro yato viṣṇuḥ pinākīti tatḥa smrṭaḥ \|\| śivaḥ sukhātmakatvena sarva-saṁrodhanād dharaḥ \| kṛtyātmakām idaṁ viśvaṁ yato vaste pravartayan \| kṛtivāsās tato devo viriñcaś ca virecanāt \| bṛṁhaṇād brahma-nāmāsāv aiśvaryād indra ucyate \|\| evaṁ nānā-vidhaḥ śabdair eka eva trivikramaḥ \| vedeṣu sa purāṇeṣu gīyate puruṣottamaḥ” iti \|\|12\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In the thousand names of Viṣṇu, the names** ***sarva, śiva, śambhu, rudra*** **and others are used for Viṣṇu. The reason for such use of Śiva’s names for Viṣṇu is explained in Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa.**

***rujaṁ drāvayate yasmād rudras tasmāj janārdanaḥ \|***  
***īśanād eva ceśāno mahā-devo mahattvataḥ \|\|***  
***pibanti ye narā nākaṁ muktāḥ saṁsāra-sāgarāt \|***  
***tad-ādhāro yato viṣṇuḥ pinākīti tataḥ smṛtaḥ \|\|***  
***śivaḥ sukhātmakatvena sarva-sarodhanād dharaḥ \|***  
***kṛtyātmakam imaṁ dehaṁ yato vaste pravartayan \|\|***  
***kṛttivāsās tato devo viriñciś ca virecanāt \|***  
***bṛṁhaṇād brahma-nāmāsau aiśvaryād indra ucyate \|\|***  
***evaṁ nānā-vidhaiḥ śabdair eka eva trivikramaḥ \|***  
***vedeṣu ca purāṇeṣu gīyate puruṣottamaḥ \|\|***

**Because he drives away pain Viṣṇu is called Rudra. Because he is the controller he is called Īśāna. Because he is the greatest he is called Mahādeva. He is called Pinākī (holder of a trident) because he is the support of persons who, liberated from the ocean of saṁsāra, enjoy (pi) the spiritual sky (nāka). He is called Śiva because he is the embodiment of happiness. He is called Hara because he destroys everything. He is called Kṛtivāsa because he lives in and operates the body which produces action (*****kṛtya).*** **He is called Viriñci because he extends beyond everything. He is called Brahmā because he expands. He is called Indra because he has power. Thus the one supreme Lord is glorified by various types of names in the Vedas and Purāṇas. \|\|12\|**

### Text 13

*“nārāyaṇādīni nāmāni vinānyāni sva-nāmāni druhiṇādibhyo dadau” iti cotkaṁ skānde \| “rṭe nārāyanādīṇi nāmāni puruṣottamaḥ\| praḍād anyatra bhagavān rājevater svakaṁ puram” iti, brahme, ca “catur-mukhaḥ śatānāndo brahmaṇaḥ padmabhur iti \| ugro bhasma-dharo nagnaḥ kapālīti śivasya ca \| viśeṣa-nāmāni dadau svakīyāny api keśavaḥ” iti \| svakīyāni rudra-viriṇcyāḍini \|\|13\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Skanda Purāṇa says:**

**śrī-nārāyaṇādīni nāmāni vinānyāni sva-nāmāni druhiṇādibhyo dadau**

**Except for the names like Nārāyaṇa, the Lord gave the other names to Śiva and others. (Skanda Purāṇa)**

**rṭe nārāyanādīṇi nāmāni puruṣottamaḥ\|**  
**pradād anyatra bhagavān rājevarte svakaṁ puram**

**The supreme Lord gave names other than Nārāyaṇa etc. to others, just as a king gives places except for his own city to others.**

**caturmukhaḥ śatānāndo brahmaṇaḥ padmabhur iti \|**  
**ugro bhasma-dharo nagnaḥ kapālīti śivasya ca \|**  
**viśeṣa-nāmāni dadau svakīyāny api keśavaḥ\|\|**

**The Lord gives some particular names to his servants. To Brahmā he gives the names Caturmukha, Śatānanda and Padmabhū. To Śiva he gives the names Ugra, Bhasmadhara, Nagna and Kapālī. Brahma Purāṇa**

***Svakīyāni*** **refers to Śiva and Brahmā. \|\|13\|\|**

### Text 14

*brahmādino vadantīty upakrāntaṁ brahmaiva taiḥ śabdaiḥ tasyām abhidhīyate \| itarathā “yā te rudra śivā tanur aghorā papa-nāśinī” “mahān prabhur vai puruṣaḥ sattvastaiṣa pravartakaḥ \| sunirmalām imāṁ śāntim iśāno jyotir avyayaḥ” ityādi viśeṣokti-vyākopaḥ \| kapālinas tu śivasya ghora-rūpatā mumukṣu-heyatā ca smṛtā – “mumukṣavo ghora-rūpāṇ hitvā bhūta-patīnatha \| nārāyaṇa-kalāḥ śāntā bhajanti hy anasūyavaḥ” ityādau \|\|14\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The brāhmavādīs say that Brahman is indicated by words describing Viṣṇu in the previous text. But statements like the following (referring to Viṣṇu) contradict this.**

**yā te rudra-śivā tanur aghorā papa-nāśinī**

**O Rudra! Your auspicious body destroys sin. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

**mahān prabhur vai puruṣaḥ sattvasyaiṣa pravartakaḥ \|**  
**sunirmalām imāṁ śāntim iśāno jyotir avyayaḥ**

**The ruler is the great master, the puruṣa, the instiller of sattva, the eternal light and pure peace.**

**Moreover Śiva is known to have a fierce form and to be repulsive to persons desiring liberation.**

***mumukṣavo ghora-rūpān hitvā bhūta-patīn atha \|***  
***nārāyaṇa-kalāḥ śāntā bhajanti hy anasūyavaḥ*** **\|\|**

**Rejecting the frightful forms Śiva or others** ***devatās*****, persons desirous of liberation (what to speak of the devotees), without criticizing those** ***devatās,*** **worship the** ***avatāras*** **of Nārāyaṇa. SB 1.2.26 \|\|14\|\|**

### Text 15

*“śivasya hrḍayaṁ viṣṇuḥ” ityādiṣu tu tayor īśvaratvenaikyaṁ vedyate, na tu svarūpābhedaḥ prāg ukta-śrutiyādibhyaḥ \| “śivasya śrī-viṣṇoḥ” ityādau tu tasya svatantra-devatātva-dṛṣṭir niṣiddhā \| tad-dattādhipatyābhidhānatvena tad āyatta-vṛttikatvāt tad-rūpatvaṁ veti na kāpi kṣatiḥ \| apare tv āhuḥ “nāmādi sakalam” ityādinā sarūpaṁc a tad bhimmaṇ yaḥ paśyed ith arthaḥ \| “brahmāṇaṁ śitikaṇṭhaṁ ca” ityādi pūrvoktāt “yas su nārāyaṇaṁ devaṁ” ityādi-vakṣaymāṇaś ca \| tasmāt sarūpaikyaṁ tayor nāstīti siddha viṣūnr eva pāramyam \|\|15\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Statement like “the heart of Śiva is Viṣṇu” indicate that they are one in being īśvara, but do not indicate that their svarūpas are identical. This was understood from previously quoted śruti statements. In statements concerning the offense of seeing non-difference in the names of Viṣṇu, what is forbidden in those statements is seeing devatās to be independent of Lord. Or there is no harm in taking the secondary meaning that Śiva’s and Viṣṇu’s names are nondifferent, from accepting that Śiva is given powers by the Lord. Other say the meaning is that those who see difference between the Lord and his name are offenders, as shown from previous statements like** ***brahmāṇaṁ śiti-kaṇṭaṁ ca*** **(The devotees do not worship Brahmā or Śiva or other devatās. and** ***yas tu nārāyaṇaṁ devam*** **(quoted later in the text). Thus it is proved that Śiva and Viṣṇu do not have the same svarūpa and that Viṣṇu is supreme. \|\|15\|\|**

### Text 16

*anye tv āhuḥ – māstu trayāṇāṁ abhedo, dvayor va kintu trayo devāḥ samā ity aṅgīkāryaṁ “yatha sasarja deva ‘sau devarṣi-pitṛ-dānavān \| manuṣya-tiryag-yakṣādīn bhū-vyoma-salilauakasaḥ \| yad guṇaṁ yat svabhāvaṁ ca yad rūpaṁ ca jagad dvija \| svargādau sūtavān brahmā tan mamācakṣa vistarāt \|\| carācarasya sarvasya rudraḥ saṁhāra-kārakaḥ \| tataḥ kālāgni-rūdro ‘sau saṁharaty akhilaṁ jagat \|\| na hi pālana-sāmarthyam ṛte sarveśvaraṁ harim” ityādi-smṛtiṣu kārya-traye trāyāṇaṁ nirapekṣa-hetutva-pratīteḥ \| tataṣ ca viṣnor eva pāramaśvarya-nirṇayo nopayuka iti naitad caturasraṁ pūrvoksa-śruti-smrtibhyaḥ \|\|16\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Others will say that, though not taking the three or two as non-different, the three should be accepted as equal.**

**yatha sasarja devo ‘sau devarṣi-pitṛ-dānavān \|**  
**manuṣya-tiryag-yakṣādīn bhū-vyoma-salilaukasaḥ \|**  
**yad guṇaṁ yat svabhāvaṁ ca yad rūpaṁ cva jagad dvija \|**  
**svargādau sṛṣtavān brahmā tan mamācakṣa vistarāt \|\|**  
**carācarasya sarvasya rudraḥ saṁhāra-kārakaḥ \|**  
**tataḥ kālāgni-rūdro ‘sau saṁharaty akhilaṁ jagat \|\|**  
**na hi pālana-sāmarthyam ṛte sarveśvaraṁ harim \|**  
**sthitaṁ sthitau mahāprājña bhavaty anyasya kasyacit \|\|**

**Brahmā created in Svarga and other places the devatās, sages, pitṛs, dānavas, humans, animals, Yakṣas, and entites of the earth, air, and water. Describe to me in detail about their qualities, natures and forms, O brāhmaṇa! Śiva is the destroyer of all beings and destroys the whole universe. Without Viṣṇu who has the power of maintenance, nothing exits. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

**In such statement discussing the functions of the three, there is no differentiation of one being superior to the other as the cause. Therefore it is not proper to say that Viṣṇu is supreme. This is not correct, since the previous śruti and smṛti statements say otherwise. \|\|16\|\|**

### Text 17

*“na tat-samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate” “na tasya pratimā hy asti yasya nāma mahad yaśaḥ” “Na tat samaś cābhyadhikaḥ kuto ‘nyaḥ” “svayaṁ tv asāmyātiśayas tryadhīśaḥ” “nityaṁ hi nāsti jagati bhutaṁ sthavara-jaṅgamam ṛte tam ekaṁ puruṣaṁ vāsudevaṁ sanātanam” ityadi śruit-smṛtiṣu sāmya-pratiṣedhāc ca \|\|17\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Equality of the Lord with others is also refuted by the following śruti and smṛti statements.**

**na tat-samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate**

**No one is seen to be equal of greater than the Lord. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8**

**na tasya pratimā hy asti yasya nāma mahad yaśaḥ**

**There is no equal to the Lord in great qualities. Yajur Veda**

**na tat samaś cābhyadhikaḥ kuto ‘nyaḥ**

**There is no other who is equal or greater. BG 11.43**

**svayaṁ tv asāmyātiśayas tryadhīśaḥ**

**No one is equal to him or superior to him. He is master of the three energies and three Viṣṇus SB 3.2.21**

**nityaṁ hi nāsti jagati bhutaṁ sthavara-jaṅgamam**  
**ṛte tam ekaṁ puruṣaṁ vāsudevaṁ sanātanam**

**There is nothing eternal in the universe except the one eternal person Vāsudeva, the source moving and nonmoving beings. Mahābhārata\|\|17\|\|**

### Text 18

*sarveśvaro harir eva sṛṣṭi-saṁhārau karoti caturmukha-kapālinau tu tad vaśyau tayor nimittamātram eva—“nimmtaṁ paramīśasya viṣvasvarganirodhayoḥ \| hiraṇyagarbhaḥ sarvaś ca kālasyārūpaṇas tava \|\| brahmāṇam indraṁ rudraṁ ca svayaṁ varuṇam; eva ca \| nigṛhya harate tasmāt tasmād dharir ihocyate\|\|” ityādi-smaraṇāt \|\|18\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord Viṣṇu is the creator and destroyer. Brahmā and Śiva are under his control. They are only his instruments. It is said in the smṛtis:**

**nimittaṁ param īśasya viṣva-svarga-nirodhayoḥ \|**  
**hiraṇyagarbhaḥ śarvaś ca kālasyārūpaṇas tava \|\|**

**Even Lord Brahmā and Lord Śiva act only as your instruments in cosmic creation and annihilation, which are ultimately done by you, the Supreme Lord, in your invisible aspect of time. SB 10.71.8**

**brahmāṇam indraṁ rudraṁ ca svayaṁ varuṇam eva ca \|**  
**nigṛhya harate yasmāt tasmād dharir ihocyate\|\|**

**Since he controls and subdues (*****harate*****) Brahmā, Indra, Śiva, and Varuṇa he is called Hari. Padma Purāṇa \|\|18\|\|**

### Text 19

*śrī-viṣṇu-purāṇe ‘piy etad abhipretyāha śrīmān parāśaraḥ—“juṣan rajoguṇaṁ tatra svayaṁ viśveṣvaro hariḥ \| brahma bhutvātha jagato visṛṣṭe saṁ pravartate \|\| sṛṣṭaṁ ca yāty anuyugaṁ yāvat kalpa-vikalpanā \| sattvabhug bhagavān viṣṇur apameya parākramaḥ \| tamodrekī ca kalpante rudra-rūpī janārdanaḥ \| maitreyākhila-bhūtāni bhakṣayaty atibhīṣaṇah” iti \|\|19\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Parāsara states the same in Viṣṇu Purāṇa:**

**juṣan rajoguṇaṁ tatra svayaṁ viśveṣvaro hariḥ \|**  
**brahma bhutvātha jagato visṛṣṭe saṁpravartate \|\|**  
**sṛṣṭaṁ ca yāty anuyugaṁ yāvat kalpa-vikalpanā \|**  
**sattva-bhug bhagavān viṣṇur aprameya-parākramaḥ \|**  
**tamodrekī ca kalpante rudra-rūpī janārdanaḥ \|**  
**maitreyākhila-bhūtāni bhakṣayaty atibhīṣaṇah**

**Absorbed in rajoguṇa the Lord becomes Brahmā and, manifesting from the universe creates everything. Viṣṇu of immeasurable strength, enjoyer of sattva, maintains the creation for the life of Brahmā. O Maitreya! Viṣṇu, becoming terrifying Śiva, dominated by tamoguṇa, destroys all beings. Viṣṇu Purāṇa\|\|19\|\|**

### Text 20

*tatraiva raivataṁ prati brahma caivam āha—“yasya prasdādad ahaṁ acyutasya bhutaḥ prajā-srṣṭikaro antakārī \| krodhāc ca rudraḥ sthiti-hetu-bhuto yasmāc ca madhye puruṣaḥ parastāt \|\| mad rūpam asthaya sṛjaty ajo yaḥ sthitau ca yo ‘ sau puruṣa-svarūpī \| rudrasya rūpeṇa ca yo tti viśvaṁ dhatte tathānanta-vapuḥ samastam” ityādinā \| tad evaṁ samābhyadhikaśūnyatvāt pāramaiśvaryaṁ śrīviṣṇau siddham \| tat-sāmya-darṣinas tu doṣaḥ smaryate—“yat tu nārāyaṇaṁ devaṁ brahma-rudrādi-daivataḥ \| samatvenābhijānati sa pāṣānḍī bhaved druvaṁ” titi \|\|20\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Brahma says to Raivata in Viṣṇu Purāṇa:**

**yasya prasādād ahaṁ acyutasya bhutaḥ prajā-srṣṭikaro antakārī \|**  
**krodhāc ca rudraḥ sthiti-hetu-bhuto yasmāc ca madhye puruṣaḥ parastāt \|\|**  
**mad rūpam asthaya sṛja-tyago yaḥ sthitau ca yo ‘ sau puruṣa-svarūpī \|**  
**rudrasya rūpeṇa ca yo atti viśvaṁ dhatte tathānanta-vapuḥ samastam**

**By the mercy of Viṣṇu I was born as the creator of the living beings, and out of his anger, Śiva is the destroyer. From that Supreme Being arose the puruṣa who is the cause of maintenance. The Lord with an infinite body, who, situated in my form, created the world, and then, during maintenance, gave up that form, and who destroys the whole universe as Śiva, supports the whole universe. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

**yat tu nārāyaṇaṁ devaṁ brahma-rudrāadi-daivataḥ \|**  
**samatvenābhijānati sa pāṣānḍī bhaved druvaṁ**

**The person who considers Viṣṇu to be equal to Brahmā and Śiva is a pāṣāṇḍi (heretic beyond the Vedas) for certain. \|\|20\|\|**

### Text 21

*yat tu bhāratāntargataupamanyavākhyāne śrī-kṛṣṇena jāmbavatī-putrārthāṁ tapasā rudraḥ samāradhitaḥ rudrāṅgebhyaḥ saviṣṇunāṁ devānām utpattir iti pāramaiśvaryaṁ rudrasya durnivāram ity ajñāḥ kalpayanti, tad atīva-sthūlam \| pūrvokta-śrutyādibhya eva \| rudraḥ khalu bāṇa-yuddhe bhagavatā parābhutas tuṣṭāva ca mūlabhutaṁ taṁ mohitaś ca mohinī-veśeṇa tena mohitaś ca vṛka-kleśādito brahma-vadha-pātakāc ceti smaraṇāc ca \|\| 21\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The foolish argue, “In the story about Aupamanyava in the Mahābhārata, Kṛṣṇa worshipped Śiva to obtain a son from Jāmbavatī. From the limbs of Śiva arose Viṣṇu and the devatās. The supreme power of Śiva cannot be denied.” This is most stupid since the previous statements of śruti and smṛti refute this. Rather, Śiva was defeated in Kṛṣṇa’s battle with Bāṇa and then praised Kṛṣṇa as his source. Śiva was bewildered by the Lord in guise of Mohinī and was bewildered by Vṛka’s torment (and saved by the Lord). He was saved from the sin of killing a brāhmaṇa. These incidents are related in the smṛtis. \|\|21\|\|**

### Text 22

*tasmāt svetareṣu sarveṣu sakāmeṣu rudropāsanā-sthemne svakīyasya tasya tathārādhanaṁ khyāpayaṁs tad antaryāminam ātmānam asau satkarotīti mantayvam \| “aham ātmā hi lokānāṁ viśveṣāṁ pāṇḍu-nandana \| tasmād ātmānam evāgre rudraṁ saṁpūjayāmy aham \| mayā kṛtaṁ pramāṇaṁ hi lokaḥ samanuvartate \| pramāṇāni hi pūjyāni tatas taṁ pūyayāmy aham \| na hi viṣṇuḥ praṇamati kasmaicid vibudhāya ca \| ata ātmānam eveti tato rudraṁ bhajāmy aham” iti nārānanīye bhagavad-vākyād eva \|\|22\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It should be understood that the Lord arranges himself as Śiva’s antaryāmī and then tells people who are not his devotees and have material desires to worship Śiva in order to make them steady in their worship. The Lord says:**

**aham ātmā hi lokānāṁ viśveṣāṁ pāṇḍu-nandana \|**  
**tasmād ātmānam evāgre rudraṁ saṁpūjayāmy aham \|**  
**mayā kṛtaṁ pramāṇaṁ hi lokaḥ samanuvartate \|**  
**pramāṇāni hi pūjyāni tatas taṁ pūjayāmy aham \|**  
**na hi viṣṇuḥ praṇamati kasmaicid vibudhāya ca \|**  
**ata ātmānam eveti tato rudraṁ bhajāmy aham**

**O Arjuna! I am the soul of all people of the universe. Therefore, in worshipping Śiva, I worship myself. Whatever I do, the people follow. Whatever I do is respected by all. Thus I worship Śiva. Viṣṇu does not offer respects to any devatā. In worshipping Śiva I worship myself alone. Nārāyanīya, Mahābhārata \|\|22\|\|**

### Text 23

*atra viśveṣāṁ antaryāmy aham atas taptāyaḥ piṇḍavad aviviktaṁ rudrāveśinaṁ mad-aṁśaṁ ahaṁ pūjayāmi \| rudrādayo devāḥ pūjyā iti pramāṇaṁ mayā kṛtaṁ tad anyathā vyākupyet tad artham ahaṁ tān pūjayāmi svotkrṣṭasyābhāvad eva tad buddhyāhaṁ na kiñcid bhajāmi kintu tādṛśaṁ mad-aṁśam ahaṁ bhajāmīti viṣphuṭaṁ \| brahma-rudrādi-sarvāntaryāmī viṣṇur iti tatraiva rudraṁ prayuktaṁ brahmaṇā – “bhavāntarātmā mama ca ye cānye dehi saṁjñitāḥ \| sarveṣāṁ sākṣibhuṭo ‘sau na grāhyaḥ kenacit kvacit” \|\|23\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**I am the antaryāmī of the lords of the universe (I enter them). Thus I worship my aṁśa Śiva not distinguishing him from myself. An iron ball become like fire by contact with fire. Śiva and others are worthy of worship. I set the example by worshipping him. If I did otherwise this statement would be contradicted. Thus I worship Śiva and others. If I think there is no one of my status, then I should not worship anyone. But because Śiva is my aṁśa I worship him.**

**Brahmā says to Śiva that Viṣṇu is the antaryāmī of himself and Śiva:**

**bhavāntarātmā mama ca ye cānye dehi-saṁjñitāḥ \|**  
**sarveṣāṁ sākṣibhuto ‘sau na grāhyaḥ kenacit kvacit**

**The witness of all beings, who is the antaryāmī of Śiva, me and other beings, cannot be perceived by anyone at any time. Brahmā Purāṇa \|\|23\|\|**

### Text 24

*tasmāt svabhakta-rudrasya sakāmeṣūpapsanādṛḍhatāyai tat-satkṛtis tad arthitā tena kṛṭeti na tasya pāramāiśvaryam \|\|24\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus in order to make the faith of Śiva’s devotees who desire material benefits strong, the Lord on his request makes this arrangement (and statements). This does not indicate that Śiva is supreme \|\|24\|\|**

### Text 25

*yatra sāmānya-viśeṣābhyāṁ praśnottare bhavetāṁ tatraiva tan niścīyate \| yatra viśeṣeṇaiva te syātāṁ tatra tātparyāntaraṁ kalpanīyam iti, yathā bharate—“kim ekaṁ daivataṁ loke kiṁ vāpy ekaṁ parāyaṇam \| stuvantaḥ kaṁ kam arcantaḥ prāpnuyur mānavāḥ śubhaṁ \| ko dharmāḥ sarva-dharmāṇāṁ bharataḥ paramo mataḥ \| kaṁ japan mucyate jantur janma-saṁsāra-bandhanāt” iti \| yudhiṣṭhreṇa sāmānyataḥ para-tattvaṁ pṛṣṭo bhīṣmo viśeṣeṇottarayati – “jagat prabhuṁ deva-devam anantaṁ puruṣottamam \| stuvan nama sahasreṇa puruṣah sa tatotthitaḥ” ityādibir iha hi viṣṇur eva paramaṁ tattvaṁ tad-bhaktyā saṁsāra-nivṛttir iti viśiṣyottaritaṁ \| evaṁ tatraivānyatra bahutra sāmānya-viśeṣa-rūpa-praśnottarābhyaṁ bhagavato viṣṇor eva para-tattvaṁ nirṇītaṁ dṛśyate \| śāstra-vistara-bhiyā nātra tan nirdiṣṭaṁ \|\|25\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One can determine who is superior where there are specific answers to general questions. Where specific answers are given, coming to other conclusions is mere concoction.**

**kim ekaṁ daivataṁ loke kiṁ vāpy ekaṁ parāyaṇam \|**  
**stuvantaḥ kaṁ kam arcantaḥ prāpnuyur mānavāḥ śubhaṁ \|**  
**ko dharmāḥ sarva-dharmāṇāṁ bhavataḥ paramo mataḥ \|**  
**kaṁ japan mucyate jantur janma-saṁsāra-bandhanāt**

**Who is the supreme Lord for the people? Who is the only shelter? Who should be praised and who should be worshipped so that mankind attains auspiciousness? What do you consider to be the supreme dharma of all dharmas? What name should be uttered so that people can attain liberation from the bondage of birth and death?**

**When Yudhiṣṭhira asked this general question, Bhīṣma gave a specific answer.**

**jagat-prabhuṁ deva-devam anantaṁ puruṣottamam \|**  
**stuvan nama-sahasreṇa puruṣah sa tatotthitaḥ**

**A person gains liberation from saṁsāra by chanting the thousand names, praising the infinite supreme Lord, master of the universe, lord of lords.**

**In answer to the question it is specifically said that Viṣṇu is the Supreme Being and by devotion to him one attains liberation from saṁsāra. In Mahābhārata there are many other specific answers to general questions which prove that Viṣṇu is the supreme Lord. These have not been supplied for fear of enlarging this work. \|\|25\|\|**

### Text 26

*aupamanyavākhyāne tu viśeṣeṇaiva praśnottarayoḥ sattvāt tatra tātparyantaraṁ kalpanīyam \| tac ca darśitaṁ eva \| itarathā samudrasyāpīśvaratāpattiḥ śrī-rāmeṇa tat pūjāyā vidhānāt \|\|26\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since there is a specific answer given the questions in the story of Aupamanyava, coming to any other conclusion on this topic of who is supreme is a concoction. That was shown already. If one does not accept the true conclusion one must conclude (by similar logic) that the ocean is the supreme Lord since Rāma worshipped the ocean. \|\|26\|\|**

### Text 27

*evaṁ kvacid bhagavat-pārṣadānāṁ daivatāntarārādhanam api tad rādhyatāvyākhyapanārtham līlā-rūpam eva na hi tat siddhānta-kakṣām ārokṣyati \|\|27\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Similarly when sometimes the Lord’s devotees worship devatās, it is a pastime to teach worship of devatās to others. Those actions cannot be used to conclude that everyone should worship devatās. \|\|27\|\|**

### Text 28

*maheśādi-samākhyā tu mahendrādi-samākhyāvan niṣphalaiva bodhyā \| drone-parvante rudrasya yac chatarudrīyārthatvaṁ uktaṁ tad dhi tad antaryāmi-paraṁ anyathāsaṅgateḥ \| yaś cānte drauṇinā yudhyamānasya rudrasya tejo-marīcibhyaḥ keśavānāṁ bahūṇāṁ prādurbhāvaḥ smaryate, so ‘pi nāyuktas tad antaḥ sthitasya tasya tat-sāhāyyāturasya tathāvirbhāvāt tasmād viṣṇur eva sarvārādhyaḥ rudraṣ tu tad-ārādhako jagad ārādhyaś ceti viṣṇor eva tan niścitam \|\|28\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Arguments about Śiva’s supremacy by citing his names like Mahakam (great lord) are useless. This name is similar to Indre’s name Mahindra. In the Droṇa-parva of the Mahābhārata where Śiva is glorified as supreme in the Śata-rudrīya verses, the meaning is that within Śiva resides the supreme Lord as antaryāmī. Otherwise the meaning would contradict other statements of śruti and smṛti. At the end of that section of Mahābhārata, it is described how, when Śiva was fighting, from the rays of his effulgence many Viṣṇus appeared. But this is no contradiction since Viṣṇu, who resides within Śiva, manifested many forms to help Śiva. Thus it is established that Viṣṇu is to be worshipped by all and that Śiva, because he worships Viṣṇu, is worshipped by everyone. \|\|28\|\|**

### Text 29

*iha kaścit evaṁ manyate brahma-viṣnu-rudrā yat kāryaṇi bhavanti, tat sadāśivākhyaṁ mūlaṁ tattvaṁ bhavet \| kaivalyopaniṣadi –“atyāśrama-sthaḥ sakalendriyāṇi nirudhya bhaktyā svaguruṁ praṇamya \| hṛt-puṇḍarīkaṁ virajaṁ viśuddhaṁ vicintya madhye śivadaṁ viśokaṁ \|\| acintyam avyakam ananta-rūpaṁ śivaṁ praśāntaṁ amṛtaṁ brahma-yonim \| tad adi-madhyānta-vihīnam ekaṁ vibhuṁ cid-ānandam arūpaṁ adbhutaṁ \|\| umā-sahāyaṁ paremeśvaraṁ prabhuṁ trilocanaṁ nīla-kaṇṭhaṁ praśantam \| dhyātvā munir gacchati bhūta-yoniṁ samasta-sākṣiṁ tamasaḥ parastāt \|\| sa brahma sa śivaḥ sendraḥ so ‘ksāraḥ paramaḥ svarāt \| sa eva viṣnūḥ saprāṇaḥ sa kālo ‘gniḥ sa candramāḥ \|\| sa eva sarvaṁ yad bhūtaṁ yac ca bhavyaṁ sanātanaṁ \| jñatvā tvaṁ mṛtyuṁ atyeti nāṇyaḥ panthā vimuktaye” ityādi śravaṇāt \| upariṣṭāc ca – “triṣu dhāmasu tad bhogyaṁ bhoktā bhogaś ca tad bhavet \| tebhyo vilakṣaṇaḥ sākṣī cinmāto ‘haṁ sadāśivaḥ” iti \| anyatra ca “śivam advaitaṁ caturthaṁ “ manyante iti \|\|29\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Some persons think that whatever actions Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva perform are actions of the root entity Sadāśiva. In the Kaivalya Upaniṣad it is said:**

**atyāśrama-sthaḥ sakalendriyāṇi nirudhya bhaktyā svaguruṁ praṇamya \|**  
**hṛt-puṇḍarīkaṁ virajaṁ viśuddhaṁ vicintya madhye śivadaṁ viṣokaṁ \|\|**  
**acintyam avyakam ananta-rūpaṁ śivaṁ praśāntaṁ amṛtaṁ brahma-yonim \|**  
**tad adi-madhyānta-vihīnam ekaṁ vibhuṁ cid-ānandam arūpaṁ adbhutaṁ \|\|**  
**umā-sahāyaṁ paremeśvaraṁ prabhuṁ trilocanaṁ nīla-kaṇṭhaṁ praśantam \|**  
**dhyātvā munir gaccati bhuta-yoniṁ samasta-sākṣiṁ tamasaḥ parastāt \|\|**  
**sa brahma sa śivaḥ sendraḥ so ‘ksāraḥ paramaḥ svarāt \|**  
**sa eva viṣnūḥ saprāṇaḥ sa kālo ‘gniḥ sa candramāḥ \|\|**  
**sa eva sarvaṁ yad bhūtaṁ yac ca bhavyaṁ sanātanaṁ \|**  
**jñatvā taṁ mṛtyuṁ atyeti nāṇyaḥ panthā vimuktaye \|\|**

**Completely renounced, the sage, offering respects to his guru and controlling his senses, meditated on Śiva situated in the heart, who is pure, without contamination, without sorrow, and who gives auspiciousness. By meditating on the supreme lord Śiva who was endowed with three eyes and blue throat, who was accompanied by his consort Umā, and who is inconceivable, invisible, peaceful, with countless forms, eternal, without beginning, middle or end, the one powerful entity, composed of knowledge and bliss, the astonishing person with no form, the sage attained the original source, beyond the material realm. He is Brahmā, he is Śiva, he is Indra, he is the indestructible, the supreme independent. He is Viṣṇu, Prāṇa, Kālāgni and the moon. He is all beings who have appeared in the past and who will appear in the future. Knowing him, one surpasses death. There is no other path for liberation. Kaivalya Upaniṣad**

**Later in the same work it is said:**

**triṣu dhāmasu tad bhogyaṁ bhoktā bhogaś ca yad bhavet \|**  
**tebhyo vilakṣaṇaḥ sākṣī cinmāto ‘haṁ sadāśivaḥ**

**I, Sadāśiva, the conscious witness, am superior to whatever has been enjoyed and is to be enjoyed and to whoever is an enjoyer in the three worlds.**

**śivam advaitaṁ caturtham**

**Śiva is the fourth goal (beyond the three conditioned states). Māṇḍukya Upaniṣad  \|\|29\|\|**

### Text 30

*tasmāt sadāśivo mūla-kāraṇaṁ brahmādayas tu tasya kāryāṇi “brahma viṣṇur upendras te sarve prasūyante” iti śruty-āntarāt ca \| vijñānānanda-rūpāt viśuddhāt tasmād acintya-śakti-yogād brahmādi-sṛṣtis tad upāsanayā vimuktiḥ \| śvetāśvataropanisādi caiṣa eva hara-rudrādi-śabdair abhidhīyate \|\|30\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Therefore based on these scriptural statements, Sadāśiva is the basis and Brahma, Viṣṇu and Śiva are his products.** ***Brahmā viṣṇur upendras te sarve prasūyante*****: Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Upendra arise from Sadāśiva. Since Sadāśiva has a form of knowledge and bliss, is pure and endowed with inconceivable śakti, he creates Brahmā and by worshipping him one can attain liberation. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad also describes him as such, with words like Hara and Rudra. \|\|30\|\|**

### Text 31

*atrocyate sadāśivo ‘sau viṣṇur eva tasyaiva sakala-doṣa-śūnyasya sarvadāiva maṅgalātmakatvāt, smṛtiś ca—“evaṁ prakāram amalaṁ nityam avyākhyam aksayam \| samasta-heya-rahitaṁ viṣṇvākhyaṁ paramaṁ padam” iti, “pavitrāṇāṁ pavitraṁ yo maṅgalānāṁ ca maṅgalām \| daivataṁ daivatānāṁ ca bhūtānāṁ yo ‘vyayaḥ pitā” iti ca \| udadhi-manthanodbhūtayā śrī-devyāpi sa eva sumaṅgala-rūpo nirṇītaḥ \|\|31\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In these statements, Sadāśiva refers to Viṣṇu, since only Viṣnu is always auspicious, being free of all faults. Smṛti says:**

**evaṁ prakāram amalaṁ nityam avyākhyam aksayam \|**  
**samasta-heya-rahitaṁ viṣṇvākhyaṁ paramaṁ padam**  
**Viṣṇu is the supreme goal. He is pure eternal beyond words, without decay and devoid of all bad qualities. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**  
**pavitrāṇāṁ pavitraṁ yo maṅgalānāṁ ca maṅgalām \|**  
**daivataṁ daivatānāṁ ca bhūtānāṁ o ‘vyayaḥ pitā**

**Viṣṇu is the purest of the pure, the most auspicious of all auspicious persons, the supreme lord among all lords and the instructible father of all beings. Viṣṇu-sahasra-nāma \|\|31\|\|**

### Text 32

*subalopaniṣadi ca—“antaḥ śarīre nihito guhāyam aja eko nityo yasya pṛṭhivī śarīraṁ” ity upakramya “sa eva sarva-bhūtāntarātmāpahata-pāpmā divyo deva eko nārāyaṇaḥ “ ity apahata-pāpmatayā sumaṅgalaṭvaṁ narāyaṇsya viṣnor evābhihitam \| nārāyaṇopaniṣadi ca “atha puruṣo ha vai nārāyaṇo \[kāmayata prajāḥ sṛjeteti nārāñaṇā dvandhā jāyate” ityādinā nārāyaṇa-śabditād devakī-putrād viṣṇor brahma-rudrādi-sṛṣṭir jāyate sa ca samasta-heya-rāhityāt sumaṅgālo bhavati, tad upāsanayā vimuktir iti corddham upadiśyate, “atha nityo niṣkalāṅko nirākhyāto nirvikalpo nirañjanah śuddho deva eko nārāyaṇo na dvitīyo ‘sti kaścit \| ya evaṁ veda sa viṣṇur eva bhavati” ityādinā \| anyatra ca śruyate, “eko ha vai devo narāyaṇa āsīn na brahmā na īśānaḥ \| eko da vai devo nārāyaṇa āṣīn na brahmā na ca śaṅkaraḥ \| sa munir bhutvā samacintayat \| tatra ete vyajāyanta viśve hiraṇyagarbhāgni-yama-varuṇa-rudrendrāḥ” ityādi \|\|32\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Subala Upaniṣad says:**

**antaḥ śarīre nihito guhāyam aja eko nityo yasya pṛṭhivī śarīraṁ**  
**sa eva sarva-bhūtāntarātmāpahata-pāpmā divyo deva eko nārāyaṇaḥ**

**He resides in the body within the heart. He is the one eternal. The earth is his body. He is the soul within all beings. He is devoid of sin. He is the most attractive Lord. He is the one Nārāyaṇa.**

**Since he is devoid of all sin, he is most auspicious. Nārāyaṇa means Viṣṇu. Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad says:**  
**atha puruṣo ha vai nārāyaṇo ‘kāmayata prajāḥ sṛjeteti nārāyaṇād brahmā jāyate**

**Nārāyaṇa desired that the living entities manifest. From Nārāyaṇa Brahmā was born.**

**From Nārāyaṇa, son of Devakī, or Viṣṇu, entities like Śiva, Brahmā were created. Because he is devoid of all bad qualities, he is most auspicious. He give liberation. These qualities will be discussed now.**

**atha nityo niṣkalaṅko nirākhyāto nirvikalpo nirañjanaḥ śuddho deva eko nārāyaṇo na dvitīyo ‘sti kaścit \| ya evaṁ veda sa viṣṇur eva bhavati.**

**Viṣṇu is one without a second. He is eternal without fault, without change, beyond description, without contamination, and pure. The person who knows Viṣṇu attains a form like Viṣṇu. Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad**

**Elsewhere it is said:**

**eko ha vai devo narāyana āsīn na brahmā na īśānaḥ \|**  
**eko da vai devo nārāyaṇa āṣīn na brahma na ca śaṅkaraḥ \|**  
**sa munir bhutvā samacintayat \|**  
**tatra ete vyajāyanta viśve hiraṇyagarbhāgni-yama-varuṇa-rudrendrāḥ**

**Nārāyaṇa existed before all else. Brahmā and Śiva did not exist. Becoming inspired, he began to meditate. Then the universe, Brahmā, Agni, Varuṇa, Śiva and Indra appeared. Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad \|\|32\|\|**

### Text 33

*taittirīyake ca śiva-śabdo nārāyaṇa-śabda-sāmānādhikaraṇenopadiṣṭaḥ śruyate—“sahasra-śīrṣaṁ tam devaṁ viśvākṣaṁ viśva-śambhuvam \| viśvaṁ nārāyaṇaṁ devam akṣaraṁ paramaṁ padam \| viśvataḥ paramaṁ nityaṁ viśvaṁ nārāyaṇaṁ hariṁ \| viśvam evedaṁ puruṣaṁ tad-viśvaṁ upajīvati \| patiṁ viśvasyātmeśvaraṁ śāśvataṁ śivam acyutam \| nārāyaṇaṁ mahājñeyaṁ viśvātamānaṁ parāyaṇam \|\| nārāyaṇaḥ parajyotir ātmā nārāyaṇaḥ param \| nārāyaṇaḥ paraṁ brahma tattvaṁ nārāyaṇaḥ param \|\| nārāyaṇaḥ paro dhyātā dhyanaṁ nārāyaṇaḥ param \| yac ca kiñcit jagaty asmin dṛśyate śruyate ‘pi vā \|\| antar bahiṣ ca tat sarvaṁ vyāpya nārāyaṇaḥ sthita” ityādinā \| ata evaṁ uktaṁ nārāyaṇīye –“ tattvaṁ jijñāsamānānāṁ hetubhiḥ sarvatomukhaiḥ \| tattvam eko mahāyogī harir nārāyaṇaḥ prabhuḥ” iti \|\|33\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In the Taittirīya Upaniṣad Śiva’s name is equated with that of Nārāyaṇa.**

**sahasra-śīrṣaṁ tam devaṁ viśvākṣaṁ viśva-śambhuvam \|**  
**viśvaṁ nārāyaṇaṁ devam akṣaraṁ paramaṁ padam \|**  
**viśvataḥ paramaṁ nityaṁ viśvaṁ nārāyaṇaṁ hariṁ \|**  
**viśvam evedaṁ puruṣaṁ tad-viśvaṁ upajīvati \|**  
**patiṁ viśvasyātmeśvaraṁ śāśvataṁ śivam acyutam \|**  
**nārāyaṇaṁ mahājñeyaṁ viśvātamānaṁ parāyaṇam \|\|**  
**nārāyaṇaḥ parajyotir ātmā nārāyaṇaḥ param \|**  
**nārāyaṇaḥ paraṁ brahma tattvaṁ nārāyaṇaḥ param \|\|**  
**nārāyaṇaḥ paro dhyātā dhyanaṁ nārāyaṇaḥ param \|**  
**yac ca kiñcit jagaty asmin dṛśyate śruyate ‘pi vā \|**  
**antar bahiṣ ca tat sarvaṁ vyāpya nārāyaṇaḥ**

**Nārāyaṇa has a thousand heads. He is the eye of the universe and the cause of the universe. He is the universe. He is the supreme indestructible abode. He is superior to the universe. He is the eternal universe. This universe is the Lord. He gives life to the universe. He is the master, the soul and the lord of the universe. He is eternal. He is Śiva. He is acyuta. Nārāyaṇa is to known. He is the soul of the universe and the shelter of the universe. He is the supreme light. He is the supreme ātmā. Nārāyaṇa is supreme Brahman. He is the highest truth. He is the supreme meditator and the supreme object of meditation. What is seen or heard within the universe, is Nārāyaṇa. Nārāyaṇa is situated inside and outside the universe. He is spread everywhere.**

**In Nārāyaṇīya it is said:**

**tattvaṁ jijñāsamānānāṁ hetubhiḥ sarvatomukhaiḥ \|**  
**tattvam eko mahāyogī harir nārāyaṇaḥ prabhuḥ**

**Lord Nārāyaṇa alone, the great yogī, is the truth, discerned by inquirers through all reasoning. \|\|33\|\|**

### Text 34
