*nārāyaṇa-śabdaḥ khalu lakṣmī-pateḥ saṁjñā, tasyām eva ṇatva-vidhānāt \| tad āha bhagavān pāṇinīḥ –“pūrva-padāt saṁjñāyām agah” iti \| pūrva-pada-sthān nimittāt parasya nasya ṇaḥ syāt saṁjñāyaṁ na tu ga-kara-vyavadhāne iti tad arthaḥ \| nārāyaṇah agāh kiṁ ṛg yanam iti \| tasmād viṣṇur eva sadāśivaśabditaḥ itarathā sarva-śruti-smṛti-vyākopah tatraiva ca brahma-vidyātmātva-vyapadeśa-paramatma-śabdā virudhyeran tau śabdau kila viṣnor evabhidhāyakau prasiddhau \| śivādi-śabdās tatraiva pravartante prāg uktam \|\|34\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Nārāyaṇa indicates the husband of Lakṣmī. This can be derived using the rule for** ***ṇa*****. Pāṇinī says** ***pūrva-padāt saṁjñāyām agaḥ*****: the syllable n becomes ṇ after an r, if the syllable** ***ga*** **does not intervene at the end of the first word. (Pāṇini 8.4.3) (Thus** ***ayana*** **(shelter) becomes** ***ayaṇa*****. Nārāyaṇa means the shelter of the tattvas (*****nāra*****).) Thus Sadāśiva refers to Viṣṇu. To interpret otherwise would mean that all the śrutis and smṛtis would be contradicted. In Kaivalya Upaniṣad as well, use of words like Paramātmā to indicate ātmā in the section on brahma-vidyā would be contradicted. Brahman and Paramātmā are both used to indicate Viṣṇu. That is well known. That the word Śiva is used to denote Viṣṇu as was already mentioned. \|\|34\|\|**

### Text 35

*nanūmā-sahāya-trilocana-līlakaṇṭha-śabdāḥ kathaṁ atra pravarter animitta-virahād iti cen mandam etat teṣāṁ arthāntara-sattvāt \| tathāhi –“umā kīrtiḥ umā sate haimavatī haridrā-kīrtiṣu iti viśva-prakāśāt \| sā sarādau sahāyā yasya tam \| trinetraṁ trīṇi bhūta-vartamāna-bhaviṣyad visayāṇi jñānāṇi yasya taṁ trikālajñam ity arthaḥ –“vedāhaṁ samatītāni vartamānani cārjuna \| bhaviṣyāṇi ca bhūtāni maṁ tu veda na kaścana” iti smṛteḥ \| nīlo maṇi-viśeṣaḥ sva-varāsādṛṣyātipriyatvāt kaṇṭhe yasya tam indranīla-mānihāriṇam ity arthaḥ \| nidhi-viśeṣo va nīlāḥ kaṇṭhe yasya sa tathā \| parokṣavādena vidyopadeśa-pravṛtter atra na śabda-svārasya-bhaṅgo doṣaḥ \|\|35\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“There is no good reason to use words like ‘assistant of Umā,’ ‘three eyed lord’ and ‘blue-throated lord’ to indicate Viṣṇu.” That is a foolish argument since those words have another meaning. According to Viśva-prakāśa the word** ***umā*** **means fame, chastity, wife of Śiva, turmeric and beauty. Thus** ***umāsahāya*** **means he who is assisted by his own fame to create the universe.** ***Trinetra*** **means he who has knowledge (*****netra*****) of past, present and future (*****tri*****). Smṛti says:**

**vedāhaṁ samatītāni vartamānani cārjuna \|**  
**bhaviṣyāṇi ca bhūtāni maṁ tu veda an kaścana**

**I know everything past, present and future. But no one knows me. BG 7.26**

**Nīla is a particular gem (sapphire). Nīla-kaṇṭha thus means “he who wears a sapphire on his throat. It is dear because it resembles his complexion.” Or it means “he who has on his throat the special gem called nīla.” nīla.” There is a tendency when discussing secret knowledge to use indirect expression (parokṣa-vāda). Thus there is no fault in giving an unusual meaning to these words in this case. \|\|35\|\|**

### Text 36

*tad abhimate sadāśive tv ete śabdā na pravarteran pārvatī-patitvādīnāṁ nimittānāṁ tatrābhāvāt—kārye kapāliny eva teṣāṁ bhāvāt \|\|36\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The meaning of words used to describe Sadāśiva (cause of all causes etc.) does not correspond to the actual qualities of Śiva. These other names are suitable for he who has skulls around his neck (Kapalī). \|\|36\|\|**

### Text 37

*śvetāśvataropaniṣadi ca para-brahma-bhuto viṣṇur evopadiṣṭaḥ, “brahmavādino vadanti” ity upakrāntasya brahma-śabdasyāpūrter abhyāsāt “sarva-vyāpī bhagavān”\| “evaṁ sa devo bhagavān vareṇyaḥ” ii bhagavac-chabdo ‘py abhyastaḥ \| sa ca viṣṇau mukhyaḥ \| viṣṇu-prameyaṁ hi purāṇaṁ “bhāgavatam” ity ākhyāyate \| “saṁbharteti tathā bhartā “ityādinā tasmin evāmum vyutpādya punar nigamayati—“evam eṣa mahā-śabdo maitreya bhagavān iti \| parama-brahma-bhutasya vāsudevasya nānya-gaḥ” iti śri-viṣṇu purāṇe \| tasmād viṣṇu-paraiveyam upaniṣat \| yadi sadāśivo ‘pi brahma-śabditatvādi-dharmeti vācyaṁ tarhi saṁjñā-mātra eva visaṁvādo na tu saṁjñinīti yat kiñcit etat \|\|37\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, Viṣṇu is designated as para-brahman. Starting with the words** ***brahma-vādino vadanti*****, the word** ***brahman*** **is repeated many times (in reference to Viṣṇu). The word** ***bhagavān*** **is used in phrases like** ***sarva-vyāpī bhagavān*** **and** ***evaṁ sa devo bhagavān vareṇyaḥ*****. And the word** ***bhagavān*** **applies to Viṣṇu mainly. The Purāṇa which proves that Viṣṇu is supreme is called Bhāgavatam. In explaining the meaning of** ***bhagavān,*** **it is said** ***saṁbharteti tathā bhartā*****: the syllable** ***bha*** **means the bestower and the maintainer. Then it is said:**

**evam eṣa mahā-śabdo maitreya bhagavān iti \|**  
**parama-brahma-bhutasya vāsudevasya nānya-gaḥ**

**O Maitreya! Thus the great word** ***bhagavān*** **refers to no one except Vāsudeva, the supreme Brahman. Therefore the Upaniṣad refers to Viṣṇu. It can be said that Sadāśiva has the qualities of Brahman. This is only a matter of names. There should be no disagreement about who is designated by the names. \|\|37\|\|**

### Text 38

*nanu trimūrti-madhyo viṣṇur utpattimān—“brahma-viṣṇu-rudrendrās te sarve prasūyante” iti śravanāt garbhodakaśāyinaḥ puruṣād brahma-viṣnu-rudrās trayo guṇāvatārā bhavantīti bhāgavata-prakriyā-darṣanāc ceti cet? satyaṁ brahma-rudrayor jīvatvād eva karma-vaśyotpattyādi-prakīrtanam \| viṣnos tu “eko ‘pi san bahudhā yo vibhāti” “ekaṁ santaṁ bahudhā dṛśyamānam” “ajāyamāno bahudhā vijāyata” ityādiṣu karma-vaśotpattyādi nirāsād apracyuta-svarūpa-śakti-svabhāvasya tasya sṛṣṭi-pālanāya trimūrti-madhye sveccāvatārāvirbhāvadi-paryāyābhivyaktir iti \| ataeva “sṛjāmi tan niyukto ‘ham” ityādau “viṣṇuḥ puruṣa-rūpeṇa paripāti” ityuktam, na tu tad-vaśyaṭa tasyocyate \|\|38\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Some argue as follows. Amongst the three forms, Viṣṇu has birth. It is said in the smṛtis:**

**brahma-viṣṇu-rudrendrās te sarve prasūyante**  
**Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Śiva and Indra are born. Atharva-śiras Upaniṣad**

**From the puruṣa Garbhodakaśāyī appear Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva, who are gunāvatāras. This is stated in Bhāgavatam.**

**This is true. Because Brahmā and Śiva are jīvas, it is stated that they have birth under the control of karma. However Viṣṇu is different. He does not have a birth under control of karma.**

**eko ‘pi san bahudhā yo vibhāti**

**The Lord is one but appears as many. Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad**

**ekaṁ santaṁ bahudhā dṛśyamānam**

**The one form is seen as many. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.4.19)**

**ajāyamāno bahudhā vijāyate**

**Though not born, the Lord appears in many forms. (Yajur Veda 31.19)**

**In order to maintain the creation, the Lord endowed with unfailing svarūpa-śakti and nature appears by his will as an avatāra. Thus it was said:**

**It is never said that Viṣṇu is under the control of karma. \|\|38\|\|**

### Text 39

*subālopaniṣadi ca tamaḥ śabdita-sūkṣma-śaktikāt puruṣāt brahmāṇḍaṁ jātam tasmin sahasra-śīrṣākhya-puruṣa-vapuṣā sa evavirbhūtas tasmād brahma-rudrādi-sṛṣṭir abhūd iti śruyate tad āhuḥ—“kim tad āsīt tasmai sa hovāca, na san nāsan na sad asad iti tasṁāt tamaḥ saṁjāyate tamaso bhūtādir bhūtāder ākāśam ākāśād vāyur vāyor agnir agner āpo ‘dbhyaḥ prthivī tad āṇḍam samabhavat \| tat-samvatsaram uṣitvā dvidhākarot –adhastād bhumir uparṣtād akāśaṁmadhye puruṣo divyaḥ sahasra-śīrṣā puruṣaḥ sahasrākṣaḥ sahastrapāt sahasra-bāhur iti \| so ‘gre bhūtānāṁ mṛtyum asṛjat tryakṣaṁ triśiraskaṁ tripādaṁ khaṇḍa-paraśuṁ tasya brahmā bibhet \| so ‘yaṁ brahmāṇam āviveśa \| sa mānasān saha putrān aṣrjat \| paraśuṁ te ha virājaḥ sapta-mānasān asṛjat \| te ha prajapatayaḥ \| brahmaṇo ‘sya mukhaṁ āsid bāhū rājanyaḥ kṛtaḥ \| uru tad asya tad vaiśyaḥ padbyām śudro ‘jyayata \|\| candramā manso jātaś cakṣoh sūryo ajāyata \| śrotrād vāyuś ca prāṇaś ca hrḍayāt sarvaṁ idaṁ ajāyata \| apānāt niṣādā yakṣa-rakṣa-gandharvā asthibhyaḥ parvatā lomebhyaḥ oṣadhivaṇaspatayo, lalaṭāt kroda-jo rudro ‘jāyata \| tasyai tasya mahato bhūtasya niḥśvasitaṁ etad yad ṛgvedaḥ” ityādinā \|\|39\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In Subala Upaniṣad, it is described how the Lord endowed with his subtle energy called** ***tamas*** **created the universe. In the universe the Lord manifests himself with a body having a thousand heads. From that form, Brahmā and Śiva were created.**

**kim tad āsīt tasmai sahovāca na san nāsan na sad asad iti tasmāt tamaḥ sañjāyate tasmād bhūtādir bhūtāder ākāśaṁ ākāśād vāyur vāyor agnir agner āpo 'dbhyaḥ pṛthivī tad-aṇḍam abhavat \| tat-samvatsaram uṣitvā dvidhākarot –adhastād bhumir uparṣtād akāśaṁ \| madhye puruṣo divyaḥ sahasra-śīrṣā puruṣaḥ sahasrākṣaḥ sahastrapāt sahasra-bāhuḥ\| so ‘gre bhūtānāṁ mṛtyum asṛjat tryakṣaṁ triśiraskaṁ tripādaṁ khaṇḍa-paraśuṁ tasya brahmā bibhet \| so ‘yaṁ brahmāṇam āviveśa \| sa mānasān saha putrān aṣrjat \| te ha virājaḥ sapta-mānasān asṛjat \| te ha prajapatayaḥ brahmaṇo ‘sya mukhaṁ āsid bāhū rājanyaḥ kṛtaḥ \| uru tad asya tad vaiśyaḥ padbyām śudro ‘jyayata \|\| candramā manso jātaś cakṣoh sūryo ajāyata \| śrotrād vāyuś ca prāṇaś ca hrḍayāt sarvaṁ idaṁ ajyaāyta \| apānāt niṣādā yakṣa-rakṣa-gandharvā asthibhyaḥ parvatā lomebhyaḥ oṣadhivaṇaspatayo, lalaṭāt kroda-jo rudro ‘jāyata \| tasyai tasya mahato bhūtasya niḥśvasitaṁ etad yad ṛgvedaḥ \|\|**

**What existed in the beginning? The teacher said to him, "There was no sat. There was no** ***asat*****. There was no** ***sat*** **or** ***asat.*** **From this arose** ***tamas,*** **from** ***tamas*** **came** ***bhūtādi,*** **from** ***bhūtādi*** **came ether, from ether came air, from air came fire, from fire came water and from water came earth. This became the egg. After a year, this divided in two: the lower portion was earth and upper portion was the sky. In the middle there was an attractive puruṣa with a thousand heads, a thousand eyes, a thousand feet and a thousand hands.**

**First he created death for all creatures. Death had three eyes, three heads, three feet and an axe. Brahmā was frightened of him. The Lord entered Brahmā and created his sons with minds. They, the first progeny, created seven minds. These were the prajāpatis. The Lord’s mouth was the brāhmaṇas. From his arms he created kṣatriyas. From his thighs he created vaiśyas. From his feet the śūdras were born. His mind produced the moon. His eyes created the sun. From his ears arose air and prāṇas. From his heart arose everything. From apāna arose the niṣadas, yakṣas rakṣāsas and gandharvas. From his bones arose mountains. From his body hairs arose herbs and trees. From his forehead arose Śiva, born of his anger. From the Lord’s breathing arose the Vedas. \|\|39\|\|**

### Text 40

*śatapathe ca śruyate—“bhūtānam patiḥ saṁvatsare uṣasi reto ‘siñcat tat saṁvatsare kumāro ‘jāyata so ‘rodīt taṁ prajāpatir abravīt \| kumara kiṁ rodiṣi? yat paśo vijāto ‘sīti so ‘bravīd anapahata-pāpmā vā aham asmi, nāma me dhehi pāpmano ‘pahatyā iti \| taṁ prajāpatir abravid rudro ‘sīti \| tasya tan-nāmākarod agnis tad rupam abhavat \| agnir vai rudro yad arodīt tasmād rudraḥ \| so ‘bravīt jyāyan vā aham asmi dehy evaṁ nāmeti \| taṁ prajāpatir abravid bhavo sīti śarvo ‘sīti īṣāno ‘sīti paśupatir asīti ugro ‘sīti bhiṁo ‘sīti mahādevo ‘sīti \| prajāpatir devān asṛjat te pāpmanā saṁvītā ajāyanta \| virupākṣāya brahmaṇaḥ putrāya jyeṣṭhāya śreṣṭhāyāmoghāya karmādhipataye so ‘bravīd varaṁ vṛṇiṣva \| aham eva paśūnām adhipatir asānīti tasmāt rudraḥ paśūnām adhipatir iti” \|\|40\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is said in the Śatapatha-brāhmaṇa:**

**bhūtānam patiḥ saṁvatsare uṣasi reto ‘siñcat tat saṁvatsare kumāro ‘jāyata so ‘rodīt taṁ prajāpatir abravīt \| kumara kiṁ rodiṣi ? yat paśo vijāto ‘sīti so ‘bravīd anapahata-pāpmā vā aham asmi, nāma me dhehi pāpmano ‘pahatyā iti \| taṁ prajāpatir abravid rudro ‘sīti \| tasya tan-nāmākarod agnis tad rupam abhavat \| agnir vai rudro yad arodīt tasmād rudraḥ \| so ‘bravīt jyāyan vā aham asmi dehy evaṁ nāmeti \| taṁ prajāpatir abravid bhavo sīti śarvo ‘sīti īṣāno ‘sīti paśupatir asīti ugro ‘sīti bhiṁo ‘sīti mahādevo ‘sīti \| prajāpatir devān asṛjat te pāpmanā saṁvītā ajāyanta \| virupākṣāya brahmaṇaḥ putrāya jyeṣṭhāya śreṣṭhāyāmoghāya karmādhipataye so ‘bravīd varaṁ vṛṇiṣva \| aham eva paśūnām adhipatir asānīti tasmāt rudraḥ paśūnām adhipatiḥ\|**

**Lord of creatures (Brahmā) sprinkled semen into the womb called samvatsara. In samvatsara, a young male was born. He cried. Prajapati spoke to him. “Child, why are you crying?” He said, “I have sin. My sins have not been destroyed. Give me a name for destroying my sins.” Prajāpati spoke to him. “You are called Rudra.” He gave him that name. Śiva became the form of fire. Rudra is fire. Since he cried, he was called Rudra. Rudra spoke. “I am the eldest. Give me a name.” Prajāpati said, “You are called Bhava, Śarva, Iśāna, Paśupati, Ugra, Bhīma, and Mahādeva.” Prajāpati created the devatās. They arose, covered with sin. (Receiving names, they became followers of Śiva, the eldest son of Brahmā, the excellent, pure lord of actions.) Brahmā said to Śiva, “Please accept a boon.” “I should be lord of all beings.” Therefore Śiva became the lord of all beings. \|\|40\|\|**

### Text 41

*iha hi parasya bhagavataḥ parama-karuṇatvaṁ tasmāt brahma-rudrayor utpattiḥ \| rudrāt brahmaṇo bhītaḥ rudrasyānapahala-pāpamatvaṁ cāvagamyate \| na caivaṁ viṣṇoḥ śruyate \| tasmāt sarveśvaro viṣṇuś caureṣu milito rājeva jagatkāryāya deveṣu praviṣtās tasya sveccābhivyaktir janmety abhidhīyate \| yac ca divyam iti jñānān nṛṇāṁ janma-nivṛttiḥ iti śri-bhagavatābhihitaṁ “janma karma ca me divyam” ityādi \| tasmāt tri-mūrti-madhe ‘vatīrṇo viṣṇuḥ sākṣāt sarvesvara iti siddha tasya devatā-viśeṣatvam \|\|41\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In these texts, one sees that Viṣṇu is most merciful. From him Brahmā and Śiva arose. Brahmā was afraid of Śiva since Śiva was full of sin. This is never said of Viṣṇu. Therefore the lord all beings, Viṣṇu, like a king mingling with thieves, enters among the devatās for performing actions in the universe, and by his will manifests himself. This is called his birth. From knowledge of the Lord comes destruction of birth. The Lord himself says:**

***janma karma ca me divyam evaṁ yo vetti tattvataḥ \|***  
***tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti mām eti so ’rjuna \|\|***

**He who understands in truth my spiritual birth and activities attains me on giving up his body, and does not take birth again. BG 4.9**

**Therefore Viṣṇu appears among the three forms, but is directly the Supreme Lord. This has been proven. He is a special devatā. \|\|41\|\|**

## Chapter Four

**Viṣṇu is to be known by all the Vedas**

### Text 1

*tatrāhuḥ – dvividhāni vākyāni śruyante saguṇa-viṣayāṇi nirguṇa-viṣayāṇi ca \| “yaḥ sarva-jñaḥ sarva-vid yasya jñāna-mayaṁ tapaḥ”, “ya ātmāpahata-pāpmā vijaro vimṛtyuh” ityādīni saguṇa-brahma-parāṇi; “eko devaḥ saevabhuteṣu gūḍhaḥ sarva-vyāpī sarva-bhutāntarātmā karmādyakṣaḥ sarva-bhutādhivāsaḥ sākṣī cetāh kevalo nirguṇaś ca” “satyaṁ jñānam anatantaṁ brahma” ityādīni tu nirguna-brahma-parāṇi \|\|1\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The following argument is given. There are two types of statements in the scriptures: those that describe the supreme as having qualities and those that describe the supreme without qualities. Saguṇa-brahman is described in the following:**

**yaḥ sarva-jñaḥ sarva-vid yasya jñāna-mayaṁ tapaḥ**

**Brahman is omniscient, knowing everything, and his austerity is filled with knowledge. Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad**

**ya ātmāpahata-pāpmā vijaro vimṛtyuh**

**The Lord is without sin, without old age, without death. Chāndogya Upaniṣad**

**Nirguṇa-brahman is expressed in the following:**

**eko devaḥ sarva-bhuteṣu gūḍhaḥ**  
**sarva-vyāpī sarva-bhutāntarātmā**  
**karmādhyakṣaḥ sarva-bhūstādhivāsaḥ**  
**sākṣī cetāh kevalo nirguṇaś ca**

**The Lord alone has entered all beings. He is all-pervading. He is the** ***antaryāmī*** **of all beings. He is the controller of karma. He is the basis of all beings. He is the witness and the giver of consciousness. He is pure (kevala). He is without qualities*****. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad*** **6.11**

**satyaṁ jñānam anantaṁ brahma**

**Brahman is infinite jñāna and existence. Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.1.1 \|\|1\|\|**

### Text 2

*atra saguṇa-vākyānāṁ na guṇa-vidhāne tātparyaṁ tad anuvāda-mātreṇaiva caritārthāt nirguṇa-vākyā-virodhāya tad ekarthatāyā yukteś ca \| tasmāt sarvair vedair nirguṇam eva lakṣyaṁ, na tu sarveśvaro viṣṇur eva teṣāṁ artha iti \|\|2\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The conclusion of the saguṇa statements is not that Brahman has qualities. They can only be interpreted as confirmation of nirguṇa statements by saying that they are repetition (*****anuvāda*****) of commonly known statements (which are incorrect from the absolute point of view), since such statements contradict the nirguṇa statements and since there should only be one meaning in the Vedas. Thus all the Vedas proclaim nirguṇa-brahman, and not Viṣṇu, the lord of all beings. That is the meaning of these statements. \|\|2\|\|**

### Text 3

*mandam idaṁ guṇānāṁ anantarāprāpyānuvādāsambhavāt \| tat-prāptasya hi kīrtanam anuvādaḥ \| na ca te guṇāḥ pratyakṣadi-prāptāḥ kintv aupaniṣadā eva pāramārthikāś ca te, “parāsya śaktiḥ “ ityādau “svābhāvikī” śravaṇāt “ime cānye ca bhagavan nityā yatra mahāguṇāḥ \| prārthyā mahattvami icchadbhir na viyanti sma karhicit” iti nityātvā-smarṇāc ca \| ataeva dahara-vākye “tasmin yad antaḥ tad anveṣṭavyaṁ “ iti mumukṣmṛgyatvaṁ teṣāṁ uktam \|\|3\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is a foolish argument since it is impossible to claim** ***anuvāda*** **for statements which are not contiguous. Glorification of what has just been stated is** ***anuvāda*****. And the qualities mentioned are not obtained by sense perception but through Upaniṣads and are spiritual. The śrutis say** ***parāsya śaktiḥ*****: the Lord has spiritual śakti, which are intrinsic to him (*****svābhāvikī*****). The qualities are eternal:**

**ime cānye ca bhagavan nityā yatra mahāguṇāḥ \|**  
**prārthyā mahattvami icchadbhir na viyanti sma karhicit**

**In the Lord reside eternal, great qualities, which do not disappear at any time, desirable for those who want greatness. SB 1.16.30**

**At the end of the discusion about dahara, it is said** ***tad anveṣṭavyam*****: the Lord with qualities is to be sought by persons desiring liberation. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 8.1.1) \|\|3\|\|**

### Text 4

*yat tv āha –“vācaṁ dhenum upāsītetyādivat saguṇa-vākyāny upāsanārthāni upāsanayā vimṛṣṭā hrd-vṛtittir nirguṇaṁ sūkṣmaṁ praveksyatīti \| itarathopāsanāyāh kāryatve guṇeṣu tātparye ca vākya-bhedāpattir iti \| tadi idaṁ avicāritābhidhānam, teṣu vakyeṣūpāsanā-padādarśanāt tat-pada-virahiṇāṁ teṣāṁ tad arthatva-kalpanāyām “ ātmeti caivopāsīta” ityadinām api tad ārthāpatteḥ \| kiṁca, “ānandādayaḥ pradhānasya” “ vyatihāro viśīṁṣanti hītaravad” ity atrānandāder jīveśābhedasya copāsyatve ‘pi tāttvikatvaṁ parair uktaṁ tadvat sārvajñyāder api ta kiṁ na syāt \|\|4\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The opponent further argues as follows. The statements about saguṇa-brahman are like the statement “He should respect the word as one respects a cow.” The saguṇa statements simple inculcate worship. When the heart is purified (by that worship), the mind may then concentrate on the subtle nirguṇa-brahman. If this reasoning is not accepted, then the result of worship will be realization endowed with qualities, and this will produce a contradiction in the statements of scripture.**

**But this is speaking without thinking, since one does not see the word “worship” used in connection with the statements just quoted. With such reasoning, in the statement “one should worship the ātmā” (where worship is directly mentioned) one should also imagine qualities of the ātmā (since one cannot worship without qualities according to the proponent of nirguṇa-brahman). In the sutras** ***ānandādayaḥ pradhānasya (*****Brahma-sūtra 3.3.12) and** ***vyatihāro viśiṁsanti hītaravat*** **(Brahma-sūtra 3.3.38) the proponent of nirguṇa-brahman equates the jīva with Brahman and makes them the subject of worship with qualities like bliss** ***(ānāndādayaḥ*****). So what harm is there is attributing omniscience and other qualities to the supreme Lord? \|\|4\|\|**

### Text 5

*na cobhaya-vidha-vākyād vikalpaḥ –kadācit saguṇaṁ brahma, kadācin nirguṇamit vācyaṁ, siddhe vastuni vikalpābhāvāt, na hi vahnir uṣṇaḥ kadāacit tu śītala iti śakyam abhidhātum \| anuṣṭhane tu sādhye sa syāt \| ṣoḍaśi-grahaṇāgrahaṇabhyām atirātrivat uditānudita-homavac ca \|\|5\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Brahman should not have alternative versions because of two types of statements. It is impossible for a perfect entity to be sometimes saguṇa and sometimes nirguṇa. It is not possible to define fire as being sometimes hot and sometimes cold. Such interpretation arises in performance of sacrifices where there are two alternatives. Similarly by accepting or not accepting the vessel for soma, two types of atirātra rituals are conducted. \|\|5\|\|**

### Text 6

*yat tu saguṇa-vākyānāṁ vyavahārika-guṇa-vidhāyitvaṁ nirguṇa-vākyānaṁ tu pāramārthika-guṇābhāva-bodhakatvaṁ ity āhus tad atīva-mandaṁ, vyavahārikatva-vāci-pādābhāvād guṇānāṁ mrṣātvena tad-vidhāyināṁ teṣām aprāmāṇyāpatteḥ, ṣoḍaśi-grahanāgrahaṇayos tu tad āpattyā pūrvatantra-virodhāpatteś ca, sad eva samya” iti śruty-uktasya mṛṣātvena brahmaṇaḥ śūnya-bhāvāpatteś ca \| “asan neva sa bhavati asad brahmeti veda cet “asti brahmeti ced veda santam enaṁ tato viduḥ” ity atrāsadvādinaṁ nindatvā sadvādī prastūyate tac ca pīḍyeta \|\|6\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is very foolish to say that the saguṇa statements indicate conditional (*****vyavahārika*****) qualities and the nirguṇa statements indicate absence of qualities in Brahman. The word** ***vyavahārika*** **is never used when mentioning the qualities of Brahman in the scriptures. Since qualities in Brahman are false according to the proponents of nirguṇa-brahman, the scriptural statements attributing qualities to Brahman would all be false. This idea of conditional and spiritual alternatives would also contradict the pūrva-mimāṁsa view of the injunctions about two statements concerning the soma vessel. It would also negate statements such as “Previous to the creation there was sat.” Brahman itself (being described) would become false.**

**asan neva saṁbhavati asad brahmeti veda cet asti brahmeti ced veda santam enaṁ tato viduḥ**

**If one knows that Brahman to be non-existent, he becomes not-existent. If one knows the Brahman to be existent, that person we know to exist. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 6.1)**

**The proponent of asat is criticized and the proponent of sat is praised. But that is also to be rejected according to you. \|\|6\|\|**

### Text 7

*etasmāt saguṇa-vākyāni mānantarāprāptān alaukikān vidadhur eva \| nirguṇa-vākyāni tu prākṛtāṁs tān niṣedhur iti vyavasthā \| “māṁ bhajanti guṇāḥ sarve nirguṇaṁ nirapekṣakam \| suhṛdaṁ priyam ātmānaṁ sāmyāsaṅgādayo ’guṇāḥ \|\| iti smaraṇāt \| “sa sarvabhuta-prakṛtir vikāra-guṇāṁś ca doṣāṁś ca mune vyatīta sattvādayo na santīśe yatra ca prākṛtā guṇāḥ \|sa śuddhaḥ sarva-śuddhebhyaḥ pumān ādyaḥ prasīdatu \|\|” iti vikārātmakān guṇān niṣedhatā punar ananta-kalyāṇa-guṇo ‘sāv iti svarūpānubanidhinaḥ kalyāṇa-guṇān svīkruvatā śrī-parāśareṇa sphuṭīkṛtāsau \|\|7\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The saguṇa statements describe spiritual qualities in the Lord, without resort to interpretation, and the nirguṇa statements indicate absence of material qualities in the Lord.**

**māṁ bhajanti guṇāḥ sarve nirguṇaṁ nirapekṣakam**  
**suhṛdaṁ priyam ātmānaṁ sāmyāsaṅgādayo ’guṇāḥ**

**All superior qualities, which are eternal, such as equality to all beings and attachment to my devotee, reside in me. I am beyond the material** ***guṇas,*** **not dependent on material qualities, and am the devotees’ friend, giving love to them. SB 11.13.40**

***sattvādayo na santīśe yatra ca prākṛtā guṇāḥ \|***  
***sa śuddhaḥ sarva-śuddhebhyaḥ pumān ādyaḥ prasīdatu \|\|***

**In Viṣṇu there are no material guṇas. May the Lord, purer than all pure beings, be pleased with me.** ***(*****Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.9.44)**

**Material qualities are denied in the following.**

***guṇāṁś ca doṣāṁś ca mune vyatīta***

**O sage, the Lord is devoid of good qualities and bad qualities. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa)**

**But later it says** ***samasta-kalyāṇa-guṇātmako hi*****: the Lord is endowed with all auspicious qualities. Thus Parāśara agrees with our proposal by accepting qualities in the Lord which are related to his svarūpa. \|\|7\|\|**

### Text 8

*nirguṇa-vākye ‘pi sākṣitvādayo dharma nirdiṣṭāḥ \| itarathā sakṣyādi-śabdās tasmin na pravarteran, samanvayādhyāyaś ca pīḍyeta \| tatra khalu guṇānāṁ pravṛtti-nimittatvaṁ svīcakāra bhagavān sūtrakāraḥ, “anantas tadeśāt” “antaryāmyadhidaivādiṣu tad dharma vyapadeśāt” “adṛṣyatvādiguṇako dharmotkeḥ” “sarva-dharmopapatteś ca” iti \|\|8\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Even in the nirguṇa statements, the Lord’s qualities such as being the witness etc. are indicated. If one does not accept qualities in the Lord, then words like “being the witness” etc. should not be used to describe the Lord, and the connection between words and statements would be lost. The author of the sutras accepts that the words in this context indicate qualities of the Lord.**

**antas tad-dharmopadeśāt**

**Paramātmā is the person in the eye and the sun, because the teachings describe the Lord's qualities. Vedānta-sūtras 1.1.20**

**The** ***antaryāmī*** **is the Lord because the qualities mentioned in the passage are suitable to the Lord. Vedānta-sūtras 1.2.17**

**The verses refer to the Lord full of qualities like invisibility because they mention particular qualities appropriate to the Lord. Vedānta-sūtras 1.2.21**

**sarva-dharmopapatteś ca**

**The Lord favors the devotee because the Lord is endowed with all qualities. Vedānta-sūtras 2.1.37 \|\|8\|\|**

### Text 9

*sarva-śabdāvācye lakṣaṇā tu na sambhavatīty uditaṁ prāk \| cinmātrādi-śabdasya punar laksaṇāyāṁ lakṣyasyācaitanyatvaṁ bhāga-tyāga-lakṣaṇā tv atra na sambhaved viruddha-bhāgāsambhavād iti tuccham etat \| itthaṁ ca nirguṇa ityanena guna-niṣedhāya sākṣitvādi dharmānuktir iti viphala-jalpitam api dūrapāstam \| śruti-prāptasya sākṣitvādeḥ śrutyānuvādo na sambhavatīty uktaṁ prāk \| tasmād aprākrṭānanga-guṇa-ratnākaro hariḥ sarva-veda-vācyaḥ \|\|9\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It was explained previously that all words cannot be taken as lakṣaṇā for Brahman which cannot be described by words. If the word** ***cinmātrā*** **(consciousness alone) is used to signify Brahman by lakṣaṇā, Brahman would also become an unconscious entity (since** ***cinmātrā*** **cannot describe any quality in Brahman). Accepting bhāga-lakṣaṇā (especially to interpret** ***tat tvam asi*****.) is rejected since it is not possible to have contrary parts in words. words.} This is an insignificant argument.**

**In this way, by taking the word** ***nirguṇa*** **to mean absence of all qualities, words like “being the witness” would be improper expressions. Such useless arguments are thus completely rejected. Previously it was stated that to take śruti statements about “being the witness” as anuvāda is impossible. Thus the Lord, a wealth of infinite spiritual qualities is described in all the Vedas. \|\|9\|\|**

### Text 10

*nirguṇa-cinmātraṁ tv alīlam eva “na tat-samaścābhaydikaś ca dṛśyate” “mattaḥ parataraṁ nānyat kimśit asit dhanañjaya” ityādi śruti-smṛtibhyaḥ \| guṇavān evānurāga-viṣayo na tv aguṇas taucchyāt \| mokṣaś ca saguṇa-jñanād eva—“yadā paśyaḥ paśyate rukma-varṇaṁ kartāram īśaṁ puruṣaṁ brahma-yonim \| tadā vidvān punña-pāpe vidhuya nirañjanaḥ paramaṁ sāmyam upaiti” “bhoktāraṁ yajña-tapasāṁ sarva-bhutam-maheśvaram suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānām jñātvā māṁ śāntim ṛcchati “ ityādi śruit-smṛtibhyaḥ \|\|10\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is false to say that Brahman is nirguṇa and just cin-mātra (consciousness alone) since śrutis and smṛtis say the contrary.**

**na tat-samaś cābhyadikaś ca dṛśyate**

**No one is equal or greater than the Lord. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8**

**mattaḥ parataraṁ nānyat kimcit asit dhanañjaya**

**O Arjuna! There is nothing superior to me. BG 7.7**

**Brahman possesses qualities and is the object of love. Brahman is not without qualities since that is worthless. Śrutis and smṛtis declare this.**

**yadā paśyaḥ paśyate rukma-varṇaṁ kartāram īśaṁ puruṣaṁ brahma-yonim \|**  
**tadā vidvān puṇya-pāpe vidhuya nirañjanaḥ paramaṁ sāmyam upaiti**

**When the observer sees the golden person, the cause, the agent, the Lord, the source of Brahmā, the person, being pure, attains a form similar to the supreme Lord. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 3.1.3)**

**bhoktāraṁ yajña-tapasāṁ sarva-bhutam-maheśvaram**  
**suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānām jñātvā māṁ śāntim ṛcchati**

**That** ***muni,*** **realizing me as the protector of sacrifice and austerities, the master of all controllers, and the protector of all living beings, then attains liberation. BG 5.29 \|\|10\|\|**

### Text 11

*nanu viṣṇor eva tad-vācyatve tu –“yato vāco nivartante apṛāpya manaṣa saha” “yad vācānabhyuditaṁ yena vāg abhyudyate tad eva brahma tvaṁ viddhi nedaṁ yad idaṁ upāsate” “ tat śrotreṇa na śrṇoti yena śrotram idaṁ śrutaṁ tad eva brahma tvaṁ viddhi nedaṁ yad idaṁ upāsate” ityādi śruteḥ, “yato ‘prāpya nirvatante vācaś ca manasā saha \| ahaṁ cānye ime devas tasmai bhagavate namaḥ” \|\| ityadi smṛteś ca kā gatir iti cen na \| sākalyāvācyatayā tad gater darśayiṣyamāṇatvāt \| sarva-jñādayaḥ śabdāḥ sārva-jñyadinā nimittena bhagavati pravartante nirguṇādayas tu nairguṇyādineti kṛtsnasya vedasya bhagavat-paratvam \| itthaṁ ca “sarve vedā yat padam āmananti” ityādi śruti-smṛty-avirodhaḥ \|\|11\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If the Vedas indicate Viṣṇu, what do the following śruti and smṛti statements mean?**

**yato vāco nivartante aprāpya manaṣa saha**

**Not understood by the mind, the Lord cannot be described by words. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.4.1)**

**yad vācānabhyuditaṁ yena vāg abhyudyate tad eva brahma tvaṁ viddhi nedaṁ yad idaṁ upāsate**

**Know this Brahman who is not described by words but by whom words manifest. Brahman is not that which people worship.** ***Kena Upaniṣad*** **1.5**

**tat śrotreṇa na śrṇoti yena śrotram idaṁ śrutaṁ tad eva brahma tvaṁ viddhi nedaṁ yad idaṁ upāsate**

**That is not heard by the ears. Know that what is heard by the ears and what is worshipped by people is not Brahman. Kena Upaniṣad**

**yato ‘prāpya nyavartanta vācaś ca manasā saha \|**  
**ahaṁ cānye ime devās tasmai bhagavate namaḥ**

**I offer respects to that Supreme Lord whom words, along with mind, the** ***devatās*** **such as Bṛhaspati, Rudra, and others, could not understand and gave up. SB 3.6.40**

**That is not so. The meaning will be shown to indicate that all the qualities of the Lord cannot be described in words. Words like omniscience are used to indicate that he has all knowledge and words like** ***nirguṇa*** **(without qualities) are used to indicate that the Lord has no material qualities. All the Vedas indicate Bhagavān with qualities. This does not contradict the śrutis and smṛtis:**

**sarve vedā yat padam āmananti**

**All the Vedas describe the Lord. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad 1.2.15) \|\|11\|\|**

### Text 12

*tad idaṁ śuddhasya pūrṇasya viṣnor vedāvācyatvaṁ bhagavatā sutra-kāreṇoktam “īkṣater nāśabdaṁ” ityāsmin adhikaraṇe, vedeṣu vācyatva-darśanād aśabdaṁ brahma na bhavati kintu śabada-vācyam eva bhavatīti bhāṣitaṁ can \| yat tv aśabdam iti pradhānaṁ vyacaṣṭa tan na tasyāpy ajām ekām ityādi śabda-vācyatvaṭ \|\|12\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The topic of whether Viṣṇu who is pure and complete can be described by the Vedas is discussed by Vyāsa in the sutra** ***īkṣater nāśabdam*****. nāśabdam.**

**ajām ekāṁ lohita-śukla-kṛṣṇāṁ**  
**bahvīḥ prajāḥ sṛjamānāṁ sarūpāḥ**  
**ajo hy eko juṣamāno 'nuśete**  
**jahaty enāṁ bhukta-bhogām ajo 'nyaḥ**

**One unborn entity, satisfied, follows another unborn entity--a female entity who is red, white and black and who produces many offspring of similar nature. Another male gives up this female who has been enjoyed. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.5 \|\|12\|\|**

### Text 13

*kiṁca jñāna-kāṇḍaṁ sākṣad eva brahma prāha—“tam tv aupanisadaṁ purṣaṁ pṛccāmi” iti śruteḥ \| karma-kāṇḍaṁ tu jñānāṅga-karma-pratipādanāt paramparayeti—“tam etaṁ vedānuvacanena brāhmaṇā vividiṣanti, yajñena dānena tapasā nāśakena” iti śruteḥ “yataḥ pravṭtir bhūtānāṁ yena sarvam idaṁ tatam \| svakarmnā tam abhyarcya siddhim vindati mānavaḥ” iti smrteś ca \|\|13\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Moreover the jñāna-kāṇḍa directly speaks of Brahman.**

**tam tv aupaniṣadaṁ puruṣaṁ pṛccāmi**

**I ask about the Lord described in the Upaniṣads.** ***Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad*** **3.9.26**

**Even karma-kāṇḍa speaks directly of Brahman when performing karma with jñāna as its aṅga.**

**tam etaṁ vedānuvacanena brāhmaṇā vividiṣanti, yajñena dānena tapasānāśakena**

**The seekers of Brahman try to know him by study of the Vedas (*****brahmacārī),*** **by sacrifices, by acts of charity (*****grhastha*****), by austerities (*****vanaprastha*****) and by fasting (*****sannyāsī*****). (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.4)**

***yataḥ pravṛttir bhūtānāṁ yena sarvam idaṁ tatam***  
***sva-karmaṇā tam abhyarcya siddhiṁ vindati mānavaḥ \|\|***

**Men achieve steadiness in** ***jñāna*** **by worshiping the Lord who is the origin of all entities and is spread everywhere, through mentally offering their prescribed duties. BG 18.46 \|\|13\|\|**

### Text 14

*nanu vṛṣṭi-putra-sargādi-phalakāni karmāṇi vidadhāno ‘sau pratīyate? Maivaṁ tathā tad vidhānasya prarocanājanakatvāt kārīryādi phalasya vṛṣṭyāder dṛṣṭatvena vede saśraddhasya tad abhirucyā tad arthaṁ samyag vicārayato “yo vā etad akṣaram aviditvā gārgy asmāl lokāt praiti sa kṛpaṇaḥ” ity ābrahma-jñasya dainyaṁ “tam etaṁ vedānuvacanena” ityādinā karmaṇāṁ taj jñāna-janakatāṁ ca vijānato niṣkāmāṇi tāny anutiṣthato brahma-dhiṣaṇaṁ asau janayatīti siddha tasya sarvasya tat-paratvam \|\|14\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“Karma-kāṇḍa propounds only methods to obtain rain or sons.” It is not so. Activities to get a son are only for stimulating the person. By seeing rain as a result of the kārīrya sacrifice, the person gains faith in the Vedas. By that taste, the person begins to consider the real meaning of the Vedas. He hears about the miserable condition of a person who does not know about Brahman.**

**yo vā etad akṣaram aviditvā gārgy asmāl lokāt praiti sa kṛpaṇaḥ**

**O Gargī! The person who leaves this world without knowing the Lord is a miser. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.8.10)**

**He then performs niṣkāma-karma after understanding that such actions will produce that knowledge.**

***tam etaṁ vedānuvacanena brāhmaṇā vividiṣanti brahmacaryeṇa***

**The brāhmaṇas desire to know the Lord by reciting the Vedas and by brahmacārya. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.4.22)**

**The goal of karma is to produce knowledge of Brahman. Brahman is the meaning of all the Vedas. \|\|14\|\|**

### Text 15

*yad uktam “parokṣa-vādo vedo ‘yam bālānāṁ anuśāsanam \| karma-mokṣāya karmāṇi vidhatte hy agadaṁ yathā \|\| vedoktam eva purvāṇo niḥsaṅgo ‘rpitam īśvare \| naiṣkarmyaṁ labhate siddhiṁ rocanārthā phala-śrutiḥ” iti kāmitasyaiva svargādeḥ phalatvena pratīter akāmito ‘sau na bhavet kinru citta-śuddhir eva jñānodayaṛthā bhavet iti \|\|15\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is said:**

**parokṣa-vādo vedo 'yaṁ bālānām anuśāsanam**  
**karma-mokṣāya karmāṇi vidhatte hy agadaṁ yathā**

**The Vedas, speaking indirectly, prescribe** ***karma-yoga*****, in order to liberate people from** ***karma,*** **just as a father promises candy in order to get children to drink medicine. SB 11.3.44**

**vedoktam eva kurvāṇo niḥsaṅgo 'rpitam īśvare**  
**naiṣkarmyaṁ labhate siddhiṁ rocanārthā phala-śrutiḥ**

**A person who performs the** ***karmas*** **mentioned in the Vedas becomes detached from the results, offers the results to the Lord and attains destruction of all k*****armas.*** **Only for attracting the people are material results mentioned in the Vedas. SB 11.3.46**

**A person with material desires will not give up those desires simply by experiencing the results of Svarga. Only when the person becomes purified in heart will he begin inquiry into jñāna. \|\|15\|\|**

### Text 16

*etenedaṁ ca nirastam \| tathāhi navīnāḥ kalpayanti – āmnāyasya kriyārthatvād ānarthakyam atad-arthānāṁ tasmād anityaṁ ucyate tad-bhūtānāṁ kriyārthena samāmnāyo ‘rthasya tan nimittatvād iti vidhi-kāṇḍe krtsnasya kriyā-paratva-nirūpaṇān na tasya viṣṇu-paratvam \| tatra karma-kāṇḍaṁ sākṣad eva kriyāṁ nirūpayati jñāna-kāṇḍaṁ tu tad aṅga-bhūta-kartṛ-devatā- prakāśana-dvārā ceti \|\|16\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**By this conclusion, the following is also refuted. The mīmāṁsaka imagine as follows. Since the Vedas conclusion is only action, the sections which teach inaction are useless. Therefore jñāna is not eternal. The final meaning of all the texts is action. Because the meaning is action, because action is the final meaning in the karma-kāṇḍa, the Vedas do not indicate Viṣṇu as the final goal. The Karma-kāṇḍa indicates action (for Svarga). Jñāna-kāṇḍa is merely karma-kāṇḍa’s secondary portion and, by revealing devatās like Viṣṇu, indicate action ultimately. \|\|16\|\|**

### Text 17

*yat tu prakaraṇa-bhedāt “tad yatheha karma-jito lokaḥ kṣīyata evam evāmutra puṇya-jito lokaḥ kṣīyate” ityādinā kriyā-phala-vigānāc ca na jñāna-kāṇḍaṁ kriyā-paraṁ kintu brahma-param eveti vadanti tan na—bhūtārtha-visayatve nairarthakya-prāpteḥ \| “svara-kāmo yajeta” tasmād brāhmaṇaḥ surāṁ na pibet” ityādīni pravṛtti-nivṛrti-bodhakāny eva vākyānyarthavanti vīkṣyate na tu “sapta-dvīpā vasundharā” ityādīni bhūta-vastu-parāṇi \| tasmāt krṭsnasya tasya kriyā-paratvam iti \|\|17\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Another section says:**

***tad yatheha karma-cito lokāḥ kṣiyante evam evāmutra puṇya-cito lokaḥ kṣīyate***

**Just as places attained in this life as a result of actions in this life perish, places in a future life attained by pious acts of this life are also destroyed. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 8.1.6)**

**Though results of karma are criticized and jñāna-kāṇḍa does not promote karma, the scriptures do not promote Brahman as the sole goal. Concentrating on Brahman does not give results since it simply promotes the idea that the jīva is the Lord. Śruti says** ***svarga-kāmo yajeta*****: the person desiring Svarga performs worship. (*****Āpastamba Śrauta Sūtra,*** **Kṛṣṇa** ***Yajur Veda*****, 10.2.1)** ***Tasmād bṛahmaṇaḥ surām na pibet*****: therefore the brāhmaṇa should not drink soma. (Kṛṣṇa Yajur Veda, Maitrayani samhita) One sees statements indicating actions for Svarga and others than forbid such actions, but not actions for attaining Brahman. Thus, the śrutis do not indicate attaining Brahman. All the scriptures indicate action (for Svarga). \|\|17\|\|**

### Text 18

*tad idaṁ karakrṭa-dyumaṇi-pidhānavad upahāsāspadaṁ kubuddhi-vijṛmbhataṁ \| “sarve vedā yat padam āmananti” ityādi śruit-smṛtibhyaḥ—īdṛṣa-jaiminy ukti-darṣanāc ca \| āmnāyasyetey anena kṛtsnasya karma-kāṇḍasya kriyāparatve saṁśrite tad antargatānāṁ “so’ rodīt” ityādīnāṁ keṣāṁcid vākyānāṁ tad eka-vākyatvāya “tad-bhūtānām” iti samarthanam \|\|18\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**These arguments are laughable, and full of bad logic, like a person covering his eyes to block out the sun. The śrutis and smṛtis proclaim Viṣṇu as supreme.**

***sarve vedā yat padam āmananti***

**All the Vedas honor the Lord's feet. (*****Kaṭha Upaniṣad*** **1.2.15)**

**Even Jaimini agrees with this. If one harmonizes all the statements which indicate action in karma-kāṇḍa section with statement statements like** ***so ‘rodīt*****, which occur within the same section, then the conclusion is that Viṣṇu is the goal \|\|18\|\|**

### Text 19

*tathāhi—“āmnāyasya” iti pūrva-pakṣa-sūtraḥ asyārthaḥ—“codanā-lakṣaṇo dharmaḥ” ity upakramāt “tasya jñānam upadeśaḥ” iti madhye parāmarśāt, “tad bhūtānām” ity upasaṁhārāś cāmnāyaḥ kriyārtha ity avagamyate \| tataś cāmnāyasya vedasya kriyārthatvād yajñādi-kriyā-paratvād atad-arthānāṁ puruṣānadīnārthatvena vidhau paryavasāyitum aśakyānāṁ “so ‘rodīt” “rudrasya rudratvaṁ” “prajāpatir ātmano vapām udakhidat” “ deva vai devayajanam adhyavasāya diśo na prajānan” ity evam ādīnāṁ ca veda-vākyānāṁ ānarthakyaṁ dharma-pramiti-rūpārtha-pratipādakatva-virahaḥ tasmād devaṁ jātīyakaṁ vākya-jātam anityam ucyate \| anityaṁ iva na tv anityaṁ eva apauruṣeyatvān nityatvābhyupagati-virodhāc ca \| rodana-vapotkhāta-dig-ajñānādi-rupa-siddhārtha-bodhakatvāt tasya kriyārthatvam \|\|19\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Jaimini from “*****codanā-lakṣaṇo dharmaḥ*****” at the beginning, “*****tasya jñānam upadeśaḥ*****” in the middle and “*****tad bhūtānām*****” at the conclusion indicates that the Vedas indicate only action. Since the Vedas indicate only action such as yajña, statements which contradict this idea, which do not conclude in action as the goal—such as “*****so ‘rodīt*****” “*****rudrasya rudratvaṁ”*** **“*****prajāpatir ātmano vapām ud akhidat*****” “deva vai devayajanam adhyavasāya diśo na**

### Text 20

*“tad bhūtānām” iti siddhanta-sūtram \| tasyāyam arthaḥ --teṣu padārtheṣu rodanādiṣu bhūtānāṁ vartamānānāṁ padānāṁ “so ‘rodīt” ityādīnāṁ kriyārthena padena “yajeta” ityādinā samāmnāyāḥ samuccāraṇaṁ sambandha iti yāvat \| kutaḥ? --arthasya tan nimittatvāt \| arthaḥ padārthas tasya tan-nimittatvād vākyārtha-pratyaya-nimittatvāt \| padāni tāvat svārthān abhidadhati \| paścāt te padārthā ākāṅkṣa-yogyatā-sannidhi vāci-vākyārtham avabodhayantīti \| tasmād ākāṅkṣādi-mahimnā “so “rodīt” ityādi-vākyānāṁ kathaṁcid vidhy-eka-vākyatā kalpyeti, te kriyāyāṁ tathā tathāviṣṭā babhuvas tasmād anyair apy aveśanaṁ kāryam iti \| na ca jaimininā teṣāṁ vākyānāṁ svārthe prāmāṇaṁ tyaktam “autpattikas tu śabdasyārthena sambandhaḥ” iti śabdārtha-sambandha-nityattva-svīkāra-virodhāt sarveṣāṁ pramāṇānāṁ sva-visayāvagati-paryavasāyitvāc ca \|\|20\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Jaimini’s philosophy has been summarized in the phrase** ***tad-bhūtānāṁ kriyārthena samāmnāyo ‘rthasya tan nimittatvād*****. The meaning is this. Whatever meanings exist (*****bhūtānām*****) in phrases like** ***so ‘rodīt*****, must be related (*****samānāyaḥ*****) to what is express in phrases like “one must perform sacrifice (*****yajeta*****)” which indicate actions of sacrifice (*****kriyārthena*****) since the meaning of the Vedas is ascertained in those phrases (*****arthasya tan nimittatvāt*****).**

**Words reveal their specific meaning (*****svārtha*****). After that, the meaning of words is determined by yogyatā (suitability), ākāṅkṣā (expectancy) and connection in the same sentence. By applying these principles, phrases like** ***so ‘rodīt*** **can be harmonized to the injunction to perform action. These phrases are covered by action. It is necessary to apply this principle of covering to other statements also. Jaimini does not however reject the original meaning of words (*****svārtha*****). There is a natural relationship between a word and its meaning. It would amount to rejection of what is normally accepted by all pramāṇas as the meaning, which is based on perception of objects. \|\|20\|\|**

### Text 21

*na ca jñāna-kānḍasyāpi karma-kāṇḍaika-vākyā śakyā vaktuṁ prakaraṇa-bedhāt, śruta-hāny aśruta-kalpana-prasaṅgāt prayuta karma-tat-phalayos tucchatayā pratipādanāc ca \|\|21\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Reply) One cannot apply the one statement of action expressed in karma-kāṇḍa to jñāna-kāṇḍa since they are two different subjects in two different sections of the Vedas. By applying this principle, injunctions to understand ātmā would be destroyed and injunctions to perform sacrifices for Svarga would be imposed. Moreover, jñāna-kāṇḍa states that karma and its results are all insignificant. \|\|21\|\|**

### Text 22

*na ca pravṛrti-nivṛrti-paratā-virahiṇo bhūtātmā-vastu-visāyasya jñāna-kāṇḍasya “sapta-dvīpā vasundharā” ityādi vākyavad ānarthakyaṁ \| tasya brahmāstitva-bodhanenaivārthavattvāt nidhi-sattā-vāci-vākyavat, yathā “tvad gṛhe nidhir asti” ity āpta-vākyāt puṁso harṣa-rūpo ‘rtho bhavet tathā paramānanda-rūpaṁ mad-aṁśi brahmāstīti tat-sattvāvagamāt sa iti, nānarthakya-prāptis tatas tad-artha-pravṛttiś ca \| “putras te jāto” “nāyaṁ sarpaḥ” iti svarūpa- pareśv api vākyeṣu harṣa-bhayā-nivṛtti-rūpo’ rtho dṛṣṭaḥ \| tasmād ānarthakya-vādaḥ pumarthānupayogy-upākhyānādi-bhūtārtha-viṣayo bodhyāḥ \| itarathā partiṣedhakānām api tat-prāptir akriyārthatvāt \| na surāṁ pibet iti hi nivṛttir upadiṣṭā \| na tu sā kriyā kintu prasakta-kriyā-nivṛtty-audāsīnyam eveti \|\|22\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One cannot say that statements like** ***saptadvīpa vasundharā*** **(the earth consists of seven islands) of the jñāna-kāṇḍa which concern ātmā, and which are devoid of either pravṛtti or nivṛtti intent, are meaningless. They have meaning because they give understanding of Brahman, just as saying “There is a treasure in your house” has meaning. By understanding that statement, a man obtains joy when he obtains the treasure. Similarly a person can understand that Brahman, cause of the highest joy, exists. Thus such statements are not meaningless. Then the person performs actions (pravṛtti) to attain realization of Brahman. The meaning becomes** ***pravṛtti.*** **Statements like “You have a son” or “That is not a snake” produce joy and destroy fear. They have pravṛtti and nivṛtti meanings. Saying that such statements are meaningless makes statements about Brahman into mere stories unsuitable for attaining the highest human goal. All negative statements concerning attaining Brahman would also be considered statements of inaction. Statements which teach giving up certain actions such as “one should not drink soma” do not promote action. They promote indifference to certain actions one is performing. \|\|22\|\|**

### Text 23

*kiṁca dvi-rūpaṁ brahmeti jaiminer abhimatam anuṣṭheyaṁ kriyā-rūpaṁ prāpyaṁ cit-sukha-rūpaṁ ceti \| “yajño vai viṣṇuḥ” iti “brahmavid āpnoti param tad eṣābhyukṭā” “satyaṁ jñānam anantaṁ brahma” “ānando brahma’ iti caivam adi-śruteḥ \| tatra pūrvānuṣṭhānan antarā para-prāptir na syād iti tatraiva tad-bharaḥ –brahma-niṣṭhā tu tasya bādarāyaṇa-bhagavat-śiṣyatvāt, tena tan-mato ‘panyāsāc ca \| tasmāt brahmaiva veda-vācyam iti nirṇītam \|\|23\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**According to Jaimini, Brahman has two forms: a form of action (*****kriyā-rūpa*****) in performance and a form of spiritual happiness (*****citsukha-rūpa*****) to be realized. Thus śruti says:**

**yajño vai viṣṇuḥ**

**Viṣṇu is sacrifice (action).** ***Taittirīya Saṁhitā*** **1.7.4**

**brahma-vid āpnoti param**

**The knower of Brahman attains the supreme. Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.5.**

**tad eṣābhyuktā**

**Turning to Brahman, accepting Brahman as explained (*****abhi*****), the verse is uttered by the sacrificer (*****uktā*****). Taittirīya Upaniṣad**

**satyaṁ jñānam anantaṁ brahma**

**Brahman is eternal existence and knowledge. Taittirīya Upaniṣad.**

**ānando brahma**

**Brahman is bliss. Taittirīya Upaniṣad**

**Without first acting (*****kriyā-rūpa*****), one cannot attain the result (*****citsukha-rūpa*****). By the action one attains the result. Jaimini was fixed in Brahman since he was the student of Vyāsa and Vyāsa presented his ideas through Jaimini’s writings. Thus it is concluded that Brahman can be expressed in the Vedas. \|\|23\|\|**

### Text 24

*nanu “sarve veda yat padam āmamanti” “yat vācānabhyuditaṁ” ityādiṣu vācyatvā-vācyatvayoḥ śravaṇād evaṁ pratīyate \| māyopādhir īśvaro veda-vācyo nirviśeṣaḥ śuddhas tu lakṣya eva bhavet \| na ca śuddhe śabda-pravṛtti-nimittaṁ nāma-jātyādi kimcid asti anāma-rūpatva-smāraṇād iti ? maivaṁ veda-vācyasyaiveśvarasya śuddhatvāt “sattvadayo na santīśe” ityādi-smṛtibhyaḥ, tasya nirviśeṣatvaṁ tu na pūrvatra svarūpānubandhi-guṇa-smāraṇāt \| na vā ḍitthādivat kalpitaṁ nāmādi \| tasys svarūpānubandhi divyantu tad astīti \|\|24\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It appears that Brahman can be described by the Vedas and simultaneously cannot be described by the Vedas because there are statements like “All the Vedas describe the Lord” and “He cannot be described by words.” The Lord endowed with material coverings (*****upādhi*****) can be described by the words but the pure Brahman with no qualities can only be indicated indirectly (*****lakṣya*****). Pure Brahman is not subject to words since scripture says that Brahman has no name or classification.**

**That is not so. The Lord is pure and can be expressed by word of the Vedas.**

***sattvādayo na santīśe yatra ca prākṛtā guṇāḥ \|***  
***sa śuddhaḥ sarva-śuddhebhyaḥ pumān ādyaḥ prasīdatu \|\|***  
***hlādinī sandhinī saṁvit tvayy ekā guṇa-saṁśraye \|***  
***hlāda-tāpa-karī miśrā tvayi no guṇa-varjite \|***

**The material guṇas of sattva, rajas and tamas do not exist in the Lord. May that person, the source of all, purer than the purest, be pleased with me. In you who are the shelter of all spiritual qualities exist the energies of hlādinī, sandhinī and saṁvit. In you who are devoid of material qualities, the mixture of happiness and distress found in the material world does not exist. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.9.44-45**

**The Lord is not without qualities since his qualities related to his svarūpa were previously noted. The names of the Lord are not concocted like the name “Ḍhittha.” The names are related to the Lord’s svarūpa. \|\|24\|**

### Text 25

*tasmād evam atra bodhyam, sa bhagavān svayam eva veda-rūpaḥ svarūpānubandhibhis tat-sardhair nāmabhir gocarībhavatīti \| “namo vedādi-rūpāya omkārāya namo namaḥ “ tasya vā etasya mahato bhutasya niḥśvasitam etad yad ṛgveadaḥ” ityādi śravaṇāt \| “vedo nārāyaṇaḥ sākṣāt svayambhur iti śuśrumaḥ” ityādi smṛteś ca \| ata aupaniṣada iti paramātmanaḥ samākhyā \|\|25\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It should be understood that the Lord is the very form of the Vedas and is revealed by his names which are related to his svarūpa.**

**namo vedādi-rūpāya omkārāya namo namaḥ**

**I offer respects to the Lord, who is the form of the Vedas. I offer respects to omkāra. Rāma-tāpanī Upaniṣad**

**tasya vā etasya mahato bhutasya niḥśvasitam etad yad ṛgvedaḥ**

**The Ṛg Veda is the breathing of the great Lord. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad**

**Smṛti says:**

**vedo nārāyaṇaḥ sākṣāt svayambhur iti śuśrumaḥ**

**We hear that the Vedas are directly Nārāyaṇa. (SB 6.1.40)**

**Therefore the Lord is called** ***aupaniṣada*** **(he who is revealed in the Upaniṣads). \|\|25\|\|**

### Text 26

*vedasya satyatvaṁ bhagavatā sūtrakāreṇabhihitam –“ataeva ca nityatvaṁ” iti na ca varṇa-mātrasya tasya sā stutir iti vācyam \| āvirbhāvantaravad varṇātmako’ yaṁ parasyāvirbhāva iti \| tenaiva śrutyādi-balena gṛhītena tad abhedena tat tat sambhavet \|\|26\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The author of the Vedānta-sūtras declares that the Vedas are eternal.**

**ataeva ca nityatvam**

**Therefore the Vedas are eternal. Vedānta-sūtra 1.3.29**

**This is not just praise using words. Like other appearances of the Lord, the Vedas, the Lord composed of sound, is an appearance (not a birth or creation). This is possible because the Vedas are nondifferent from the Lord, as proven by the scriptures. \|\|26\|\|**

### Text 27

*ataeva tad-bhūtānāṁ nāmnāṁ cid-rūpatā mocakatā kārsnyāgocaratā ca svarūpavad eva nirupitā dṛṣyate—“tam u stomatāraḥ pūrvaṁ yathāvida ṛtasya garbhaṁ januṣ piparttan” “asya jānanto nāmid vivaktan mahas te viṣno sumatim bhajāmahe” oṁ tat sat” iti ṛk-śrutau.”sakṛd uccāritaṁ yena harir ity ākṣara-dvayaṁ \| bandhah parikaras tena mokṣāya gamanaṁ prati” ityādau, “nāma-karma-svarūpāṇi na pariccheda-gocare \| yasyākhila-pramāṇānāṁ sa viṣnūr garbhagatas tava” ityādau ca smṛtau \|\|27\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus the scriptures explain that the name of the Lord has a spiritual form, gives liberation and is beyond material senses, just like the Lord himself.**

**tam u stomatāraḥ pūrvaṁ yathāvida ṛtasya garbhaṁ januṣā piparttan**  
**asya jānanto nāmid vivaktan mahas te viṣno sumatim bhajāmahe oṁ tat sat**

**O Viṣṇu! Your name is spiritual and self-revealed*****.*** **Knowing that name a little*****,*** **not completely, just by chanting it, we have attained fine knowledge of you because that oṁ is self accomplished. (Ṛg Veda 1.156.3)**

**sakṛd uccāritaṁ yena harir ity ākṣara-dvayaṁ \|**  
**bandhah parikaras tena mokṣāya gamanaṁ prati**

**Whoever chants the two syllables “Hari” only once is ready for attaining liberation. (Skanda Purāṇa)**

**nāma-karma-svarūpāṇi na pariccheda-gocare \|**  
**yasyākhila-pramāṇānāṁ sa viṣnūr garbha-gatas tava**

**The Lord’s names, activities and forms are not to be understood any proofs. Viṣṇu has entered your womb. Viṣṇu Purāṇa \|\|27\|\|**

### Text 28

*evaṁ sati tad anāmatvaṁ kvacid ucyate tat tu “aprasiddes tad guṇānām anāmāsau prakīrtitiaḥ \| anāmā tv aprasiddhatvād arūpo bhuta-varjanāt” ityādi smrtyā samādheyam \| aprasiddhis ca prākṛta-vailakṣaṇyenāgrahāt kārtsneynāviditatayā vā \|\|28\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Though all this is true, sometimes it is said that the Lord is without a name. This claim is resolved as follows:**

***aprasiddhes tad-guṇānām anāmāsau prakīrtitaḥ***

**The Lord is glorified as nameless because his qualities are not known in this world. (Vāsudevādhyātma)**

***anāmā so’prasiddhatvād arūpo bhūta-varjanāt***

**He is without material name because he is not known in the world. He is without material form because he is devoid of material elements. (Brahmā Purāṇa)**

***Aprasiddha*** **means that one cannot perceive the Lord because he is beyond material perception, or that that he cannot be known at all by ordinary means. \|\|28\|\|**

### Text 29

*tathā “na yatra nātha vidyante nāma-jātyādi-kalpanāḥ \| tad brahma paramaṁ nityaṁ avikāri bhavān aja \| na kalpanām ṛte ‘rthasya sarvasyādhigamo yataḥ \| ataḥ kṛṣṇācyutānanta-viṣṇu-nāmabhir īḍyase” ity uttaroktau sphuṭārtho ‘pi nirastaḥ \|\|29\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The following statement is found in scriptures:**

**na yatra nātha vidyante nāma-jātyādi-kalpanāḥ \|**  
**tad brahma paramaṁ nityaṁ avikāri bhavān aja \|**  
**na kalpanām ṛte ‘rthasya sarvasyādhigamo yataḥ \|**  
**ataḥ kṛṣṇācyutānanta viṣṇu-nāmabhir īḍyase**

**O Lord! Your names, birth and other characteristics are not manufactured. You are the supreme Brahman, unchanging, O unborn Lord! Just as objects are not know except by making names for them, you are worshipped by spiritual names, O Kṛṣṇa, Acyuta, Ananta! Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

**The meaning of the second two lines (making names) is modified by the first two lines (the Lord’s names are not made). \|\|29\|\|**

### Text 30

*atra kalpanā-śabdo vyarthaḥ nāma-jātyādayo netyānenaiveṣṭa-siddheḥ svayam eva tatraiva tad brahma paramaṁ nityam iti paratra ca rūpaṁ paraṁ sad iti vācakam akṣaraṁ yad iti brahmāja-sac-cabdānāṁ pāramarthika-nāmatayā svīkarāc ca \| ajām ekam” ityādiṣv ajatva-lakṣaṇā jātiś ca pratītā \| tathā nāmādi-kalpanāṁ niṣidhya punaḥ kṛṣṇādi-nāmā-kalpanoktir viruddhā \| na ca kalpanayā tasyeḍya-bhāvaḥ \| nāpi kṛṣṇādi-nāma-niyamas tasyām aniyamātmakatvāt \|\|30\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The word** ***kalpana*** **(manufacturing) in the second verse has no meaning since the first verse states that the name and birth etc. are not manufactured. The words** ***brahma paramam nityam*** **are used in the first verse. Three verses later in Viṣṇu Purāṇa the following is found:** ***rūpaṁ paraṁ sad iti vācakam akṣaraṁ yat*****: the Lord whose form is eternal, supreme, and unchanging is expressed in words. One must accept that the names are spiritual since he is called** ***brahman, sat*** **and** ***aja*** **(unborn). Though the Lord is born (*****jāti*****) he is unborn (*****ajām*****).**

**ajām ekāṁ lohita-śukla-kṛṣṇāṁ bahvīḥ prajāḥ sṛjamānāṁ sarūpāḥ**

**One unborn entity, satisfied, follows another unborn entity--a female entity who is red, white and black and who produces many offspring of similar nature. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.5**

**Since manufacturing the names etc. is denied, saying that Kṛṣṇa’s names are manufactured (*****kalpanam*****) in the second verse is a contradiction. If the names were temporary creations, the Lord would not be worshipped by those names. If the names were manufactured, then naming the Lord would be random, since making up material names follow no rules (and only personal whim). \|\|30\|\|**

### Text 31

*tasmād ayam eva tad arthaḥ \| yatra nāmnāṁ kṛṣṇādi-śabdānāṁ jātīnāṁ devatva-manuṣatvādīnām adi-padāt karmaṇāṁ ca kalpanā na, kintu “viśuddha-vijñāna-ghanaṁ sva-saṁsthayā \| samāpta-sarvārtham amogha-vāñchitam \| sva-tejasā nitya-nivṛtta-māyā-*

*guṇa-pravāhaṁ bhagavantam īmahi” ity ukta-diśā svarūpa-śakti-vilāsātmakāny eva tān santi \| tad brahma nityam ity anvayaḥ \| prapañca-vailakṣaṇyāt tathetyāha neti \| sarvasyārthasya manuṣya-paśvādi drṣṭa-vastunaḥ kalpanāṁ nāmādi-ghaṭanāṁ vinā ādhigamo vyavahārika-bodho na syāt –kalpitair eva nāmādibhis tasyāsau bhaved ity arthaḥ \| atas tad vilakṣaṇstvaṁ tad-vilakṣaṇair eva viṣṇu-nāmabhir viṣṇvātmakair nāmābhir vyāpaka-nāmābhinnair iti yāvat īḍyase stūyase—vyakta-māhātmyī kriyase ity arthaḥ tāḍṛśair eva tais tava mahimā vyaktībhavtīty arthaḥ \| nāmāṁ tādṛktvaṁ tu “nāma-cintāmaṇiḥ kṛṣṇaś caitanya-rasa-vigrahaḥ \|\| pūrṇaḥ śuddho nitya-mukto ‘bhinnatvān nama-nāmiṇoḥ” iti smṛteḥ \| kṛṣṇādi-trikaṁ sambuddhyantam \|\|31\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Therefore the meaning of the verse is this. The Lord’s names such as Kṛṣṇa and his jāti--being devatā or human-- and his activities (indicated by the word** ***ādi*****) have no creation (*****kalpanam*****). However it is said:**

**viśuddha-vijñāna-ghanaṁ sva-saṁsthayā**  
**samāpta-sarvārtham amogha-vāñchitam**  
**sva-tejasā nitya-nivṛtta-māyā-**  
**guṇa-pravāhaṁ bhagavantam īmahi**

**Let us approach you, the Supreme Lord, for shelter. You are full of perfectly pure spiritual awareness and are always situated in your original identity. Since your will is never thwarted, you have already achieved all possible desirable things, and by the power of your spiritual energy you remain eternally aloof from the flow of the qualities of illusion. SB 10.37.21**

**According to this statement, all these are manifestations of the Lord’s svarūpa-śakti. The Lord is Brahman and nityam (eternal). He is distinct from matter. He is not like humans or animals which we see. His names and birth etc. are not manufactured. They are not understood by material intelligence. Material objects such as humans and devatās are understood by giving manufactured names to them. But you are different. You are to be praised (*****īḍyase*****) by names which are different--names which are nondifferent from Viṣṇu, names which are all-pervasive. By such names, your greatness becomes evident. The names are nondifferent from the Lord.**

**nāma-cintāmaṇiḥ kṛṣṇaś caitanya-rasa-vigrahaḥ \|\|**  
**pūrṇaḥ śuddho nitya-mukto ‘bhinnatvān nāma-nāminoḥ**

**The name of the Lord fulfills all desires like** ***cintāmaṇi*****. It is the very form of Kṛṣṇa. It is full of consciousness and** ***rasa.*** **It is complete, pure, and eternally liberated. This is because of the non-difference of the name and Kṛṣṇa. Padma Purāṇa**

**The three names Kṛṣṇa, Acyuta and Ananta are in the vocative case. \|\|31\|\|**

### Text 32

*tasmāt pūrṇaḥ śuddho harir eva veda-vācyaḥ \| sa ca vedaḥ kṛṣṇādi-śabdair āśraya-bhūtaṁ śuddhaṁ vibhu-caitanyaṁ pratipādayati \| jīva-prakṛti-kālādi-śabdais tu cij-jaḍātmakam āśritam iti siddhaṁ sarva-veda-vedyatvam itthānāmādi-śabdāḥ sarvatra vyākhyātāḥ \| yas tu teṣāṁ sphuṭārthaṁ, jalpati, sa evaṁ praṣṭavyāḥ, tair brahmaṇo bodhaḥ syan na veti? Ādye te ‘pi tasyākhyāḥ, ante tad āramba-vaiyarthyāpattiḥ \| sarva-śabdāvācye lakṣaṇā tu na sambhavati \|\|32\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus, the pure, perfect Lord can be described by the Vedas and the Vedas describe the pure Lord of infinite consciousness, who acts as the shelter of words like Kṛṣṇa. The world made of conscious entities and matter is described by words like jīva, prakṛti and time. It has been proved that the Lord is to be known by all the Vedas. As well, words like** ***anāma*** **(having no name) have been explained. To the person who quarrels about the clear meaning of the names of the Lord, I ask, “Is Brahman, the Lord, understood or not by words like** ***anāma*** **(having no name)?” If he is understood by such words, then these words are his names. If he is not understood by these names, use of these words by the Veda would be meaningless. Since some claim that the Lord cannot not be described by any words, even lakṣaṇā (using words) would be impossible. \|\|32\|\|**

### Text 33

*“yato vāco nivartante” “yad vācānābhyuditam” ityādiṣu tu sākalyena vācyatvaṁ tasya niṣidhyate, na tu sarvathaivāvācyatvam ucyate, “sarve vedā yat padam āmananti “ ityādi-śruit-smṛti-nirviṣayāpatter yat padāmanana-nirdeśānupapatteś ca \| atas tatra “tatraivānandaṁ brahmaṇo vidvān” iti “tad eva brahma tvaṁ viddh” iti ca punas tasya gocaratvam uktam \|\|33\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Statements like** ***yato vāco nivartante*** **and** ***yad vācanābhyuditam*** **(the Lord cannot be described by words) indicate that the Lord cannot be described at all. But yet they do not say that the Lord is completely indescribable, since other statements in śruti and smṛti like** ***sarve vedā yat padam āmananti*** **(All the Vedas praise the Lord) and instructions to meditate on the Lord’s lotus feet would be useless. The scriptures also say that the Lord is describable.**

***ānandaṁ brahmaṇo vidvān na bibheti kutaścana***

**Knowing Brahman as bliss, one fears nothing he knows the bliss of Brahman. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.4.1)**

***yad vacānabhyuditaṁ yena vāg abhyudyate tad eva brahma tad viddhi nedaṁ yad idam upāsate***

**Know this Brahman who is not described by words but by whom words manifest. Brahman is not that which people worship. Kena Upaniṣad \|\|33\|\|**

### Text 34

*evam eva kenopaniṣadi jñatatvājñātatve dṛśyete—“yadi manyase suvedeti daharam evāpi nṛnaṁ \| tvaṁ vetsyasya brahmaṇo rūpaṁ yad asya tvaṁ yad asya deveṣv atha mīmāṁsyam eva te manye viditaṁ \| nāhaṁ manye suvedeti no na vedeti veda ca \| yasyāmataṁ mataṁ tasya mataṁ yasya na veda saḥ \| avijñātaṁ vijānatāṁ vijñātam avijānatām” iti \| iha hi kārtsnyena jñātatvaṁ ca nāsti kiṇcij jñātatvam asty eveti nirṇiyate \| evaṁ hi smaranti—“kārtsnyena nājo py abhidhātuṁ iśa” iti “aprasiddher avācyaṁ tad vācyaṁ sarvāgamoktitaḥ \| atarkyaṁ tarkyaṁ ajñeyaṁ jñeyam evaṁ paraṁ smrṭam” iti \| aprasiddhiḥ kārtsneynāvācyatādiḥ \|\|34\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus Kena Upaniṣad describes how the Lord is both knowable and unknowable.**

**yadi manyase suvedeti daharam evāpi**  
**nūna tvaṁ vettha brahmaṇo rūpaṁ**  
**yad asya tvaṁ yad asya deveṣv atha mīmāṁsyam eva te**

**O student, if you think you know Brahman well, then you know minutely the qualities of Brahman. Therefore, since you know very little, you should now inquire into the Brahman which is in you and in the devatās. Kena Upaniṣad**

**manye viditaṁ \|**  
**nāhaṁ manye suvedeti no na vedeti veda ca**

**Yo nas tad veda tad veda no na vedeti veda ca**

**Others say that I know Brahman completely, but I do not think I know Brahman. It is not that I do not know. I know somewhat. Kena Upaniṣad**

***yasyāmataṁ tasya mataṁ mataṁ yasya na veda saḥ***  
***avijñātaṁ vijānatāṁ vijñātam avijānatām***

**The person who thinks he cannot know Brahman completely can know Brahman. He who thinks he knows Brahman completely do not know Brahman. For those who think they know Brahman, Brahman cannot be realized. For those who understand that they cannot know Brahman completely, Brahman can be realized. Kena Upaniṣad**

**This means that one cannot know the Lord completely but can know him to some degree.**

**kārtsnyena nājo py abhidhātum iśa**

**Even Brahmā cannot describe the Lord completely. (SB 12.4.39)**

**aprasiddher avācyaṁ tad vācyaṁ sarvāgamoktitaḥ \|**  
**atarkyaṁ tarkyam ajñeyaṁ jñeyam evaṁ paraṁ smrṭam**

**The Lord cannot be described since he is unknowable. But he is described in all the statements of scripture. It is said that he is beyond logic and subject to logic. He is unknowable and knowable. Garuḍa Purāṇa**

***Aprasiddhi*** **means that the Lord cannot be fully described. \|\|34\|\|**

### Text 35

*nanu kārtsneynāvācyatvādikaṁ kuta iti ced ānantyād iti gṛhāṇa; yad uktam “janma-kārmābhidānāni santi me ‘ṅga sahaṣraśaḥ \| na śakyante ‘nusaṁkhyātuam anantatvān mayāpi hi” iti “dyupataya eva tena yayur antam anantatayā tam api yad antarāṇḍa-nicayā nanu sāvaraṇāḥ \| kha iva rajāmṣi vānti vayaṣa saha yat śrutayas tvayi hi phalanty atan nirasanena bhavan nidhanāḥ” iti ca \| yasya kārtsnenādhigamo na syāt tad ajñātam iti vyavahriyase \| “na tadīdṛg iti jñeyaṁ na vācyaṁ na ca kartyate \| paśyano ‘pi na paśyanti merorūpaṁ vipaśitaḥ” iti \| tasmād veda-vācyam eva paraṁ brahma \| tad eva jīva-jādātmakāt prapañcād bhinnam; jñātaṁ dhyātaṁ ca sad avidyām nibarayati dadati ca paramānandaṁ svapadam iti sarveṣām tattva-vidāṁ siddhaṇtaḥ \|\|35\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Why can the Lord not be completely described? It is because he is unlimited.**

**janma-kārmābhidānāni santi me ‘ṅga sahaṣraśaḥ \|**  
**na śakyante ‘nusaṁkhyātam anantatvān mayāpi hi**

**My birth and activities are in the thousands. They cannot be counted by me since they are unlimited. SB 10.51.36**

***dyu-pataya eva te na yayur antam anantatayā***  
***tvam api yad-antarāṇḍa-nicayā nanu sāvaraṇāḥ***  
***kha iva rajāṁsi vānti vayasā saha yac chrutayas***  
***tvayi hi phalanty atan-nirasanena bhavan-nidhanāḥ***

**Because you are unlimited, neither the lords of heaven nor even You Yourself can ever reach the end of your glories. The countless universes, each enveloped in its shell, are compelled by the wheel of time to wander within you, like particles of dust blowing about in the sky. The śrutis, following their method of eliminating everything separate from the Supreme, become successful by revealing you as their final conclusion. SB 10.87.41**

**Since the Lord cannot be completely known he is said to be unknowable.**

**na tad īdṛg iti jñeyaṁ na vācyaṁ na ca tarkyate \|**  
**paśyanto ‘pi na paśyanti mero rūpaṁ vipaścitaḥ**

**The Lord is not be known in this way (by limited measurement). He cannot be described or inferred. Though the wise see Meru, they do not know it. (quoted in Madhva’s Vedānta-sūtra commentary)**

**Therefore param brahma can be described by the Vedas. The Lord is different from the material world made of jīvas and matter. He is to be known and meditated upon. He destroys the jīva’s ignorance and gives the greatest bliss and his abode. This is the conclusion of all learned people. \|\|35\|\|**

### Text 37

*evam eva śrī-gītāsu bhagavatā paṅkajākṣena nigaditam “ vedaiś ca sarvair aham eva vedyo vedānta-kṛd vedavid eva cāham” “ dvāv imau puruṣau loke kṣaraś cāksāra eva ca \| kṣaraḥ sarvāṇi bhūtāni kutāstho ‘ksāra ucyate \|\| uttamaḥ ūrusās tv anyaḥ; paramātmety udāhrataḥ \| yo loka-trayam aviṣya bibhary avyaya īśvaraḥ \|\| yasmāt kṣaram aīto ‘ham akṣarād api cottamaḥ “ ato ‘si loke veda ca prathitaḥ puruṣottamaḥ \|\| yo mām eveam asaṁmūḍho jānāti puruṣottamam \| sa sarvait bhajati māṁ sarva-bhāvena bhārata \|\| iti guhyatamaṁ śāstram idaṁ uktaṁ payānagha \| tad buddvā buddmān syaṭ kṛakṛyaṣ ca bhārata “ iti evam anyatrāpi \|\|37\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The lotus eyed Lord also states this in Bhagavad-gītā:**

**vedaiś ca sarvair aham eva vedyo**  
**vedānta-kṛd vedavid eva cāham**

**I alone am known by all the** ***Vedas*****. I am the maker of the** ***Vedānta,*** **the conclusion of the** ***Vedas,*** **since I alone know the** ***Vedas*****.**

**dvāv imau puruṣau loke kṣaraś cākṣara eva ca \|**  
**kṣaraḥ sarvāṇi bhūtāni kūṭastho ’kṣara ucyate \|\|**

**There are two conscious beings mentioned in the** ***Vedas*****: the conditioned** ***jīvas*** **and the liberated** ***jīvas*****. The bound** ***jīvas*** **are all these living entities with destructible bodies. The liberated** ***jīvas*** **are fixed with one form for all time.**

**uttamaḥ puruṣas tv anyaḥ paramātmety udāhṛtaḥ \|**  
**yo loka-trayam āviśya bibharty avyaya īśvaraḥ \|\|**

**The highest person is different from this. He is described in the scriptures as the Paramātmā, who, by entering the three worlds, supports the living entities, as the unchanging controller.**

**yasmāt kṣaram atīto ’ham akṣarād api cottamaḥ \|**  
**ato ’smi loke vede ca prathitaḥ puruṣottamaḥ \|\|**

**Because I am superior to the conditioned and liberated** ***jīvas*****, I am celebrated in the** ***Vedas*** **and the** ***smṛtis*** **as the Supreme Person.**

**yo mām evam asaṁmūḍho jānāti puruṣottamam \|**  
**sa sarva-vid bhajati māṁ sarva-bhāvena bhārata \|\|**

**He who without confusion knows me as the Supreme Person according to what I have just said is the knower of everything. He worships me by all methods, O descendent of Bharata.**

**iti guhyatamaṁ śāstram idam uktaṁ mayānagha \|**  
**etad buddhvā buddhimān syāt kṛta-kṛtyaś ca bhārata \|\|**

**Thus I have spoken the most secret scripture in three verses to you, O sinless Arjuna, because you are qualified. Knowing this, you will have full knowledge of scripture and then achieve direct realization, O descendent of Bharata. BG 15.15-20 \|\|37\|\|**

## Chapter Five

**Refuting advaita-vāda**

### Text 1

*tatrāha svataḥ para-brahma-bhūtasyaiva jīvasyājñāna-hetuḥ prapañcaḥ \| sa punar advaita-jñāna-mātra-nivartanīya ity ato ‘syānartha-bījasya vināśāya parāvarātmanor advaitam eveha vijijñāsyaṁ tatraiva śāstra-tātparyaṁ iti \|\|1\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Some claim as follows. The world arises from the ignorance of the jīva, who is actually para-brahman. This ignorance is removed by advaita-jñāna. One should inquire about the nondifference of jīva and Brahman in order to destroy the problems caused by ignorance. That is the essence of scriptures. \|\|1\|\|**

### Text 2

*idam asat advaitāsiddheḥ \| tathā hi—tad advaitaṁ brahmātiriktaṁ brahmātmakaṁ vā; nādyaḥ advaita-hānāt, tad atiriktasya mithyātvena śāstrasyātattvāvedakatvāpātāc ca, satyatā ca bhedasya mitho viruddhayor anyatara-niṣedhasyānyatara-vividhyāptatvāt \| nāpy antya ātmanaḥ svaprakāśatvena nityasiddhatvāc chāstrasya siddha-sādhanatāpatteḥ \|\|2\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is not true, since nondifference or oneness is not proved. Is this “nondifference” separate from Brahman or the essence of Brahman (*****brahmātmaka*****)? It cannot be the first alternative since nondifference would be destroyed**

**It cannot be the second alternative (oneness being the nature of Brahman) since the jīva (being Brahman) would be naturally perfect with self-manifesting knowledge. The scriptures which explain sādhana for the jīva to attain Brahman would be useless. \|\|2\|\|**

### Text 3

*nanu tattvena tasya nitya-siddhatve ‘py advaitādi-viśeṣākāreṇānadhigate na tasya tad-dūṣaṇṁ yathā tava guṇa-guṇinor advaito ‘pi guṇināh sparśenopalambhe ‘pi guṇasya nādhigatir evam iti cet, mandam etat \| ātmano nirviśeṣatvād anadhigata-viśeṣābhāvāt ata evea guṇi-dṛṣṭānto ‘pi nirastaḥ \| yato rūpaṁ ghaṭābhinnam api tad-viśeṣayatayā mataṁ na tu tanmātram \|\|3\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“Though Brahman is eternally perfect in essence, if it is not understood in the special form of advaita, there is no harm in performing sādhana (to attain realization of oneness). Quality and object are also nondifferent. When the object is finally realized, one is not aware of its quality.”**

**This argument is foolish. The example of quality and object cannot be accepted, since ātmā is claimed to be without qualities and thus lack of awareness of qualities could not arise. Though a pot and its form are nondifferent, one conceives of their difference, and understands not just the form of the pot, but its qualities also. \|\|3\|\|**

### Text 4

*nanu syād idaṁ dūṣaṇaṁ śāstraṁ cet sadadvaitam āha iti brumaḥ \| kiṁ tarhi āvarṇa-rūpam ajñānaṁ nivartayati \| nivṛtte tv āvaraṇe advaitaṁ svata eva siddham iti cet, naitac caturasram—na hy ajñānaṁ kasyacit āvarakaṁ svaprakāśatvena nityatvāt svarūpasya viśesābhāvāc ca \|\|4\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“It may be a fault in scriptures to proclaim perfect Brahman as the object of sādhana, but still we claim that scriptures proclaim advaita as the truth. The ignorance which covers Brahman is removed by scripture. When the covering is destroyed, advaita, nondifference, is spontaneously manifested.”**

**This is not logical. Ignorance cannot be a covering on anything, since Brahman is eternally a self-manifesting entity with no particulars in its svarūpa that could be covered. \|\|4\|\|**

### Text 5

*tarhi āvaraṇaṁ hy āvriyamāṇeṇa vyāptam \| na cājñānasya tad asti \| tad dhi kim āvṛnoti tad-viśeṣaṁ vā? Nādyaḥ—tasya nityasiddha-prakāśatvāt, ne cetaraḥ—asvīkārāt \| tathā ca vyāpakābhāvād vyāpyam āvaraṇam apy asambhavād iti na tan nivartakatayā śāstraṁ adoṣam \|\|5\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Covering consists of an object being covered by some agent. This cannot not apply to ignorance as the agent. Does ignorance cover Brahman or does it cover Brahman’s qualities? Ignorance cannot cover Brahman, since Brahman is eternally manifested. It cannot cover its qualities since Brahman has no qualities. The covering agency of ignorance is impossible since it has nothing to cover. Scripture would not be without fault, since it could not remove anything. \|\|5\|\|**

### Text 6

*evaṁ ca sati siddha-sādhanatve ‘nupādeyatāprāmāṇyaṁ ca \| śāstraṁ khalu parāthaṁ tasya ca parasiddha-bodhane ‘nupādeyatā “anadhigatārthagamtṛ prāmāṇyaṁ” iti hi y tad abḥāvād aprāmāṇyaṁ ceti \|\|6\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If this is so, scriptures would be non-authoritative and unsuitable for advising sādhana. The purpose of scriptures is to give spiritual knowledge. Since Brahman is already full of knowledge, scriptures are not necessary to give knowledge of it. Scriptures are authoritative because they give knowledge of something unknown. If it lacks that function, it is not authoritative. \|\|6\|\|**

### Text 7

*evam ajñānāsambhavād viṣayādy api na sambhavati \| ajñāto hi viṣayaḥ—anyathātiprasaṅgāt \| prayojanaṁ ca mokṣaḥ sa cājñāna-nivṛtti-rūpaḥ \| yad uktam—“avidyāstamayo moksāḥ sā saṁsāra udāhṛta” iti \| na cājñānāsaṁbhave tan-nivṛttir upapadyate \| yac cājñāna-hāner ātma-rūpatvaṁ iṣṭaṁ tad asat—ātmanaḥ pūrva-siddhatvenājñāna-haner asiddhatāpatteḥ \| na cājñāta ātmaivājñāna-hānir nātma-mātram iti vācyaṁ—jñāna-karmatānaṅgīkārāt \|\|7\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since it is impossible that ignorance can overcome Brahman, that cannot be the subject of scriptures. If something that does not exist is the subject of scriptures, scriptures would be untrue. And the goal of liberation which is destruction of ignorance could not be described (since ignorance does not exist).**

**It is said “Liberation is destruction of ignorance and ignorance is the cause of saṁsāra.” If ignorance does not exist, one cannot describe its state of absence, Brahman. Destruction of ātmā’s ignorance cannot exist since ātmā is already perfect. Brahman does not need destruction of ignorance. “Destruction of ignorance simply means that the ātmā is unknown and scripture reveals it.” But that argument also cannot be accepted because you say that Brahman cannot be subjected to knowledge (senses or mind). \|\|7\|\|**

### Text 8

*yā ca sattvādi-pakṣa-traye doṣam āśaṅkya caturtha-prakāratām ajñānasya prakalpya tan nāśasya punaḥ pañcama-prakāratā svīkṛtā, sāpy ajñānāsambhavenaiva nirastā \|\|8\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The faults of ignorance being** ***sat, asat*** **and** ***sad-asat*** **in relation to ignorance are now listed. A fourth alternative concerning ignorance is then discussed and refuted. A fifth alternative is discussed and refuted, showing the impossibility of ignorance existing. \|\|8\|\|**

### Text 9

*evaṁ hi parair aṅgīkṛtam ajñānasya na sattvam—advaita-hānāt \| nāpy asattvaṁ pratīti-vaiparītyāt \| na ca sad asattvaṁ virodhāt \| atha caturtha-prakāro ‘nirvacanīyatvaṁ svīkāryaṁ \| na ca tan nāśo ‘pi tādṛk pratiyogī tan-nivṛttau vailakṣaṇyasyāvaśyakatvād iti pañcama-prakāratāṅgīkāryā \| yad āha ̮̮ “na san nāsan na sad-asan na nirvācyaś ca tat-kṣayaḥ \| yakṣānurūpo balir ity ācāryāḥ pratyapīpadan” iti \| idam apy apeśalaṁ \| ukta-rīty-ājñānāsambhavāt tena kalpyamānāyās tasyāḥ kha-puṣpa-taulyāt \|\|9\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Ignorance, accepted by the advaita-vādīs, cannot exist (*****sat*****), since by accepting its existence oneness is destroyed (there is something existing besides Brahman). And not accepting it (*****asat*****) opposes perception (in which we acknowledge our ignorance). Its simultaneous existence and nonexistence (*****sad-asat*****) cannot be accepted as they are contradictory states (and thus cannot coexist.) “Let ignorance be accepted in a fourth category called indescribable.” But that the destruction of ignorance cannot be of the same nature--neither existing nor non-existing--since what remains (pratiyogi) should actually be different from what is destroyed.**

**“Let there be a fifth alternative to explain ignorance:**  
**na san nāsan na sad-asan nānirvācyaś ca tat-kṣayaḥ \|**  
**yakṣānurūpo balir ity ācāryāḥ pratyapīpadan**

**Ignorance is non-existent. Ignorance is not non-existent. It is not both existent and non-existent. It is not anirvācya. Rather, destruction of ignorance is like an offering suitable for a Yakṣa.**

**This is not proper, since it like a flower in the sky, a concocted idea, arising because ignorance cannot exist in your system. \|\|9\|\|**

### Text 10

*atha pañcamam-prakārājñāna-nivṛttir ātmetarātma-svarūpā vā? ādye ‘py ājñāna-kāryaṁ na vā? na tāvad ādyaḥ, tadāpy ajñānāvasthiteḥ sambhavāt, na hy upādānena vinopādeyasya sthitir asti \| na cāntyaḥ, ātmetarasya jñāna-tatkāryaṁ anyataratvāvaśyambhāvābhyupagamāt \| nāpi dvitīyaḥ, darśita-ḍoṣāpatteḥ \| evam ajñāna-nivṛtter ubhayathāpy asambhavena svarūpābhāvāt sā punaḥ sudūrāpāsteti \|\|10\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Is this fifth alternative called destruction of ignorance something other than ātmā or the very form of ātmā? If this destruction of ignorance is something other than ātmā, is it the result of ignorance or not? This destruction of ignorance which is different from ātmā cannot be the result of ignorance, since then ignorance would have to exist while destruction of ignorance is taking place. If it is not the result of ignorance, it must be the result of knowledge. But the destruction of ignorance arising from something other than ātmā cannot be the result of knowledge since destruction of ignorance takes place in the functions of consciousness (which is also ignorance).**

**Is destruction of ignorance the very essence of the ātmā? It cannot be, since it was already shown that the ātmā is already perfect and does not need destruction of ignorance. Thus, destruction of ignorance is impossible either as something different from ātmā or the essence of ātmā. Thus the fifth alternative has been completely rejected. \|\|10\|\|**

### Text 11

*kiṁca ghaṭānīnāṁ sattvaṁ śaśa-śṛṅgādīnām asattvaṁ ghaṭādīnām eva deśa-kāla-vyavasthayā sad-asatttvam iti prakāra-trayasyaivānanubhavāc caturtha-prakāra eva nāsti—tad ābhāvāt \| pañcama-prakāratāpy aṣṭama-rasavakaśakyāsambhavāt neti \|\|11\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, pots and other objects exist and rabbit horns do not exist. And pots either exist or do not exist according to place and time. The state of sat and asat simultaneously (third explanation) is never experienced,**

### Text 12

*evaṁ ca viṣaya-prayojanayor abhāvād adhikārī tad abhāvāt sambandhaś ca nirastaḥ \| satām eva hi sambandhaḥ sa cājñānāsambhave viṣayāder abhāvān neti vicārārambha-vaiyarthyaṁca \| tasmād eva tathāvaraṇa-rūpam ajñānaṁ nivartayac cāstram adoṣam iti riktaṁ vacaḥ \|\|12\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since there is absence of object and goal (viṣaya and prayojana), the ādhikārī (qualified person) is also absent.
