And with absence of a qualified person, sambandha (relationship of scripture to subject it reveals and relationship of person to the scripture) is also absent. Relationship with the spiritual aspirant is absent, since there is no ignorance (since only Brahma exists) and no proper object (Brahman cannot be described) to be revealed.**

## Chapter Six

**More refutation of Advaita-vāda**

### Text 1

*atrāhuḥ --phalavaty ajñāto ‘rthe śrutes tātparyād abhedaḥ paramārthaḥ \| anantānanda-brahma-bhāvo hi phalam \| śāstraika-gamyo brahmābhedaḥ \| tasmin advitīye brahmaṇi nānā-vidha-jñātr-jñeya-jñāna-bhedādi sarvaṁ prakalpitam ato mithyaiveti \| evaṁ āha śrutiḥ –“brahma-vid brahmaiva bhavati”, “brahmaiva san brahmāpyeti” “sad eva samyedam agrāsīd ekam evādvitīyam” “ neha nānāsti kiṁcana” “yatra hi dvaitam iva bhavati tad itara itaraṁ paśyati” “yatra tv asya sarvam ātmaivābhiut tatra kena kaṁ vijānīyāt” “vācārambhaṇaṁ vikāro nāmadheyaṁ mṛttiketyeva satyam” “ito ‘nyadārtam” “enaṁ brahma-lokam na vidanti anṛtena hi pratyuḍāḥ” ityādikā \|\|1\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The advaitavādī claims that the result is realization of the infinite bliss of Brahman. From the meaning of śruti, which is to reveal something previously unknown to the reader, one learns that jīva and Brahman are nondifferent. The nondifference of jīva and Brahman can only be known through scripture. Differences between knowledge, the knower and object of knowledge in non-dual Brahman are manufactured and thus false. According to them, the śruti explains this.**

**brahma-vid brahmaiva bhavati**

**The knower of Brahman becomes Brahman. Praśna Upaniṣad**

**brahmaiva san brahmāpyeti**

**Becoming Brahman one attains Brahman. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.4.6**

**sad eva samyedam agrāsīd ekam evādvitīyam**

**O gentle one, in the beginning only** ***sat*** **existed. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.2.1)**

**neha nānāsti kiṁcana**  
**yatra hi dvaitam iva bhavati tad itara itaraṁ paśyati**

**Where one sees difference, one sees something other than the truth. Bṛhad-ārāṇyaka Upaniṣad**

**yatra tv asya sarvam ātmaivābhūt tatra kena kaṁ vijānīyāt**

**When a persons realizes Brahman, he knows nothing else. Bṛhad-ārāṇyaka Upaniṣad**

**vācārambhaṇaṁ vikāro nāmadheyaṁ mṛttiketyeva satyam**

**Clay takes on a name as it undergoes transformation by actions initiated by words. But clay alone is proven by authority. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.1.4**

**ato ‘nyad artam**

**Everything else is false. Bṛhad-ārāṇyaka Upaniṣad**

**enaṁ brahma-lokam na vidanti anṛtena hi pratyuḍāḥ**

**All beings covered by ignorance day after day go to that world of Brahman, but do not know it. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 8.3.2 \|\|1\|\|**

### Text 2

*evam evāhaikādaśe śrī-bhāgavān—“guṇāḥ sṛjanti karmāṇi guṇo ’nusṛjate guṇān\| jīvas tu guṇa-saṁyukto bhuṅkte karma-phalāny asau \|\| yāvat syād guṇa-vaiṣamyaṁ tāvan nānātvam ātmanaḥ \| nānātvam ātmano yāvat pāratantryaṁ tadaiva hi \|\| yāvad asyāsvatantratvaṁ tāvad īśvarato bhayam \|\|” iti \|\|2\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord explains this in the Eleventh Canto of Bhāgavatam.**

**guṇāḥ sṛjanti karmāṇi guṇo ’nusṛjate guṇān**  
**jīvas tu guṇa-saṁyukto bhuṅkte karma-phalāny asau**

**The material senses create material activities with results, either pious or sinful, and the modes of nature set the material senses into motion. The living entity, being fully engaged by the material senses and modes of nature, experiences the various results of action.**

**yāvat syād guṇa-vaiṣamyaṁ tāvan nānātvam ātmanaḥ**  
**nānātvam ātmano yāvat pāratantryaṁ tadaiva hi**

**As long as the** ***jīva*** **acts through his senses and creates various destinations, he will attain a variety of bodies (nānātvam). As long as such bodies exist, he will remain dependent on** ***karma.***

**yāvad asyāsvatantratvaṁ tāvad īśvarato bhayam**

**As long as the** ***jīva*** **is dependent on** ***karma,*** **he will fear the controller (of** ***karma).*** **SB 11.10.31-33 \|\|2\|\|**

### Text 3

*idam atra rahasyam—kartṛvtva-bhoktṛtvādi-dharma-śūnyaś cinmātro ‘dvitīyo hy ātmā \| sa cātmany avidyayā guṇa-mayīm māyāṁ tad vaiṣamya-jām; kārya-saṁhatiṁ ca kalpayan asmad-artham ekaṁ yuṣmad arthāṁś ca bahūn kalpayati \| tatrāsmad-arthaḥ sva-svarūpaḥ puruṣaḥ : yuṣmad-arthās trividhāḥ—puruṣāntarāṇi, jaḍāni, sarveśvarākhyaḥ puruṣa-viśeṣaś ceti \| “jīveśāvābhāsena karoti māyā cāvidyā ca svayam eva bhavati” iti śrutiś ca \| kartṛtvaṁ bhoktṛtvaṁ ca guṇa-sambandhād ātmany asmad artho ‘dhyasyate\| \| atha tasyām avidyāyāṁ praṇaṣṭāyaṁ tat-kāryā-rūpā māyā vinaśyati; tan-nāśān nānātvaṁ ca \| tad abhāvād iśvara-tat-pāratantryaṁ; tad-bhayādīni sudūrotsāritānīti cinmātrādvitīyam ātmāvastv iti \|\|3\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Here is the secret. Ātmā alone exists, which is consciousness alone (cinmātrā), devoid of qualities of being the doer and experiencer. By ignorance that ātmā produces māyā endowed with guṇas, which manifest from disturbance,. Ātmā then produces action and various identities of me and you. The identity of “I” becomes the svarūpa, which is a person. “You” is of three types: other persons, forms of matter and the supreme Lord, who is a special person. Śruti says:**

**jīveśāv ābhāsena karoti māyā cāvidyā ca svayam eva bhavati**

**Māyā produces shadows in the form of jīva and the Lord. This is ignorance. Nṛsiṁha-tāpanī Upaniṣad**

**The doer and enjoyer, as well as identity as I, are imposed on the ātmā (Brahman) by a relationship with the guṇas. When ignorance is destroyed, māyā, its effect, is destroyed. With destruction of māyā, variety (*****nānā*****) is destroyed. With absence of variety, the Lord and dependence on the Lord is destroyed. Thus fear of the Lord, difficult to uproot, is destroyed. All that remains is ātmā, consciousness alone. \|\|3\|\|**

### Text 4

*athottaratra kiṁ bhadraṁ kim abhadraṁ vā dvaitasyāvastunaḥ kiyat \| vācoditaṁ tad anṛtaṁ manasā dhyātam eva ca \|\| chāyā-pratyāhvayābhāsā hy asanto ’py artha-kāriṇaḥ \| evaṁ dehādayo bhāvā yacchanty ā-mṛtyuto bhayam \|\| ātmaiva tad idaṁ viśvaṁ sṛjyate sṛjati prabhuḥ \| trāyate trāti viśvātmā hriyate haratīśvaraḥ \|\| tasmān na hy ātmano ’nyasmād anyo bhāvo nirūpitaḥ \| nirūpite ’yaṁ tri-vidhā nirmūla bhātir ātmani \| idaṁ guṇa-mayaṁ viddhi tri-vidhaṁ māyayā kṛtam \|\| 4\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Later in Bhāgavatam it is said:**

**kiṁ bhadraṁ kim abhadraṁ vā dvaitasyāvastunaḥ kiyat**  
**vācoditaṁ tad anṛtaṁ manasā dhyātam eva ca**

**That which is expressed by material words or contemplated by the mind in duality is false. What, therefore, is actually good or bad within this insubstantial world of duality, and how can the extent of such good and bad be measured?**

**chāyā-pratyāhvayābhāsā hy asanto ’py artha-kāriṇaḥ**  
**evaṁ dehādayo bhāvā yacchanty ā-mṛtyuto bhayam**

**Although reflections, echoes and mirages are only illusory reflections of real things, they produce real experiences. In the same way, although the identification of the conditioned soul with the material body, mind and ego is illusory, this identification generates fear within him even up to the moment of death.**

**ātmaiva tad idaṁ viśvaṁ sṛjyate sṛjati prabhuḥ**  
**trāyate trāti viśvātmā hriyate haratīśvaraḥ**  
**tasmān na hy ātmano ’nyasmād anyo bhāvo nirūpitaḥ**  
**nirūpite ’yaṁ tri-vidhā nirmūla bhātir ātmani**  
**idaṁ guṇa-mayaṁ viddhi tri-vidhaṁ māyayā kṛtam.**

**The Lord creates the universe and is created. He maintains the universe and is maintained. He destroys the universe and is destroyed. Nothing other than Paramātmā exists but he is different from everything. Perceptions of three types of suffering in Paramātmā are baseless. Know that the universe made of** ***guṇas*** **and three types of suffering are created by my inconceivable energy. SB 11.28.4-7 \|\|4\|\|**

### Text 5

*“etad vidvān mad-uditaṁ jñāna-vijñāna-naipuṇam \| na nindati na ca stauti loke carati sūrya-vat\|\| pratyakṣeṇānumānena nigamenātma-saṁvidā \| ādy-antavad asaj jñātvā niḥsaṅgo vicared iha” iti \|\|5\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**etad vidvān mad-uditaṁ jñāna-vijñāna-naipuṇam**  
**na nindati na ca stauti loke carati sūrya-vat**

**One who has completely understood knowledge and specialized knowledge, as described herein by me, does not indulge in material criticism or praise. Like the sun, he acts equally to all.**

**pratyakṣeṇānumānena nigamenātma-saṁvidā**  
**ādy-antavad asaj jñātvā niḥsaṅgo vicared iha**

**By direct perception, logical deduction, scriptural testimony and personal realization, one should know that this world has a beginning and an end and so is merely temporary. Thus one should live in this world without attachment. SB 11.28.8-9 \|\|5\|\|**

### Text 6

*tathā śri-viṣṇu-purāṇe sanandādayaḥ: paramārthas tvam evaiko nānyo ‘sti jagataḥ pate \| tavaiśa mahimā yena vyāptam etac carācaram \|\| yad etad dṛśyate mūrtam etaj jñānātmanas tava \| bhrānti-jñānena paśyanti jagad rupam ayoginaḥ \| jñāna-svarūpam akhilaṁ jagad detad abuddhayaḥ \| artha-svarūpaṁ paśyanto bhrāmyante moha-saṁplave \|\| ye cājñāna-vidaḥ śuddhās te paśyanty akhilaṁ jagat \| jñānātmakaṁ prapaśyanti tvad rūpaṁ parameśara” iti \|\|6\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Kumāras speak in the same way in Viṣṇu Purāṇa:**

**paramārthas tvam evaiko nānyo ‘sti jagataḥ pate \|**  
**tavaiśa mahimā yena vyāptam etac carācaram \|\|**

**O master of the universe! O Lord! You are the supreme goal. There is no other. Your greatness is spread among all moving and nonmoving beings.**

**yad etad dṛśyate mūrtam etaj jñānātmanas tava \|**  
**bhrānti-jñānena paśyanti jagad rupam ayoginaḥ \|**

**Out of mistaken knowledge, those who are not yogīs accept the form of the universe which is visible to be your form, though you are composed of knowledge. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.4.39**

**jñāna-svarūpam akhilaṁ jagad etad abuddhayaḥ \|**  
**artha-svarūpaṁ paśyanto bhrāmyante moha-saṁplave \|\|**

**The universe is a form of knowledge. Foolish people see the universe as a material entity and are bewildered by a flood of illusion**

**ye tu jñāna-vidaḥ śuddhās te paśyanty akhilaṁ jagat \|**  
**jñānātmakaṁ prapaśyanti tvad rūpaṁ parameśara**

**O supreme Lord! Those in knowledge who are pure, see the whole universe as knowledge and see your form everywhere. \|\|6\|\|**

### Text 7

*tathā śrī-gītāsu bhagavān- kṣetra-jñāṁ cāpi māṁ viddhi sarva-kṣestreṣu bhārata \| na tad asti vinā yat syān māya bhūtaṁ carācaraṁ” iti \|\|7\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In the Gītā the Lord says:**

***kṣetra-jñaṁ cāpi māṁ viddhi sarva-kṣetreṣu bhārata \|***

**O descendent of Bharata, know that I alone am the knower of the field in all bodies. BG 15.3**

***na tad asti vinā yat syān mayā bhūtaṁ carācaram \|\|\|***

**No moving or non-moving entity can exist without me. BG 10.39 \|\|7\|\|**

### Text 8

*mithyātvaṁ tv adhiṣṭhāna-bhūtaṁ brahma-jñāna-bādhyatvam eva śuktyādy adhiṣṭhāne doṣa-vaśād eva parikalpitaṁ rajatādi vijñāte hi tasmin bādhyate \| doṣaś ca svarūpāvṛti-vividhha-vikṣepa-karī sad-asad-vilakṣaṇatvād anirvacanīyānādir-avidyaiva tamo ‘jñānamāyādi-śabdair api caivābhidhīyate—“na sad āsīn no ‘sad āsīt tadānīṁ tama āsīt tamasā gūḍham agre praketam” “indro jāyābhiḥ prur-rūpa īyate “ “māyāṁ tu prakṛtiṁ vidyān māyinam tu maheśvaram” ityādau \|\|8\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The falsity of the world is negated by knowledge of Brahman, the basis of the world. Similarly, by mistake, silver which is being perceived, based on a sea shell, is negated by knowledge of the shell. The fault is ignorance, which causes the covering of the svarūpa and various types of agitation. It is indescribable by words because it is neither existing nor non-existing, and is without beginning. It is called tamas, māyā, and avidyā.**

**na sad āsīn no ‘sad āsīt tadānīṁ**

**There was no sat and no asat at that time. Aitareya Upaniṣad**

**tama āsīt tamasā gūḍam agre praketam**

**In the beginning, there was darkness because of ignorance. Yajur Veda 2.8.9**

**indro māyābhiḥ puru-rūpa īyate**

**The Lord appears as many by his māyā*****.*** **Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.5.19**

**māyāṁ tu prakṛtiṁ vidyān māyinam tu maheśvaram**

**Know that** ***prakṛti*** **is** ***māyā*** **and the Lord of** ***māyā i*****s the supreme lord. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.10 \|\|8\|\|**

### Text 9

*iyaṁ punar brahmātmaikya-vijñānān nivartate—“ na punar mṛtyave tad ekaṁ paśyati” “na paśyo mṛtyuṁ paśyati” “ tam eva viditvā ti mṛtum eit “ “tatra ko mohaḥ kaḥ śoka ekatvam anupaśyati” ityādi –śravaṇāt \| atra mṛtyu-śabdo hy avidyābhibhdhāyī --“pramādaṁ vai mṛtyum ahaṁ bravīmi, sadāpramādād amṛtatvaṁ bravīmi” iti smṛtau tasyāṁ tasya prayogāt \|\|9\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Ignorance is removed by knowledge of oneness in Brahman.**

**na punar mṛtyave tad ekaṁ paśyati**

**Not being in ignorance a persons sees oneness.**

**na paśyo mṛtyuṁ paśyati**

**Seeing oneness one does not see ignorance. Chāndogya Upaniṣad (7.26.2)**

**tam eva viditvā ti mṛtum eti**

**Knowing this one surpasses death (ignorance.) Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 3.8**

**tatra ko mohaḥ kaḥ śoka ekatvam anupaśyati**

**There is no illusion and no lamentation. One sees oneness. Īśopaniṣad**

**The word** ***mṛtyu*** **means ignorance. That is how the word is used in the smṛtis:**

**pramādaṁ vai mṛtyum ahaṁ bravīmi, sadāpramādād amṛtatvaṁ bravīmi**

**I call inattention ignorance. If one is always attentive, I say he is not in ignorance. Mahābhārata \|\|9\|\|**

### Text 10

*nirviśeṣa-cinmātraṁ khalu brahmaṇaḥ svarūpam “satyaṁ jñānam anantam” “niṣkalaṁ niṣkriyam śāntaṁ niravadyaṁ nirañjanam” “neha nānāsti kiñcana” “athāta ādeśo neti neti” “na hy etasmāditi nety anyat param asti” ityadbhiḥ śrutibhiḥ “pratyastamita-bhedaṁ yat sattāmātram agocaram \| vacasām atma-saṁvedyaṁ taj-jñānaṁ brahmasannitam \| śaśvat praśāntam abhayaṁ pratibodha-mātraṁ śuddhaṁ samaṁ sad asataḥ paramātma-attvam” ityādibhi smṛtibhiś ca samasta-viśesa-pratiṣedhāt \|\|10\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The svarūpa of Brahman is consciousness alone with no qualities. This is confirmed by śruti:**

**satyaṁ jñānam anantam**

**Brahman is infinite knowledge.**

**niṣkalaṁ niṣkriyam śāntaṁ niravadyaṁ nirañjanam**

**The Lord is without parts, without action, peaceful, without fault, without blemish. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.19)**

**neha nānāsti kiñcana**

**There is no variety at all. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.4.19)**

**athāta ādeśo neti neti na hy etasmād iti nety anyat param asti**

**This is the teaching, not this, not this. There is nothing superior. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.3.6)**

**All qualities are denied in the smṛtis.**

**pratyastamita-bhedaṁ yat sattāmātram agocaram \|**  
**vacasām atma-saṁvedyaṁ taj-jñānaṁ brahma-saṁjñitam \|**  
**śaśvat praśāntam abhayaṁ pratibodha-mātraṁ**  
**śuddhaṁ samaṁ sad asataḥ param ātma-tattvam**

**That knowledge is Brahman. It is without any kind of difference, is pure existence, is beyond the power of words to express and is self-knowing. It is eternal, peaceful, without fear, knowledge alone. It is pure, homogenous the supreme ātmā beyond sat and asat. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 6.7.53) \|\|10\|\|**

### Text 11

*tad asya pratipatyur upāsakasyātmaiveti mantavyam—“ātmety evopāsīt”etyādi śravanāt “atmeti tūpagacchanti grāhayanti ca” iti śāstra-kṛtāpi svīkṛtatvāc ca \|\|11\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**For the person who receives teachings, the worshipper, ātmā, means Brahman.**

**ātmety evopāsīt**

**Brahman should be worshipped as ātmā. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 1.4.7)**

**atmeti tūpagacchanti grāhayanti ca**

**Knowers of truth worship the Lord as** ***ātmā*** **and teach this to their students. Vedānta-sūtra 4.1.3ь \|\|11\|\|**

### Text 12

*na ca satyādibhiḥ padaiḥ nirviśeṣasyaivāvagatau teṣāṁ paryāyatāpattiḥ, paryāya-bhūtānāṁ yugapad-vacane paunar uktyam ca \| tasmat satyatvādi-guṇa-viśiṣṭābhidhāyīni tānīti vācyaṁ nirguṇa-śruti-virodhāpatteḥ \|\|12\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One should understand that words like mean “no qualities”. They are used as repetition of “no qualities.” To use the same words in many statements would produce the fault of redundant words (*****punar ukti*****). To say these words indicate qualities like** ***satya*** **(truth) is wrong since that is against the śrutis which proclaim that Brahman is without qualities. \|\|12\|\|**

### Text 13

*tataś cāsatyāj jaḍāt paricchinnāc ca vyāvṛttiṁ kramādimāni gamayanti \| vyāvṛttes tu na bhāva-rūpo nāpy abhāva-rūpa-dharmaḥ \| kintu sarvetaravyāvṛtta-brahmātmakaiva \| yathā śauklāder nailyadivyāvṛttiḥ śauklyādi-rūpair na tu dharmāntaram \| evaṁ ca sati teṣām aikārthyaāparyāyatā ceti na kāpi kṣatir asti \|\|13\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**These words are used to deny non-existence, lack of consciousness and limitedness. By denial they do not indicate existent (bhāva) or nonexistent (abhāva) of qualities. They simply indicate denial of everything other than Brahman, just as whiteness denies blueness, and indicates nothing other than whiteness. All those words indicate only oneness of Brahman. There is no harm is using such words. \|\|13\|\|**

### Text 14

*yat tu “dvā suparṇā sayujā sakhāyā” ityādiṣu bhedavādo ‘vagamyate, sa khalu na śāstra-tātparya-gocaraḥ prasiddheḥ phalābhāvāc ca kintu vyavyahāra-gocara eva mṛṣabhutaḥ \| jīva-jaḍātmakaḥ prapañcaḥ khalu śuktau rūpyam iva brahmaṇy adhyasto mithyābhutas tena tad-bhedo ‘pi tathā \|\|14\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Statements like “two birds in a tree” seem to indicate difference. But those statements do not indicate the conclusion of the scriptures. Mentioning well-known objects gives no result. Such ordinary objects of this world are simply illusions. The world of jīvas and matter is an imposition on Brahman, like imposing silver on a shell. These differences are all illusory. \|\|14\|\|**

### Text 15

*evaṁ hi śrī-viṣnū-purāṇe parāśaro ‘pi niścinoti “jyotiṁṣi viṣnur bhuvanāni viṣṇuḥ sa eva viṣṇur vidiśo diśaś ca \| sarit samudrāś ca sa eva sarvaṁ yad asti nasti ca vipravarya \|\| jñāna-rūpo bhagavān yato ‘sāv aśeṣa-mūrtir na tu vastu-bhūtaḥ \| tato hi śailābdhi-dharādi-bhedān jānīha vijñāna-vijṛmbhitāni \|\| yadā tu śuddhaṁ nija-rūpi sarvaṁ karma-kṣaye jñānam apāsta-doṣam \| tadā hi saṅkalpataroḥ phalāni bhavanti no vastuṣu vastu-bhedāḥ \|\| vastv asti kiṁ kutracid ādi-mādhya-paryānta-hīnaṁ satataika-rūpam \| tac cānyathātvaṁ dvija yāti bhūyo na tat tathā tatra kuto hi tattvam \|\| mahī ghaṭatvaṁ ghatātaḥ kapālikā kapālikāc cūrna-rajas tato ‘ṇuḥ \|\| janaiḥ sva-karmāstamitātma-niścayair ālakṣyate bruhi kim atra vastu” \|\|15\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is confirmed in Viṣṇu Purāṇa.**

**jyotiṁṣi viṣnṛ bhuvanāni viṣṇuḥ sa eva viṣṇur vidiśo diśaś ca \|**  
**sarit samudrāś ca sa eva sarvaṁ yad asti nasti ca vipravarya \|\|**

**Viṣṇu is the planets. Viṣṇu is the worlds. Viṣṇu is the directions, rivers and ocean. He is the rivers and oceans. He alone is everything which is conscious (*****yad asti*****) and unconscious (*****yan nāsti*****), O best of the** ***vipras*****.**

**jñāna-rūpo bhagavān yato ‘sau viśeṣa-mūrtir na tu vastu-bhutaḥ \|**  
**tato hi śailābdhi-dharādi-bhedān jānīha vijñāna-vijṛmbhitāni \|\|**

**Because he has a form of knowledge, he has no form made of matter. Know that these forms arise from ignorance, from differences of stone, water and earth.**

**yadā tu śuddhaṁ nija-rūpi sarvaṁ karma-kṣaye jñānam apāsta-doṣam \|**  
**tadā hi saṅkalpataroḥ phalāni bhavanti no vastuṣu vastu-bhedāḥ \|\|**

**When the pure form of the self arise with the destruction of karma, knowledge which destroys all faults manifests. Fruits of the desire tree manifest and differences are destroyed.**

**vastv asti kiṁ kutracid ādi-mādhya-paryānta-hīnaṁ satataika-rūpam \|**  
**tac cānyathātvaṁ dvija yāti bhūyo na tat tathā tatra kuto hi tattvam \|\|**

**What is that one form devoid of beginning, middle and end and where is it? O brāhmaṇa! Is something else. It is not like this. How can it be defined?**

**mahī ghaṭatvaṁ ghatātaḥ kapālikā kapālikāc cūrna-rajas tato ‘ṇuḥ \|\|**  
**janaiḥ sva-karma-stamitātma-niścayair ālakṣyate bruhi kim atra vastu**

**Earth becomes a pot. A pot becomes pieces. The pieces become dust and then atoms. What object can people who have ātmā covered by karmas perceive here? \|\|15\|\|**

### Text 16

*“tasmān na vijñānam ṛte ‘sti kiṁcit kvacit kadācit dvija vastu-jātam \| vijñānam ekaṁ nija-karma-bheda-vibhinna-cittair bahudhābhyupetam \|\| jñānaṁ viśuddhaṁ vimalaṁ viśokam aśeṣa-lobhādi-nirastam aṇgaṁ \| evaṁ sadāikaṁ paramaḥ pareśaḥ sa vāsudevo na yato ‘nyad asti” \|\|16\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**tasmān na vijñānam ṛte ‘sti kiṁcit kvacit kadācit dvija vastu-jātam \|**  
**vijñānam ekaṁ nija-karma-bheda-vibhinna-cittair bahudhābhyupetam \|\|**  
**jñānaṁ viśuddhaṁ vimalaṁ viśokam aśeṣa-lobhādi-nirasta-saṇgaṁ \|**  
**evaṁ sadāikaṁ paramaḥ pareśaḥ sa vāsudevo na yato ‘nyad asti**

**Therefore there is nothing except pure consciousness anywhere, at any time, O brāhmaṇa! There is one consciousness which appears to be many by various divisions of consciousness caused by individual karma. It is pure, without contamination, without lamentation, without greed or other qualities. There is one supreme lord Vāsudeva. Nothing else exists. \|\|16\|\|**

### Text 18

*ataeva bheda-grāhiṇaṁ śrutir nindati bhayaṁ ca tasyābhidhatte—“atha yo ‘nyāṁ devatām upāste” “yo ‘nyat asāv anyo ‘ham asmīti na sa deva yatha paśur evaṁ sa devānām” iti, “yad eveha tad amutra yad amutra yad anv iha mṛtyoḥ sa mṛyum āpnoti ya iha nāneva paśyati” “yaḍ ahy evaiṣa etasmin udaram antaraṁ kurute atha tasya bhayaṁ bhavati” ityādikā \| tasmāt parāpārātmanor abheda eva pāramārthikaḥ bhedas tu vyavārika iti siddham \|\|18\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Śrutis criticize those who see difference, and instill fear in them.**

**atha yo ‘nyāṁ devatām upāste yo ‘nyat asāv anyo ‘ham asmīti na sa deva yatha paśur evaṁ sa devānām**

**If a man worships some other** ***devatā*** **and thinks "The** ***devatā*** **is one person and I am another" he does not know; he is like an animal for the** ***devatās.*** **(Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 1.4.10)**

**yad eveha tad amutra yad amutra yad anv iha**  
**mṛtyoḥ sa mṛtyum āpnoti ya iha nāneva paśyati**  
**yaḍā hy evaiṣa etasmin ud aram antara kurute atha tasya bhayaṁ bhavati**

**When the jīva deviates a little from Brahman, he becomes fearful. Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.7**

**Therefore, nondifference of the jīva and Brahman is the highest truth. Difference is conventional. That has been proved. \|\|18\|\|**

### Text 19

*“tvaṁ vā aham astim bhagavo devate aham; vai tvam asi bhagavo devate” tad yo ham so ‘sau yo ‘sau so ‘ham” iti “tat tvaṁ asi” iti caivam ādiṣv api bhāga-lakṣaṇayā viruddhān guṇāṁśān vihāya saṁvid anubhuti-paryāyaṁ caitanya-mātram ekaṁ tayoḥ svarūpam avagamanīyaṁ nirguṇa-vkākyasyānurodhād eva \| balavac ca nirguṇa-vākyaṁ svarūpa-paratvāt \|\|19\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**tvaṁ vā aham asmi bhagavo devate ahaṁ vai tvam asi bhagavo devate**

**O Lord! I am you. You are me.** ***Varāha Upaniṣad*** **1.34**

**tad yo ham so ‘sau yo ‘sau so ‘ham**

**That which is I is Brahman. That which is Brahman is I. Aitareya Upaniṣad**

**tat tvaṁ asi**

**You are that Brahman.**

**Giving up statements that Brahman has qualities, since this contradicts the main statements, by using bhāga-lakṣaṇā, one should understand that the svarūpa of Brahman is oneness of jīva and Brahman, consciousness alone, since this agrees with statements of Brahman without qualities. The statements of Brahman without qualities has strength because they indicate the svarūpa of Brahman. \|\|19\|\|**

### Text 20

*yat tasya kvacid rūpaṁ varṇayanti, tat kila kapilatam eva “cinmayasyādvitīyasya niṣkalasyāśarīriṇaḥ \| upāsanakānāṁ kāryārthaṁ brahmaṇo rupa-kalpanā” ityādi śravaṇāt \| kāryaṁ ca teṣām arundhatīdarśana-nyāyena krameṇa pratyak-pravaṇa-cittatāiva \|\|20\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Where form of Brahman is described it is merely imagined.**

**cinmayasyādvitīyasya niṣkalasyāśarīriṇaḥ \|**  
**upāsanakānāṁ kāryārthaṁ brahmaṇo rupa-kalpanā**

**A form of Brahman is manufactured for use of the worshippers, though Brahman is only one conscious entity, without parts and without a body. Rāma Upaniṣad**

**By doing this worship, gradually the worshippers develop concentrated consciousness, in the manner of using other bright stars to point out Arundhati star in the sky. \|\|20\|\|**

### Text 21

*evaṁ cāsatyair eva śāstrādibhih satya-bhuta-brahma-bhavāptir asatyair eva svāpnika-strī-saṅga-śiraś-chedādibhiḥ satya-bhūta-sukha-duḥkhāder upālambhāt ata uktam “chāyā-prayāhvayābhāsā hy asanto ‘py artha-kāriṇaḥ” iti \| tasmān nirviśeṣa-cinmātrādvaitaṁ brahmaiva satyaṁ tad anyat sarvaṁ tatra parikalpitam mithyābhutam iti \|\|21\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One can attain realization of Brahman, which is real, by scriptures and other factors which are not real. One can realize happiness or distress which is real through dreams in which one associates with a woman or has one’ head severed. Therefore it is said:**

**chāyā-pratyāhvayābhāsā hy asanto ‘py artha-kāriṇaḥ**

**Though shadows, echoes and reflections are not real objects, they convey meaning. SB 11.28.5**

**Therefore Brahman, consciousness alone, one entity is real and everything else is imagined. It is all false. \|\|21\|\|**

### Text 22

*atrācakṣmahe—na hy abhede phalam asti \| ajñāna-nivṛttir ānandāvāptiś ca phalam \| tatra nādyaḥ, prāk pratyākhyānāt, nāntyaḥ, nirviśeṣatva-kṣateḥ \| na cāvāptiḥ svarūpam eva dharma iti vācyaṁ tathā sati sādhana-vaiyarthyādyāpatteḥ \| nāpi brahmatāpattau sā śrutiḥ pramāna-bhāvam āvahati – eva-śabdasya tatra sādṛśyārthakatvāt \| “va vā yathā tathevaivaṁ sāmye” iti hy anuśāsanam \| tataś ca brahmaiva brahmasama ity arthaḥ \| ataeva brahma-bhāvānantaraṁ brahmapyayaḥ saṁgacchate nānyathā; śrutiś ca—“nirañjanaḥ paramaṁ sāmyam upaiti” iti “yathodakaṁ śuddhe śuddam āsiktaṁ tādṛg eva bhavati \| evaṁ muner vijānata ātmā bhavati gautama” iti smṛtiś ca “idaṁ jñānam upāśritya mama sādharyam āgatāḥ \| sarge ‘pi nopajāyante pralaye na vyathanti ca” iti, “bhoga-mātrā-sāmya-liṅgāc ca” iti nyāyaś ca \|\|22\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**To this we respond. There is no result from realizing nondifference. They say the result is destruction of ignorance and obtaining bliss. There can be no destruction of ignorance. That was previously explained. There is no attainment of bliss. That attainment would ruin absence of qualities in Brahman (which should be** ***nirviśeṣa*****). One may argue that bliss is not a quality of Brahman but is the svarūpa of Brahman. If that were so, then sādhana (to attain bliss) would be useless. You quote śruti and say** ***brahma-vid brahmaiva bhavati*****: one who knows Brahman becomes Brahman. But such a meaning has no authority. The word** ***eva*** **in this case (*****brahmaiva*****) means one attains similarity to Brahman. According to the rule** ***va vā yathā tathevaivaṁ sāmye*** **(Amara-koṣa) the word** ***eva*** **means “similarity.” Therefore, the meaning is “One who knows Brahman becomes similar to Brahman.” Attaining the bhāva of Brahman, one then attains Brahman.**

**nirañjanaḥ paramaṁ sāmyam upaiti**

**The pure jīva attains similarity to the Lord (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 3.1.3)**

**Just as water poured into pure water becomes pure like that water, so the ātmā of the intelligent sage becomes similar to the Lord. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 4.15**

**Smṛti says:**

***idaṁ jñānam upāśritya mama sādharmyam āgatāḥ \|***  
***sarge ’pi nopajāyante pralaye na vyathanti ca \|\|*****2*****\|\|***

**Those who have attained qualities similar to mine, by realizing this knowledge through** ***guru,*** **do not take rebirth in this world, and do not experience pain at the time of death. BG 14.2**

**bhoga-mātrā-sāmya-liṅgāc ca**

**The jīva is not equal to the Lord because there are indications that equality refers only to their enjoyment. Vedānta-sūtra 4.4.21 \|\|22\|\|**

### Text 23

*na ca loke ajñāto jīva-brahmābhedaḥ śāstreṇaiva jñāyate, atas tatra tasya tātparyam iti vācyaṁ --śāstra-tātparya-nirṇetṛbhiḥ ṣaḍbhir liṅgais tad-bhedasyaiva nirṇeṣyamāṇatvāt \| tad advaitaṁ brahmātiriktaṁ brahmātmakaṁ vetyādibhis tad asya purā nirāsāc ca tasmān nara-śṛṅgādivad asattvād eva tathātvam tasya \|\|23\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One cannot say “People do not know that jīva and Brahman are nondifferent and thus should know this through scriptures. That is the meaning of the scriptures.” Knowers of the meaning of scriptures have concluded that the scriptures proclaim difference by using the six characteristics. Previously it was asked “Is advaita different from Brahman or identical with Brahman?” Nondifference was at that time defeated. To say that nondifference is unknown and should be known through scriptures is thus like a horn growing from a man--which does not exist. \|\|23\|\|**

### Text 24

*evaṁ ca “sad eva saumyedam” ityādāv idaṁ-śabdasya jagataḥ śaktimad brahmaivopādānaṁ nimittaṁ ceti vivakṣitam eka-vijñānena sarva-vijñāna-pratijñānāt padebhas tathābhidhānāc ca \| na caitad abheda-mātre mānam—eka-padenaiva tat-siddhau padāntara-vaiyarthyāpatteḥ \| na ca sajātīya-vijātīya-svagata-bheda-nirāsāt pada-trayam arthavad eveti vācyam abhedasya nirastatvāt \| tasmād evādvitīya-pade tad itara sarvābhāvas tasya ca nirūpākhyatvam adhikaraṇa-rūpatvaṁ cetyādi-kalpanaṁ sudūrotsāritam \| kimca, tena kalpanenāpi nābhimataṁ sidhyati \| abhāvenaiva sa-dvitīyatvāpatteḥ—dvitīyasa nirūpākhyatve ‘pi brahmaṇi tad ādhāratvāpatteḥ \| abhāvasyādhikaraṇa-rūpatve tu ghatābhāvo bhuvīty atra bhuvi bhur ityādy anubhavāpatter brahmaṇo nirūpākhyatāpatteś cety anye \| tasmāt tuccham etat \|\|24\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In the phrase** ***sad eva saumyedam*** **(O gentle one, in the beginning this existed),** ***idam*** **refers to Brahman, endowed with śaktis, which is the material and efficient cause of the universe, since it was said “Knowing him (material cause), everything (other than Brahman) becomes known.” It was also said “He created fire.” Thus he is also the efficient cause. Thus non-difference, with one Brahman, is not accepted.**

**In** ***ekam evādvitīyam*****, if the word** ***eka*** **means nondifference then the word a*****dvitīyam*** **becomes redundant. One cannot say that the three words (*****ekam eva advitīyam*****) indicates rejection of three types of difference--sajātīya-bheda (different entities of same class), vijātīya-bheda (different entities of different class) and svagata-bheda (internal difference)--since nondifference was already refuted.**

**One should not argue that** ***eva advitīyam*** **means that nothing besides Brahman exists and that all descriptive words simply indicate Brahman. This was already refuted. Furthermore, such imagined meanings do not fulfill your goal, since absence of everything else (abhāva) entails that something else besides Brahman existed. In defining a second object (nonexistence) Brahman would have to be its substrate. And if nonexistence is the very nature of Brahman, then Brahman would be totally void. It is like a pot which does not exist on the ground. One then equates the nonexistence of the pot with the ground. This is a silly argument. \|\|24\|\|**

### Text 25

*“neha nānā” ity atra vyuheṣu pratītasya bhedasya niṣedhaḥ \| teṣu nānātvena pratītir abhede satyeveti śrutir āha—“eko ‘pi san bahudhā yo vibhāti” iti “ekaṁ santaṁ bahudhā dṛṣyamānam” iti ca \| dharma-bhedasya vāyaṁ pratiṣedhaḥ , śrutiś ca—“yathodakaṁ durge vṛṣṭam” ityādyā \| na cātra nānā-vidha-jñātṛ-jñeyatvādi pratiṣedhaḥ, tasya śruti-pratipāditatvāt \|\|25\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The phrase** ***neha nānā*** **(there is no variety here) indicates that there is no difference when perceiving the various forms of the Lord. In those forms which are nondifferent there is variety. Śruti says:**

**eko ‘pi san bahudhā yo vibhāti**

**Though he is one, the Lord manifests as many. Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad 1.19**

**ekaṁ santaṁ bahudhā dṛṣyamānam**

**The one is seen as many.** ***Brhad-āranyaka Upaniṣad***

**Or one should not see difference between the qualities and the Lord’s svarūpa. Śruti says:**

**yathodakaṁ durge vṛṣṭaṁ parvateṣu vidhāvati**  
**evāṁ dharmān pṛthak paśyaṁs tān evānuvidhāvati**

**As water falling on mountains flows to inaccessible places, a man who sees the Lord's qualities different from the Lord flows into the material world of** ***saṁsāra*****. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 2.1.14**

**There is no denial of existence of various knowers and objects which are known in the statement, since the scriptures describe both. \|\|25\|\|**

### Text 26

*“yatra hi dvaitam iva” ityadau ca na bhedasya pratiṣedhaḥ pāramārthikatvāt “pṛthag ātmānaṁ preritāraṁ ca matvā juṣṭaṁs tatas tenāmṛtatvam eti” “juṣṭaṁ yadā paśyati anyam īśam asya mahimānam iti vīta-śoka” iti taj-jñānasya mokṣa-hetutva-śravaṇāt \|\|26\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The statement** ***yatra hi dvaitam iva*** **does not deny difference.**

**yatra hi dvaitam iva bhavati tad itara itaraṁ paśyati**

**In the universe there appears to be duality, difference from the Lord. In this world the jīva sees separate objects. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.5.15)**

**Duality in the spiritual world is not forbidden, since seeing duality is a cause of liberation:**

***pṛthag ātmānaṁ preritāraṁ ca matvā***  
***juṣṭas tatas tenāmṛtatvam eti***

**Knowing himself to be different from he who inspires actions, invoking the pleasure of the Lord, that person attains liberation. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 1.6**

**juṣṭaṁ yadā paśyati anyam īśam asya mahimānam iti vīta-śoka**

**When he sees the glorious Lord who is different, he gives up lamentation. Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 3.1.2 \|\|26\|\|**

### Text 27

*kintv ayam evārthaḥ –brahmāyatta-vṛttikatvād brahmātmakam idaṁ jagad vyapadiṣyate \| prāṇāyatta-vṛttikatvād yathā prāṇātmakaṁ vāgādi vyapadiṣtām \| chāndogye---“na vai vāco na cakṣūṁṣi na śrotrāṇi na manāṁsītyācakṣate \| prāṇa ityevācakṣate \| prāṇo hy evaitāni sarvāṇi bhavati” iti \|\|27\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is the meaning. Since what is seen as dual is** ***dependent*** **on Brahman, the world is said to be** ***brahmātmaka*** **(composed of Brahman). Similarly, speech is dependent on prāṇa and thus is said to be** ***prāṇāmaka*****. Chāndogya Upaniṣad says:**

**na vai vāco na cakṣūṁṣi na śrotrāṇi na manāṁsīty ācakṣate \| prāṇa ity evācakṣate \| prāṇo hy evaitāni sarvāṇi bhavati**

**These are not called voices, eyes, ears or minds. They are called** ***prāṇa. Prāṇa*** **is all these things. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 5.1.15 \|\|27\|\|**

### Text 28

*tathā brahma-niṣṭhatvāt brahma-vyāpyatvād brahmātmakaṁ taṁ smaryate—“anyaś ca paramo rājas tathānyaḥ pañca-viṁśakaḥ \| taṁ sthatvād anupaśyanti hy eka eveti sādhava” iti, sarvaṁ samāpnoṣi tato ‘si sarvam” iti ca \|\|28\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Because the jīva becomes fixed in Brahman and is pervaded by Brahman it is called** ***brahmātmaka*****.**

**anyaś ca paramo rājas tathānyaḥ pañca-viṁśakaḥ \|**  
**taṁ sthatvād anupaśyanti hy eka eveti sādhava**

**O king! Though the jīva and the Lord are different, because the jīva is situated in the Lord, the wise see them as one. Mahābhārata**

**sarvaṁ samāpnoṣi tato ‘si sarvam**

**You pervade everywhere, and therefore you are everything. BG 11.40 \|\|28\|\|**

### Text 29

*tathā ca saṁsāra-daśāyāṁ tattva-jñānābhāvāt svatantram iva tad ajñasya bhavati tatretara itaraṁ paśyati svatantram iva santaṁ jānānīty arthaḥ \| yadā tu śāṣtracārya-prasādād vidhyastājñānaḥ satatānucintanena sañjāta-brahma-sākṣātkāras tat-svarūpa-śakty-anugrahe ‘ṇopalabda-pārṣada-bhāvas tadā “kena kaṁ paśyet” api tenaiva taṁ paśyed ity arthaḥ \|\|29\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In saṁsāra, because of true knowledge, the jīva thinks that the Lord and Brahman are completely different. When by the mercy of ācāryas of scriptur, the jīva destroys his ignorance and, by continual contemplation, develops realization of Brahman, by the mercy of the Lord’s svarūpa-śakti, he becomes an associate of the Lord. He does not see anything except the Lord. \|\|29\|\|**

### Text 30

*śrutir apy evam āha—“yam evaiṣa vṛṇute tena labhyas tasyaiṣa ātmā vivṛṇute tanuṁ svāṁ” iti, smṛtiś ca—“prayujyamāne mayi tāṁ śuddhāṁ bhāgavatīṁ tanum \| ārabdha-karma-nirvāṇo nyapatyat pāñśa-bautikaḥ” iti \| “ādatte hari-hastena hari-dṛṣṭyaiva paśyati \| gacchec ca hari-padena muktasyaiṣā sthitir bhavet” iti ca \| itarathā sarva-pada-saṁkocāpattiḥ \|\|30\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Śruti says:**

**yam evaiṣa vṛṇute tena labhyas tasyaiṣa ātmā vivṛṇute tanuṁ svāṁ**

**The Lord can be attained by the person whom the Lord chooses. To him the Lord reveals his form. Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 3.2.3**

**Smṛtis confirm this:**

**prayujyamāne mayi tāṁ śuddhāṁ bhāgavatīṁ tanum \|**  
**ārabdha-karma-nirvāṇo nyapatyat pāñca-bautikaḥ**

**Having been awarded a transcendental body befitting an associate of the Lord, Nārada’s body made of five material elements, with karmas relating to the present body, fell away. (SB 1.6.28)**

**ādatte hari-hastena hari-dṛṣṭyaiva paśyati \|**  
**gacchec ca hari-padena muktasyaiṣā sthitir bhavet**

**The devotee grasps by the hand of the Lord and sees by the eyes of the Lord. He moves by the feet of the Lord. This is the situation of the liberated jīva. Bhaviṣyottara Purāṇa**

**If this meaning is not accepted, the meaning of scriptures becomes restricted. \|\|30\|\|**

### Text 31

*“vācārambhanām” ityādau kāranāt brahmaṇaḥ kāryaṁ jagad abhinnam ityevārthaḥ \| āha caivaṁ bhagavān sūtrakāraḥ—“tad ananyatvaṁ ārambhaṇa-śabdādibhyaḥ” iti \| tatra ca brahmavat satyaṁ jagad iti pratipannaṁ \| itarathā satyāsatyayor abhedānupapattiḥ \|\|31\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In statements like** ***vācārambhaṇam*****, the effect of Brahman, the universe, is nondifferent from the cause which is also Brahman. That is the meaning. Thus the author of the Brahma-sūtras says:**

**tad ananyatvaṁ ārambhaṇa-śabdādibhyaḥ**

**From the Brahman arises the world which is non-different from it. This is proved by statements starting with** ***vācārambhaṇa.***  **Vedānta-sūtra 2.1.14 \|\|31\|\|**

### Text 32

*“ito ‘nyadārtam” ity atrānyat jagad ārtaṁ duḥkhīty evārthaḥ –“ārto jijñasur arthārthi” ity atra tathāvagamāt \| duḥkhi cedam akhilam—“tasmād itaṁ jagad aśeṣam asat-svarūpam svapnābhamastadhiṣaṇaṁ puru-duḥkha-duḥkham” ity ukteḥ \| “”dvaitaṁ” ityādau pratyak-paratāvirodhi-karkaśa-kapaṭa-rūpam apriyam asatyaṁ vākyam anṛtam ucyate tena yasmāt prajāḥ pratyuḍāḥ, tasmād evaṁ brahma-lokaṁ na vidantīy evam ity arthaḥ \| ṛtaṁ khalu sunṛtaṁ vākyam—“ṛtaṁ ca sunrṭā vāṇī” iti bhagavad-vyākhyānāt \| tad-bhinnam anṛtam \|\|32\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**ato ‘nyad ārtam**

**Everything else is suffering (or false). Bṛhad-ārāṇyaka Upaniṣad**

**This statement actually means everything other than the Lord is filled with suffering (*****ārtam*****). That is the meaning given to the word in Gītā:** ***ārto jijñasur arthārthi jñānī ca*****: four types of people surrender: ṭhe suffering person (*****ārtaḥ*****), the inquisitive, the person desiring wealth and the jñānī. The whole world is suffering.**

**tasmād itaṁ jagad aśeṣam asat-svarūpam**  
**svapnābham astadhiṣaṇaṁ puru-duḥkha-duḥkham**

**Therefore this entire universe, which like a dream, is by nature unreal, nevertheless appears real, and thus it covers one’s consciousness and assails one with repeated miseries. SB 10.14.22**

**When dvaitam (difference) (difference) Anṛta-vākyammeans words which are not true, which are not pleasing. Thus the world is rough and full of cheating (anṛtam), contrary to the nature of the Lord.**

**sarvāḥ prajā aharahar gacchantya etaṁ brahma-lokaṁ na vindanty anṛtena hi pratyūḍhāḥ**

**All beings covered by ignorance day after day go to that world of Brahman, but do not know it. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 8.3.2**

**Because all beings are covered (*****pratyūḍhāḥ*****) they do not know Brahman. (It is does not mean that all beings are false.) The word** ***ṛṭam*** **means existing. It refers to the Lord. Everything else is different from the Lord,** ***anrtam.*** **\|\|32\|\|**

### Text 33

*The verses from Eleventh canto (SB 11.10.31-33) quoted in text 2 do not indicate that the lotus-eyed Lord teaches advaita-vāda, with consciousness alone. It is impossible that Brahman would take on a false identity as the Lord and teaches that the world is false. Such an interpretation also contradicts previous and later statements of the Lord. The scripture explains that the jīva’s saṁsāra arises from hostility to the Lord who is the supreme object, and that jīva’s samsara ends by the jīva’s accepting the Lord. \|\|33\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**tasmād ayam evārthaḥ—naśvaratayā viśvaṁ vipaśyan hṛdaya-śuddhi-loka-saṁgrahecchayā nivṛttaṁ karmānutiṣṭhan yathāyathaṁ yama-niyamān bhajan mad-abhijña-gurūpasatti-vidita-tattva-trayo vivicya praṇihita-sad-rūpas tad upasatti-labdhayātma-vidyayā saṁsṛtiṁ**

### Text 34

*vidhūnotīti “mayoditeṣu” ityādibhiḥ svataṁ prāg upadarśitaṁ \| atha tasya pariśuddhaye pratipakṣa-bhūtāni matāntarāṇi nirākiryas te viṁśatyā; teṣu “athaiṣām” ityādibhiḥ sapta-daśabhiḥ karma-jaḍānāṁ mataṁ svayaṁ upanyasya dūṣitam \| jīvasya svataḥ katṛtva-bhoktṛtva-rūpam iti tadaṁśas tu “guṇāḥ sṛjanti” ity ekena sāṁkhya-mataṁ āśritya nānābhutāḥ svatantrāś ca puruṣā iti sāṁkhya-mataṁ ca “yāvat syāt” ityādinā sārdhena jñāna-mātrādvaitam āśritrya iti \| atha tad idaṁ mata-trayaṁ kumatim iti svayaṁ prayācaṣṭe—“ya etat samupāsīraṁs te muhyanti śucāpitā” ity anantarokta-vākyenārdheneti \|\|34\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The meaning of the Bhāgavatam verses is this. Perceiving that the world is temporary, performing detached actions with a desire to teach the people and purify one’s heart, practicing yama and niyama, considering the three truths (prakṛti, jīva and īśvara) which are known by surrendering to a knowledgeable guru, one destroys ignorance by the knowledge of ātmā, obtained from surrender to guru. The Lord has already explained:**

**mayoditeṣv avahitaḥ sva-dharmeṣu mad-āśrayaḥ**  
**varṇāśrama-kulācāram akāmātmā samācaret**

**Taking full shelter in me through** ***bhakti*** **as explained by me, while being without material desire, one should practice** ***varṇāśrama.***  **SB 11.10.1**

**The Lord then in twenty verses presents various opponents’ view in order to distinguish his own view. Starting with verse 14, for seventeen verses he discusses and refutes karma-jaḍa. Then in verse 31 (*****guṇāh sṛjyanti*****) he explains and refutes sāṅkhya with its idea of the jīva as the independent doer and enjoyer, taking various forms as the puruṣa. In verse 32 he presents advaita-vāda. In verse SB 11.10.33 he concludes that all three ideas (karma-jaḍa, saṅkhyā and advaita-vāda) are false theories with the words** ***ya etat samupāsīraṁs te muhyanti śucārpitāḥ:*** **those who devote themselves to this are bewildered and subject to lamentation. \|\|34\|\|**

### Text 35

*itthaṁ cedam ityādinaika-jīva-vāda-rūpaṁ rahasya-kalpanam api nirastam—“nityo-nityānāmś cetanānām eko bahūnāṁ yo vidadhāti kāmān” iti śruteś ca \| evaṁ sati vibhu-jīveśāv iti tarka-śāstra-kalpitāv eva, na tu śruti-siddhāv iti—tasyāstu nitya-siddhānādiguṇakam aṇu-caitayaṁ jīva iti nitya-siddhānādi-guṇaka-vibhū-cit-sukha-vigraha-svarūpas tv īśvar iti pratipādanāt \|\|35\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Similarly the idea of one jīva (or one conscious being) is refuted. Śruti says:**

**nityo nityānāmś cetanaś cetanānām eko bahūnāṁ yo vidadhāti kāmān**

**The Lord is the chief eternal and the chief conscious entity. He fulfills the desires of the many. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad 2.2.13)**

**The idea presented in text 3 as a secret (*****idam atra rahasya*****)--that jīva and the Lord are a single powerful entity--presented in the words of logicians, is not supported by śruti. The jīva is a conscious particle which is endowed with qualities and which has no beginning and end, and the Lord is the greatest form of happiness and knowledge endowed with qualities without beginning and end. \|\|35\|\|**

### Text 36

*tathā “kiṁ bhadram” ityādāv apy asau sva-śaktimayasya dvaita-praprañcasya mithātvaṁ āheti śaṅkitum apy anucitam—“ya eko varṇo bahudhā śakti-yogād varṇananekān nihitārtho dadhati” ityād-śruti-vyākopāt, ṣaḍ-viṁśati-pakṣasya svayam evāṅgīkārāt, etasmād upadeśāt prāg ūrddhaṁ ca tasya svātmakatvāpādānāc ca upadeṣṭṛtvāsambhavāc ca \|\|36\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(refutation of text 4)**

**It is wrong to conclude that the verses starting with** ***kim bhadram*****48 present the idea that the world made of dualities is false. This would contradict the śruti:**

**ya eko varṇo bahudhā śakti-yogād varṇān anekān nihitārtho dadhati**

**The one Lord with his own purpose, by his** ***śakti*** **produces many forms of great variety.**

**Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.1**

**The Lord himself accepts twenty-six tattvas. Furthermore, in that section, previous and later, the Lord accepts the creation as himself, and he could not even teach these instructions if the world and himself were false. \|\|36\|\|**

### Text 37

*prāg yathā “viśvam ekātmakām paśyan prakṛtyā puruṣeṇa ca” iti \| ūrddhaṁ yatha “jñānaṁ viveko nigamas tapaś ca pratyakṣam aitihyam athanumānan \| ādhyantayor asya yad eva kevalaṁ kālaś ca hetuś ca tad eva madhye \|\| yaṭha niraṇyam sukrṭaṁ purastāt paścāc ca sarvasya hiraṇmayaysa \| tad eva madhye vyavaharyamāṇaṁ nānāpadeśair aham asya tadvaṁ” iti \|\| 37\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Previously it was said:**

**para-svabhāva-karmāṇi na praśaṁsen na garhayet**  
**viśvam ekātmakaṁ paśyan prakṛtyā puruṣeṇa ca**

**One should neither praise nor criticize the conditioned nature and activities of other persons. Rather, one should see this world along with** ***prakṛti*** **and the** ***jīva*** **as one with the Lord. SB 11.28.1**

**Later it is said:**

**jñānaṁ viveko nigamas tapaś ca**  
**pratyakṣam aitihyam athānumānam**  
**ādy-antayor asya yad eva kevalaṁ**  
**kālaś ca hetuś ca tad eva madhye**

***Jñāna*** **gives power of discrimination. It is produced by knowledge of the Vedas, by following one’s** ***dharma*****, by personal realization, by instructions and by logical analysis. By this one realizes Brahman, the final cause, which reveals everything and which exists at the beginning and end of the universe, and during the interim as well.**

**yathā hiraṇyaṁ sv-akṛtaṁ purastāt**  
**paścāc ca sarvasya hiraṇ-mayasya**  
**tad eva madhye vyavahāryamāṇaṁ**  
**nānāpadeśair aham asya tadvat**

**Gold alone is present before its manufacture into gold products, the gold alone remains after the products’ destruction, and the gold alone is the essential reality while it is utilized under various designations. Similarly, I alone exist before the creation of this universe, after its destruction and during its maintenance. SB 11.28.18-19 \|\|37\|\|**

### Text 38

*kiṁ ca bhrāntaṁ brahmaiva jagad iti tanḍula-pākavat saṁyuktam eva dvaita-mato nityam abadhitam iti śukti-rajata-vad āropitatvād asatyaṁ tad iti ca avāstavatvopadeśānte svayam evan nirākariṣyate—“etāvānātma-saṁmoho yad vikalpas tu kevale \| ātman ṛte svamātmānam avalambo na yasya hi \|\| yan nāmākṛti bhir grāyaṁ pañca-varṇam abādhitam \| vyarthenāpy artha-vado ‘yaṁ dvayaṁ paṇitam āninām” iti \| vikalpa-bhramaḥ \|\|38\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**They claim that Brahman in illusion becomes the universe. Becoming the jīva, uniting with prakṛti like cooked rice, Brahman as the jīva develops duality and is eternally in this state, unimpeded. But it is false, like imposing the idea of silver on a piece of shell. After receiving instructions that the world is false, the Lord himself refutes the idea of a false world.**

**etāvān ātma-saṁmoho yad vikalpas tu kevale \|**  
**ātmany ṛte svam ātmānam avalambo na yasya hi \|\|**  
**yan nāmākṛtibhir grāhyaṁ pañca-varṇam abādhitam \|**  
**vyarthenāpy artha-vado ‘yaṁ dvayaṁ paṇdita-māninām \|\|**

**The jīva transformed by ignorance and bewildered in this world has no shelter except the supreme Lord. The material world known by names and forms is real and distinct from the Lord. Persons who think they are learned argue about this uselessly. SB 11.28.36-37 \|\|38\|\|**

### Text 39

*tasmād atrāyam arthaḥ--pūrvātra prāpti-hetu-bhutāsya bhaktir upadiṣṭā \| tat-pratikūlayoḥ para-nindāpraśaṁsayor vinaiva tasya vikāritvaṁ pāratantryaṁ copadarśayati kim iti \| avastunaḥ pariṇāminaḥ \|\|39\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Therefore the meaning is this. Bhakti which causes attainment of the Lord was taught. Then the Lord shows that matter is transformed and dependent on the Lord. There is no need to criticize or praise it. The meaning of** ***avastu*** **is simply something subject to transformation or something temporary (rather than unreal). \|\|39\|\|**

### Text 40

*“yat tu kālānareṇāpi nānya-saṁjñām upaiti vai \| pariṇāmādi-saṁbhūtaṁ tad-vastu nṛpa tac ca kim \|\| anāśī paramārthaś ca prājñair abhyupagamyate \| tat tu nāśī na sandeho nāśi-dravyopapāditaṁ” iti smṛteḥ \| tac ca dvaitam anṛtaṁ na ṛtaṁ satyaṁ priyaṁ vākyaṁ yasmin tat prayak paratā-pratikūla-karkaśa-kapaṭa-vākyavad ith arthaḥ tādṛśaṁ vācā manasā gocarībhūtaṁ sad avagamyata ity aha –vāceti \| sphuṭārthas tu na sambhavet “yat kiṁcit manasā grāhyaṁ yad grāhyaṁ cakṣurādibhiḥ \| buddhyā ca yat paricchinnaṁ tad bhūyam akhilaṁ tava “ it smṛteḥ \| tasmād alam tat praśaṁṣayeti \| asvatantraṁ ca dvaitam \| na tasmāt kiṁcit paramārtha-rūpaṁ phalaṁ kindv anuṣaṅgād apumarthaṁ janayaty evetyāha –chāyeti, asanto ‘svatantrāḥ –“satyaṁ sātantryam uddisṭaṁ tac ca kṛṣṇe ne cāpare asvātantryāt tad anyeṣām asatyaṁ viddhi bhārata” iti smaraṇāt, tasmād alaṁ tan nindayeti \|\|40\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**yat tu kālānareṇāpi nānya-saṁjñām upaiti vai \|**  
**pariṇāmādi-saṁbhūtaṁ tad-vastu nṛpa tac ca kim \|\|**  
**anāśī paramārthaś ca prājñair abhyupagamyate \|**  
**tat tu nāśī na sandeho nāśi-dravyopapāditaṁ**

**O king! What is that thing which is subjected to change (parināma), that has no other name at other times? The wise call the supreme entity unchanging. The changing substance is without doubt called destructible substance. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

**The world is which dvaita (different from the Lord) is not false (*****anṛtam*****), but true. The word** ***anṛtam*** **is used to indicate that the world is not truthful and affectionate, just as rough, deceptive words may be described as** ***anṛtam (vācoditaṁ anṛtam*** **SB 11.28.4*****).*** **That world can be understood by words and mind. The meaning “nonexistent” is not possible.**

**yat kiṁcit manasā grāhyaṁ yad grāhyaṁ cakṣurādibhiḥ \|**  
**buddhyā ca yat paricchinnaṁ tad bhūyam akhilaṁ tava**

**Whatever is received by the mind, whatever is perceived by the eye, and whatever is discerned by the intelligence is all yours. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

**What is the use of praising this? What is dual (matter) is dependent. It is does not give any spiritual result, and some unsatisfying results. This is the meaning of** ***chāya.*** **(SB 11.28.5) The word** ***asantaḥ*** **means not independent. (SB 11.28.5)**

**satyaṁ svātantryam uddisṭaṁ tac ca kṛṣṇe ne cāpare**  
**asvātantryāt tad anyeṣām asatyaṁ viddhi bhārata**

**What is really existent (*****satyam*****) is independent. This applies to Kṛṣṇa and no one else. O Bhārata! Know that everything else is non-existent (*****asatyam)*** **in the sense that it is not independent. Mahābhārata**

**What use is there in criticizing this? \|\|40\|\|**

### Text 41

*asvātantryaṁ ca tasyeśvara-śakti-mayatvād iti yojayatyātmeti sārddhena, prabhus tat-tac-cakti-yogāt samarthaḥ \| tarhi vikārāpattiḥ syān nety āha, anyasmād iti \| sraṣṭṛ-sṛjyādi-bhāvam āpanne ‘pi tasmin avicintya-svarūpa-mahimnā kathamcit api vikāro nety anyatvaṁ tasyety arthaḥ \| idaṁ khalu madīyaṁ mataṁ yā tv abhīkṣnaṁ sāttvikādi-rūpādhyātmādi-rūpā vā trividhā bhātir ātmani jīve sva-karma-mātra-hetukaivājñāir nirūpitā, sā tu nirmūlaiva; nātra kiṁcit pramāṇam astīty arthaḥ, īśvara-śakti-mayatvaṁ tasya sphūtayatei, idaṁ guṇamayam iti \| etad vidvān iti, para-stuti-nindābhyāṁ bhakti-prābalya-vikṣater visphuṭaiva tad-abhāvād bhakti-tejaḥ parivṛddheś ca sūryavad iti \| pratyakṣeṇeti \| mad-adhīnotpatti-bhaṅgam idaṁ jagad-asvatantram iti pratyakṣādibhir avagatya tatrāsakteś cared iti –naitādṛśasya kvacid api tad-vikṣatir ity arthaḥ \| evaṁ cāmūlam etad ity atrāprāśastye nañ, akeśa-veṇītivat, īśa-vaimukyaṁ khalu manaḥ prabhṛter mūlaṁ tac cāpraśastam eva \| itham eva paro ‘pi sandarbho jīveśvarayoh svarūpa-nirūpaṇa-paratayā pareṇa prapatti-hetuka-mokṣa-paratayā ca vyākhyeyaḥ \|\|41\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(refutation of text 5)**

**The material world is dependent, because it is the śakti of the Lord. This is the meaning of verse 6. He creates the world by his śakti and thus is not transformed by the creation. (verse 7) Though he creates the universe, he does not undergo transformation by the power of his inconceivable form. He is different from the world (*****anyasmāt*****). This is my opinion. The appearance of three guṇas or the three types of suffering are determined by the ignorant to be caused by karma in the jīva. But that proposal is baseless. This means that the proposal has no basis in scriptures. Actually the three guṇas manifest through the śakti of the Lord. “Know that the universe made of** ***guṇas*** **and three types of suffering are created by my inconceivable energy.” (SB 11.28.7)**

**The intelligent person (text 8) does not indulge in criticism or praise of anyone since that will harm the strength of his bhakti. Without criticism or praise, his bhakti increases. Like the sun (*****sūrya-vat*****), he wanders in the world. One should wander about unattached, understanding by pratyākṣa etc. (*****etad-vidvān*****) that the universe is dependent on the Lord, being created and destroyed by him. He is not affected by the world. It is without basis and thus not worthy of praise, like a braid devoid of hair. The basis of the jīva’s mind is opposition to the Lord. That also is not to be praised. Later scriptural references will be given to define the jīva and the Lord, and surrender as a cause of liberation. \|\|41\|**

### Text 42

*nanu māyāmaya-māyā-kṛtāsac chabda-prayogāt kuhaka-racitavad avastu etaj-jagad iti cet bhrāntam idaṁ; tathātve—“kavir manīṣī paribhuḥ svaymbhur yathātathyato ’rthān vyavadhāc śāṣvatībhañ samābhyaḥ” iti śruteḥ “brahma satyaṁ tapaḥ satyaṁ satyaṁ caiva prajāpatiḥ \| satyād bhūtāni jatāni satyaṁ bhūta-mayaṁ jagat \|\| tad etad akṣayaṁ nityaṁ jagan muni-varākhilam \| āvirbhāva-tirobhāva-janma-nāśa-vikalpavat” iti smṛteś ca vyākopāpattiḥ kuhaka-racitam api na mṛṣā kuhakena deśāntarād ānīya satyasyaiva darśitatvāt kvacit tadānīṁ tasyāpi sthiteś ca \|\|42\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is wrong to say that the world is false, insubstantial, a deception, simply because the word** ***asat*** **is used to describe it. This would contradict both śruti and smṛti statements.**

**kavir manīṣī paribhuḥ svaymbhur yathātathyato ’rthān vyavadhāc śāśvatībhañ samābhyaḥ**

**The Lord, who is omniscient, wise, the controller of all beings and independent, has established all real objects eternally. Īśopaniṣad**

**brahma satyaṁ tapaḥ satyaṁ satyaṁ caiva prajāpatiḥ \|**  
**satyād bhūtāni jatāni satyaṁ bhūta-mayaṁ jagat \|\|**  
**tad etad akṣayaṁ nityaṁ jagan muni-varākhilam \|**  
**āvirbhāva-tirobhāva-janma-nāśa-vikalpavat**

**Brahmā is existing. Austerity is existing. Truth is existing. Prajāpati is existing. From the Lord came all beings. The material worlds is existing. The Lord is eternal. He is the whole universe. O best of the sages! He is the whole universe. He possesses appearance, disappearance as the Lord and birth and death (for embodied jīvas). Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

**Though the world is deceptive (*****kuhataka)*****, it is not an illusion. A deceptive object may be brought from another place and it is seen to be real. It may also remain at that place. \|\|42\|\|**

### Text 43

*satyeśvara-śakti-mayatvāj jagat satyam \| jīva-prakṛtyoś ca satyatvam ajāmantrāt parasmād vaiṣṇava-vākyoccayāc ca vyaktam \| jīva-prakṛtyor īśvara-śaktitvam tu “bhūmir āpo ‘nalo vāyuḥ khaṁ mano buddhir eva ca \| ahaṅkāra itīyam me bhinnā praṛktir aṣṭadhā \|\|apareyam itas tv anyaṁ prakṛtiṁ viddhi me parām \| jīva-bhutāṁ mahābāho yayedaṁ dhāryate jagat” \|\| it smṛteḥ \| jagatas tat-kāryatvam tu “ya eko ‘varṇo bahudhā śakti-yogāt” ityādi śruteḥ “mama yonir mahad brahma tasmin garbhāṁ dadhāmy aham” sambhavaḥ sarva-bhūtānaṁ tato bhavati bhārata” “eka-deśasthitasyāgner jyotsnā-vistāriṇī yathā \| parasya brahmaṇḥ śaktis tathedam akhilam jagat” iti smrṭeś ca \| etena janma-bhaṅgānyathānupapattyā jagan-mithyā brahmavat satyatvena tad anupapatter iti pratyuktam \| janmādikaṁ hy anityatva-vyāpyaṁ satyatvaṁ nityānityā-sādhāraṇam ataḥ satyam anityaṁ jagat, “anityam asukhaṁ lokam” iti bhagavat-kaṇṭhokteś ca \| trikāla-bādhyaṁ tu mithyā yathā kha-puṣpādi \|\|43\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since the universe is made of the Lord’s real śakti, the universe is real. Jīva and prakṛti are real. This is clear from the śruti and plenty of Vaiṣṇava statements:**

**ajām ekāṁ lohita-śukla-kṛṣṇāṁ**  
**bahvīḥ prajāḥ sṛjamānāṁ sarūpāḥ**

**One unborn entity, satisfied, follows another unborn entity--a female entity who is red, white and black and who produces many offspring of similar nature Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.5**

**The jīva and prakṛti are śaktis of the Lord:**

**bhūmir āpo ‘nalo vāyuḥ khaṁ mano buddhir eva ca \|**  
**ahaṅkāra itīyam me bhinnā praṛktir aṣṭadhā \|**

**Earth, water, fire, air, ether (as well as their sense objects),** ***pradhāna*****,** ***mahat-tattva*****, and false ego (with the ten senses) are my separated energy in eight divisions.**

**apareyam itas tv anyaṁ prakṛtiṁ viddhi me parām \|**  
**jīva-bhutāṁ mahābāho yayedaṁ dhāryate jagat**

**This is my inferior energy. But understand my superior energy which is different from this inferior energy. It is the** ***jīvas*****, who employ the inferior energy for their enjoyment. BG 7.4-5**

**The universe is a product of that śakti. Śruti says:**

**ya eko ‘varṇo bahudhā śakti-yogāt**

**The one became many by means of** ***śakti.*** **Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.1**

**mama yonir mahad brahma tasmin garbhāṁ dadāmy aham**  
**sambhavaḥ sarva-bhūtānaṁ tato bhavati bhārata**

**The great expanse of** ***prakṛti*** **is my womb. In that womb, I place the seed of all** ***jīvas*****. The birth of all beings comes from that, O descendent of Bharata. BG 14.3**

**eka-deśa-sthitasyāgner jyotsnā-vistāriṇī yathā \|**  
**parasya brahmaṇaḥ śaktis tathedam akhilam jagat**

**Just as the light of a fire situated in one place spreads its illumination, the śakti of the supreme Brahman spreads out as the whole universe. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.22.55)**

**This demolishes the claim that the world is false and only Brahman is real, by showing that the creation and destruction of the worlds is real.**

**The universe, which have creation and destruction, is definitely temporary. A real object can be either temporary or eternal. The universe, thought temporary, is real. The Lord himself says:**

**anityam asukhaṁ lokam**

**This world is temporary and filled with suffering. BG 9.33**

**What is nonexistent in past, present and future is false, like a flower in the sky. \|\|43\|\|**

### Text 44

*paramārthas tvam evaiko nānyo sti ity atra na kadācid anīdṛṣaṁ jagad ity evam abhidhāyibhir nitya-svatantryatayā niścita-prapañcasya niṣedhaḥ, na tu brahmātmakasyāpi tasya tad-grahaṇenaiva gṛhītatatvāt \| itarathā jagat-patitvokti-vyākopaḥ \| brahmādhīnas tu iti darśayati—taveti \| itarathā mahimno bādhitatvāt stavana-virodhāpattiḥ \| tad etad iti – jñānātmakasya tavaitad jagat tvat-sambandhi tvac-cakti-mayatvād ityarthaḥ \| ye tv ayogina eva jñāna-yoga-śūnyāḥ, te deva-manusyādi-rūpaṁ jagat paratantram idaṁ na tvat-sambandhīti bhrāntyaiva paśyanti, na ca teṣāṁ saṁsāra-nivṛttir ityāha—jñāna-svarūpam iti \| jñānaṁ brahmaiva svarūpaṁ vṛtti-pradaṁ yasya tathābhūtam idam jagat paratantram ity arthaḥ \| tac cārtha-svarūpaṁ phala-rūpaṁ svatantraṁ nityam iti yāvat \| abuddhayaḥ kecit karma-jaḍā evaṁ vāda-ratā ity arthaḥ \| ye tv iti jñānaṁ śāstraṁ tad-vidaḥ \| jñānaṁ brahmaivātmā pravṛtti-kṛd yasya tat \| etad evāha—tvad-rūpam iti rūpa-padaṁ khalv asvarūpaṁ dṛṣṭaṁ prāṇa-saṁvādādiṣu \|\|44\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Refuting text 6)**

**Previously the opponent quoted Viṣṇu Purāṇa to support his view of oneness:**

**paramārthas tvam evaiko nānyo ‘sti jagataḥ pate \|**  
**tavaiśa mahimā yena vyāptam etac carācaram \|\|**

**O master of the universe! O Lord! You are the supreme goal. There is no other. Your greatness is spread among all moving and nonmoving beings.**

**“There is no other” indicates a denial that the universe is eternal and independent (separate from Brahman), which is proposed by the karma-mīmāṁsakas. The universe is also not different from the Lord, since if one accepts that Brahman is real, one should accept what is non-different from Brahman as real. If the reality and dependence of the universe on the Lord is not accepted, the words “Lord of the universe (*****jagataḥ pate*****)” in the verse would have no meaning. The universe is thus real and dependent on Brahman. The word** ***tava*** **(your) in the above verse indicates this, since to propose otherwise would nullify the praise of the Lord by nullifying his greatness (*****mahimā*****).**

**yad etad dṛśyate mūrtam etaj jñānātmanas tava \|**  
**bhrānti-jñānena paśyanti jagad rupam ayoginaḥ \|**

**Out of mistaken knowledge, those who are not yogīs accept the form of the universe which is visible to be your form, though you are composed of knowledge. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.4.39**

**The universe is yours (*****jñāntāmanas tava*****). This indicates that the universe is related to the Lord, since it is his śakti. Persons without jñāna-yoga (*****ayoginaḥ*****) mistakenly think that the universe with devatās and humans is not related to the Lord.**

**jñāna-svarūpam akhilaṁ jagad etad abuddhayaḥ \|**  
**artha-svarūpaṁ paśyanto bhrāmyante moha-saṁplave \|\|**

**The universe is a form of knowledge. Foolish people see the universe as a material entity and are bewildered by a flood of illusion**

**For them there is no end to saṁsāra. That is expressed in the above verse. The universe operates by the power of Brahman (jñāna). It is dependent on the Lord. The unintelligent, those involved in material karmas and debating, see the universe as the goal, independent and eternal.**

**ye tu jñāna-vidaḥ śuddhās te paśyanty akhilaṁ jagat \|**  
**jñānātmakaṁ prapaśyanti tvad rūpaṁ parameśara**

**O supreme Lord! Those in knowledge who are pure, see the whole universe as knowledge and see your form everywhere.**

**Those who know scriptures (*****jñāna-vidaḥ*****) see the universe to be operating by power of the Lord (*****jñānātmakam*****). It is your form (*****tvad-rūpam*****). The word form (*****rūpam*****) indicates that others do not see the true nature of the universe (thinking it independent and eternal). \|\|44\|\|**

### Text 45

*ye tv anyathā vyācakṣate, teṣāṁ pūrvapara-grantha-virodhāś ca \| tathāhi pūrvatra hi maitreya-parāśarayor evaṁ praśnottare dṛṣṭe—“nirguṇasyāprameyasya śuddhasyāpy amalātmanaḥ \| kathaṁ svargādikartṛtvaṁ brahmano ‘bhyupagamyate” iti praśṇaḥ \| sattvādi-guṇa-yogiṣu karmādhīneṣv apūrṇeṣūtpādanādi-kāryaṁ dṛṣṭaṁ brahmaṇi tu tad-vilakṣaṇe kathaṁ tad aṅgīkāryam iti tasyārthaḥ \| tatrottaraṁ ca “śaktayaḥ sarva-bhāvānām acintya-jñāna-gocarāḥ \| yato’to brahmaṇas tās tu sargādyā bhāva-śaktayaḥ \| bhavanti tapasāṁ śreṣṭha pāvakasya yatoṣṇatā \|\|” atrāyaṁ arthaḥ –sarveṣāṁ bhāvānām evācintya-buddhi-bodhyā mitho vilakṣaṇāḥ kārya-kalpyāḥ śaktayo yasmāt pratīyante, ataḥ sarva-bṛhattamasya sarvānugrāhakasya brahmaṇas tathābhūtāḥ sargādyā bhāva-śaktayo bhavantīti na tasya tat-kartṛtva-virodhaḥ \| tāḥ svābhāvikya evety āha—pāvakasya yatheti \| “parāsya śaktiḥ” ityādyā śrutiś ca \| tu-śabdaḥ “kaimutyaṁ” dyotayati \| iha vicitra-śaktimad brahmeti suvyaktam \|\|45\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**To explain the statement any other way would be contrary to the previous and later statements of Viṣṇu Purāṇa. Previously Maitreya asks a question and Parāśara replies. The question is:**

**nirguṇasyāprameyasya śuddhasyāpy amalātmanaḥ \|**  
**kathaṁ svargādikartṛtvaṁ brahmano ‘bhyupagamyate \|\|**

**How can one understand that Brahman, which is without qualities, immeasurable, pure and uncontaminated, creates Svarga and other places?**

**How can one accept that activates of creation of things which are endowed with guṇas, dependent on karma and imperfect, exist in Brahman which is completely different? Parāśara answers:**

***śaktayaḥ sarva-bhāvānām acintya-jñāna-gocarāḥ \|***  
***yato’to brahmaṇas tās tu sargādyā bhāva-śaktayaḥ \|***  
***bhavanti tapasāṁ śreṣṭha pāvakasya yatoṣṇatā \|\|***

**O best of ascetics! The śaktis of things in this world are in the realm of inconceivability. From these arise the powers of Brahman to create the universe. It is like the power of heat in fire. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.3.2)**

**The meaning is this. Since the śaktis (ability to produce certain effects) of things in this world cannot be understood by intelligence, the Lord, who is the greatest and most merciful (*****brahmaṇaḥ*****) must have śaktis to create Svarga and other things. There is no problem if he creates Svarga. These śaktis are natural to the Lord, just like heat is natural to fire. Śruti says** ***parāsya śaktiḥ*****: the Lord has śakti. The word** ***tu*** **indicates kaimutya: how much more the Lord can do. Thus it is clear that Brahman (the Lord) possesses inconceivable śaktis. \|\|45\|\|**

### Text 46

*evam eva paratrāpi dṛśyate, yatha “śuddhe mahā-vibhūtyākhye pare brahmaṇi śabdyate \| maitreya bhagavac chabdaḥ sarva-kāraṇa-kāraṇe \|\| saṁbharteti tathā bhartā bha-bhāro ‘rtha-dvayāṇvitaḥ \| netā gamayitā sraṣṭa ga-kārārthas tathā mune aiśvaryasya samagrasya vīryasya yaśasaḥ śriyaḥ \| jñāna-vairāgyayoś caiva ṣaṇṇāṁ bhaga itīṅganā \|\| vasanti tatra bhūtāni bhūtātmany akhilātmani \| sa ca bhūteṣv aśeṣeṣu vakārārthas tato’vyayaḥ \|\|jñāna-śakti-balaiśvarya-vīrya-tejāṁsy aśeṣataḥ \| bhagavac-chabda-vācyāni vinā heyair guṇādibhiḥ \|\| evam eṣa mahā-śabdho bhagvān iti sattamaḥ \| parama-brahma-bhūtasya vāsudevasay nānyagaḥ \|\| tatra pūya-padārthokti-paribhāṣāsamanvitaḥ \| ābdo ‘yaṁ nopacāreṇa anyatra hy upacārataḥ\|\|” \|\|46\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Later the following is also seen in Viṣṇu Purāṇa.**

**śuddhe mahā-vibhūtyākhye pare brahmaṇi śabdyate \|**  
**maitreya bhagavac chabdaḥ sarva-kāraṇa-kāraṇe \|\|**  
**saṁbharteti tathā bhartā bha-bhāro ‘rtha-dvayāṇvitaḥ \|**  
**netā gamayitā sraṣṭa ga-kārārthas tathā mune \|\|**  
**aiśvaryasya samagrasya vīryasya yaśasaḥ śriyaḥ \|**  
**jñāna-vairāgyayoś caiva ṣaṇṇāṁ bhaga itīṅganā \|\|**  
**vasanti tatra bhūtāni bhūtātmany akhilātmani \|**  
**sa ca bhūteṣv aśeṣeṣu vakārārthas tato’vyayaḥ \|\|**  
**jñāna-śakti-balaiśvarya-vīrya-tejāṁsy aśeṣataḥ \|**  
**bhagavac-chabda-vācyāni vinā heyair guṇādibhiḥ \|\|**  
**evam eṣa mahā-śabdo bhagavān iti sattamaḥ \|**  
**parama-brahma-bhūtasya vāsudevasya nānya-gaḥ \|\|**  
**tatra pūjya-padārthokti-paribhāṣā-samanvitaḥ \|**  
**śābdo ‘yaṁ nopacāreṇa anyatra hy upacārataḥ \|\|**

**O Maitreya! The word** ***bhagavān*** **refers to the supreme Brahman, also called the greatly powerful, who is the cause of all causes. The syllable** ***bha*** **means the bestower and the maintainer. The syllable** ***ga*** **means the giver of prema, creator of qualities in the devotee and the cause of going to Vaikuṇṭha. The six** ***bhagas*** **are complete control, influence, good qualities, wealth, knowledge and renunciation. The syllable** ***va*** **means the indestructible Lord in whom all beings dwell and who dwells in all beings. The word** ***bhagavān*** **means that he is complete with power of mind (omniscience), power of senses, power of body, influence, control, and beauty, and is devoid of all inferior qualities. O best of devotees! The great word** ***bhagavān*** **means only the supreme Brahman Vāsudeva. It indicates the object most worthy of worship. The word is used for Viṣṇu in a primary sense. For others like devatās, the word is used in a secondary sense only. \|\|46\|\|**

### Text 47

*“sa sarva-būta-prakṛti-vikāra-guṇāṁś ca doṣāṁś ca mune vyatītaḥ \| atīta-sarvāvaraṇo khilātmā tenāstrṭaṁ yad bhuvanāntarāle \|\| samasta-kalyāṇa-guṇātmako’sau sva-śakti-leśāvṛta-bhūta-vargaḥ \| icchā-gṛhītābhimatoru-dehaḥ saṁsādhitāśeṣa-jagad-dhito yaḥ \|\| tejo-balaiśvarya-mahā-bodha- suvīrya-śakty-ādi-guṇaika-rāśiḥ \| paraḥ parāṇāṁ sakalā na yatra kleśādayaḥ santi parāvareśaḥ \|\| sa īśvaro vyaṣṭi-samaṣṭi-rūpo vyakta-svarūpo’prakaṭa-svarūpaḥ \| sarveśvaraḥ sarva-dṛk sarva-vic ca samasta-śaktiḥ parameśvarākhyaḥ \|\| sa jñāyate yena tad-asta-doṣaṁ śuddhaṁ paraṁ nirmalam eka-rūpam \| saṁdṛśyante vāpy avagamyate vā taj-jñānam ajñānam ato’nyad uktam \|\|” \|\|47\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**He has a wealth of beauty, strength, power, intelligence, courage and śakti. He is supreme among superior entities. He is the lord of superior and inferior creatures. In him there are no types of suffering. The pure, supreme, faultless form, devoid of imperfections, by which he is known, seen and attained, is called knowledge and all else is ignorance. \|\|47\|\|**

### Text 48

*atra hi prāṇinām abhyudaya-niḥśṛeyasa-prado nirasta-samasta-doṣa aiśvarya-vīryādi-viśuddha-guṇa-rāśiś cid-acit-padārthānāṁ svetareṣāṁ vṛtti-hetutvādinā tad-rūpas teṣām utpattyadikārī śāstriya-jñāna-gocaro bhagavān iti nirūpaṇān na nirviśeṣa-cinmātraṁ brahmeti vibhudyate \| evam anyac ca draṣṭavyam \|\|48\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Bhagavān has been defined as the person who bestows happiness and the highest good to all beings, who is a reservoir of pure qualities like power and ability, who is the form all conscious and unconscious entities since he is their cause, who is their very cause, and who is understood by scriptural knowledge. He is not consciousness alone with no qualities. One can see this conclusion elsewhere as well. \|\|48\|\|**

### Text 49

*kṣetra-jñam cāpi māṁ viddhīti ca-kāraḥ kṣetra-jñaṁ samuccinoti \| apir avadhāraṇe \| kṣetraṁ kṣtra-jñām ca mām eva viddhi mad-adhīna-vṛrtikatvādinā mad-ātmakaṁ jānīhīty arthaḥ \| evam evānuktam—“ksetra-ksetra-jñayor jñānaṁ yat tad-jñānam mataṁ mama” iti \| tayor mad-adhīna-vṛttikatvādi-visāyatayā yad jñānaṁ tat jñānaṁ mama matam ity artha \| etenaiva “na tad asti vinā yat syāt” ity api vyākhyātam \|\|49\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(refutation of text 70)**

**The word ca indicates “all else”. The word** ***api*** **is for emphasis. The meaning is “Know about the field and the knower of the field which are filled with my presence (*****mām*****), since they are dependent on me.” Then it is said “That is knowledge which understands that the kṣetra and the kṣetra-jñā are dependent on me.” This also explains the statement (quoted by the opponent)** ***na tad asti vinā yat syān*****: no moving or non-moving entity can exist without me. (BG 10.39) \|\|49\|\|**

### Text 50

*nanu kaivalyopaniṣadi śruyate—“sa eva māyāparimohitātmā śarīram āsthyāya karoti sarvam \| striyanna-pānādi-vicitra-bhogaiḥ sa eva jāgrat parituṣṭim eti \|\| svapne sa jīvaḥ sukha-duḥkha-bhoktā sva-māyayā kalpita-viśva-loke \| susupit-kāle sakale vilīne tamo ‘bhibhūtaḥ sukha-rūpam eti \|\| punaś ca janmāntara-karma-yogāt sa eva jīvaḥ svapiti prabuddhaḥ \| puru-traye krīḍati yaś ca jīvas tatas tu jātaṁ sakalaṁ vicitraṁ \| ādhāram ānandam akhaṇḍa-bodham yasmin layaṁ yāti pura-trayaṁ ca \|\| etasmāj jāyate prāṇo manaḥ sarvendriyāṇi ca \| khaṁ vāyur-jyotir āpaś ca pṛthivī viṣvasya dhāriṇī \|\| yat parām brahma sarvātmā viśvasyāyatanaṁ mahat \| sūkṣmāt sūkṣmataraṁ nityaṁ satyam eva tvam evan tat \| jāgrat-svapna-suṣuptyādi praprañcaṁ yat prakāśate \| tad brahmāham iti jñātvā sarva-bandhaiḥ pramucyate” \|\|50\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In the Kaivalya Upaniṣad it is said:**

**sa eva māyāparimohitātmā śarīram āsthyāya karoti sarvam**  
**striyanna-pānādi-vicitra-bhogaiḥ sa eva jāgrat parituṣṭim eti \|\|**  
**svapne sa jīvaḥ sukha-duḥkha-bhoktā sva-māyayā kalpita-viśva-loke \|**  
**susupti-kāle sakale vilīne tamo ‘bhibhūtaḥ sukha-rūpam eti \|\|**

**Paramātmā, bewildered by māyā, becomes situated in a body and causes creation. In waking state he is satisfied with women, food and drink. In dream state, the jīva is the experiencer of happiness and distress in the world created by his māyā. In deep sleep the jīva is overcome with ignorance and enjoys nicely with everything dissolved.**

**punaś ca janmāntara-karma-yogāt sa eva jīvaḥ svapiti prabuddhaḥ \|**  
**puru-traye krīḍati yaś ca jīvas tatas tu jātaṁ sakalaṁ vicitraṁ \|**  
**ādhāram ānandam akhaṇḍa-bodham yasmin layaṁ yāti pura-trayaṁ ca \|\|**

**The jīva again wakes up and enjoys by the karma from previous lifetimes. Becoming the jīva, Paramātmā enjoys in these three states. From īśvara arises all beings of the universe.**

**Brahman which is the support, which is bliss and limited consciousness, dissolves into himself these three states.**

**etasmāj jāyate prāṇo manaḥ sarvendriyāṇi ca \|**  
**khaṁ vāyur-jyotir āpaś ca pṛthivī viṣvasya dhāriṇī \|\|**

**From Hiraṇyagarbha or īśvara arises all the senses, the mind and prāṇas, ether, air, fire, water and earth which support the universe.**

**yat paraṁ brahma sarvātmā viśvasyāyatanaṁ mahat \|**  
**sūkṣmāt sūkṣmataraṁ nityaṁ satyam eva tvam evan tat \|**  
**jāgrat-svapna-suṣuptyādi praprañcaṁ yat prakāśate \|**  
**tad brahmāham iti jñātvā sarva-bandhaiḥ pramucyate**

**Knowing supreme Brahman which is the ātmā of all things, the great shelter of the universe, which is subtler than the most subtle, which exists eternally, which is you, which is manifested as the universe with the state so waking, dream and deep sleep, one becomes free from all bondage. \|\|50\|**

### Text 51

*tasmād ittham atra vyakhyeyam—pareśyaiva sato ‘vidyayaiva kṣetra-jña-bhāvaḥ \| rajjor iva bhujaṅgatvaṁ tan-nivṛtty-artham āptatamasya bhagavato ‘yam upadeśaḥ “kṣetra-jñaṁ cāpi maṁ viddhi” iti \| “rajjur iyaṁ na bhugaṅgaḥ” ityāptopadeśād bhujaṅga-bhrāntir iva kṣetra-jña-bhrāntir asmād vākyān nivartata iti cen mandam etat upadeśāsambhavāt \| tatha hi –ayam upadeṣṭā bhagavāṁs tattva-jño na vā? Ādye ‘dvitīya-jñāna-mātraṁ vijānatas tasya nārjunādi-bheda-dṛṣṭir iti na tān praty upadeśaḥ sambhavatī; antye ‘py ajñatvād eva nāma jñānopadeśitvaṁ \| nāpi sarveśvarasyāvidyāsti “sa eva māyā” vedāhaṁ samatītāni iti śrutim āśritya śakyaṁ gaditum—“ya sarva-jñāḥ sarva-vit” “parāsya śaktiḥ ityādi-śruteḥ, “” ityādi- smṛteś ca \|\|51\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“Thus the above quote from the Gītā should be explained as follows. The supreme Lord by ignorance becomes the jīva, like mistaking a rope for a snake. To remove the illusion, the wise Lord teaches, “Know that the kṣetra-jña is I.” Just as, by the instruction “That is a rope, not a snake” the illusion of a snake is destroyed, so the illusion of being jīva is removed by that instruction.”**

**That is a foolish argument since instruction would be impossible. Is the instructor, who is the Lord, in knowledge of truth or not? If he is in knowledge, he would be aware of non-duality alone and would not see difference in the form of Arjuna. And instructions themselves would not be possible. If the Lord is not in knowledge, he would be giving instructions on knowledge when he was in ignorance.**

**It is not possible to say that the Lord is in ignorance by quoting** ***sa eva māyāparimohitātmā*****: the Lord becomes bewildered by māyā (Kaivalya Upaniṣad), because śruti says** ***ya sarva-jñāḥ sarva-vit*****: the Lord has all knowledge. (Muṇḍaka 2.7)** ***Parāsya śaktiḥ*****: the Lord is endowed with śakti. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad) Smṛti says** ***vedāhaṁ samatītāni*****: I know everything. (BG 7.26) \|\|51\|\|**

### Text 52

*athādhigatādvaitasyāpi bhagavatobādhitānuvṛtti-rūpeyaṁ bheda-dṛṣṭir ato nopadeśānupapattir iti cen na, dṛṣṭānta-virodhāt marīcikā-vāri-buddhir vādhitānuvṛttāpi na vāryāraṇādau pravartayaty evam advaita-jñāna-bādhitā bheda-dṛṣṭir anuvṛttāpi mithyārthāvadhāraṇān nāndhopadeśādau pravartayiṣyatīti \|\|52\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The opponent speaks as follows. “The Lord’s seeing with duality, though the Lord is fixed in nonduality, is a case of** ***bādhita anuvṛtti*****, reappearance of what was sublated. Therefore it causes no obstacle for the Lord to teach.”**

**This cannot be, because it is contradictory. When a mirage of water is annulled by knowledge, one does not go to the mirage for water even though it appears again. If the illusion of duality appears again after duality has been sublated by advaita-jñāna, the jñānī should not engage in teaching the ignorant, since he would realize that the vision of duality was an illusion. \|\|52\|\|**

### Text 53

*kiṁca bādhitānuvṛrtir api na śakyā vaktuṁ—bādhakena tādṛśātma-jñānenātmetara-dvaita-jñāna-hetor ajñānader vinaṣṭātvāt \| dvicandra-jñānadau tu candrekatva-jñānena satya-tamistrādi-doṣasya dvicandra-jñāna-hetor avināśāt sā yuktā \| iha tv advitīya-jñānena dvaitasya taj-jñānasya vināśasya ca vināśat kathaṁcid api sā na sambhaved iti tuccham etat \| tasmāt tad-āyatta-vṛttikatvādibhir īśvarābhedo jīvasyehopacaryate \| ataeva jagaj-janmādi-kartṛ-pareśa-gāḍha-sakhyāveśād asya jīvasya vimuktir iti tatraivocyate “yat paraṁ brahma” ityādinā \|\|53\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, one cannot even speak about** ***bādhitānuvṛtti*** **(reemergence of what was destroyed). Ignorance caused by seeing duality has been destroyed by knowledge of ātmā.** ***Bādhitānuvṛtti*** **is applicable to the case where a man continues to see two moons because of a fault in his sight, even though he has been told there is one moon, not two. In the present case however, when knowledge of duality is destroyed by knowledge of oneness, how can the illusion continue, since it has been destroyed? This idea is useless. Nondifference of the jīva and the Lord is metaphorical only, since the jīva is dependent on the Lord. The jīva becomes liberated by deep friendship of the Lord who is the cause of the creation of the universe. That is expressed in the Kaivalya Upaniṣad with the words** ***yat paraṁ brahma*** **etc. \|\|53\|\|**

### Text 54

*yat tu śuddhe caitanye ‘vidyākalpitaṁ viśvaṁ vidyayā nāśyam ity etac caitena nirastam—kalpakānirūpaṇāc ca; na cāsya brahma kalpakaṁ vaiśiṣṭyāpatteḥ nāpi jīvaḥ, kalpanāt prāk tasyeva siddheḥ na cāvidyā jāḍyāt \|\|54\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“By ignorance the world is created. By knowledge the world is destroyed. Pure consciousness remains.” This is refuted by the following. The creator cannot be determined. One cannot say that Brahman is the creator, since that would provide Brahman with qualities. The jīva cannot be the creator, since previous to the creation the jīva did not exist. The creator of the universe cannot be ignorance, since it is unconscious. \|\|54\|\|**

### Text 55

*kiṁca, neyaṁ satyā vaktuṁ śakyā, anivṛtti-prasaṅgāt bhedopapatteś ca; nāpy asatyā, pratīti-virodhāt; na ca sad-asad-vilakṣaṇatvād iṣṭa-siddheḥ, tādṛśi pramāṇābhāvāt; na ca “na sad āsīt” ityādi śrutir atra pramāṇaṁ sad-asac-chabda-vācye cid-acid-vyaṣṭī tayoḥ pralaye pṛthaṅ avasthitir na syād acit-samaṣṭau tamaḥ-śabda-vācyāyāṁ tadānīṁ vilīnatvādity arthābhidhānāt \| tamaḥ śabditā tv acit-samaṣṭhir iti subālopaniṣadi dṛṣtaṁ “bhutādir mahati vilīyate vyaktam akṣare vilīyate ‘ksāram tamasi vilīyate tama ekībhavati parasmin parasmān na sad asat” iti, smṛtiś ca “nāho na ratrir na tamo na bhumir nāsīt tamo jyotir abhuc ca nānyat \| śrotrādi-buddhyānupalabhyam ekaṁ prādhānikaṁ brahma pumāṁs tadāsīt” ity atra prakṛti-brahma-jīvāḥ pralaye tiṣṭhantīti visphuṭam \|\|55\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(refutation of text 8)**

**One cannot say that ignorance is real, since that would mean it was not destroyed and would mean difference still exists. One cannot say that ignorance does not exist, as this contradicts perception (ignorance is seen in the world). Ignorance cannot be something different from existing and non-existing since there is no scriptural authority for such a thing.
