The statement** ***na sad āsīt*** **cannot be used as proof, since** ***sat*** **and** ***asat*** **there refer to conscious and unconscious entities. At final destruction** ***sat*** **and** ***asat*** **will not exist. They all merge into an unconscious conglomerate called** ***tamas.*** **Subala Upaniṣad calls the unconscious entity** ***samaṣṭi- tamas.***

**bhutādir mahati vilīyate vyaktam akṣare vilīyate ‘ksāram tamasi vilīyate tama ekībhavati parasmin parasmān na sad asat**

***Ahaṅkāra*** **merges into** ***mahat-tattva. Mahat-tattva*** **merges into** ***avyakta. Avyakta*** **merges into** ***akṣara. Akṣara*** **merges into** ***tamas. Tamas*** **merges into the Lord. There is nothing gross or subtle or a combination of both that is higher than this. Subala Upaniṣad**

**nāho na ratrir na tamo na bhumir nāsīt tamo jyotir abhuc ca nānyat \|**  
**śrotrādi-buddhyānupalabhyam ekaṁ prādhānikaṁ brahma pumāṁs tadāsīt**

**There was no day or night, no tamas, no earth, no darkness or light. Only pradhāna, the jīva and Brahman, which cannot be perceived by sense organs or intelligence, existed. Subala Upaniṣad**

**It is clear from this that prakṛti, jīva and Brahman exist after final destruction. \|\|55\|\|**

### Text 56

*nanv etasyās tamaḥ śabditācit-samaṣṭeḥ; sūksmāyāḥ “māyan tu prakrtiṁ vidyāt” iti māya-śabdenoker anivācyataṁ? Maivaṁ tasya sadasad-vilakṣaṇārthatvādarśanāt \| na ca tādṛśaḥ kvāpy artho ‘sti yad-vācinā tena bhāvyaṁ; nāpi sarvatra tasya dambha-vācakatayā nānārthatvāt \| tathāhi pathānti—“māyādambhe kṛpāyāṁ ca” iti, “māyāsyāc canvarī buddhyāḥ” iti “ māyā vayunaṁ jñānam” iti ca \| tad-vācyasya mithyātve vedāprāmāṇyān nāstikatāpattir iti vakṣyāmaḥ \| tasmān māyā-śabdenātra vicitra-sarga-karī pārameśvarī śaktir ucyate \| sā tu satyaiva tasyās tathābhūtatvaṁ śrutir darśayati—“asmān māyī sṛjate viśvam etat” iti \|\|56\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“Tamas or the unconscious conglomerate is subtle.** ***Māyaṁ tu prakṛtiṁ vidyāt*****: one should know māyā. Does not the word** ***māyā*** **here mean what is beyond expression of words?”**

**That is not so. Māyā does not refer to something which is neither existent nor nonexistent. Māyā never has that meaning. The word māyā does not always mean cheating.** ***māyādambhe kṛpāyāṁ ca*****: māyā means cheating and mercy.** ***Māyā syāc cāhnvarī buddhyāḥ*****: māyā means deception and knowledge.** ***Māyā vayunaṁ jñānam*****: māyā means knowledge. To claim that everything is illusory is actually nāstika, since it has no proof in the Vedas. Therefore the word** ***māyā*** **refers to a śakti of the Lord, which creates variety in the material world.** ***Māyā*** **is real. The śrutis prove this.**

**asmān māyī sṛjate viśvam etat**

**From** ***pradhāna*** **the Lord created this universe. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.9 \|\|56\|\|**

### Text 57

*yat tūktam iyaṁ punar brahmātmaikya-jñānān nivartate iti, tan-mandam \| kīdṛśaṁ khalu jñānaṁ nivartakam? Na kevalam caitanyam tasya nityatvenāvidyāyā nitya-nivṛtti-prasaṅgāt \| tataś ca tan-mūlaka-saṁsāropalambhānupapatteḥ sarva-śāstrānārambha-prasaṅgo ‘nubhava-virodhāś ca \| nāpi vṛtti-rūpaṁ tasya satyatve dvaitāpattiḥ, mithyātve katham ajñāna-nyavartakatā \| satyasya hi rajjvādi-jñānasya bhujaṅgādi-bhramopādānājñāna-nivartakatvam upalabdham \| na ca nirindha-vahnivat tasya svarūpa-bādha-kāraṇatvam iti vācyaṁ—kāraṇatā-nirvāhi-kāla-sattvenādvaita-hānāt \|\|57\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is foolish to claim that ignorance is destroyed by knowledge of one Brahman. What is this knowledge which destroys ignorance? It cannot be pure consciousness. Being eternal, that pure consciousness eternally has destruction of ignorance. Since ignorance is eternally not present in pure consciousness alone, then there can be no saṁsāra which is based on ignorance. Absence of saṁsāra is contrary to our perception and would make scriptures useless (since they provide methods for destroying saṁsāra).**

**That knowledge which destroys ignorance cannot be Brahman in the form of the antaḥkaraṇa, since one would have to admit existence of antaḥkaraṇa. Thus duality would be established. If one claims that antaḥkaraṇa is illusory, how could it destroy ignorance? By real knowledge of a rope, the illusion of perceiving a snake is destroyed. One also cannot claim that the knowledge which destroys ignorance itself disappears after destroying ignorance, like a fire without wood, for, by admitting the existence of time which produces a cause, nonduality would be destroyed. \|\|57\|\|**

### Text 58

*evaṁ ca svāpnika-strī-saṅgād iva satyasya satya-sukhādi-janakatva-darśanād asatyenaiva śāstrādinā satya-mokṣa-siddhir ity api nirastaṁ – tatra satyasyaiva jñānasya tat-taj-janakatvāt \| itarathā marīcakājalādibhir hariṇa-tṛṣṇādi-nāśāpattiḥ \| nanu rekhāropita-varṇo ‘rthasya śaṅkāv iyaṁ maraṇasya sādhanaṁ dṛṣṭam iti cen na—padasyārthe iva rekhāyā varṇe saṅketitatvena rekhāropita-varṇasya satyasyaivārtha-bodhakatvāt, śaṅkāviṣe ‘pi śaṅkā-nimitta-bhī-janya-dhātu-vyākulatvasya satya-maraṇa-hetutvāt \|\|58\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is claimed that scriptures, though unreal, can produce real liberation, just as associating with a woman in a dream can give real happiness. This is not true, since only real knowledge will produce real happiness. Otherwise, a deer could extinguish its thirst through a mirage of water. “But one sees that accidental lines which form a letter produce meaning, and worry about poison (not the actual poison) can produce death.” That is not so. By indications of lines, one infers real letters which produce meaning. Fear produced from worry causes disruption of the bodily elements and this produces real death. \|\|58\|\|**

### Text 59

*yat tu satyam ityādiṣu vākyeṣu pravṛtti-nimittāni lakṣaṇāṁ ca vinā śuddha-cid-brahma pratīyate śabda-śakter avicintyatvād atad-vyāvṛttyaiveti mataṁ tad atīva-mandam—bhāṣyarādibhir evam asvīkārāt \|\|59\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(refutation of text 10)**  
**“In statements like** ***satyam jñāna anantaṁ brahma*****, without resorting to pravṛtti-nimitta and lakṣaṇā, pure conscious Brahman can be understood, by exclusion of what is not Brahman, through the inconceivable power of words.” This is a very foolish idea since it is not accepted by commentators on the sūtras. \|\|59\|\|**

### Text 60

*pramāṇāvrṭteś ca nirviśeṣaṁ tan na śraddheyam, tathā hi—na tāvat pratyakṣaṁ tatra pramāṇaṁ, rūpādy-abhāvāt, nopamānam II60II*

**Перевод:**

**One cannot believe in Brahman without qualities since there is no pramāna. Pratyakṣa cannot be used since Brahman has no form to be grasped by the senses. Metaphor (upamāna) cannot be used, since nothing can be compared to Brahman. Śabda (scripture) cannot be used because it lacks pravṛtti-nimitta.**

### Text 61

*“athāta ādeśo neti neti” itdyadau rupa-gateyattā pratiṣiddhā, na tu rūpa-mātraṁ “prakṛtaitāvattvāṁ hi pratiṣedhati tato bravīti ca bhūyaḥ” it nyāyā \|\|61\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The statement** ***athāta ādeśo neti neti*** **cannot be used as proof of Brahman devoid of qualities, because limitation of the form of Brahman is denied by that statement. The form of Brahman is not denied.**

**prakṛtaitāvattvāṁ hi pratiṣedhati tato bravīti ca bhūyaḥ**

**The passage denies that what was described concerning** ***prakṛti*** **is everything about Brahman. It says that Brahman is much more. Brahma-sūtra 3.2.33 \|\|61\|**

### Text 62

*na cātma-śabdāt pratipattṛ-svarūpam brahmeti vācyam – tasya puruṣākāra-vibhu-cit-sukha-vastu-paratvāt bheda-śrutibhyaś ca \|\|62\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(refutation of text 11)**

**One should not say that the word** ***ātmā*****, the svarūpa of the person who hears, is Brahman, since many śrutis state the difference between jīva and Brahman and that Brahman is the supreme entity of consciousness and bliss with a human form. \|\|62\|\|**

### Text 63

*na ca niṣphalo loka-jñāto vāyaṁ bhedaḥ—“pṛthag-ātmānaṁ preritāraṁ ca matvā” ityādau bheda eva phala-śravaṇāt; viruddha-dharmāvacchinna-pratiyogikasya tasya śāstra-mātra-gocaratvāt \|\|63\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Difference which is known this world is not fruitless, since scriptures explain that seeing difference brings results.**

***pṛthag ātmānaṁ preritāraṁ ca matvā***  
***juṣṭas tatas tenāmṛtatvam eti***

**Knowing himself to be different from he who inspires actions, invoking the pleasure of the Lord, that person attains liberation. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 1.6**

**But the difference in the Lord and the jīva, who possesses contrary qualities, is understood only through scriptures. \|\|63\|\|**

### Text 64

*kiṁca “upakramopasaṁhārāv abhyāso ‘pūrvatā phalam \| arthavādopapattī ca liṅgaṁ tātparya-nirṇaye” \|\| ity atra yāni śāstra-tātparya-nirṇetṛṇi ṣaḍ-vidhani liṅgāni smaryante tāni ca tatraiva vīksyante \|\|64\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**There are six functions for determining the meaning of scriptures: upakrama and saṁhāra, abhyāsa, apūrvatā, phalam, arthavāda and upapatti. These are used to determine the meaning of the statements of scripture. \|\|64\|\|**

### Text 65

*tathā hi śvetāśvataropaniṣadi—“ajo hy eko juṣāmāṇo nuśete” ityādyupakramaḥ “ juṣtaṁ yadā paśyaty anyam iśam asya mahimanam eti vīta-śokaḥa ity upasaṁhāraḥ, “dvā suparṇā” “tayor anyo ‘naśnann anyaḥ’ ity abhyāsaḥ tāḍṛśasya bhedasya vedāntād vinā lokād apratīter apūrvatā, “vīta-śokaḥ” iti phalam; “asya mahimānam eti” ity arthvādaḥ; “any ‘naśnan” ity upapattir iti \| evam anyāni ca “pṛthagātmanam” ityādīnitasyām eva draṣṭavyāni \|\|65\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad** ***ajo hy eko juṣāmāṇo nuśete*** **is the** ***upakrama*** **(beginning),** ***anyam īśam*** **(the Lord is different) is the** ***upasaṁhāra*** **(conclusion),** ***tayor anyo' naśnann anyo 'nyam īśam*** **(the other person does not eat) is the** ***abhyāsa*** **(repetition). The** ***apūrvata*** **(uniqueness) is that, without scripture, the difference between the Lord and jīva could not be known.** ***Vīta-śoka*** **is the** ***phalam*** **(result).** ***Asya mahimānam*** **(he sees the Lord's greatness) is the** ***artha-vāda*** **(praise).** ***Anyo 'naśnan*** **(the other does not eat) is the** ***upapatti*** **(proof). One can find other examples elsewhere such as** ***pṛthag ātmānam*****. \|\|65\|\|**

### Text 66

*muṇḍakopaniṣadi ca “dvā supārṇā” ity upakramaḥ; “paramaṁ sāmam upaiti” ity upasaṁhāraḥ; “tayor anyonyam īśam” ity abhyāsaḥ; “kāla-trayodhyasya bhedasyānyato ‘pratipatteḥ” apūrvatā; “puṇya-pāpair vidhūya” ityādi phalam; “asya mahimānam eti” ity arthavādaḥ; “anaśnan” ity upapattiś ca” evam anyāsu copaniṣatsu tāni dṛśyāni \|\|66\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(refutation of text 14)**

**In Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad,** ***dvā supārṇā*** **is the upakrama,** ***paramaṁ sāmam upaiti*** **is the upasaṁhāra,** ***tayor anyonyam īśam*** **is the abhyāsa,** ***kāla-trayodhyasya bhedasyānyato ‘pratipatteḥ*** **is the apūrvatā,** ***puṇya-pāpair vidhūya*** **is the phalam,** ***asya mahimānam eti*** **is the arthavādaḥ and** ***anaśnan*** **is the upapatti. These six items can be seen in other Upaniṣads also. \|\|66\|\|**

### Text 67

*yat tu praprañcasyādhyastatvān mithyātvaṁ sādhayati, tasya vedāprāmānyāpattiḥ—“yato vā imāni” ityāder agni-hotrādi-kārma-parasya ca vākya-jātasya tad-viṣayatayā prāmāṇyāt; visayābhāve “eṣa bandhyāsuto bhāti” ityādi-vākya-sāmānyāt \| tataś ca nāstikatāpattiḥ \|\|67\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“Since the world is a covering over Brahman, it is illusory.” This makes the Vedas lose their authority. For instance** ***yato imāni*** **speaks of the world as being real. The Vedic statements concerning fire sacrifices accept a real world. If the world were unreal, all these statements would have no subject and would be equivalent to a son of a barren mother. Thus by claiming an unreal world they reject the Vedas. \|\|67\|\|**

### Text 68

*nanu na vayaṁ kha-puṣpavat tasya mithātvaṁ brumaḥ, vyavahārika-sattā-svīkārāt; tena na vedāprāmāṇyaṁ, nāpi nāstikāpattir iti cen na, anavadhānāt \| tathāhi dvidhaṁ khalu satyaṁ pāramārthikam aparamārthikaṁ ca tatrāntaṁ dvi-vidhaṁ—vyavaharikaṁ, prātītikaṁ ca \| tad etat satyāsatyā-catuṣṭayaṁ kramād brahma-prapaṇca-śukti-rūpyeṣu vartate \| tatra vyavahārika-satyasya praprañce aṅgīkārāt na tad bodhi vedāpramāṇyam \| pāramārthika-satyatvyābhāvāt tu tasya mithyātvam iti hi matam \| tad etad ayuktam, bādhyārtha-bodhakatayā vedāprāmāṇyānuddhārāt \| bādhyo hi prapañcaḥ, yad uktam—“tattvam asyād-vākyārtha-samyagdhījanma-mātrataḥ \| avidyā saha kāryeṇa nāṣīd asti bhaviṣyati” iti \|\|68\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“We do not say that the world is false like a flower in the sky, since we say the world has conventional existence (*****vyavayārika-sattā*****). That is not against the Vedas and thus we cannot be said to have rejected the Vedas.”**

**That is not correct since you have overlooked the facts. You claim there are two realities: spiritual reality and material reality. Material reality has two types: conventional and mental.**

**Brahman is spiritual reality, the world is material reality. Shell is conventional reality and illusion of silver is mental reality. Conventional reality is accepted in this world. That is not rejected by the Vedas. Because the conventional world lacks spiritual reality, you call it false.**

**But this is not logical. Though the Vedas destroy the illusion of a real world, this does not rescue the Vedas from having lack of authority. (According to you) The world should disappear on gaining knowledge.**

**tattvam asy adi-vākyārtha-samyag-dhī janma-mātrataḥ \|**  
**avidyā saha kāryeṇa nāsīd asti bhaviṣyati**

**On directly understanding the meaning of the statements like “You are that” along with ignorance, its effect, the world ceases to exist in past, present and future. Bṛhād-āraṇyaka-vartikā, Sureśvara \|\|68\|\|**

### Text 69

*nāpi satya-dvaividhyā-svīkṛtyā bāhyebhyo vailakṣaṇyam—tair api tasyāṅgīkārāt; yad uktam—“sattvam tu dvi-vidhaṁ proktam sāṁvṛtaṁ pāramārthikam \| sāṁvṛtaṁ vyavhāryaṁ syān nivṛttau paramārthikam \|\| dve dattve samupaśritya baudhānāṁ dharma-codanā \| loke sāṁvrṭaṁ satyatvaṁ satyaṁ ca pāramārthikam \|\| vicāryamāṇe nāsattvaṁ sattvaṁ cāpi pratīyate \| yasya tat sāṁvṛtaṁ satyaṁ vyavahāra-padaṁ ca tat \|\|” iti tasmān na tebhyas tat “māyāvādam accchāstraṁ pracchannṁ baudham ucyate” ityādi-smaraṇāt ca \|\|69\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**By accepting two types of reality these persons are not different from the Buddhists since Buddhists also accept these two realities. It is said:**

**sattvam tu dvi-vidhaṁ proktam sāṁvṛtaṁ pāramārthikam \|**  
**sāṁvṛtaṁ vyavahāryaṁ syān nivṛttau paramārthikam \|\|**  
**dve sattve samupaśritya baudhānāṁ dharma-codanā \|**  
**loke sāṁvrṭaṁ satyatvaṁ satyaṁ ca pāramārthataḥ \|\|**  
**vicāryamāṇe nāsattvaṁ sattvaṁ cāpi pratīyate \|**  
**yasya tat sāṁvṛtaṁ satyaṁ vyavahāra-padaṁ ca tat \|\|**

**There are two types of reality: sāṁvṛta and pāramārthika. Sāṁvrṭa mean conventional reality. When it is destroyed, only the spiritual reality remains. Taking shelter of these two realities the Buddhists preach dharma. In this world, reality is covered. On considering reality from the spiritual point of view, one sees reality and not unreality. When reality is covered it becomes conventional reality. Mādhyamika-kārikā, Quoted in Tattvoddyota--Madhva**

**The māyāvādīs are not different from them. Smṛti says:**

**māyāvādam accchāstraṁ pracchannaṁ baudham ucyate**

**Māyāvāda, which is a wrong interpretation of scriptures, is said to be covered Buddhism. Padma Purāṇa \|\|69\|**

### Text 70

*api ca sattva-śabdo na nānārthaḥ, sattva-bhede pramāṇābhāvāt \| yadi sattva-śabdasya mithyāparamārtha-vācyatayārtha-bhedaḥ syāt tadā sadākārānugata-pratyayānupapattiḥ nānārtha saindhavadi-pade tad adarśanāt \| “mrṣā sad” iti vadato vyāghātāc ca \| tasmāt sāṁvrṭa-śabdavan mṛṣārthako vyavahārika-śabdo lālāvaktrāsavādi-śabdavat pratāraṇāya prayukta iti satya-sāmānye vyavasthā nopapannā \|\|70\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The word** ***sattva*** **does not have various meanings. There is no proof for giving the word** ***sattva*** **different meanings. If the word means false and real then it has different meanings. With different meanings, the idea of reality that the word conveys is lost. One does not see that in using a word such as** ***saindhav*****a (which means salt, a horse or something from Sindh province). There will be a contradiction in saying “the unreal is real.” Therefore the word** ***vyavahāraka*** **or** ***sāmvṛta,*** **meaning “false” is used for deceiving, like calling saliva “nectar of the mouth.” The word** ***vyavahāraka*** **cannot be used in relation to existence. \|\|70\|\|**

### Text 71

*syād etat brahma-sattayaiva praprañca-sattā, tena na vedāprāmāṇyaṁ,--tad anya-sattābhāvāc ca mṛṣātvoktir na tu niḥsvarūpatvāt \| “neha nānāsti kiṁcana” iti śrutir api tad anya-sattā-niṣedha-parā \| na cāti-prasaṅgaḥ kanaka-mukuṭayor iva upādānopādeya-bhāvasya niyāmakatvāt \| itarathā “sarvaṁ khalv idaṁ brahma” ity atra samānādhikaraṇyopapattaye taj-jalān iti taj-jatvād anupapattir iti \| idam apy apeśalaṁ kṣodākṣamatvāt \|\|71\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“Let the world exist, because of the existence of Brahman. That is authorized by the Vedas. Since nothing exists without Brahman, one cannot speak of the world as being false. That is because it has no svarūpa. The statement** ***neha nānāsti kiṁcana*** **indicates that existence other than Brahman is rejected. This is not stretching the rules, since the Lord and the world are related like gold to a crown, or a material and the object made of the material. Otherwise identity of Brahman with the world as stated in** ***sarvaṁ khalv idaṁ brahma*** **would not be true.** ***Taj-jalān*** **should not mean that Brahman is the creator.”**

**All this is not correct, having been stated without proper consideration \|\|71\|\|**

### Text 72

*tathāhi brahma-satteti kiṁ te vivakṣitaṁ? Kiṁ brahma-niṣṭhā sattā, kiṁ braham-sarūpā sā, uta brahmābhedaḥ, āhosvid brahma vyatirekeṇābhāvaḥ \|\|72\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**What does existence of Brahman (brahma-sattā) along with existence of the world mean? Does it mean an existence fixed in Brahman? Does it mean having existence as the svarūpa of Brahman? Does it mean its existence is nondifferent from Brahman? Does it mean non- existence of everything except Brahman? \|\|72\|\|**

### Text 73

*ādye, sa pāramārthikī bādhyā vā? prathame ‘pasiddhāntāpattiḥ, adhiṣthāna-jñāna-bodhyasya dharmasya brahmaṇy-asvīkārāt; paramārthika-sattopetasya prapañcasya brahmavat satyatvaṁ ca \| na ca tat-sattve ‘pi svarūpābhāvād asatyatvaṁ, pāramārthika-sattopetasya dharmasya kutrāpi tad adarśanāt \| sattvopetaś cen na mṛṣā \| mrṣa cen na sattopetaḥ \| sattā-sāmānya-śūnyāpi sattā satī dṛṣṭā: sattopeto ‘py asan na dṛṣṭacaraḥ \| kiṁca samānāmāṁ bhāvaḥ sāmānya, yat sattā-śabdenocyate na ca tan-mṛṣā-satyayoḥ sambhavet \| yad uktam “saytavaṁ na ca sāmānyaṁ mṛṣārtha-paramārathayoḥ \| virodhān na ca vṛkṣatva-samānyaṁ siṁha-vṛkṣayoḥ” iti \| na cetaraḥ, vedāprāmāṇyānuddhārāt \| na hi bādhya-sattā bodhakaṁ pramāṇam \| kiṁcāsmin pakṣe brahmaṇo ‘satyatāpattiḥ, bādhya-sattā-yogāt vedāntāprāmāṇyaṁ ca nirviṣayatvaṭ \| na ca sattābhave pi sad-rūpatvāt brahmaṇo ‘satyatāpattiḥ tadvat prapañcasyāpi tad-anāpatteḥ “sad eva saumyedam” ityādinā tasyāpi sad-rūpatva-śravaṇāt \| yat tu dīrgha-bhrama-janakatvād vedasya prāmāṇyaṁ, tan-mandam, nabho nīlama-candrālpatva-jñāna-janakasyāpi tathātvāt \|\|73\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If word’s existence is fixed in Brahman, then is it actually true, or is it to be negated (*****bādhya*****) as illusion? If it is real, then it is against one’s conclusion because one cannot accept a quality in Brahman—Brahman acts as a basis of material existence. And if the world is endowed with similar existence then the world must be truly existing like Brahman.**

**One cannot say that the existence of the world fixed in Brahman is false, because then it would not have a svarūpa. One never sees qualities of real existence which are devoid of a svarūpa. If one says that something endowed with existence is actual false, then it is not endowed with existence at all. One can accept existence other objects with absence of a universal (for instance, absence of unicorn-ness), but one never sees nonexistence of an object endowed with existence (if you claim the world has a false existence in Brahman). Moreover the general state of existence can never be both false and true. It is said:**

**satyatvaṁ na ca sāmānyaṁ mṛṣārtha-paramārthayoḥ \|**  
**virodhān na ca vṛkṣatva-samānyaṁ siṁha-vṛkṣayoḥ**

**The false and the real cannot both be in the category of being true, because they are opposites, just a tree and a lion do not share similarity as having treeness. (Śloka-vartikā, Kumārila Bhaṭṭa)**

**Is the existence of the world something to be negated? That lacks proof in scriptures. Negating existence is not explained in the scriptures. Brahman’s existence would also be negated, for by combining Brahman with something which should be negated, Brahman itself becomes untrue. Brahman could not be proved by Vedānta, since it could not be the subject of discussion.**

**One may argue that though the world related to Brahman has no existence, Brahman is not unreal, since it is real (*****sad-rūpa*****). But by the same argument, since Brahman is not unreal, the world should not be unreal.** ***Sad eva saumyedam*** **indicates that the world is real (*****sat*****).**

**One should not argue that the world is discussed in the Vedas simply because it has caused long standing illusion (though it is unreal). The knowledge produced would be like the meagre knowledge of a black moon in the sky. \|\|73\|\|**

### Text 74

*nāpi brahma-svarūpeti dvitīyaḥ—abādhita-sattā-yogena prapañcasya mithyātvāsiddheḥ: na tu tṛtīyaḥ, viruddhayos tayor aikyānupapatteḥ \| na ca caturthaḥ sarvaṁ khaliv idam ityaikārthyānupapatteḥ; na hi tad abhāve tadaikārthyasambhavaḥ; na ca yaś cauraḥ saḥ sthāṇūr itivad bādha-daśāyāṁ tad iti vācyaṁ, vedāprāmāṇyānistārād iti \|74\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The existence of the world cannot be the svarūpa of Brahman (second option) since then the world could not be unreal. It would be related to un-negated existence.**

**That existence cannot be the third alternative. The existence of the world cannot be nondifferent from Brahman, since there cannot be oneness of Brahman and the world. They are contrary to each other.**

**The existence of the world cannot be the fourth alternative, absence of Brahman (something other than Brahman), since that would contradict the oneness of the statement** ***sarvam khalv idam brahma*****. It is not possible to equate Brahman with non-existence, which is something other than Brahman. One cannot equate a thief with a tree stump. It is impossible to escape the fact that this idea cannot be proved by the Vedas. \|\|74\|\|**

### Text 75

*api ca māyinā dṛṣṭi-sṛṣṭiḥ svīkṛtā \| dṛṣtī-sama-samayā sṛṣṭir na tu sṛṣṭi-sama-samayā dṛṣṭir iti \| sā caiṣā kṣaṇika-vijñāna-pakṣaṁ nātivartate—tatrārthānām arthāt kṣaṇikatvāt \| na cātrākṣaṇikaṁ vijñāna-mātram astīti svīkārāt tato bhedaḥ tatra pramāṇābhāvat, darśitaṁ caitat prāk \|\|75\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Śaṅkara accepts dṛṣti-sṛṣti (creation through perception alone). Creation of the world takes place when one perceives it, rather than sight arising with the creation. This is not different from the momentary consciousness theory of the Buddhists (*****kṣaṇīka-vijñāna*****) since they also claim all objects have a momentary appearance. One should not argue that advaitavāda is different on the grounds that vijñāna alone exists eternally. (It may be different from Buddhism) but there is no scriptural proof for consciousness alone. That was previously stated. \|\|75\|\|**

### Text 76

*kiṁ ca, māyimataṁ śūnyavādān nātiricyate; avidyāvacchinnaṁ jñānaṁ saṁvṛtty-avacchinnaṁ śūnyaṁ ca \| “sarvaṁ jñānam iti śūnyaṁ” iti ca bhāvanā-prakārṣād avidyāyaḥ saṁvṛtteś ca vināśe sati jñāna-mātraṁ śūnya-mātraṁ cāvaiśiṣyate \| kārya-nāśasya kāraṇa-rūpatvāt, saiva muktiḥ \| tasya tasya ca para-sāmāna-rūpaṁ sattvaṁ bhāva-pratiyogikatva-rūpa-sattva; anukūla-vedyatva-rūpam sukhatvaṁ pratikūla-vedyātva-rūpam duḥkhatvaṁ ca kiṁcid vāstavaṁ nāsti \| ubhayaṁ nirlepam ajaram amaram asambādhaṁ sarvam ānavedyām svayām prabhātam \|\|76\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Māyāvāda is not different from śūnyavāda (Buddhism). It consist of knowledge devoid of ignorance, devoid of limitations and void (*****śūnya*****). “Everything is mere knowledge and it is void.” Thinking in this way, ignorance and limitations are destroyed. What remains is mere knowledge (*****jñāna-mātra*****) or mere emptiness (*****śūnya-mātra*****). With the destruction of effects, from knowledge alone there is liberation. In both these states (knowledge and emptiness) there are no samānas (universals), there is no existence of an object or its qualities and no opposite counterpart. There is no knowing in a favorable way—happiness-- and no knowing in an unfavorable way-- distress. Both states are uncontaminated, without age, without death, without pain, unknowable by material conception, and self-manifesting. \|\|76\|\|**

### Text 77

*nanv avedyatve saty aparokṣa-vyavahārārhatvam ananyādhīnāparokṣatvaṁ vā brahmaṇaḥ sva-prakāśatvaṁ, tac ca na śūnyasyātīti katham ubhayoḥ mānyam iti cet tuccham etat \| aparokṣaṁ hi svenaiva sva-viṣayakam anya-visayakaṁ vā? Nādyaḥ sva-viṣayakatāyā māyinānaṅgīkārāt \| aṅgīkāre vā cid-visayaktve ‘pi svarūpa-dṛṣyatvāpattyā brahmaṇo mithyātvāpattir viṣayatvāviṣaytvābhyāṁ vaiśiṣṭyāpattiś ca \| netaraḥ, mokṣe ‘nyābhāvenānya-viṣayāparokṣāsad-bhāvāt \| tasmāc chūnya-saṁvṛttyoḥ paryāyatayaiva jñānāvidyāyoḥ sthitir iti māyī mādhyamika eva \| vedāntānām akhaṇḍārtha-bodhakatvaṁ saṁsargāgocara-pramā-janaktvaṁ svīkurvatā māyinā “satyam jñānam anantam: ity atra asaj-jaḍa-paricchinna-vyāvṛttaṁ brahma-svarūpam eva tatheti vyākhātaṁ—yathā gaura-go-vyāvṛttā ity adikaṁ śūnyavādinā bauddhena \|\|77\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“How can it be said that this is the same as Buddhism, since Brahman can be perceived or is self-perceived, and is self-manifesting, whereas the void cannot be known?” That is foolishness. Is Brahman perceived by itself or by someone else? It cannot be self-perceived since that is not accepted by any māyāvādī. If one accepts that Brahman perceives itself, it would mean that Brahman is perceivable. Thus Brahman would be false (since anything perceivable is illusory) and would have qualities, since it would be the object of perception.**

**Is Brahman perceived by someone else? It cannot be, since in the state of liberation, there is nothing else and thus there is no one to be aware of Brahman. Jñāna and avidyā of the māyāvādī is the same as śūnya and saṁvṛtti of the Mādhyamika Buddhists. The māyāvādī claims that Vedānta can give understanding and knowledge beyond the guṇas. The phrase** ***satyaṁ jñānam anantaṁ brahma*** **indicates that Brahman’s svarūpa is absence of matter. That is the Buddhist claim as well. \|\|77\|\|**

### Text 78

*api ca jainam-sakhyām ca māyinovilokitam \| jainaḥ khalu jāvādīn ṣaṭpadārthān sattvāsattvadi-virudha-dharma-yoginaḥ varṇayati, kathaṁcit sad-asad-ity evam ādinā sapta-bhaṅgī-nyāyena \| māyī ca viśvaṁ sad-asad-bhinnam; aupaniṣadam api brahma-sarva-sabdā-vācyam ityadi \| tasmāc chabdāntareṇa sva-chadma samāvṛṇvan ayaṁ nāstika-viśeṣa evety asya vedāpramāṇya-kāriṇaḥ sparṣād bhetavyaṁ vaidikair iti \|\|78\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Māyāvāda is similar to Jain concepts. The Jains describe combinations of contrary natures such as existing and non-existing according to the logic of sapta-bhaṅgī. The māyāvādīs describe the universe as different from existing and non-existing, which is described by all the words of the Vedas as Brahman in the Upaniṣads. Māyāvāda is Jain philosophy expressed in other words. It is a variety of Jain atheism. The ideas are not authorized by the Vedas. Those following the Vedas fear to touch such atheism. \|\|78\|\|**

### Text 79

*yat tu “jyotīṁṣi viṣṇuḥ” ityādau jñāna-mātre brahmaṇi vyavahārasyādhyastatvāt tasya tad-bhedasya ca bādhyatvam ity uktam tad asat, āsti-nāsti-śabda-vācyam cij-jaḍātmakaṁ kṛtsnam; parasya viṣṇoḥ kaya-bhūtaṁ tadāyatt-vṛrtikatvādibhis tad ātmakaṁ vyapadiśyate \| sa u sarvātmāpi sarva-vilakṣaṇaḥ kṣetra-jñasya jīvasya jñāna-svarūpasyāpi deva-manuṣādi-bhāva-prāptau tad-bhogya-jaḍa-pariṇatau ca svarūpāvaraka-karmaiva hetur viṣṇūpasattyaiva tasya karmaṇo vimuktir ity etasyaivārthasya nirūpaṇāt \|\|79\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(refutation of text 15)**

**To identify everything as Brahman, making everything a superimposition on Brahman, and negating all difference by quoting verses like** ***jyotiṁsi viṣṇuḥ*** **nāstiindicate everything conscious and unconscious (not a negation of the world). The section teaches that the universe is nondifferent from Viṣṇu, being his body, since it is dependent on him. Though Viṣṇu is the soul of all beings, he is very different.**

**Though the jīva, knower of the field is a form of knowledge (jñāna-svarūpa), he identifies as a devatā, human or other being in a body meant for enjoyment, caused by karmas which cover his svarūpa. By worshipping Viṣṇu, the jīva becomes free of karma. This is the actual meaning. \|\|79\|\|**

### Text 80

*tathāhi jyotīṁṣīti \| iha viṣṇvāyatta-vṛttikatvādibhiś cid-acitos tādrūpyam abhihitam \| tābhyāṁ viṣnor vailakṣaṇyam āha—jñāna-svarūpa iti \| yasmād asau bhagavān jñāna-svarūpaḥ śruyate, ato ‘śeṣa-mūrtiḥ pariṇāmī prapañcākāro na tu bhavati, kintu vastu-bhūto ‘pariṇāmi-cid-rūpa ity arthaḥ \| prapañcas tu tasmād eva nimittād udbhūta ity āha --tato hīti \| klīvatvam ārṣaṁ \| vijñānārthaṁ jīvārthaṁ vijṛmbhitān ity arthaḥ, “buddhīndriya-manaḥ prāṇān jananām asṛjat prabhuḥ \| mātrārthaṁ ca bhavārthaṁ ca ātmano ‘kalpanāya ca” it smaranāt \| jīvasya jñāna-rūpasyāpy anādibhagavad-vaimukhya-kṛtaiḥ karmabhiḥ saṁsāras tat-sāmukhye saty adau vilīyate svarūpaṁ ca sphuratīty aha yadā tu śuddham iti \| yadāiva jñāna evātma-vastu svāsmin devādi-vaividhyānusandhi-hetoh sarvasya karmaṇaḥ sat-tīrthanugraha-labdhayā jñāna-pūrvakopāsanayā kṣaye sati śuddhaṁ pariśodhitaṁ san nija-rūpi bhavati, tadā devādi-dehābhimāninaḥ asyātmanaḥ saṅkalpa-pūrvaka-karma-phala-bhūtā devādi-deha-bhogyāh śailābdhi-dharādayo vastu-bhedā na bhavanti, tad-hetoh karmaṇaḥ praṇāśād ity arthaḥ \| itthaṁścācid-aṁśasya prati-kṣaṇa-pariṇāmitayā vinaṣṭa-prāyatvān nāsti-śabda-vācyataṁ darśitam \| pratikśaṇam avasthā-bhaṅgas tatra nimittam ity āha—vastv asti kim iti \| cit-aṁśaḥ khalv ādi-madhyāvasāna-hīnaḥ sadaika-rūpas tasya na kadācin nāsti buddhi-yogaḥ \| acid-aṁśas tu ko ‘pi kadāpi tathātvato na dṛṣṭaḥ \| tatḥ kim? tatrāha yac ceti \| yad anyathatvaṁ pariṇāmaṁ muhuḥ prāpnoti , tat punas tathā pūrvavan na bhavati \| tatra pūrvāvasthā-parāvasthā-śūnye aparāvasthā-bhāji-vastuni tad eveti pratīty-abhāvāt tattvam asti-śabda-vācyataṁ kutaḥ syān nety arthaḥ \| pratīyate itāha—mahīti \| piṇḍādi dvārā ghaṭatvaṁ mahī prāpnoti, ghaṭād bhagnāt kapālikā, mardinatāyāś ca tasyāś cūrṇa-rajaḥ \| tato ‘ṇur iti sūkṣmaś cety evaṁ janair avastv eva svabhogya-bhūtaṁ satata-pariṇāmi-jāḍam evālakṣyate \| atra pariṇāma-parasparāyāṁ satataika-rūpam asti-śabda-vācyaṁ vastu jīveśānyatarat kim alakṣyate tvaṁ bruhīti naivālakṣya ity arthaḥ tatra hetuḥ svakarmeti \|\|80\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since the jīva and matter are dependent on the Lord, they are said to be the form of the Lord. To make this clear, the Lord is distinguished from the jīvas and matter with the words** ***jñāna-svarūpaḥ*** **(the Lord has a form of pure knowledge). knowledge). vastu-bhūtaḥ). The material world arises because of the Lord. Know that these forms arise for the jīva’s use (vijñana-vijṛmbhitāni).**

**buddhīndriya-manaḥ prāṇān jananām asṛjat prabhuḥ \|**  
**mātrārthaṁ ca bhavārthaṁ ca ātmano ‘kalpanāya ca**

**The Supreme Lord manifested the material intelligence, senses, mind and vital air of the living entities so that they could indulge their desires for sense gratification, take repeated births to engage in fruitive activities, become elevated in future lives and ultimately attain liberation. (SB 10.87.2)**

**Though the jīva has knowledge as his nature, he remains in saṁsāra because of karmas produced by beginningless disinterest in the Lord. When he shows interest in the Lord, saṁsāra is destroyed and his svarūpa of knowledge manifests. When all karmas which cause various bodies for the jīva are destroyed by mercy of a guru and worship of the Lord along with attainment of knowledge, one becomes pure (*****śuddham*****). One becomes situated in one’s own form (*****nija-rūpi*****), and no longer sees differences in objects (*****vastu*****-*****bhedāḥ*****) which were enjoyed in various bodies, which arise as a result of karmas along with desires (*****saṅkalpa-taroḥ phalāni*****) in the jīva which is endowed with material identity—since karma has been destroyed.**

**Because the body is destructible, being transformed at every moment, it is described by the word** ***nāsti*** **(not existing). This is then illustrated. Because the body changes at every moment, it is described by the words** ***vastv asti kim*** **(What is the reality of this?)**

**The conscious portion, jīva, is devoid of beginning, middle and end. It is always in one form. It is never without consciousness.**

**The unconscious portion, matter, never has these qualities. It is not like the jīva, but is constantly changing (*****anyathātvam*****). It is devoid of having a former and latter state (since it is created and destroyed). It is not perceived as an object of a previous state (since it keeps changing). One cannot even say that it exists. It is then described. Earth takes the form of a pot. Then it becomes broken. Then it becomes pulverized into dust. Then it becomes very small particles. Thus all the enjoyable objects, constantly transforming, are seen as insubstantial. Being constantly transformed, how can matter, different from the jīva and the Lord, be seen by people to be existing (*****asti*****), always having one form? They can never see this since they are covered by karma. \|\|80\|\|**

### Text 81

*yasmād evaṁ tasmād acid-aṁśo ‘sti-śabda-vācyo na bhavatīty āha tasmān neti \| cid-aṁśas tu pariṇāmābhāvāt sadeika-rūpo devādi-deha-vilakṣaṇo ‘pi tad-ādi-prāpakena nija-karma-bhedena vibhinna-cittair janair bahudhā deva-manuṣyādi-rūpeṇānusaṁhita ity āha – vijñānam eka iti \| etac ca bhagavad-vaimukya-hetuh kena karmanaiva kṛtaṁ, na tu svarūpa-prayuktam ity āha jñānaṁ viśuddham iti \| viśuddhaṁ karma-rahitaṁ vimalaṁ prakṛti-sparśa-rahitaṁ viśokaṁ tat-krṭa-duḥka-rahitaṁ lobhādi-heya-guṇa-rahitaṁ ca \| evaṁ mad-ukta-prakāraṁ sadaikam sarvadaika-rasam iti viśvāntarvartinaḥ ksetra-jñasya śuddhaṁ svarūpaṁ nirupitam \| yasyopasattyā mokṣas tam īśaṁ nirūpayati –paraṁ iti \| pareśas tu paramo nitya-lakṣmīkaḥ, vāsudevo viśuddha-sattāvirbhāvī—“sattvaṁ viśuddhaṁ vasudeva-śabditaṁ yad īyate tatra pumān apāvṛtha”’ iti smaraṇāt \| yato ‘nyan nāsti śaktimatas tasmāj jagad anyan na bhavatīti jīvād bhedaḥ \|\|81\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, the acid-aṁśa or body made of matter cannot be called** ***asti*** **or existing. existing. vijñānam ekam ekam} ekam} vimalam), without sorrow caused by karma (viśokam), without bad qualities like greed. As explained, it is one (sadāikam), having only one object of affection. The pure form of jīva, dwelling within the universe, has been described.**

**Then the Lord by whose worship one attains liberation is described. That Lord is Vāsudeva, pure existence, accompanied by Lakṣmī.**

**sattvaṁ viśuddhaṁ vāsudeva-śabditaṁ yad īyate tatra pumān apāvṛtaḥ**

**The pure state is called Vasudeva, because there the Supreme Lord appears without coverings. SB 4.3.23**

**Since he is the possessor of all śakti, the universe (made of śakti) is not different from him (*****yato ‘nyad na asti*****). The Lord is different from the jīva. \|\|81\|\|**

### Text 82

*upasaṁharati—sad-bhāva iti \| evaṁ nitya-lakṣmīkatayā viśuddha-sattvāvirbhāvatayā cinmayatayā sato brahmaṇo bhāvo vibhūtiḥ svabhāvaś ca tavokteḥ \| jñānaṁ cid-aṁśaḥ, anyad acid-aṁśa; \| pūrvasya sarvadaika-rūpeṇāsti-śabda-vācyatvaṁ satyatvaṁ ca \| uttarasya pratikṣaṇa-pariṇāmitayā vināśa-garbhatvān nācit-śabda-vācyataṁ asatyatvaṁ ca tavoktaṁ \| nanv asyācid-aṁśasyoktiḥ kim arthā tatrāha, etat tu yad iti \| tatrāpi vicṇu-tattva-nirūpaṇe yad etad acid-amśa-bhūtaṁ bhuvanāśritaṁ śailābdhi-dharādi-gatiaṁ tat samyak paramārathatayā vyavahāra-bhutaṁ, na tu śukti-rajatādivan mṛṣābhutam ity arthaḥ \| deva-manuṣyādi-bhāvaḥ karmanaiveti viśadayati yajñāḥ paśur iti \| acid-aṁśasya saṁvyavayāra-bhutām āḥa;--yac caitad iti \| karma-vaśyo jīvo nānā-yonir vrajatīti jñātvā viraktaḥ san iha jagati tat-krūyāt yena vāsudevaṁ viśati prāpnoti \| “puraṁ praviśati” ity atra pura-prāptir avagamyate \| praveśa-hetuś ca bhaktir eva “bhaktyā māṁ abhijānāti ityādi smṛtibhyaḥ \|\|82\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Then everything is summarized. I have spoken to you about the powers and nature of Brahman (*****sad-bhāvah*****), with pure, spiritual manifestation, along with eternal Lakṣmī. I have spoken about the jīva (*****jñānam*****) and matter (*****anyat*****). The jīva exists at all times (*****asti*****) in one form and is real (*****satyam*****). (*****satyam*****). asatyam.**

**What is the purpose of the acid-āṁśa or matter? All the stones, oceans and earth (*****bhuvanāśritam*****), which are unconscious (*****yad etat*****) are to be used for spiritual purposes (*****vyavahāra-bhūtam*****).**

**There is then an explanation of how the identity as devatā or human produces bodies by karma.**

### Text 83

*yat tv iha bādhāyāṁ sāmānādhikaraṇyam āśritya brahmaikyaṁ nirviśeṣaṁ satyaṁ tato ‘nyat sarvam asatyam iti kalpayanti na tac caturasraṁ pūrvāpara-virodhād eva bādhād draṣṭur anirūpyatvāt ca \|\|83\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The māyāvādīs imagine that only Brahman without qualities is real and everything else is false, taking shelter of** ***sāmānadhikaraṇya*** **to negate the world. But this is not proper because of a series of contradictions and because the observer cannot be defined, since he is negated as well. \|\|83\|\|**

### Text 84

*tasmin śāstra śrīmān parāśaraḥ pūrvam īśa-jīva-prakṛti-kālān nityān evocat tatha hi “avikārāya śuddhāya pradhāna-paramātmane \| sadāika-rūpa-rūpāya viṣṇave sarva-jiṣnave \|\| pradhānaṁ puruṣaṁ cāpi praviṣyātmecchayā hariḥ \| kṣobhāyām āaa saṁprāpte sarga-kāle vyayāvyayau \|\| avyaktaṁ kāraṇam yat tat pradhāna-ṛṣi-sattama \| procyate prakṛtiḥ sūkṣmā nityaṁ sad-asad-ātmakam \| anādir bhagavān kālo nānto ‘sya dvija vidyate \| avyucchinnās tv ete sarga-sthity-anta-saṁyamāḥ” iti \| na caiṣāṁ bhāvanayaiva nāśaḥ nityatva-vyākopāt \|\|84\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Previously Parāśara spoke of the Lord, the jīva, prakṛti and time as all being eternal.**

**avikārāya śuddhāya pradhāna-paramātmane \|**  
**sadāika-rūpa-rūpāya viṣṇave sarva-jiṣnave \|\|**

**I offer respects to the Lord Viṣṇu who is without change, pure, eternal, who is always in one form and who conquers all beings.**

**pradhānaṁ puruṣaṁ cāpi praviṣyātmecchayā hariḥ \|**  
**kṣobhāyām āsa saṁprāpte sarga-kāle vyayāvyayau \|\|**

**By his will the Lord enters pradhāna which is subject to change and also enters the jīva not subject to change, and agitates them at the time of creation.**

**avyaktaṁ kāraṇam yat tat pradhāna-ṛṣi-sattamaiḥ \|**  
**procyate prakṛtiḥ sūkṣmā nityaṁ sad-asad-ātmakam \|**

**The best of sages say that prakṛti, subtle and eternal, composed of cause and effect, is called avyakta, kārana and pradhāna.**

**anādir bhagavān kālo nānto ‘sya dvija vidyate \|**  
**avyucchinnās tv ete sarga-sthity-anta-saṁyamāḥ**

**There is no end for the Lord and time which have no beginning. The two are continuous, and control creation, maintenance and destruction.**

**They are not destroyed by meditation, since they are eternal. \|\|84\|\|**

### Text 85

*bhedaś caiṣām iha sambhāṣyate—“pradhāna-puruṣa-vyakta-kālānāṁ paramaṁ hi yat \| paśyanti sūrayaḥ śuddhaṁ tad-visṇoh paramṁ padam \|\| viṣnu-svarūpāt parato hi te ‘nye rūpe pradhanaṁ puruṣaś ca vipra \| tasyaiva te ‘nyena dhṛte viyukte rūpeṇa yat tad tad dvija kāla-saṁjñām \|\| na santi yatra sarveśe nāma-jātyādi-kalpanāḥ \| sattā-mātrātmake jñaye jñānātmāny ātman pare \|\| sa brahma tat paraṁ dhāma paramātmā sa ceśvaraḥ \| sa viśṇuḥ sarvam evedaṁ yato nāvartate yatiḥ \| prakṛtir yā mayākhyāta vyaktāvyakta-svarūpiṇi \| puruṣaś cāpy abhavete līyate paramātmani” ityādibhiḥ \|\|85\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The difference between the Lord and prakṛti and jīva is shown.**

**pradhāna-puruṣa-vyakta-kālānāṁ paramaṁ hi yat \|**  
**paśyanti sūrayaḥ śuddhaṁ tad-visṇoh paramṁ padam \|\|**

**The wise see the pure, supreme abode of Viṣṇu which is superior to** ***pradhāna,*** **the** ***jīva,*** **the universe and time.**

**viṣnu-svarūpāt parato hi te ‘nye rūpe pradhanaṁ puruṣaś ca vipra \|**  
**tasyaiva te ‘nyena dhṛte viyukte rūpeṇa yat tad tad dvija kāla-saṁjñām \|\|**

**O brāhmaṇa! The** ***svarūpa*** **of Viṣṇu is different from time, from** ***pradhāna*** **and the jīva. That form by which** ***pradhāna*** **and the jīva are joined and then separated is called time.**

**na santi yatra sarveśe nāma-jātyādi-kalpanāḥ \|**  
**sattā-mātrātmake jñaye jñānātmāny ātman pare \|\|**

**The Lord has no manufactured names or varieties. He is existence, knowledge itself and is to be known. He is different from the jīva.**

**sa brahma tat paraṁ dhāma paramātmā sa ceśvaraḥ \|**  
**sa viśṇuḥ sarvam evedaṁ yato nāvartate yatiḥ \|**

**He is Brahman, he is the supreme abode, the supreme ātmā and the controller. Viṣṇu is everything. The sannyāsī does not return on attaining him.**

**prakṛtir yā mayākhyāta vyaktāvyakta-svarūpiṇi \|**  
**puruṣaś cāpy ubhav ete līyate paramātmani**

**The jīva and prakṛti, which is manifested and unmanifested, merge into Paramātmā. Viṣṇu Purāṇa \|\|85\|\|**

### Text 86

*etenaiva paratra bharata-rahūgana-saṁvādo vyākhyātaḥ \| tatra hi brahma-jīvayor abhedo ‘paramārthaḥ śuddha-brahmānubhavas tu paramārthaḥ sphuṭam uktaḥ \|\|86\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Elsewhere in the discussion between Bharata and Rahūgaṇa, this is explained. Non-difference of Brahman and jīva is not the conclusion. Realizing the supreme Brahman is clearly the goal. \|\|86\|\|**

### Text 87

*yas tv ṛbhu-nidāgha-saṁvādas tena svoktārtha-poṣāyodāhṛtaḥ, so ‘pi vyākyātaḥ \| tatra hi kṣudhādi-śūnyaṁ jīvasya śuddhaṁ svarūpaṁ tasya vyāpīśa-tantratvaṁ tattvam tu nivāsa-gatyāgatyāditvaṁ ceti vyapakādvaitam evaṁ nirūpyate na tv anyat \| upasaṁhāre ‘pyaikyam evānayoḥ \| viṣṇo nānaṭva-dṛṣṭer niṣedhas tatrādhikaḥ \|\|87\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**To support their theory, they quote the conversation of Ṛbhu and Nidāgha (also in Viṣṇu Purāṇa). The pure jīva is described as being devoid of hunger and other problems and as being dependent on the all-pervading Lord. All pervading oneness in terms of dwelling place, coming and going, is described. Nothing else exists. Jīva and the Lord are one. There is extreme denial of seeing variety in Viṣṇu. \|\|87\|\|**

### Text 88

*evam uttaratra keśidhvaja-khāṇḍikya-saṁvāde ‘pyātma-bhāvādi-śabdān nirguṇa-cidaikyāśāna kāryā—tatra dhyeya-dhyātṛ-bhāvena vyāya-vyāpaka-bhāvena ca tasya sphūtavāt \|\|88\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Later in Viṣṇu Purāṇa, in the discussion between Keśidhvaja and Khāṇḍikya, one cannot interpret the word** ***ātma-bhāva*** **to mean oneness without qualities, since clearly there are distinctions made between the object of meditation and the meditator, and the pervader and the pervaded. \|\|88\|\|**

### Text 89

*yat punar uktam atha yo ‘nyām ityādibhir bheda-grāhiṇo nindanād bhaya-saṁśanāc ca na bhede śāśtra-tātparyam iti; naitat supeśalam—bhede tat-tātparyasya prāg ukteḥ \| athety atra hi sakāma-sevakatvāt karma-jaḍo nindyate, na tu svasmād abhyadhikatvena svāmino bhedaṁ vidvān niṣkāma-atat-bhakto ‘pi, tasya juṣṭam ityādinā stutatvāt \| yad eveheti \| brahma-vyuheṣu bheda-darśino yadā hy eveti \| brahma –nisṭā-viccheda-kartuś ca saṁsṛti-bhayam uktam \|\|89\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(refutation of text 17)**

**It is not proper to conclude nondifference because of passages such as** ***yo ‘nyām*** **(text 18), juṣṭam yadā yadā} Yad eva hi yad eveha. \|\|89\|\|**

### Text 90

*etena “tvaṁ vā” ityādikam api vyākhyātam, upāsakānām iyādau ca na nirviśeṣatvaṁ śraddheyam—uttaratrātma-mūrtaye brahmānandaika-vigraha iti saviśeṣatvā-śravaṇāt, “vedāham” ityādau “aditya-varṇaṁ tamasaḥ parastāt” it śruteś ca \| tasmāt prākṛta-rūpam ādāya tat saṅgamanīyam \|\|90\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Refutation of text 19 and 20)**

***tvaṁ vā aham asmi bhagavo devate ahaṁ vai tvam asi bhagavo devate*** **has already been explained.**

**upāsanakānāṁ kāryārthaṁ brahmaṇo rupa-kalpanā**

**A form of Brahman is manufactured for use of the worshippers, though Brahman is only one conscious entity, without parts and without a body. Rāma Upaniṣad**

**This should not be interpreted to mean that Brahman is without qualities since later in the same Upaniṣad words denoting a form of the Lord and a form of bliss are mentioned (*****ātma*****-*****mūrtaye*** **and** ***brahmānandaika-vigraha*****).**

**Śruti also says:**

**vedāham etaṁ puruṣam mahantam**  
**āditya-varṇaṁ tamasaḥ parastāt**  
**tam eva viditvāti mṛtyum eti**  
**nānyaḥ panthāḥ vidyate 'yanāya**

**I know this great person, shining like the sun, beyond darkness. Knowing him alone one surpasses death. There is no other path to tread. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

**Thus the statements negating form refer to material forms. \|\|90\|**

### Text 91

*ya tu bhedam āvidyakaṁ mokse tadābhāvād ity ucyate tad rabhasābhidhānam “nirañjanaḥ paramam sāmyam upaiti” ityādi-vākyais tatra tasyodāhṛtatvāt “karma-kṣaye yāti sa tat tato ‘nyaḥ” “so ’śnute sarvān kāmān” ityādi-vākyāc ca \| tasmāt pāramārthike jīveśa-bhedḥaḥ \|\|91\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is rash to say that, since there is no differences in liberation, difference arises from ignorance. There is difference in liberation according to statements like the following:**

***yadā paśyaḥ paśyate rukma-varṇam nirañjanaḥ paramaṁ sāmyam upaiti***

**When the seer sees the golden person, he, being pure, attains a form similar to the supreme. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 3.1.3)**

**karma-kṣaye yāti sa tattvato ‘nyaḥ**

**With destruction of karma, the jīva attains another existence. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

**so ’śnute sarvān kāmān**

**He enjoys all pleasures. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.1.1)**

**Thus jīva and the Lord are different in the liberated state. \|\|91\|\|**

### Text 92

*ata evaṁ smarati “yatheśvarasya jīvasya satyo bhedo viniścayāt \| evaṁ eva hi me vācaṁ satyākarum ihārhasi \| yatheśvarasya jīvaś ca satya-bhedau parasparam \| tena satyena māṁ devās trāyantu saha-keśava” iti \|92\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**yatheśvarasya jīvasya bhedo satyo viniścayāt**  
**evam eva hi me vācaṁ kartum ihārhasi**

**You should announce in this world that the Lord and the jīva are different and real.**

**yatheśvarasya jīvaś ca satya-bhedau parasparam \|**  
**tena satyena māṁ devās trāyantu saha-keśava**

**As much as the jīva and the Lord are different, with that certainty may the devatās and Viṣṇu deliver me! Bhāllaveya-śruti \|\|92\|\|**

## Chapter Seven

**Refuting Kevalānubhūti-vāda.**

### Text 1

*atrāhuḥ --“ekam evādvitīyam” “satyaṁ jñāmam anantam” ityādiṣu śaktyādi-viśeṣāpratīteḥ sajātīyādi-bheda-traya-śūnyaṁ jñānam eva paraṁ tattvam iti pratīyate \| tasya ca tarhy evaikādi-pada-labdhena bheda-trayābhāvenānantatvaṁ satyatvaṁcopapadyeta yadi jñānam ity etad-bhāva-sādhanaṁ bhavet \| itirathā kāraka-sādhane jñātṛ-jñeya-tat-sādhanaiḥ pravibhāge sāntatvam eva syāt \| tathā kartṛ-sādhane jñānasya kartṛtayā vikriyamāṇasya karaṇa-sādhane ca vāsyādi-vaj jaḍatayā pratipannasyāsatyatvaṁ syāt \| tasmāt bhāva-sādhanam eva syāt \| tathāca saṁvid-anubhūti-jñapti-paryāyaṁ jñānaṁ nāma tattvaṁ nirviśeṣam eva; tad anyat sarvām mithyā-bhūtam eva “neha nānāsti kiṇcana” ityādeḥ \| jñāna-svarūpo bhagavān; yato ‘sau viśeṣa-mūrtir na tu vastu-bhūta ityādeś ceti cet \|\|1\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The impersonalist gives the following argument. In statements like** ***ekam evadvitīyam*** **and** ***satyam jñānam anantam*****, what is understood is the supreme tattva, devoid of three types of difference, because no particular śaktis (qualities etc.) are perceived.**

**If the word** ***jñānam*** **indicates infinity (*****anantatvam*****) and existence (*****satyatvam*****), devoid of three types of difference as indicated by the word** ***eka*** **(one), then it would describe a condition.**

**If jñāna were to indicate action (he knows), then it indicates a divided being, with a knower and an object of knowledge. If it indicates a knower (producing knowledge) with a change involved, or if it indicates a method (with a material instrument), then, being subject to change, it would become temporary. Thus, it must indicate only a state. It is a special tattva with no qualities. It is also called saṁvit, anubhuti and jñapti. Everything else is false, as shown in statements like** ***neha nānāsti kiṇcana*****. Bhagavān, whose svarūpa is knowledge, is false, since he has a form. \|\|1\|\|**

### Text 2

*naitat paṭutaraṁ bhāva-rūpasyāpi tasya tattvasya “gale grhīta” nyāyena śaktis tāvad avaśyaṁ svīkaranīyā jagadādi-kārya-dārśanānyathānupapattyā tasyā avaśyambhāvāt \| na cārthāpatti-mātraṁ tatra pramāṇam—“ya eko ‘vaṛno bahudhā śakti-yogāt” ityādi-śuti-smṛtibhyaḥ \| nanv astu śaktiḥ, kintu kalpitatvān mithyaiva seti can maivaṁ bhramitavyam ̮̮”parāsya śaktiḥ” ityādinā, “śaktayaḥ sarva-bhāvānām” ityādinā ca tasyāh svābhāvikatvokteḥ, kalpakānirūpaṇāc ca \| nāpy ukta-yuktyāsāntatvaṁ “yac ca kiṁcij jagat sarvaṁ” ityādiṣu bahir-antaś ca vyāpti-śruteḥ \| “tileṣu tailaṁ dadhinīva sapiḥ” iti sarva-karmikām tām pratyāyati dṛṣṭāntena \| na ca kartrādi-sādhane sati vikārādyāpattyā tasyāsatyatva-prasaṅgaḥ \| yato nīrūpasyaiva tasyātarkya-svarūpa-śaktyā kartṛtvādi-bhajamānasyāpi svarūpa-sāmarthyaṁ pracakāstīti śruti-siddhatvāt \|\|2\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is not a skilful conclusion. One must accept śakti in Brahman. Without śakti, one cannot explain the creation of the universe. One cannot say that the statements such as** ***ya eko ‘vaṛno bahudhā śakti-yogāt*** **(the one Lord becomes many by śakti, Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad), indicating śakti in Brahman, are just arthāpatti (interpretation).**

**“Even if I accept śakti, it is imaginary and thus false.” One should not make this mistake. The scriptures say that śakti in inherent in the Lord and it is not produced by Lord (since it is eternal and intrinsic to the Lord).**

***parāsya śaktir vividhaiva śrūyate***  
***svā-bhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca***

**His supreme śakti is manifold. His actions are naturally revealed with knowledge and strength. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8**

***śaktayaḥ sarva-bhāvānām acintya-jñāna-gocarāḥ \|***  
***yato’to brahmaṇas tās tu sargādyā bhāva-śaktayaḥ \|***  
***bhavanti tapasāṁ śreṣṭha pāvakasya yatoṣṇatā \|\|***

**O best of ascetics! The śaktis of things in this world are in the realm of inconceivability. From these arise the powers of Brahman to create the universe. It is like the power of heat in fire. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.3.2)**

**Since the śakti is intrinsic, the śakti does not make the Lord divisible into parts, since the Lord is spread everywhere inside and outside.**

**yac ca kiñcit jagat sarvaṁ dṛśyate śrūyate’pi vā \|**  
**antar bahiś ca tat sarvaṁ vyāpya nārāyaṇaḥ sthitaḥ \|\|**

**Nārāyaṇa, pervading everything, is situated inside and outside of whatever is seen or heard in the world. Taittirīya-āraṇyaka**

**The Lord pervades like oil in sesame seeds or ghee in yogurt. One cannot argue that the Lord would be illusory on account of a change by being the knower or doer, since the Lord’s svarūpa is capable of actions without change, by his inconceivable śakti. This is proved by śruti. \|\|2\|\|**

### Text 3

*“nanu jñatṛtvaṁ nāma jñāna-kriyā-kartṛtvaṁ tac ca vikāra-rūpaṁ jaḍam asatyam eva—ahaṁ vijñātety aham arthābhedena pratīteḥ \| aham arthas tv anātmaiva sthūlo ‘ham ityādi-dehābhedena pratyayāt, taṁ vināpi suṣupty ādāv ātmānubhava-darśanāc ca \| tasmād ahaṅkāra-vad deha-vac ca vijñātṛtvam ātmani, śuddhe ‘dhyastam iti cen mandam etat \| jñana-guṇāśrayatvam eva jñātṛtvaṁ, jñānam tu nityasyāpy autpattika-dharmatvān nityam \| śrutiś ca “parāsya śaktiḥ” ityādyā, “avināśī vā are ayam ātmānucchitti-dharmā” ityādyā ca \| jñāna-rūpasya jñānāśrayatvaṁ tu prakāśa-svarūpasya raveh prakāśakatvavad aviruddham iti bhāvaḥ \| sādhanatāpi na virudhyate \| evaṁ ca “vijñātāram are kena vijānīyāt” ityādi-śruty avirodhaḥ \| tasmāj jñānādi-śaktimad eva brahma na tv anubhūti-saṁvit-paryāyaṁ jñāna-matram \|\|3\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“A knower indicates that someone performs the act of knowing. That is a change in Brahman, making it material and illusory. In knowing, one identifies oneself as the knower. This is false ego and material, and is the same as identifying with a material body. We can see identity as ātmā in deep sleep when ahaṅkāra is absent. Being a knower is imposed on the pure ātmā like ahaṅkāra and the body.”**

**This is foolish. The knower is the shelter of the quality of knowing. Knowledge is the eternal quality of the eternal ātmā or Brahman. Śruti says:**

***parāsya śaktir vividhaiva śrūyate***  
***svā-bhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca***

**He has no body and no senses. No one is equal to him or higher. His supreme power is manifold. His actions are naturally revealed with knowledge and strength. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8**

**avināśī vā are ayam ātmānucchitti-dharmā**

**The ātmā is indestructible and has indestructible qualities. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.5.14**

**It is not contradictory to say that the Lord, the form of knowledge, is the shelter of knowledge, just as the sun, the form of light, is the revealer of light. Having qualities of knowing is not contrary to the Lord being a state of knowledge. And it is not contrary to śruti.**

**vijñātāram are kena vijānīyāt**

**By what senses can the knower be known? Brhād-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad**

**Therefore, Brahman possesses śaktis such as knowledge and not just knowledge, anubhūti or saṁvit. \|\|3\|\|**

### Text 4

*nanv anubhūtitvaṁ nāma vartamāna-dāśāyāṁ sva-sattayaiva svāśrayaṁ prati prakāśamanatvaṁ tayaiva sva-viṣaya-sādhanaṁ vā bhavatu \| tatrobhayatra tanmātra-vādinaḥ śaktimattva-prasaṅgaḥ \| visāya-prakāśanatvenaiva tasyāḥ svayaṁprakāśatvaṁ tvayā sādhitam \| tattva-svabhāva-virahe tattvāsiddher anubhavāntarāgocaratvāc ca tucchataiva syāt \|\|4\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“Awareness in our present state means revealing one’s own self or becoming aware of other objects by the fact of one’s own existence.” But in both these cases, the proponents who propose consciousness alone must accept that consciousness possesses śakti. By its ability to reveal an object, one must admit the self-manifesting property of that existent consciousness. Without such a property, consciousness would lose its self-manifesting function. It would become something unconscious, since it could not be aware of anything. \|\|4\|\|**

### Text 5

*nanu bodha-svarūpāyās tasyā na ko ‘pit bodhyo dharma iti cet na, tvayāiva nityatva-svayaṁprakāśatvādi-dharmāṇāṁ pramāṇa-siddhānāṁ svīkṛtatvāt \| na ca teṣām anityatva-jaḍatvādy-abhāva-tātparyād iṣṭa-siddhiḥ, tathā bhutair api caitanya-dharmair iṣṭa-vyāghātāt \| tasyām svarūpātirekeṇa nānityatvādi-pratyanīkatvam ity abhāva-rūpasya dharmasyānaṅgīkāre tan-niṣeḍhoktyā kim api noktaṁ syāt \|\|5\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“That awareness has no quality of being aware of anything.” That cannot be, since you accept that this consciousness has qualities such as being self-manifesting (being aware of itself), which are stated in scriptures. You cannot maintain your position by saying that consciousness is merely absence of unconsciousness and absence of temporariness. That contradicts your own position since you give consciousness attributes such as a self-manifesting nature. And not accepting any qualities except the absence of qualities such as temporariness, and insisting only on the svarūpa of awareness, have no meaning at all. \|\|5\|\|**

### Text 6

*kiṁca sā sidhyati na vā? ādye sadharmakatā tasyāḥ praptā; antye kha-puṣpādivat tucchatāpattiḥ \| siddhir eva seti cet kasya kaṁ pratīti vācyaṁ \| yadi na kasyacit kiṁcit prati sā tarhi na siddhiḥ \| siddhir hi kasyacit kaṁcit prati bhavati \| anubhūtair iti cet, tarhy anubhūti-siddhyor bhedāv agateḥ sā tasyāḥ śaktir evety avasīyate, na tu svarūpa-mātraṁ \| tad evam āgataṁ jñāma-mātra-svarūpe ‘pi svābhāvika-jñātrṭva-nityatvādi-dharmakatvam \|\|6\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Can this awareness as stated actually function or not? If it does, then it has attained qualities. If it does not, then it is meaningless, like a flower in the sky. If you say that awareness functions simply as awareness, one should ask whose awareness, and awareness of what. If you say it is awareness of no object and no one’s awareness, then awareness does not function at all.**

**Awareness functions with a subject and object of awareness. If you say that awareness functions by being aware of awareness by awareness, this involves difference in awareness, which involves śakti in the awareness. It is not awareness alone. It must be concluded that even in the svarūpa of jñāna, there are inherent qualities of being a knower and being eternal etc. \|\|6\|\|**

### Text 7

*yat tu “sthulo ‘ham” iti dehābhedena pratīteḥ suṣuptau tad-virahitātma-pratipādanāc ca dehavad aham-arthasyānātmatvam uktaṁ tan na; tasyaiva śuddhātmatvāt \| tathāhi—na tāvad aham-artho ‘sau yuṣmat-pratyaya-gocaro jaḍo bhavitum arhati—asmat-pratyaya-gocaratvāt, svasmai prakāśamānatvāc ca \| yaḥ khalu svasmai prakāṣate sa tv aham ity eva prakāśate \| yas tv aham iti na prakāśāte nāsau svasmai prakāśate yathā ghatādiḥ \| tasmāt svaṁ prati sva-sattayaiva siddhyan na jaḍo ‘ham-artha evātmā \| yo hy aham jānāmiti pratīti-siddhaṁ jñātāram; yuṣmad-arthaṁ bruyāt tasya tu “prasūr me vandhyā” itivad vyavahitārthaḥ vyāhāraḥ \| asyaivāham-arthasya jñātuḥ kevalaṁ jñānaṁ sukhaṁ cāvabhāsate \| “ahaṁ jānāmy ahaṁ sukhī” iti jñāna-svarūpasyāpi tasya jñātṛtvaṁ prakāśa-vastunaḥ sūryādeḥ prakāśakatvavad aviruddham ity abhyadhāyi prāk \|\|7\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“One thinks ‘I am fat’ with the thought that I am not different from my body. In deep sleep I am aware of ātmā without the bodily conception. But the concept of I is not ātmā in that case also.”**

**That is not a proper conclusion. That is the pure ātmā identifying itself with its existence. This sense of I is not some material object which you say is anātmā or “not ātmā.” Rather it is perceivable to the self and revealed to the self. When it is revealed to the self, one thinks “I exist.” What does not produce the sense of I, such as a pot, does not reveal itself to the self. Therefore, that which reveals I to the self is not dull matter, but ātmā. To say the knower who understands “I know” is material is meaningless, like saying “My mother is barren.” The knower, I, experiences the happiness of kevala-jñānam. Being a knower in jñāna-svarūpa with the sense of “I know and I am happy” is not contrary to the very substance of knowledge like the sun revealing light. \|\|7\|\|**

### Text 8

*nanu jñāṇa-mātra ātmany aham-artho ‘dhyasyat iti cen na, adhyāsakābhāvāt \| anahaṅkārasya jñāna-mātrasya jaḍasya cāhaṅkārasya tat-kartṛtvaṁ na sambhavati \| na ca tasmin aham-arthe jñāna-cchāyāpattiḥ dvayor nairūpyāt \| na cāyah-piṇḍa-vahni-samparka-kṛtauṣṇyavat jñāna-mātra-samparka-kṛtaṁ jñātṛtvaṁ tasmin aham-arthe mantavyaṁ—auṣṇyavat tad-dharmāpratipatteḥ \| nanv asāv ahaṅkāraḥ svānusyūtaṁ taj-jñānam abhivyañjayan jñātṛtvaṁ prāpnotīti cen maivam—ahaṅkārādi-dharmiṇas tasya dharmatvānupapatteḥ; svayaṁ jyotiṣo jñānasya jaḍātmakāhaṅkāra-vyañjakatvāyogāt; yad uktaṁ—“śāntāṅgara ivādityam ahakāro jaḍātmakaḥ \| svayaṁ jyotiṣam ātmānaṁ na vyanaktīti yuktimat” iti \| tad-āyatta-prakāśenāhaṅkāreṇa tasya prakāśyatvāsambhavaḥ \|\|8\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“The sense of I is imposed upon the ātmā, which is only knowledge.” That is not so, since there is nothing existing to cause that imposition. And it is not possible that this inferior ahaṅkāra could act upon the ātmā, pure knowledge, which is devoid of ahaṅkāra. One cannot claim that ahaṅkāra is a shadow of knowledge since both knowledge and ahaṅkāra have no form which could cause a shadow or be a shadow. One cannot say that the ahaṅkāra arises as a knower by proximity to the ātmā which is pure knowledge, just as a piece of iron becomes hot in proximity to fire, since the ātmā should not have any quality as fire has the quality of heat.**

**“Ahaṅkāra, revealing knowledge of ātmā, becomes a knower.” That cannot be, because consciousness, the source of qualities like ahaṅkāra, then becomes a quality of ahaṅkāra. Knowledge itself is illumination. It is self-manifesting. It is not proper to say that unconscious ahaṅkāra can be the revealer of knowledge.**

**śāntāṅgara ivādityam ahaṅkāro jaḍātmakaḥ \|**  
**svayaṁ jyotiṣam ātmānaṁ na vyanaktīti yuktimat**

**As a cool ember cannot reveal the sun, so the unconscious ahaṅkāra cannot reveal self-manifesting knowledge. Yamunācārya**

**Ahaṅkāra depends on knowledge. Therefore it is not possible that ahaṅkāra manifests knowledge. \|\|8\|\|**

### Text 9

*nanu mihira-kiraṇa-gaṇaysa svābhivyañjya-pāṇi-talābhivyañjyatvavat jñānābhivyañjyatvaṁ jñānasya bhaved iti cen na, tasya pāṇi-talābhivyañjyatvābhāvāt \| pāṇi-tāla-pratihata-gatir asau bāhulyāt svayam eva sphuṭataram upalabhyate iti tad-bāhulya-mātra-hetutvāt pāṇi-talasya tad-vyañjaka-svabhāvaḥ \| tasmāt svata eva jñātṛtayā siddhyan aham-artha eva pratyag ātmā, na tu jñānam-mātraṁ—“sukham aham asvāpsaṁ na kincid avedisām” iti suptotthitasya parāmarśāt \| tatrāpy aham-arthatā sukhitā jñātṛṭā cāvagamyate \| tadā tamoguṇābhibhavāt sphuṭatarā nāvagatīh \| itthaṁ ca dehavad aham-arthasyānātmatvaṁ parihṛtaṁ \| na hi supto dehas tad-virahito veha pratīyate \|\|9\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“Just as the palm of the hand, being lit by the sun’s rays, is able to manifest the sun, so ahaṅkāra, manifested by jñāna, then reveals knowledge.” That cannot be, since the palm of the hand cannot actually reveal the sun. The rays are interrupted by the dull hand and become many and the hand appears to be an illuminating object. The hand is only the cause of the rays becoming many and thus appears to be the cause of revealing the sun. Thus the ātmā with a sense of I functions as a knower by its very nature. The ātmā is not jñāna-mātra, just awareness.**

**On arising from sleep one reflects, “I slept happily and was not aware of anything.” In that state, one can understand that there is a sense of I, there is happiness and there is a knower. However, because of tamoguṇa in deep sleep, one does not have clear understanding. Thus one cannot say that the sense of I is false or material, like identity of the body. In the state of sleep however one is not aware whether one is the body or whether one is devoid of the sense of I. \|\|9\|\|**

### Text 10

*eṣa cāhaṁ bhāvaḥ na saṁsṛtir hetuḥ—śuddha-svarūpānubandhitvāt, kintu tādṛśatayāvagatis tato mocayaty eva tad-virodhitvāt \| yady ayam upādhir eva syāt tadā mukto vinaśyeyam iti vijānaṁs tat-kathāprasaṅgād api jano ‘pagacchet \| nivṛttākhila-kleśo ‘kṣaya-sukha-bhāk tejasvī bhaveyam ity evaṁ mokṣa-kāṅkṣiṇaḥ śravaṇādau pravṛttiḥ \| sāpi na syāt, aham-arthasyātmano mokṣe vināśa-bhaya-prāpteḥ \| na ca naṣṭe ‘pi mayi kiṁcij jñānam anuvartiṣyate sa evātmeti vācyam evaṁ pravṛtty-adarśanāt \| tatra pramāṇābhāvāc ca \| kintv atra pramāṇaṁ vedaḥ sākṣī vā? nādyaḥ--asvīkaṛāt, na dvtīyaḥ—tadānīṁ tasya nāśāt \| sākṣiṇo ‘ham-arthatvaṁ pratipādayiṣyate \| tasmād aham-artho jñātaiva prayag ātmeti susthiram \|\|10\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This spiritual sense of I is not the cause of saṁsāra, since it is related to the pure svarūpa of the ātmā. Identifying oneself as a jīva, full of knowledge, is a cause of liberation and destroys the material false identity. If this sense of I were an upādhi (false imposition on ātmā), then one would hear that in the liberated state one’s identity would be destroyed. Hearing that, people would give up the quest for liberation. The aspirant could not be attracted to hearing that in the liberated state he will be free of all suffering and experience indestructible happiness, but rather he would develop fear of destroying his sense of I in liberation.**

**One cannot say that when the sense of I is destroyed, there remains a small portion of knowledge with no qualities, which is ātmā, since one does not see this happening in nirviśeṣa-jñāna and there is no proof for such an event taking place. If you say there is proof, is it the Veda or some witness? Vedas cannot be accepted as the proof of this by anyone. It cannot be a witness, since that witness (I) has been destroyed at the time of liberation. The sense of I will thus be established as the witness. In this way, it is established that the sense of I is the knower, and is intrinsic to the individual ātmā. \|\|10\|\|**

### Text 11

*ataeva labdhā-vijñānasyāpi vāmadevāder evaṁ vyāhāraḥ \| “tadvaitat paśyaṇ ṛṣir vāmadevaḥ pratipede—ahaṁ manur abhavaṁ sūryaś ca” iti, “prāṇo smīti prajñātmā”iti, “naṣṭo mohaḥ smrṭir labdhā tvat-prasādān mayācuta \| sthito ‘smi gata-sandehaḥ kariṣye vacanaṁ tava” iti \| parasya brahmaṇaś ca mukta-mṛgyasya “brahma vā idam agra āsīt tadātmānam avaidahaṁ brahmāsmi” iti “seyaṁ devatāiksaṭa hantāham imās tisro devatāḥ” iti “ahaṁ sarvasya prabhavaḥ” iti “aham evāsam evāgre”iti caivam ādiḥ \|\|11\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Though Vāmadeva and others attained knowledge, they maintained the sense of I in their statements.**

**tadvaitat paśyaṇ ṛṣir vāmadevaḥ pratipede—ahaṁ manur abhavaṁ sūryaś ca**

**Seeing this, the sage Vāmadeva constantly repeated, "I have become the moon and the sun."**

**Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad**  
**prāṇo smīti prajñātmā**

**I am** ***prāṇa*****, the intelligent** ***ātmā*****. Kauśītaki-brāhmaṇa**

**naṣṭo mohaḥ smrṭir labdhā tvat-prasādān mayācuta \|**  
**sthito ‘smi gata-sandehaḥ kariṣye vacanaṁ tava**

**My delusion has been destroyed, and my correct vision has been restored by your mercy, O Acyuta. I remain without doubts, and will follow your instructions. BG 18.73**

**The Lord, who is sought for liberation, states:**

**brahma vā idam agra āsīt tadātmānam avaidahaṁ brahmāsmi**

**Brahman existed in the beginning; one should know that ātmā; I am that Brahman. (Bṛhad-araṇyaka Upaniṣad 1.4.10)**

**seyaṁ devatāikṣata hantāham imās tisro devatāḥ**

**The Lord desired "Let me enter these three** ***devatās*** **(fire, water and earth. Chāndogya Upaniṣad**

**ahaṁ sarvasya prabhavaḥ**

**I am the source of everything. BG 10.8**

**aham evāsam evāgre**

**I alone, who am non-different from that which is superior to all cause and effect, existed previous to creation of the universe. (SB 2.9.33) \|\|11\|\|**

### Text 12

*evaṁ siddhe yas tu “bhūmir apo ‘nalo vāyuḥ” ityādinā kṣetrāntarbhūto ‘haṅkāraḥ smaryate, sa khalu ātmani dehe ‘haṅkaṛa-kāraṇa-hetutvāt tathopadiṣṭo bodhyaḥ \| tasmād ahaṅkāra-śabdasya cvi-pratyayam utpādya vyutpādanaṁ draṣṭavyam \| etad ahakāra-dvayaṁ tu “sukham aham” ityādi-śrutau, “sanne yad indriya-gaṇe ‘hami ca prasupte kūṭastha āśayam ṛte tad anusmṛtir na” it smṛtau ca darśitam \| upādhy-abhimānātmakasya prasuptatvāt “sukham aham” ity anena tad anusmṛtir na ity anena cātmano ‘haṁ-prayatena parmārśāc ca \| ataḥ “mām ahaṁ nājñāsiṣaṁ” ity asmin parāmarśe ‘py upādhy-abhimānino ‘jñāna-viṣayasya nānusandhir anyasya tv ajñāna-sākṣiṇo ‘nusandhir astīti \| suṣuptāv ajñāna-sākṣitvenātmās te iti hi bhavadīyā prakriyā \| sakṣitvaṁ khalu sākṣāt jñātṛtvaṁ eva –“sākṣāt draṣṭari saṁjñāyaṁ” iti pāṇini-smāraṇāt \| sa cāyaṁ sākṣī sākṣaj jānamīti pratīti-prasiddho ‘smad-artha eva \| mokṣe py aham-artho ‘nuvartate, itarathā tasmin pravṛttir anupapattir ity uktam \|\|12\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The spiritual ahaṅkāra has been established. In enumerating the elements such as earth, water, fire, air and ether, ahaṅkāra is enumerated among the material elements. That ahaṅkāra is so called because it produces a sense of identity of the self with the body. This is formed with the** ***cvi*** **affix added to aham. The two ahaṅkāras are illustrated in “I slept happily” and the following:**

**aṇḍeṣu peśiṣu taruṣv aviniściteṣu**  
**prāṇo hi jīvam upadhāvati tatra tatra**  
**sanne yad indriya-gaṇe 'hami ca prasupte**  
**kūṭa-stha āśayam ṛte tad-anusmṛtir naḥ**

**Prāṇa follows the jīva in bodies born from eggs, wombs, seeds and perspiration (material ahaṅkāra). When the senses and ahaṅkāra cease to function in deep sleep, the fixed ātmā alone remains, without the covering of the subtle body, but with memory of the experience of deep sleep (spiritual ahaṅkāra). SB 11.3.39**

**In remembering deep sleep, one does not recollect the material ahaṅkāra but uses another ahaṅkāra, that of the ātmā. In considering, “I did not known myself” one does not refer the false ego, covered with ignorance, but makes use of another sense of I, the witness of ignorance. In deep sleep the ātmā functions as witness of ignorance. The word witness or sākṣī means a direct knower as explained by Pāṇini in** ***sākṣāt draṣṭari saṁjñāyam*****.**

**The witness understands “I directly know.” This is sense of I. That sense of I, ahaṅkāra, remains even in liberation. If it were not so, there would be no desire to attain liberation. \|\|12\|\|**

### Text 13

*śrī-viṣṇu-purāṇo ‘p ahaṁ pratyaya-bodhyatātmanaḥ svīkṛtā--“yo bhavān yan nimittaṁ vā yad āgamana-kāraṇam \| tat sarvaṁ bruyatāṁ vidvan mahyam śuṛuṣave bhavān” ity etad rahūgaṇena pṛṣṭo bharatas tasmai ahaṁśabdasyāvaktavyatvam āha—“śruyatāṁ ko ‘ham ity eetad vaktuṁ bhūpa na śakyate” iti rahūgaṇas tasyāniruktvyatvam āha—ātman eṣa na doṣāya śabdo ‘ham iti yo dvijaḥ” iti; bharataḥ punas tat svīkaroti—“śabdo ‘ham iti doṣāya nātmany esā tathaiva tat” iti \| jihvādiṣu anātmasu ahaṁ śabdo na prayujyate tiy anugaditaṁ—“anātmany ātma-vijñanaṁ śabdo vā bhrānti-lakṣaṇaḥ” ityadibiḥ \|\|13\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In Viṣṇu Purāṇa the word** ***aham*** **indicates the ātmā. Rahūgaṇa questions Bharata:**

**yo bhavān yan nimittaṁ vā yad āgamana-kāraṇam \|**  
**tat sarvaṁ bruyatāṁ vidvan mahyam śuṛuṣave bhavān**

**I desire to know why you have come. Please tell everything to me, who am desirous to hear.**

**In response, Bharata tells him not to use the word I.**

**śruyatāṁ ko ‘ham ity etad vaktuṁ bhūpa na śakyate**

**Please hear, O king! One should not say “Who am I?”**

**Rahūgaṇa explain why he can use the word.**

**ātmany eṣa na doṣāya śabdo ‘ham iti yo dvijaḥ**

**O brāhmaṇa! There is no fault in using the word to denote ātmā.**

**Bharata accepts:**

**śabdo ‘ham iti doṣāya nātmany esā tathaiva tat**

**There is no fault in using the word I in relation to ātmā.**

**One should not use the word I in relation to the body etc.**

**anātmany ātma-vijñanaṁ śabdo vā bhrānti-lakṣaṇaḥ**

**One who uses the word I indicating ātmā in relation to things which are not ātmā is in illusion. \|\|13\|\|**

### Text 14

*nanv aham-arthaś ced ātmā, tarhi—“yad ānyo ‘sti paraḥ ko ‘pi mattaḥ pārthiva-sattama \| tadaiṣo ‘ham ayaṁ cānyo vaktum evam apīsyate \|\| yadā samasta-deheṣu pumān eko vyavasthitiaḥ \|tadā hi ko bhavān ko ‘ham ity etad viphalam vaca” ity uparitano granthaḥ kathaṁ saṅgacceta” maivaṁ tenāham-arthasyāpy ātmanaḥ svātantryābhimāno nirasyate \| tathā hi ahaṁ-śābdaḥ khalv aham jānāmy ahaṁ bhuñje ‘haṁ gacchāmīti svārthe svātantraṁ samarpayati \| tad asya jīvātmano na sambhavati, pāratantryāt \| tataś ca paramātmaiva tad abhimānam arhati na tu jīvaḥ \| sa u tadāyatta-vṛttikatvādinā tato nātiricyate \| na caivam aham-arthasya kṣatiḥ, īśvara-tantro ‘ham ity adi-rūpasyāviruddhasya tasyāhānāt \| tad evaṁ saty ayam arthaḥ \| matto mad ekāśrayān mad-īśvarād anyaḥ paraḥ svātantryābhimānī ko ‘pi mal-lakṣaṇo ‘nyo vātmāsti, tadā tad-vyavacchedenaiṣo ‘ham ayaṁ tad anya iti vaktum yujyeta \| na caivam astīty āha—yadetit \| pumān svātantryābhimānī vyavasthayā sthitaḥ, tadā ko bhavān iti bahuṣu samāneṣu nirdhāra-rūpaṁ te praśnavat so ‘ham iti vacaś ca viphalam \|*  
*tad evaṁ sati “śruyatām” ity atrāpy ayam evābhisandhiḥ \| tasmāt svātantryābhimānino jīvasyātra nirasyate, na tv aham-arthatā—tathā sati svokyāhaṁ-śabda-vyākopāpatteḥ \|\|14\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**You may say that the I is the ātmā but this contradicts later statements in the same text:**

**yad anyo ‘sti paraḥ ko ‘pi mattaḥ pārthiva-sattama \|**  
**tadaiṣo ‘ham ayaṁ cānyo vaktum evam apīṣyate \|\|**  
**yadā samasta-deheṣu pumān eko vyavasthititaḥ \|**  
**tadā hi ko bhavān ko ‘ham ity etad viphalam vaca**

**O king! If there are others who are the same as me or if there are others who are different from me then one can use words like I and he. Since only one entity is situated in all bodies, words like you and I are useless. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

**There is no contradiction. What is refuted is using the word I to indicate an independent being. The word I used in statements like “I know I enjoy and I go” indicate an independent being. That is not possible for the jīva, since he is dependent. The Lord can identify himself in this way, but not the jīva. The words simply mean that the jīva is dependent on the Lord. This does no harm to the use of the word I, for without be contradictory one can say “I am dependent on the Lord.”**

**Therefore the meaning of the statements is this. If there were some independent being with qualities like mine, or if there is someone separate like me, then one could speak of I and someone else. But that is not so. Since individual jīvas, thinking they are each independent, reside in all bodies, words like “Who are you” and “I am so and so” are useless. Thus Bharata says “Please listen.” Thus the idea of an independent jīva is rejected but not the sense of I. Otherwise Bharata using the word I would also be meaningless. \|\|14\|\|**

### Text 15

*upasaṁhāre ‘pi dehāhaṁbhāvaṁ nirasya śuddhāhambhāvaḥ sthāpitaḥ \| “samastāvayavebhyas tvaṁ prthāk bhūya vyavasthitḥaḥ \| ko ‘ham ity eva nipuṇaṁ bhutvā cintaya pārthiva” “rājāhaṁ” “prajāh imāḥ” ityādi-dhīr bhrāntir eva--- tat-tad-vilakṣaṇaś cit-sukha-vapur ātmāham iti tu pramety arthaḥ \| “evaṁ vyavasthite tattve mayā ‘ham iti bhāṣitum \| pṛthak kāraṇa-niṣpādyaṁ śakyate nṛpa te katham\|\|” evaṁ dehādi-vyatirekitayātmātattve ‘vasthite mayā rājñaiṣa śatrur hataḥ, tathainam ahaṁ haniṣyāmīti bhāṣituṁ kathaṁ śakyate yadīdaṁ prthak-kāraṇena prākrṭāhaṅkāreṇa nispādyaṁ bhaved iti \|\|15\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In the conclusion, material ahaṅkāra is rejected and the pure identity of ātmā is established.**

**samastāvayavebhyas tvaṁ prthāk bhūya vyavasthitḥaḥ \|**  
**ko ‘ham ity eva nipuṇaṁ bhutvā cintaya pārthiva**

**O king! You should establish the self which is different from all your limbs. Being skilful, you should contemplate “Who am I?”**

**Thinking “I am a king” and “These are my citizens” is mistaken. One should think “I am ātmā, full of knowledge and bliss, different from this.”**

**evaṁ vyavasthite tattve mayā ‘ham iti bhāṣitum \|**  
**pṛthak kāraṇa-niṣpādyaṁ śakyate nṛpa te katham\|\|**

**Situated in this consciousness of ātmā (*****evaṁ vyavaṣthite*****) I can speak of I. O king! How can I speak to you from consciousness produced from material ahaṅkāra (*****pṛthak-kāraṇa*****)—thinking “That enemy was killed by me” or “I will kill this person”? Viṣṇu Purāṇa \|\|15\|\|**

### Text 16

*ihāpare tv evam āhuḥ—sarva-bhūteṣu sthitasyātma-jātasya jñānādi-rūpeṇaikākāratvāt vyāvartaka-jātyādy abhāvena yo bhavān so’ ham ityādi praśnottareṇa ghaṭate ity āha –yad ānyo ‘stīti \| paro vilakṣaṇa-jātyādimān \| sarveṣu deheṣv ekākāra-vyakti-nānānatvaṁ aṅgīkrtya tad-vaisādṛśyaṁ niṣiddhaṁ; itaratā ekatrānya-para-pada-dvayāsaṅgater iti \|\|16\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The word “other” in the verse (*****yad anyo ‘sti*****) should be explained as follows. In all beings, there is situated an ātmā. All the ātmās are one, in the sense of each having a form of knowledge, devoid of distinction of being different species, which is apparent in discussing while using statements like “That is you” and “This is me.” The word** ***para*** **indicates a being who is of a different species. Difference of ātmā in all bodies, accepting various types of soul in different bodies, is forbidden. Otherwise, the two words (which often have the same meaning) would not be used in the same sentence. \|\|16\|\|**

### Text 17

*yat tu sann ayam anubhuyate ceti sarveṣāṁ padārthānāṁ sattānubhūti-ghaṭitatva-vīkṣaṇāt sarvatra tayoḥ siddhiḥ—sanmātraṁ satyam anuvṛttatvāt \| śabda-sparśādikaṁ tu vyāvṛttatvād asatyaṁ rajju-bhujaṅgādivat rajjur yathādhiṣṭhānatayānuvṛttyā satyā, bhujaṅgādikaṁ tu vyāvṛttatvāt asatyaṁ tathaitad draṣṭavyam—jñāna-svarūpam akhilaṁ jagad ityādi-smaraṇāc ca \| yad rūpā hy anubhūtiḥ bhedāṅgīkāreṇānavasthānāt, svataḥ-siddhā cānubhūtitvāt \| paratas tat-siddhau śabdādivaj jāḍya-prasaṅgaḥ, na cotpatti-vināśau tasyāḥ śakyau vaktum—asākṣikayor asiddheḥ na ca saiva tayoḥ sākṣiṇī-svakīyayos tayoḥ svenaiva grahītam aśakyatvāt \|\|17\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(argument of kevalānubhūti)**

**What exists is an appearance in our awareness. Since all words are experienced as existing, both awareness and existence are realized through all words. Words simply follow after existence. What is different from this is non-existent. A rope continues to exist and is true, whereas a snake, being negated, is not true. Thus, it is said in scriptures that the whole world is simply awareness. To accept difference is an unsettled condition. The awareness is perfect in itself. To accept it as anything else would make it material like words.**

**One cannot speak of a witness of creation or destruction of this awareness. If there is no witness, creation and destruction of awareness do not exist. One also cannot say that awareness is the witness of creation and destruction since it cannot be the witness of its own creation and destruction. \|\|17\|\|**

### Text 18

*na ca tāṁ grahītum anyānubhūtir apekṣyā—sva-sattayaiva prakāśamānatvāt \|na hi śabdādy-anubhūtis tadādivad evāprakāśā pratīyate; na cārtha-gatāt prakāśātiśayāl liṅgāt tasyā bhaved anumānaṁ, śabdādau tad-bhinnasya prakāśasyānupapannatvāt ; ubhaya-sammatayā tayaiva sarva-vyavahāra-nirvāhe taṁ klpter anyāyatvāt; na ca sā nānānubhūtir ity ekākareṇāvabhāsāt \| prayogaś ca—vivādādhyāsitā anubhūtir autpattika-bheda-śūnyā vinopādhi-parāmārśam avibhāvyamāna-bhedatvāt khavat \| śabdānubhūtiḥ sparśānubhūter na bhidyate ‘nubhūtitvād gandhānubhūtivad iti \|\|18\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**To realize awareness there is no dependence on another awareness. It reveals itself by its own existence. Like words, it is self-revealing. Words do not depend on inference of some meaning through additional devices. The meaning does not depend on something other than the word itself. With these two factors, it is not necessary to make some inference in order to produce a result. This awareness is not many with variety, since it manifests in one form. It has no differences which would arise from being created at different times. It is devoid of all upādhis since it is devoid of all conceptions, like ether. Awareness of words is not different from awareness by touch, since it is only awareness, like awareness of a fragrance. \|\|18\|\|**

### Text 19

*tasmān nityā sva-prakāśā nirviśeṣādvitīyā caiṣanubhūtiḥ \| jīvasyeśvarasya ca svarūpam etad eva \| ito ‘nyat sarvam iha māyayā vijṛmbhitam iti jalpanti \| tac cānubhūti-svarūpasyātmano ‘nubavitṛtvāhaṁprayaya-bodhyatva-viṣaya-prakāśakatva svabhāva-hetuka-svayaṁ—prakāśakatva-pratipādanenaiva nirastaṁ veditayvam \|\|19\|\|*

**Перевод:**
