**Thus, awareness (anubhūti) is an eternal self-manifesting svarūpa which has no qualities and is the only existing entity. It is jīva and īśvara. Anything different from this awareness is all produced by illusion. This is the claim of the kevalānubhūti-vādī.**

**(the refutation)**

**All this is refuted by the fact that they also claim that awareness itself becomes aware of the svarūpa of ātmā which is awareness, that this awareness produces the sense of I, that it reveals objects of awareness, and that it is self-manifesting by its nature. \|\|19\|\|**

### Text 20

*kiṁca śabdānubhūty uttare sparśānubhūti-kāle vyāvṛttasya śabdasyāsatyatvaṁ ākhyātuṁ na śakyate – tadānīm eva tasyaivānyair anubhūyamānatvāt \| jñānāprasārād yugapad-anekāgrahaḥ \| prasṛta-jñānāḥ khalv aṣṭāvadhāni-pratītayo dṛśyante na teṣāṁ viṣaya-vyāvṛttir asti \| krameṇa parāparātmānubhavena ca nānubhūtasya vyāvṛttatvād asatyatvaṁ—“nityo nityānām” ityādi-śruti-vyākopāt \| etenaiva tāv anubhūtyāṁ kalpitāv atas tathety uktir api nirastā kalpakānirūpaṇāc ca rajju-bhujaṅgādikaṁ tu na dṛṣṭānto, vaiṣamyāt \|\|20\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, when awareness of touch occurs after awareness of sound, one cannot say that sound which has been negated is untrue, since it is also experienced by other persons at that time. One cannot receive huge amounts of knowledge simultaneously but persons who are restricted in knowledge do not have cancellation of objects of awareness. Realizing the jīva and the Lord does not make them false because of cancellation of knowledge. This would contradict śruti:** ***nityo nityānām*****: the Lord is the chief eternal being among the eternal jīvas. One cannot say that this realization is imaginary, since one cannot define a producer of that imagination in that theory. One cannot give the example of the rope and snake (where false knowledge is cancelled by realizing a rope), since negating real objects is completely different. \|\|20\|\|**

### Text 21

*na ca sanmātram anubhūtir viṣaya-viṣayi-bhāvena bhedasya siddhatāt; na ca śabdādy-anubhūtis tathātma-vṛtti-jñānasyānityatva-svīkārāt; na ca svarūpānubhūtiḥ saḥ—yo ‘haṁ bālye sva-pitarāv anvabhuvaṁ sa evedānīṁ vārdhakye sva-naptṛn anubhāvamīti anubhāvitur evāsmad-arthasya tattvena pratyabhijñānāt; na cānubhūter anubhūti-gocaratve śabdādi-vaj jāḍyāpattiḥ—anubhūtitvenaiva gocaratvāt \| tasmāj jñāna-grāhyatve ‘pi na jādyapattiḥ “mṛgair mṛgāṇāṁ grahaṇam” ityādiḥ “jñāna-gamyaḥ purātanaḥ” ityādi-pramāṇāc ca \| na hi prakāśaika-rūpasya raveh prakāśātmaka-cakṣur viṣayatve saty aprakāśatāpattir dṛṣṭā \| evaṁ hānopādānādi-liṅgakānumāna-jñāna-gamyatvaṁ parānubhūteḥ siddhyati—aham anubhavāmīti svānubhūteś ca \| “aupaniṣadaḥ puruṣaḥ” “jñānaṁ jñeyaṁ jñāna-gamyam” ityaḍyā śruti-smṛtayo vijñāna-svarūpasya brahmaṇo vedyatvam uktvā bodhikā noparuddhyeran \| tasmād anubhavitaivātmā anubhūtis tu tasya dharmaḥ sa ca viṣaya-prakāśana-samaye svaprakāśaḥ pratītaḥ anyadā jñāna-gamyaś ca \| “na dṛṣṭer draṣṭāraṁ na mater mantāram” ity atra tu tathābhūtaṁ jīvaṁ niṣidhya sarvāntarātmānam īśam upāsasveti vyākhyānāt na kācit śaṅkā \|\|21\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One cannot say that awareness is “simply awareness,” since it involves existence and awareness using subject and object. One cannot say that awareness of sound is simply awareness, since the proponent also accepts that knowledge, the function of ātmā, is eternal. One cannot claim that this awareness is the svarūpa of ātmā, since in childhood I recognize certain persons as parents and when they are old I do not see them as grandchildren. The experiencer has a continued recognition of I (with different experiences). (It is not simply awareness with no qualities). One cannot say that awareness becomes material on having an object of awareness since the very nature of awareness is to comprehend objects. Receiving knowledge does not make awareness material.**

**mṛgair mṛgāṇāṁ grahaṇaṁ pakṣiṇāṁ pakṣibhir yathā**  
**gajānāṁ ca gajair evaṁ jñeyaṁ jñānena gṛhyate**

**Just as deer perceive other deer, as birds perceive other birds, as elephants perceive other elephants, the ātmā receives objects of knowledge. Mahābhārata**

**jñâna-gamya? purâtana?**

**Jñâna-gamya?: he is attained by knowledge or a combination of karma and jñana. Purâtana?: he exists in all bodies. Viṣṇu-sahasra-nāma**

**Though the eye is revealed by the sun, which is the greatest revealer, the eye does not lose its power to reveal objects. (There is no harm for a conscious being to receive knowledge.) Understanding through inference about inferior, destructible objects can bring about knowledge of the supreme object. One becomes aware of the self in thinking, “I experience this.”**

**Śruti and smṛti confirms that Brahman, the svarūpa of knowledge, is knowable.**

**aupaniṣadaḥ puruṣaḥ**

**The Lord is revealed by the Upaniṣads. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.9.26)**

**jñānaṁ jñeyaṁ jñāna-gamyam**

**He is knowledge itself, the object of knowledge, and obtained by that process of knowledge. BG 13.18**

**Thus ātmā is an experiencer, and awareness is its quality. At the time of revealing an object, it is seen to be self-manifesting. At other times, it is seen to be the object of knowledge.**

**na dṛṣṭer draṣṭāraṁ na mater mantāram**

**There is nothing to see for the seer and nothing to think for the thinker. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad**

**This does not mean that the ātmā does not experience or perceive but rather this is a forbiddance of the jīva to think of himself in this way (to be a jñānī). Rather he should worship the Lord, who dwells in all beings. There should be no doubt about this. \|\|21\|\|**

## Chapter Eight

**Defining the Highest Goal**

### Text 1

*tad evaṁ jñāna-svarūpo jñanāham-arthaś cātmā kartṛtvādimān \| sa ceśo jīvaś ceti dvi-vidhāḥ –tatreśo vibhuḥ sva-dvārā jagat-kartā, svāyatta-vṛttika-prakṛti-dvāṛa tu tad upādānaṁ ceti “prakṛtiś ca pratijñā-dṛṣṭāntānurodhāt” ityādikāṁ sutra-pañcakāt tad-bhāsyāc cāvasīyate \| prakrti-jīva-rūpāt prapañcāt tad-āśrayasyeśvarasya bhedas tv ānandamayādy-adhikaraṇebhyaḥ siddhaḥ \|\|1\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Therefore the ātmā has a svarūpa of knowledge, has a sense of I and performs actions. Ātmā has two types: the Lord and the jīvas. The Lord is all-pervading, and produces the material universe. Through prakṛti which is dependent on him, he is the material cause of the universe. This is understood from the Vedānta-sūtras as well:**

**prakṛtiś ca pratijñā-dṛṣṭāntānurodhāt**

**The Lord is also the material cause because of correlation of the proposition and the example. Vedānta-sūtra 1.4.23**

**It is established that the Lord, their shelter, is different from both jīva and prakṛti, through the topic of ānandamaya in the Brahma-sūtras. \|\|1\|\|**

### Text 2

*tasya svarūpa-lakṣaṇaṁ satyānanda-jñānatvaṁ jagaj-janmādi-hetutvaṁ ca cid-acic-chaktimān asau jagan-nimittopādānatāṁ bhajatīti tad ubhaya-rūpatāyāḥ svato brahma-gatatvenaupādhikatvābhāvād upalakṣaṇatva-nāmaka-taṭasta-lakṣaṇatva-niyamo ‘pi nātīva samañjasaḥ \| jagaj-janamādi-hetutvam eva brahmaṇaḥ śāstra-kṛdbhir darśitaṁ—“janmādyasya yataḥ” iti \| itarathā lakṣaṇāsaṅgatiḥ \| jagac ca paramārthataḥ saytam ity upapāditaṁ \|\|2\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The svarūpa-lakṣaṇa of Brahman is eternal bliss and knowledge, and being the cause of the universe. Endowed with spiritual and material śakti, Brahman is the efficient (nimitta) and material (upādāna) cause of the universe. Being both nimitta and upāḍāna are the nature of Brahman. It is improper to say that these features are secondary and metaphorical (different from Brahman’s svarūpa). They are not upādhis. Otherwise, why would Vedānta-sūtras show that Brahman is the source of creation of the universe with the statement** ***janmādy asya yataḥ*****: from Brahman arises the universe? This statement indicates that the universe is real in the true sense of the word. \|\|2\|\|**

### Text 3

*ye tu mṛṣābhūtaṁ jagat rajju-bhujaṅgādivat brahmaṇyaropitaṁ ityāhus tan-mate niradhiṣṭhānaṁ tat syāt \| sāmānyato jñātatve satya-jñāna-viśeṣavataṁ khalv adhiṣṭhānatva-tantraṁ, tac ca nirviśeṣe niḥsāmānye brahmaṇi na sambhavatīti mṛd-ghaṭādayo dṛṣṭāntāḥ śrautāḥ; śukti-rajatādayas tu bauddhā eva \| tasmāj jagan-nimittopādāna-svarūpatvaṁ brahmaṇas tasya paramārthikam \|\|3\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Some claim that the universe is imposed upon Brahman, like the illusion of a snake imposed on a rope. According to them, the universe has no basis at all. Ordinarily, on knowing something, one develops particular knowledge, dependent on a real basis. Brahman cannot be the basis of the illusion since it is without qualities and not an ordinary object (like a rope). The example of a pot coming from clay (so the universe comes from Brahman) is given in the śrutis. The example of rope and snake (illusion of a universe) comes from Buddhism. Thus it is a fact that Brahman is the nimitta and upādāna causes of the universe. \|\|3\|\|**

### Text 4

*evaṁ hi smaranti—“eṣa prakṛtir avyaktaḥ kartā caiva sanātanaḥ \| paraś ca sarva-bhūtebhyaḥ tasmād buddhatamo ‘cyutaḥ \| buddhir mano mahān vāyus tejo ‘mbhaḥ kaṁmahī ca yā \| catur vidhaṁ ca tad bhūtaḥ sarvaṁ kṛṣṇe pratiṣṭhitaṁ \| catur vidhānām; bhūtānāṁ tirṣu kokeṣu mādhavaḥ \| prabhavś caiva sarveṣāṁ nidhaṁa ca yudhiṣṭhīra \|\|” iti “niṣkalaṁ niṣkriyaṁ śāntaṁ niravadyaṁ nirañjanaṁ” ityādyā śrutis; tathāpi na kāścid vikāra iti vaktīti sarvaṁ samañjasam \|\|4\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**eṣa prakṛtir avyaktaḥ kartā caiva sanātanaḥ \|**  
**paraś ca sarva-bhūtebhyaḥ tasmād buddhatamo ‘cyutaḥ**  
**buddhir mano mahān vāyus tejo ‘mbhaḥ khaṁ mahī ca yā \|**  
**catur vidhaṁ ca tad bhūtaṁ sarvaṁ kṛṣṇe pratiṣṭhitaṁ \|**  
**catur vidhānāṁ bhūtānāṁ triṣu lokeṣu mādhavaḥ \|**  
**prabhavaś caiva sarveṣāṁ nidhanaṁ ca yudhiṣṭhīra**

**The Lord is the material cause and the efficient cause. He is superior to all beings and the most intelligent. Mahat-tattva, ahaṅkāra, the five elements known as air, fire, water, ether and earth, the four types of living beings are all situated in Kṛṣṇa. O Yudhiṣṭhira! He is the creator and destroyer of all four beings in the three worlds. Mahābhārata**

**niṣkalaṁ niṣkriyaṁ śāntaṁ niravadyaṁ nirañjanaṁ**

**The Lord is without parts, without action, peaceful, without fault, without blemish. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.19)**

**He is the creator but he does not change. Thus, everything is consistent. \|\|4\|\|**

### Text 5

*jīvas tv aṇuḥ—“balāgra-śata-bhāgasya śatadhā kalpitasya ca \| bhāgo jīva sa vijñeyaḥ sa cānantyāya kalpyate” “eṣo ‘ṇur ātmā cetasā veditavyaḥ” “sūkṣmāṇām apy ahaṁ jīvaḥ” ityādi śruti-smṛtibhyaḥ \| tasya kartṛtvādikaṁ pāramārthikaṁ parāyattaṁ ceti “kartā śāstrārthavattvāt” ityādi sutra-dāśakāt tad-bhāṣyāc ca viditaṁ \|\|5\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The jīva is very small. Śruti says:**

**balāgra-śata-bhāgasya śatadhā kalpitasya ca \|**  
**bhāgo jīva sa vijñeyaḥ sa cānantyāya kalpyate**

**The jīva is known to be a hundredth part of a hundredth part of the tip of a hair and is qualified for liberation. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.9**

**eṣo ‘ṇur ātmā cetasā veditavyaḥ**  
**yasmin prāṇaḥ pañcadhā samviveṣa**

**The atomic** ***ātmā*** **in which the** ***prāṇa*** **in five forms rests is known by the mind. Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad**

**Smṛti says:**

**sūkṣmāṇām apy ahaṁ jīvaḥ**

**Among subtle things I am the spirit soul. SB 11.16.11**

**The jīva is an active agent and dependent on the Lord.**

**This is explained in Brahma-sūtras in ten sūtras.**

**kartā śāstrārthavattvāt**

**The jīva is the agent, because this gives meaning to the scriptures. Vedānta-sūtra 2.3.3.1\|\|5\|\|**

### Text 6

*paramārthato bahutvaṁ ca mantavyam—“jñānena tu tad-ajñānaṁ yeṣāṁ nāśitaṁ ātmanaḥ \| tesāṁ ādityavaj jñānaṁ prakāśayati tat-param” “idaṁ jñāmanm upāśritya” ityādiṣu—vidhvasta-tamasāṁ bhagavat-sāmya-bhājāṁ bahutvabhidhānāt \| jñāna-svarūpasyāpi tasya jñāna-guṇakatvaṁ svīkāryam—“yo vijñāne tiṣṭhan” “manta boddhā kartā vijñānātmā puruṣa” iti śruteḥ \| nitya eva sa guṇaḥ –“na hi vijñātur vijñāter viparilopo vidyate” tyādi śruteḥ “yathā na kriyate jyotsnā mala-prakṣālanātmaneḥ \| doṣa-prahāṇān na jñānam ātmanaḥ kriyate tathā \| yathodapānakhananāt kriyate na jalantaram \| sad eva nīyate vyoktim asataḥ sambhavaḥ kutaḥ \| tathā heya-guṇadhvaṁsād avabodhādayo guṇāḥ \| prakāśyate na janyante nityā evātmano hi te \|\|” iti smṛteś ca \| kevalānubhūtivādasya nirāsāc ca \| itthaṁ ceśa-tulyo ‘pi svarūpa-sāmarthyābhyām alpiyas tenopasarjanatvāt tad-aṁśo ‘sau bhaṇyata ity aṁśādhikareṇe tad-bhāṣye ca vivṛtaṁ \| tad evam nitya-jñānādi-guṇakam anū-caitanyaṁ jīva-svarūpam it siddham \|\|6\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**There are in reality many jīvas.**

**jñānena tu tad-ajñānaṁ yeṣāṁ nāśitaṁ ātmanaḥ \|**  
**tesāṁ ādityavaj jñānaṁ prakāśayati tat-param \|\|**

**When the** ***jīvas’*** **knowledge destroys ignorance, that knowledge, like the sun, reveals the Supreme Lord and the** ***jīvas.*** **BG 5.26**

**idaṁ jñānam upāśritya mama sādharmyam āgatāḥ \|**  
**sarge ’pi nopajāyante pralaye na vyathanti ca**

**Those who have attained qualities similar to mine, by realizing this knowledge through** ***guru,*** **do not take rebirth in this world, and do not experience pain at the time of death. BG 14.2**

**These verses describe how many jīvas, destroying ignorance, attain forms similar to the Lord’s. The jīvas have a svarūpa of knowledge and have knowledge as their attribute.**

**yo vijñāne tiṣṭhan**

**The jīva is situated in knowledge. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.7.22)**

**eṣa hi draṣṭā spraṣṭā śrotā rasayitā ghrātā mantā boddhā kartā vijñānātmā puruṣaḥ**

**The jīva is the seer, the toucher, the hearer, the taster, the smeller, the thinker, the comprehender, the doer, and the knower. (Praśna Upaniṣad 4.9)**

**The quality of knowledge is eternal in the jīva.**

**na hi vijñātur vijñāter viparilopo vidyate**

**The knowledge of the knower does not disappear. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.3.30)**

**yathā na kriyate jyotsnā mala-prakṣālanān maṇeḥ**  
**doṣa-prahāṇān na jñānam ātmanah kriyate tathā**

**Just as by washing away dirt, light is not created in a jewel, so by destroying faults knowledge is not created in the jīva.**

**yathodapāna-khananāt kriyate na jalāntaram**  
**sad eva niyate vyaktim asataḥ sambhavaḥ kutaḥ**

**Just as water is not created by digging a well (but simply manifests), an existing thing simply becomes manifest. How can something not existing appear?**

**tathā heya-guṇa-dhvaṁsād avarodhādayo guṇāḥ**  
**prakāśyante na jānyante nitya evātmano hi te**

**Similarly by destruction of bad qualities, the obscured qualities, which are eternal in the** ***ātmā,*** **manifest and are not created. Viṣṇu-dharma**

**By this, the proposition of pure consciousness (*****kevalānubhūti*****) is refuted. Though the jīva is similar to the Lord, jīva is small in terms of his svarūpa and abilities. Because the jīva is secondary, it is called the Lord’s aṁśa. This is explained in the aṁśādhikaraṇa of the Vedānta-sūtras (2.3.41). Thus it is established that the jīva’s svarūpa is a small particle of consciousness with constant qualities like knowledge etc. \|\|6\|\|**

### Text 7

*yat tu vadanti—brahmaivanubhūtāntaḥkaraṇa upādhi-yogāj jīva-saṁjñam ato brahmāṁśatvaṁ jīvasyeti, tad idaṁ avicāritābidhānam \| tathāhi – na tāvat ṭaṅka-chinna-pāṣāṇa-khaṇḍavad vāstavopādhi-paricchinno brahma-khaṇḍo jīvaḥ, acchedyatvākhaṇḍatvābhyupagamāc ca brahmaṇaḥ, ādimattvāpatteś ca jīvasya \| yata ekasyaiva sato dvaidhīkaraṇam chedaḥ \| nāpy acchinna evopādhi-saṁyukto brahma-pradeśa-viśesaḥ, upādhau gacchaty upādhi-saṁyukta-brahma-pradeśākarṣaṇa-yogād anukṣaṇam upādhi-saṁyukta-brahma-pradeśa-bhedāt pratikṣaṇa-bandha-mokṣāpatteḥ \|\|7\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Some claim that Brahman is the jīva when it is endowed with the upādhi of an antaḥkaraṇa which has awareness. The jīva is a portion of Brahman. This is stated without proper consideration. The jīva cannot be Brahman severed by a real upādhi, like a piece of stone cut by a chisel, since Brahman cannot be cut and cannot be separated into parts. This would also give the jīva a beginning. When one entity is divided in two it called “cutting.”**

**One cannot argue that a limited portion of Brahman become endowed with an upādhi. Since the upādhis (jīva and īśvara) move about, it is not possible for the part of Brahman endowed with upādhis to move about also (Brahman is supposed to be immovable) and at every moment there would be difference in the location of Brahman endowed with upādhis. And at every moment different parts of Brahman would be endowed or unendowed with upādhis and thus bondage and liberation would be continually taking place. \|\|7\|\|**

### Text 8

*na copādhi-saṁyuktaṁ brahma-svarūpam eva saḥ, anupahita-brahma-vyapadeśāsiddheḥ, “ya ātmani tiṣṭhan” ityādi-śruti-virodhāc ca \|\|8\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One cannot also say that the jīva is Brahman endowed with an upādhi since this would contradict the very definition of Brahman—to be unaffected. It would contradict the śruti.**

***ya ātmani tiṣṭhan***

**The Lord resides in the jīva. (Śatapatha-brāhmaṇa 14.5.30)**

### Text 9

*nāpi brahmādhiṣṭhānam upādhir eva saḥ, muktau jīva-nāśāpatteḥ \|\|9\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If the jīva were simply an upādhi on Brahman, in liberation the jīva would disappear. \|\|9\|\|**

### Text 10

*na ca bhrāntaṁ brahmaiva saḥ, sārva-jñyadeśika-śruti-vyākopāt \|\|10\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The jīva cannot be Brahman under illusion, since this would contradict śruti which states that Brahman is full of knowledge. \|\|10\|\|**

### Text 11

*na copādhi-pratibimbitaṁ brahmaiva saḥ, tasya vibhutvān nairūpyāc ca \| na ca nīrūpa-rūpavat tasya saḥ, rūpasya svāśrayatvena dṛṣṭānta-vaiṣamyāt \| na ca javā-puṣpāruṇyavat tasya saḥ, pratibimbatāvachinnaṁ prati-rūpi-dravyasya-hetutvena vivakṣitāsiddheḥ \| na cākāśavat tasya saḥ, vibhutvena nairūpyeṇa ca tan-nirāsāt \| upādhi-paricchinnākāśa-stha-jyotir aṁśasyaiva tat-tayā pratītir avaiduṣī \| itarathā vāyu-kālader api tat-prasaṅgaḥ \| yas tu brahmaṇaḥ pratibimbaṁ pratipādayan, dhvaner iva pratidhvaniṁ tatra nidarśayati, sa kila prācyaṁ prasthitaḥ pratīcim āsīd iti tayor asmad-dharmyād eva \|\|11\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One cannot say that the jīva is Brahman reflected on an upādhi, since Brahman is all pervading and without a form (and thus incapable of being reflected on anything.) One cannot say the jīva is like the reflection of a shapeless form (like air) since the shapeless form would have to be the source of itself (since Brahman cannot be the basis of anything). The reflection of Brahman cannot be like the (shelterless) redness of a hibiscus flower (reflected in a crystal.) The redness actually depends on the flower, but the proposal separates the redness from the flower. One cannot compare the reflection of Brahman to the reflection of sky on water. The sky is also formless and pervading but it simply appears to be reflected on water because of the various luminaries in a limited portion of the sky, which get reflected. Are wind and time reflected in water? To explain the reflection, the example of an echo of sound is raised. It is like a sound in the west that was previously in the east. But that simply supports our position. \|\|11\|\|**

### Text 12

*tac chāstrāṁ tu gauṇyā vṛttyā saṅgitam eta \| evam āha bhagvān sūtrakāraḥ “ambuvad agrahaṇāt tu na tathātvam” “vṛddhi-hrāsa-bhāktam antarbhāvād ubhaya-sāmañjasyād evam” iti \|\|12\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Scriptures which give these examples are given a figurative meaning. This is explained by the author of the Vedānta-sūtras.**

**ambuvad agrahaṇāt tu na tathātvam**

**There is no similarity because one does not see distance between the Lord and the jīva in that way that one sees distance between the sun and water to cause the reflection. Vedānta-sūtra 3.2.19**

**vṛddhi-hrāsa-bhāktam antarbhāvād ubhaya-sāmañjasyād evam**

**The comparison with the sun is accepted in the sense that it illustrates the nature of greatness of the Lord and dependence of the jīva, since this is included in the scriptural conclusion and harmonizes the elements of the comparison. Vedānta-sūtra 3.2.20 \|\|12\|\|**

### Text 13

*tasmād brahmavad anādi-siddho ‘pi jñānātmā jīvaḥ, “nityo nityānām” ityādi-śruteḥ \|\|13\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus it is established that the jīva is eternal with a form of knowledge, like Brahman. Śruti testifies to this.** ***Nityo nityānām*****: the Lord is the chief eternal among all eternals. (Katha Upaniṣad) \|\|13\|\|**

### Text 14

*sa ca tad-bhinno ‘pi tad-chakti-rūpatvāt tad-aṁśo nigadyate—“itas tv anyām prakrtim viddhi me parāṁ jiva-bhūtām” iti smṛtes tac-chakti-rūpatvaṁ tasya siddham \| ataeva sambandhāpekṣāvatvaṁ ca; varṇitaṁ caitad aṁśādhikaraṇa-bhāsye \|\|14\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Though the jīva is different from the Lord, the jīva is said to be his aṁśa since the jīva is a śakti of the Lord.**

**itas tv anyām prakrtim viddhi me parāṁ jīva-bhūtām**

**But understand my superior energy which is different from this inferior energy, the** ***jīvas*****. BG 7.5**

**This shows that jīva is the Lord’s śakti. The jīva is dependent on the Lord. This is confirmed by the author of Vedānta-sūtras in aṁśādhikaraṇa (Vedānta-sūtras (2.3.41). \|\|14\|\|**

### Text 15

*tasya tad-aṁśa-bhūtasya svāṁśi-bhagavād-vaimukhyāt māyayā paribhavas tat-sāmukhye tu*  
*sā vilīyate \| tat svarupa-sākṣātkāraś ca sadaiva sphurati—“kṣaraṁ pradhānam amṛtākṣaraṁ haraḥ kṣarātmānāvīśate deva ekaḥ \| tasyābhidhyānāt yojanāt tattva-bhāvād bhūyaś cānte viśva-māyā-nivṛttiḥ” \| “na sa punar āvartate” ityādi-śravaṇāt \|\|15\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Though the jīva is an aṁśa of the Lord, because of aversion to the Lord, jīva is conquered by māyā. When the jīva becomes favorable to the Lord, māyā disappears. Then the jīva directly experiences his svarūpa.**

**kṣaraṁ pradhānam amṛtākṣaraṁ haraḥ kṣarātmānāv īśate deva ekaḥ \|**  
**tasyābhidhyānāt yojanāt tattva-bhāvād bhūyaś cānte viśva-māyā-nivṛttiḥ**

***Pradhāna*** **is subject to change.**  ***Paramātmā*** **is immortal and unchanging. The one Lord controls pradhāna and the jīva. By meditation on the Lord, by samādhi, and by understanding the jīva and the Lord, the jīva attains freedom from māyā. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

**sa khalv eva vartayan yāvad āyuṣam brahmalokam abhisampadyate na ca punar avartate.**

**Living his life in this way, he attains the world of Brahman and does not return. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 8.15.1 \|\|15\|\|**

### Text 16

*tad-vaimukhyaṁ cānādi-kālikām api sat-saṅgad vinaśyati; tat-sāmmukhyaṁ cāvirbhavati—“anādimāyayāsupto yadā jīvaḥ prabudhyate \| ajam anidram asvapnam advaitaṁ budhyate tadā” “mahat-seva dvāraṁ āhur vimuktes tamodvāraṁ yoṣitāṁ saṅgi-saṅgaṁ \| mahāntas te sama-cittāḥ praśāṇtā vimanyavaḥ suhrdaḥ sādhvo ye” ityādi śruti-smṛtibhyaḥ \|\|16\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The beginningless aversion of the jīva is destroyed by association with devotees. Then an inclination to the Lord appears.**

**anādi-māyayā supto yadā jīvaḥ prabudhyate \|**  
**ajam anidram asvapnam advaitaṁ budhyate tadā**

**When the jīva bewildered by beginningless māyā awakens, Brahman with no birth, no sleep, no dreaming manifests at all times. Māṇḍukya Upaniṣad**

**mahat-seva dvāraṁ āhur vimuktes tamodvāraṁ yoṣitāṁ saṅgi-saṅgaṁ \|**  
**mahāntas te sama-cittāḥ praśāṇtā vimanyavaḥ suhrdaḥ sādhvo ye**

**Service to saintly persons is the cause of two types of liberation, whereas association with men who associate with women is the cause of bondage. The impersonalist sees no differences and is peaceful whereas the devotee has a sincere heart and his intelligence is fixed in me. Both types of saintly persons are devoid of anger, are friendly to all and do not see faults in others. SB 5.5.2 \|\|16\|\|**

### Text 17

*satsaṅga-viśuddhe jīve jñānānandātmaka-bhagavat-svarūpāvarakāvidyā-viśeṣa-vināśātmā, vedānta-janya-bhagavad-viṣayaka-mānasāparokṣa-prāg-abhāva-vināśātmā vā bhagavat-sambandhaḥ \| sa eṣa sat-saṅga-hetukād bhagavat-prasāda-śabditāt tat sāmmukhyād eva bhavati \| tato ‘viratānuvrṭtyā jīvaṁ prati bhagavad-guṇāvarako ‘vidyā-viśeṣo vinaśyati; tatas tam asau sākṣātkarotīti dvayor avidyayor dhvamṣo mokṣaḥ \|\|17\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**When the jīva is purified by the association of devotees, he establishes a relationship with the Lord which by its nature destroys ignorance which conceals the Lord’s svarūpa (the Lord’s form becomes manifested), or one establishes a relationship which destroys the previous absence of a mental perception of the Lord. This relationship is generated by Vedānta. That relationship arises from developing favorability to the Lord, which is called the Lord’s mercy, through association with devotees. Then, by continual bhakti (prema) it destroys the ignorance which conceals the qualities of the Lord (the Lord’s qualities become manifested). Then the jīva directly meets the Lord (full of qualities and pastimes). Liberation means destruction of ignorance concerning the Lord’s form and qualities. \|\|17\|\|**

### Text 18

*evam evāha śrutiḥ—“vimuktaś ca vimucyate” iti “bhūyaś cānte viśva-ṁāyā-nivṛttiḥ” iti ca \| ye tv anayā śrutyā bandhāmithyātvaṁ pratipādayanti, te khalu tad-viṣayaka-vedāprāmāṇya-karanān nāstikā evety abhāni prāk \|\|18\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Śruti explains this:**

**Situated in the body with eleven gates belonging to the unborn omniscient Lord, the liberated jīva does not lament and is free of illusion. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 2.2.1**

**bhūyaś cānte viśva-ṁāyā-nivṛttiḥ**

**After the māyā covering the Lord’s svarūpa is destroyed, the māyā covering the Lord’s qualities is destroyed. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

**Those who by quoting śruti say that bondage is illusory are actually nāstikas since they misinterpret the Vedas. This was explained previously. \|\|18\|\|**

### Text 19

*dhvaṁsa-kāryādi nityam, abhāva-rūpatvāt anuvṛtti-śāstrāc ca \|\|19\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This destruction of ignorance concerning the Lord’s form and qualities is eternal, since it no longer exists and scriptures state this. \|\|19\|\|**

### Text 20

*na ca parokṣa-svabhāvasya harer aparokṣaṁ katham iti vācyam; tādṛśasyāpi tasya devādivat ṣaḍjādivac ca sārvadikānuśīlanena tat-sambhavāt āha caivaṁ bhagavān sūtrakaraḥ—“prakāśaś ca karmaṇyabhyāsāt” iti \| evaṁ ca duḥkha-hāni-sukha-lābha-rūpo mokṣaḥ \|\|20\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One cannot argue that the Lord, being invisible, can never be seen. By intense practice (and receiving the Lord’s mercy) it is possible to meet the Lord directly, just as by intense practice it is possible to hear the notes of the scale which are originally beyond hearing or by intense practice one can see devatās. The author of the Vedānta-sūtra says:**

**prakāśaś ca karmaṇyabhyāsāt**

**The Lord appears by the practice of devotional acts. Vedānta-sūtra 3.2.26**

### Text 21

*yat tv āhuḥ—muktāv aham-artho vinaśyati cit-sukha-rūpam tu kiṁcid avaśiṣyate tad evātmā-vastu pumartha-svarūpam iti, tad atīva-mandaṁ, kenāpy ananubhūtasya jñānānandatvaṁ jñānānandānanubhavituś cātmatvaṁ iti paribhāṣā-mātratvāt \| īdṛk pumārtho hi vigītaḥ—“duḥkha-hānir sukha-prāptiḥ śreyas tatreha ceṣyate” iti smṛteḥ \| “duḥkha-śūnyaḥ sukhī syāṁ’ iti icchaiva tattve mānaṁ tad-abhāvād apramaṇaṁ tat-kalpanam \| tad ubhaya-rūpaḥ pumarthaḥ khalv ātmāvadhiko dṛṣṭaḥ \| ātmāno ‘ham-arthsya vināśe tu nāsau sambhavet \|\|21\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Someone may say, “In the state of liberation, the sense of I is destroyed. The form of consciousness and happiness alone remains. That is ātmā, the goal of all endeavors.” That is a foolish statement, since in attaining that goal there would be no one to experience the consciousness and bliss. Attaining ātmā would be a useless attainment, with no experience of knowledge or bliss. Such non-attainment of the goal is criticized:**

**duḥkha-hāniḥ sukhāvāptiḥ**  
**śreyas tan neha ceṣyate**

**It is not possible to attain the goal of destroying suffering and attaining happiness by karmas. SB 4.25.4**

**One desires eradication of suffering and attainment of happiness. These two goals are treasured. Without those attainments liberation is useless. One sees this twofold goal as the ultimate for some jīvas. But with the destruction of the sense of I, this goal becomes completely impossible. \|\|21\|\|**

### Text 22

*yat tv āhuḥ --ātmā khalu duḥkha-hetuḥ pāpārjanāt atas tan-nāśād eva duḥkha-nivṛttiḥ syād iti, tat kila kubuddhi-vijṛmbitam eva \| jāgara eva duḥkhodayo, na tu suṣuptāv ity anvaya-vyatirekābhyaṁ ātmano ‘ham-arthasya tatrāhetutvāt \| kiṁca, śarīrendriya-viśaya-vedanāder api tatra hetutvam \| jñāna-bhogābhyāṁ karmaṇo vināśe sati, hetv abhavena śarīrādy- anutpattau syāt eva duḥkha-nivṛtir ato ‘nucita evātma-vināśa-svīkaraḥ \|\|22\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Buddhists claim as follows. “Ātmā is the cause of suffering because of performing sins. With the extinction of the ātmā, the suffering ends.”**

**This is a very foolish idea. Suffering arises in the waking state (when the senses are active). During deep sleep, there should be no suffering. By positive and negative examples, it is understood that the ātmā with a sense of I is not the cause. Rather the body, senses, and awareness of objects are also causes. When karma is destroyed by either knowledge or enjoyment of the karma, there will be no cause of suffering. Body and senses will no longer arise. Suffering will be destroyed. One does not have to destroy ātmā to destroy suffering. \|\|22\|\|**

### Text 23

*kiṁca, duḥkhābhāvo ‘py ātmā-niṣṭhaḥ san pumarthaḥ, anyathā stambhādi-niṣṭho ‘pi sa tathā syāt \| tasmād ātma-bhinnānām eva duḥkha-hetūnāṁ muktau vinivṛttir na tv ātmānaś ca \| itarathā stambhādi-śūnyatāpi muktiḥ syāt \| ātmā-śūnyatva-stambhādi-śūnyatvayor viśeṣāṅgīkāre nirviśeṣatva-kṣatiḥ \| api ca—ātma-vināṣaḥ mokṣaḥ kim ātmānaḥ phalam anātmano vā? nādyaḥ—tasyaivābhāvāt; na hi phalino’ bhāve phalam upapadyate \| netaraḥ—anātmanas tenopakārābhāvāt \| tasmād upekṣyā kubuddhayaḥ \|\|23\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Moreover the absence of suffering should be related to the ātmā or self. That should be the goal. Otherwise the goal could be defined as absence of suffering related to a pillar (or any other object). Thus, when causes of suffering other than ātmā are destroyed in liberation, the ātmā should also not destroyed. If destruction of ātmā were taken as the goal, then destruction of a pillar could also be considered liberation. If we distinguish between absence of ātmā and absence of a pillar, then the concept of nirviśeṣa (not making distinctions at all) is destroyed.**

**If destruction of ātmā is liberation, is this a result for ātmā or a result which is not for the ātmā? One cannot say it is a result for ātmā, since ātmā no longer exists. A result cannot exist unless there is an enjoyer of the result. The result cannot be for “not for the ātmā,” since then liberation has no purpose at all. Thus this proposition is unintelligent. \|\|23\|\|**

### Text 24

*tad evaṁ sarveśvarasya bhagavataḥ śyāma-sundarasya jīva-jaḍātmakāt prapañcād bhedas taj-jñāna-bhaktibhyāṁ jivasyātyantika-duḥkha-hāniḥ sukha-prāpti-sambhava iti krṭsnaṁ śāstraṁ jīveśa-bheda-paraṁ nirṇītam \|\|24\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus the supreme Lord, Śyama-sundara is different from the jīva and matter of this world. It is possible for the jīva to destroy suffering with finality and attain happiness by jñāna and bhakti. All scriptures establish the difference between the jīva and the Lord. \|\|24\|\|**

### Text 25

*tatra yāny abheda-parāṇīva vākyāni kānicit pratīyante, tāni kvacit tan-mātrāyatta-vṛttikatayā, tan-niṣṭhatayā, tad-vyāpyatayā vā, viśvaṁ tad-ātmakam iti bodhayeyuḥ \| kvacij jīveśayoḥ sthānaikyān matyaikyāc cābhedaṁ bodhayanti \| kvacana śakteḥ jīva-jaḍa-rūpāyāḥ śakitmataḥ pareśād anyatvād abhedam āhuḥ \| kvacic ca bhagavad-āvirbhāveṣu pratītaṁ svagata-bhedaṁ nivārayanīti sarvam anavadyam \|\|25\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Those statements of scripture which appear to state nondifference should be understood as follows. The universe is the Lord because its existence is dependent on him, because it is supported by him, and because it is pervaded by him. Sometimes those statements indicate nondifference because of oneness of place (such as when jīva and the Lord meeting in the spiritual world) and oneness of minds (when jīva develops bhakti). Sometimes the jīva and the Lord are considered one because the Lord is the śaktimān and the jīva and matter are śakti. Sometimes nondifferent is stated to show that there is no difference between the different avatāra forms of the Lord. Thus scripture is without fault. \|\|25\|\|**

### Text 26

*pare evaṁ vākyārthaṁ varṇayanti “ātmaivedām sarvam” “sarvaṁ khalv idaṁ brahma” “neha nānasti kiñcana” ityādyāḥ śrutayaḥ sarveśvarasya brahmaṇaḥ sarvābhedam āhuḥ, “pṛthag ātmānaṁ preritāraṁ ca matvā” “juṣṭaṁ yadā paśyati anyam īśam” “dvā suparṇā sayujā sakhāyā” “karma-kṣaye yāti sa tattvato ‘nyaḥ” “nirañjanaḥ paramaṁ sāmyam upaiti” ityādās tu tasya sarvasmād bhedam \| dvi-vidhayoḥ śrutyor mitho virudhārthatayā pratītāv api tayor aprāmāṇyasyānyāyatvāt \| dvayoh prāmāṇye sambhavaty ekatarāprāmāṇyasyāpi tathātvād viṣaya-bhedena na ? vyavasthā tayor vaktavyā \| tathāhi subālopaniṣadi—“antaḥ śarīre nihito guhāyām aja eko nityaḥ” ityādau “yasya pṛthivī śarīraṁ yasyāpaḥ śarīraṁ, yasya tejaḥ śarīraṁ, yasa vāyuḥ śarīraṁ, yasākāśaḥ śarīraṁ, yasya manaḥ śarīraṁ, yasya buddhiḥ śarīraṁ, yasyāhaṅkāraḥ śarīraṁ, yasya cittaṁ śarīraṁ, yasāvyaktaṁ śarīraṁ, yasyākṣaraṁ śarīraṁ yasya mṛtyuḥ śarīram eṣa sarva-bhūtāntarātmāpahata-pāpmā divyo deva eko nārāyaṇaḥ” ityabhidhādād aikya-śrutīnāṁ śarīra-śarīri-bhāvenābheda-viṣayo dvaita-śrutīnāṁ tu svarūpa-bhedaḥ saḥ \| itarathā jīva-gata-doṣā brahmaṇi prasajyeran iti bhedābheda-śrutyor viṣayā-bheda-pradarśanān mitho-viruddhārtha-pratīti nirvartitā \|\|26\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Other Vaiṣṇavas explain as follows. Some śrutis speak of nondifference of everything from Brahman:**

**ātmaivedam sarvam**

**Everything is ātmā. Chāndogya Upaniṣad**

**sarvaṁ khalv idaṁ brahma**

**Everything is Brahman. Chāndogya Upaniṣad**

**neha nānāsti kiñcana**

**There is no variety at all. Kaṭha Upaniṣad**

**On the other hand, some śrutis state that the Lord or Brahman is different from everything.**

**pṛthag ātmānaṁ preritāraṁ ca matvā juṣṭas tatas tenāmṛtatvam eti**

**Knowing himself to be different from He who inspires actions, invoking the pleasure of the Lord, that person attains liberation.** ***Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad*** **1.6**

**juṣṭaṁ yadā paśyati anyam īśam**

**When he sees the glorious Lord who is different, he gives up lamentation. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 3.1.2**

**dvā suparṇā sayujā sakhāyā**

**There are two birds in a tree. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

**karma-kṣaye yāti sa tattvato ‘nyaḥ**

**With destruction of karma, the jiva attains another existence. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

**nirañjanaḥ paramaṁ sāmyam upaiti**

**He, being pure, attains a form similar to the supreme. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 3.1.3)**

**It would not proper to conclude that, because they are mutually contradictory, both statements have no value as proof. If both types of statements are to be accepted as authoritative, they should be explained in terms of dealing with different subjects.**

**antaḥ-śarīre nihito guhāyām aja eko nityaḥ**

**There is one eternal being dwelling within the body. Subala Upaniṣad**

**yasya pṛthivī śarīraṁ yasyāpaḥ śarīraṁ, yasya tejaḥ śarīraṁ, yasya vāyuḥ śarīraṁ, yasyākāśaḥ śarīraṁ, yasya manaḥ śarīraṁ, yasya buddhiḥ śarīraṁ, yasyāhaṅkāraḥ śarīraṁ, yasya cittaṁ śarīraṁ, yasyāvyaktaṁ śarīraṁ, yasyākṣaraṁ śarīraṁ yasya mṛtyuḥ śarīram eṣa sarva-bhūtāntarātmāpahata-pāpmā divyo deva eko nārāyaṇaḥ**

**The one Nārāyaṇa, free of sin, dwells within all beings (duality). His body is the earth (oneness), his body is water, his body is fire, his body is air, his body is ether, his body is mind his body is intelligence, his body is ahaṅkāra, his body is citta, his body is avyakta, his body is akṣara, his body is mṛtyu. Subala Upaniṣad**

**According to this, statements of oneness express nondifference in terms of body and possessor of the body (nondifference of the Lord and his form). The statements of duality express difference between the svarūpa of the Lord and the svarūpa jīvas. Without accepting difference, the faults of the jīva would be applied to the Lord. Thus the claim of contradictory statements is rejected since there are difference of subject in the two types of statements. \|\|26\|**

### Text 27

*cij-jaḍayor bhedasya cābhedasya ca svābhāvikatve vyāghātāt jaḍābheda-sādhakānāṁ ca puṁsāṁ jāḍyopapādaktvena sva-vyāghātāc ca jīva-pradhāna-brahmābhedānupapatttes teṣāṁ svarūpa-bhedaḥ sidhyatīty asmin api pakṣe jīveṣa-svarūpa-bheda-param evaṁ śāstram iti sphuṭam \|\|27\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It would be problematic to say that conscious entities and matter are both one and different by their very natures. If the practitioners were nondifferent from matter as claimed, they would have to be accepted as insentient matter with no sense of a self. Simultaneous difference and nondifference of their svarūpas would mean that jīva, matter and Brahman would be the same (and thus scriptures would be useless). Thus, it must be established that they are different in terms of their svarūpas. And thus it is clear that difference in the svarūpa of the jīva and the Lord is the conclusion of scriptures. \|\|27\|\|**

### Text 28

*navīnās tu kecin manyante kārya-kāraṇayor bhedābhedābhyāṁ tathā deha-dehinor guṇa-guṇinor jīveśayoś ca svarita-svara-vat \| svaritasya yatha udāttatvānudāttatvābhyāṁ dvirūpatvām svaritatvena tv eka-rūpyam iti \| tathāhi—kārya-kāraṇayor bhede hi kapālāder gurutvam ekaṁ ghaṭādeś caikam iti tulārope dviguṇaṁ tat syāt } abhede tu jalānayanādi-kāryāpahāraḥ kāraka-vyāpāra-vaiyarthyaṁ ca \| tasmāt tayor bhedābhedau syātāṁ \| na ca virodhaḥ—eka-rūpeṇa virodhe ‘pi bhinna-rūpeṇavirodhāt \| mṛttikātvādinābhedaḥ ghaṭattva-kapālātvādinā tu bheda iti deha-dehinor guṇa-gunīnoś ca brahmatvenābhedaḥ gauṇatvena, mukhyatvena ca bhedaḥ evaṁ jīveśayoś ca \| itthaṁ ca tat tat-viṣayakāṇi vacāṁsy āñjasyaṁ bhuñjīran iti \| naitat cāru, yadi jīveśayoḥ svarūpeṇaivābhedas tarhīśasyāpy āṁśika-sukha-duḥkha-bhogaḥ jīvasya ca jagat-karṭtvādi \| yadi “vācaṁ dhenum” ityādi-vād āropita evāsau matas tadā nayāntaratvāsiddhiḥ \| svaritas tv ardha-nārīsvarādivat dvī-rūpatvān na tatra dṛṣṭāntī-bhāvaḥ \| jalānayanādi-kāryāpahnuvādibhiḥ kārya-kāraṇābheda-bādhas tu na syāt \| tad ananyatvādhikaraṇe tad-bhāsye tad-bhedasya samarthitatvāt iti \| tasmāt kalpanā-mātram idam \|\|28\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**However, some modern thinkers proposes difference and nondifference of cause and effect. They accept difference and nondifference of body and possessor of the body (the Lord and is body), quality and possessor of the quality and jīva and the Lord, just like the intermediate note (svarita). Difference and nondifference is like the intermediate tone (svarita) between high and low pitches in chanting the Vedas. The high and low are two different pitches, but the middle pitch is one.**

**To illustrate difference of cause and effect, a pot and a broken pot, when weighed together produce double the weight. Similarly, to illustrate difference of cause and effect, carrying water would not be carried out by using a broken pot. The act of carrying would also be useless using a broken pot. Therefore cause and effect must be different (as illustrated above) and nondifferent (pot and broken pot are the same substance). There is no contradiction, since thought there is nondifference in one aspect (same clay), there is difference in another aspect. There is nondifference in being clay and there is difference in being pot and broken pot. Body and possessor of the body (Lord), quality (omniscience etc.) and possessor of the quality (Lord), by all being Brahman (knowledge and bliss), are nondifferent, and being the body or quality (secondary element) and the possessor of the body and possessor of the quality (primary element), are different. Jīva and the Lord are nondifferent and different in the same way. In this way, the two types of statements which state difference and nondifference are both accepted with literal meaning.**

**But this is not proper. If one accepts that jīva and the Lord are nondifferent by their svarūpas, the Lord to some degree would partake of some happiness and distress and the jīva to some degree would carry out creation. One may claim that the oneness and difference is imagined only, just as śruti says "Worship speech as a cow." (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 5.4.1) but then the proposition fails (becoming difference only).**

**The example of intermediate pitch, like the forms of Pārvatī and Śiva combined, cannot be given, since the higher and lower pitches are two different notes. The example of a broken pot not carrying water does not negate nondifference of cause and effect .This is explained in Vedānta-sūtra’s anyatvādhikaraṇa. Thus this proposal is imaginary. \|\|28\|\|**

### Text 29

*yat tu kecid āhaḥ—ekam eva nirdharmakam iti pare vadanti bhinnaṁ dharmīti tv apare \| paraṁ tv atrobhayatrāpi nopayuktim paśyāmaḥ, bhāgato vākyārtha-kadarthanāt \| kintv ekam eva ta paramārthitaḥ sarvākāram ity eva samyak \| etan-mate jīveśvarayoḥ svarūpaikye ‘pi vyuha-tadvator iva vailakṣaṇya-vyavahāro nityas tena na kvāpi vākya-vyākopaḥ \| nāpy atra sarvathā dharma-vyatikaraḥ kvacid aikye pi sati tad adarśanāt \| na hi ghaṭa-kapālayor guṇa-guṇinoś ca saty api tasmims tam īkṣāmahe \| saguṇa-nirguṇa-vākyayos tu hiṁsāhiṁsā-vākyavad viṣaya-bhedena vyavastheti sarvaṁ niravadyam iti \|\|29\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Some speak in the following manner. “There is only one entity without qualities according to some (Śaṅkara). Others say that Brahman is the possessor of qualities (Madhva). We see that both views are not correct since they must interpret any scriptural statements which support the opponent’s views. But Brahman is all forms (jīva, the Lord, prakṛti and time) from the absolute point of view (paramārthika). Though the jīva and the Lord are one in svarūpa, they appear to be eternally different by convention only, just as the Viṣṇu and his expansions like Vāsudeva are nondifferent. In this manner, no statements of the scriptures are contradicted. In this proposition there is no loss of qualities in the Lord, though they are not visible when oneness appears. We do not see loss of qualities in spite of the nondifference between pot and pieces of pot, and between the Lord and his qualities. The statements of difference and nondifference, like statements of violence and nonviolence, are related to different topics. topics.} Thus there is no fault in the Vedas.” \|\|29\|\|**

### Text 30

*tad etad-avicāritābhidhānaṁ, duḥkha-bhogānyathānupapatter bādhakatvāt \| yadi jīvo nāma brahmaiva syāt tarhi duḥkha-bhāk na syāt \| nanu jaḍa-prapañcasya satvaytve syād ayaṁ doṣaḥ sa tu nayāntaravad ihāpi mithyaiveti cen na, vedāprāmāṇyādi-doṣāpatteḥ \| kiṁca kāruṇyādīnām cātra dattottaratāpattir niruddeṣyatvāt \| tasmād vākyārthāvagāhanāśakteḥ kaiścin manda-matibhiḥ kalpitam idaṁ mata-dvayam ato yat-kiṇcid etat \|\|30\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is not properly considered. Nondifference of the jīva and the Lord would mean that the Lord would suffer. This would negate the scriptures. If the jīva is Brahman, the jīva should not suffer.**

**“But though the world is real, that suffering is false, just like a dream, as the advaita-vādīs claim.” This would make the śruti statements which say the world is real invalid. It would mean that qualities like compassion of the Lord mentioned in the scriptures would also be invalid, since the compassion would have no object (except illusory suffering). These two ideas produced by unintelligent minds are thus insignificant, since they are incapable of grasping the meaning of the Vedas. \|\|30\|\|**

### Text 31

*vidyārūpaṁ bhūṣaṇaṁ me pradāya khyātiṁ ninye tena yo mām udāraḥ \|\| śrī-govindaḥ svapna-nirdiṣṭa-bhāṣyo rādhā-bandhur bandurāṅgaḥ sa jīyāt \|\|31\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**May Govinda, endowed with beautiful limbs, who is the friend of Rādhā and who ordered me to write a commentary on Vedānta in a dream, and who is kind to give me the ornament of knowledge and by that produce fame for me, remain ever victorious.**

### Text 32

*yad brahma-sūtreṣu vibhāti bhāṣyaṁ kṛṣṇātmakaṁ vyakta-nava-prameyam \|*  
*tasyopaveṣāya svarṇa-pīṭhaṁ siddānta-ratnaṁ na bhavet kim etat \|\|32\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Should not this Jewel of Conclusions, which clarifies the commentary on the Vedānta-sūtras, whose subject is only Kṛṣṇa, and which reveals new topics, be suitable to place on a golden throne?**

### Text 33

*siddhānta-ratna-kiraṇāt kila bhāṣya-candrād*  
*Advaita-śārvara-harād amala-prakāśāt\|\|*  
*Dṛṣṭaṁ na kiṁ kim iha tattvam udagra-dhībhiḥ*  
*Śrīman murārir-caraṇāmbuja-bhakti-bhāgbhiḥ \|\|33\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Will the intelligent devotees dedicated to the lotus feet of Murārī not see the truth from the pure shining rays of this jewel of truths arising from the moon of the Vedānta commentary that destroys the darkness of asponsdvaitavāda?**
